**Front:** State the two Conservation International criteria for a biodiversity hotspot.
**Definition (learn):** A biodiversity hotspot must have at least 1,500 endemic vascular plant species and must have lost at least 70 percent of its original primary vegetation, i.e., retains 30 percent or less of its original natural habitat.
**Breakdown:**
- Endemism test: at least 1,500 endemic vascular plants
- Loss test: 70 percent of original vegetation lost (≤30 percent remaining)
- Both must be met; species richness alone is not enough
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 basic section 2; ✅ Wikipedia: Biodiversity hotspot, fetched 1 Nov 2026, confirms ≥1,500 endemic plants and ≥70 percent loss).
**Links:** ENV-1082, ENV-1083, ENV-1088
**Trap:** A species-rich forest is not automatically a hotspot.
**Mains hook:** Hotspot triage focuses scarce funds on endemism-to-loss ratio (qualified: non-hotspot areas can still matter).
**Back:** *"Fifteen hundred endemic plants, and seventy percent of habitat gone."*

### Flashcard ENV-1082 — Hotspot concept: origin and revision
**Front:** Who proposed the hotspot concept, and when were the criteria revised?
**Definition (learn):** Norman Myers proposed the concept in 1988 (Threatened biotas: hot spots in tropical forests) and again in 1990. The criteria were revised in the 2000 edition.
**Breakdown:**
- Origin: Myers 1988
- Second statement: 1990
- Revision: 2000 edition (Myers et al.)
- Owner says Conservation International operationalised the thresholds
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 basic: Norman Myers 1988; ✅ Wikipedia: Biodiversity hotspot, fetched 1 Nov 2026, 1988, 1990 and 2000 revision). Attribution conflict: owner credits Conservation International with adding the thresholds; the fetched page credits the 2000 revision (Myers et al.). Use both names with the year.
**Links:** ENV-1081, ENV-1083
**Trap:** Do not attribute the hotspot idea to Conservation International alone; it originated with Myers.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains item; use for accurate attribution.
**Back:** *"Myers proposed it, the 2000 edition set the numbers."*

### Flashcard ENV-1083 — India's four biodiversity hotspots
**Front:** Name India's four biodiversity hotspots.
**Definition (learn):** India hosts four global hotspots: Himalaya, Indo-Burma, Western Ghats-Sri Lanka, and Sundaland (represented in India by the Nicobar Islands).
**Breakdown:**
- Himalaya
- Indo-Burma
- Western Ghats and Sri Lanka
- Sundaland (Nicobar Islands)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 basic section 3 and 6; ✅ Wikipedia: Biodiversity hotspot, fetched 1 Nov 2026, places Nicobar within Sundaland).
**Links:** ENV-1084, ENV-1085, ENV-1086, ENV-1087
**Trap:** Only one or two Indian hotspots is wrong; there are four.
**Mains hook:** Map each hotspot to its endemism driver (qualified).
**Back:** *"Himalaya, Indo-Burma, Western Ghats-Sri Lanka, Sundaland-Nicobar: four in all."*

### Flashcard ENV-1084 — Western Ghats-Sri Lanka hotspot
**Front:** What drives endemism in the Western Ghats-Sri Lanka hotspot?
**Definition (learn):** The Western Ghats-Sri Lanka hotspot is an ancient, climatically stable, high-rainfall montane block. The owner reports very high amphibian and freshwater-fish endemism.
**Breakdown:**
- Driver: ancient, stable, high-rainfall terrain
- Endemism: amphibians, freshwater fishes, plants
- Owner example: Western Ghats amphibian endemism; name the taxon and assessment year when citing (ENV-1124)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv section 4; ✅ owner 04 basic section 5).
**Links:** ENV-1083, ENV-1063
**Trap:** Name the taxon and assessment year when citing amphibian endemism; do not cite a timeless list.
**Mains hook:** Use the Ghats to argue for landscape connectivity (qualified).
**Back:** *"Old wet mountains hold frogs and fishes found nowhere else."*

### Flashcard ENV-1085 — Himalaya hotspot
**Front:** What drives endemism in the Himalaya hotspot?
**Definition (learn):** The Himalaya hotspot has an extreme altitudinal gradient over a short horizontal distance, producing endemism banded by elevation zone.
**Breakdown:**
- Driver: altitudinal gradient
- Pattern: endemism banded by life zone (ENV-1008)
- Relevance: cold-desert and alpine zones are separately managed
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv section 4).
**Links:** ENV-1083, ENV-1008, ENV-1063
**Trap:** The Himalaya is not a single uniform forest zone; endemism follows elevation bands.
**Mains hook:** Climate-driven upslope shift threatens high-altitude bands (qualified).
**Back:** *"Climb the Himalaya, change the endemics."*

### Flashcard ENV-1086 — Indo-Burma hotspot
**Front:** What drives endemism in the Indo-Burma hotspot?
**Definition (learn):** Indo-Burma sits at a biogeographic crossroads with complex river-valley and mountain-ridge topography, producing high freshwater and reptile endemism.
**Breakdown:**
- Driver: complex river-valley and ridge topography
- Position: biogeographic crossroads
- Endemism: freshwater fish and reptiles
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv section 4).
**Links:** ENV-1083, ENV-1084
**Trap:** Do not confuse Indo-Burma with Western Ghats-Sri Lanka; their endemism drivers differ.
**Mains hook:** Northeast biodiversity links to Indo-Burma (qualified).
**Back:** *"Crossroads and valleys: fish and reptiles stay put."*

### Flashcard ENV-1087 — Sundaland and the Nicobar Islands
**Front:** Why is the Nicobar Islands' endemism concentrated in a small area?
**Definition (learn):** The Nicobar Islands are India's portion of the Sundaland hotspot. Island isolation from mainland biota produces high plant and small-vertebrate endemism confined to a small land area.
**Breakdown:**
- Driver: island isolation
- Pattern: endemism in small habitat area
- Risk: no natural recolonisation after collapse (ENV-1116)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv section 4; ✅ Wikipedia: Biodiversity hotspot, fetched 1 Nov 2026, Nicobar within Sundaland).
**Links:** ENV-1083, ENV-1116
**Trap:** Sundaland is not the whole of India's hotspot network; Nicobar is its Indian representation.
**Mains hook:** Island endemics need active protection (qualified).
**Back:** *"Islands isolate; the Nicobar holds endemics in a small space."*

### Flashcard ENV-1088 — Hotspot status is not a legal category
**Front:** Does hotspot designation give Indian legal protection?
**Definition (learn):** Hotspot designation is an international scientific prioritisation framework (from Conservation International criteria). It is not a binding Indian legal category and does not replace domestic protected-area or biodiversity law.
**Breakdown:**
- Hotspot: scientific label
- Legal categories: National Park, Sanctuary, Biodiversity Heritage Site, eco-sensitive zone (distinct)
- Hotspot informs, does not protect by itself
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 basic section 4 and 7).
**Links:** ENV-1081, ENV-1090, ENV-1129
**Trap:** A hotspot is not like a National Park.
**Mains hook:** Hotspot triage must be converted into domestic law (qualified).
**Back:** *"Hotspot is a map label, not a legal fence."*

### Flashcard ENV-1089 — Megadiverse countries and LMMC
**Front:** What is a megadiverse country, and what group does India belong to?
**Definition (learn):** Megadiverse countries hold a very high share of world species diversity. India is one. The owner states they coordinate politically as the Like-Minded Megadiverse Countries (LMMC), which India helped found and which pushes access-and-benefit-sharing positions in CBD negotiations.
**Breakdown:**
- Megadiverse: high share of species
- LMMC: political coordination group
- Negotiating position: access and benefit sharing
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-stated (owner 04 basic section 2); the LMMC founding details were not verified in this session.
**Links:** ENV-1096, ENV-1098
**Trap:** Megadiverse country lists are periodically revised; attribute the claim to its source.
**Mains hook:** Use LMMC as an example of biodiversity diplomacy (qualified).
**Back:** *"Many species, many countries, one negotiating bloc."*

### Flashcard ENV-1090 — Biological Diversity Act 2002: three tiers
**Front:** Name the three tiers established by the Biological Diversity Act, 2002, and their roles.
**Definition (learn):** National Biodiversity Authority (NBA, Chennai) at national level; State Biodiversity Boards (SBBs) at state level; Biodiversity Management Committees (BMCs) at local level.
**Breakdown:**
- NBA: prior approval for covered access by section 3(2) persons; research-result, IP and ABS functions
- SBB: prior intimation under section 7; may restrict detrimental activity
- BMC: local conservation, documentation, PBR, consultation and collection-fee functions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv section 2; NBA Chennai ✅ owner 04 adv table).
**Links:** ENV-1091, ENV-1092, ENV-1095
**Trap:** Do not reduce the architecture to "NBA for foreigners and SBB for every Indian."
**Mains hook:** The three tiers mirror the three biodiversity levels (qualified; analytical parallel).
**Back:** *"NBA national, SBB state, BMC village: three tiers, three jobs."*

### Flashcard ENV-1091 — Section 3(2) versus section 7
**Front:** Distinguish the section 3(2) route from the section 7 route under the Biological Diversity Act.
**Definition (learn):** Persons described in section 3(2), including non-citizens, NRIs and covered foreign-controlled entities, need prior NBA approval for access for research, bio-survey or commercial use. Persons outside section 3(2) use section 7 prior intimation to the SBB for commercial utilisation, subject to exemptions.
**Breakdown:**
- Section 3(2): prior NBA approval
- Section 7: prior intimation to SBB, with exemptions
- Ordinary domestic non-commercial research is not converted into an NBA case
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv section 3 and 8).
**Links:** ENV-1090, ENV-1092
**Trap:** Generic NBA approval for every Indian researcher is wrong.
**Mains hook:** The applicant-and-purpose boundary is the test for ABS compliance (qualified).
**Back:** *"Section 3(2) asks NBA first; section 7 says tell the SBB."*

### Flashcard ENV-1092 — Section 7 exemptions
**Front:** Name the groups the owner lists as exempt under section 7.
**Definition (learn):** Section 7 exemptions include codified traditional knowledge, qualifying cultivated medicinal plants or products, local people and communities, growers and cultivators, and specified vaids, hakims and registered AYUSH practitioners practising for sustenance and livelihood.
**Breakdown:**
- Traditional knowledge (codified)
- Cultivated medicinal plants (BMC certificate-of-origin process)
- Local people and communities, growers, cultivators
- Vaids, hakims, registered AYUSH practitioners (livelihood)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv section 3, statutory summary as owner states).
**Links:** ENV-1091, ENV-1090
**Trap:** Exemptions are bounded; do not treat them as blanket exemptions.
**Mains hook:** Exemptions protect livelihoods; enforcement must still check the bounds (qualified).
**Back:** *"Local hands, local herbs, local knowledge: the exempt list."*

### Flashcard ENV-1093 — Current ABS rules: 2024 Rules, 2025 amendment, 2025 Regulations
**Front:** Which delegated instruments currently govern ABS according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The Biological Diversity Rules, 2024 superseded the 2004 Rules and came into force on 21 December 2024. The Biological Diversity (Amendment) Rules, 2025 apply from 1 November 2025 and revise the certificate-of-origin process. The 2025 ABS Regulations superseded the 2014 ABS Regulations and cover digital sequence information (DSI).
**Breakdown:**
- 2024 Rules: supersede 2004 Rules (in force 21 Dec 2024)
- 2025 Amendment Rules: apply from 1 Nov 2025 (Rule 19 web-portal certificate)
- 2025 ABS Regulations: supersede 2014 Regulations; DSI benefit-sharing modes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-stated, time-sensitive instrument dates (owner 04 adv sections 3 and 10). Not fetched; verify before quoting.
**Links:** ENV-1091, ENV-1098
**Trap:** Do not apply superseded 2004 procedures as current law.
**Mains hook:** DSI extends the genetic level into digital data (qualified).
**Back:** *"2024 Rules replace 2004; 2025 Regulations replace 2014."*

### Flashcard ENV-1094 — Biopiracy and ABS: turmeric and neem
**Front:** What does biopiracy mean, and what examples does the owner give?
**Definition (learn):** Biopiracy is unauthorised commercial use of India's genetic resources or traditional knowledge. The owner gives historical patent disputes over turmeric and neem properties as examples.
**Breakdown:**
- Problem: benefit goes out, community gets nothing
- Examples: turmeric and neem patent disputes (owner)
- ABS aims to share benefits and deter unauthorised use
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-cited examples (owner 04 adv section 3); not verified against a dated case record here.
**Links:** ENV-1090, ENV-1093
**Trap:** Naming biopiracy is not proof of a current violation; cite the case and date.
**Mains hook:** ABS needs both approval and benefit-tracking (qualified).
**Back:** *"Turmeric and neem: knowledge taken, benefit missing."*

### Flashcard ENV-1095 — People's Biodiversity Register gap
**Front:** What implementation gap does the owner flag about People's Biodiversity Registers?
**Definition (learn):** People's Biodiversity Registers (PBRs) are mandated widely, but their quality and completion vary across India's BMCs. This is a gap between statutory design and ground execution.
**Breakdown:**
- PBR: local documentation register
- Gap: uneven completion
- A PBR or local collection fee alone does not prove ABS is complete
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner analytical critique (owner 04 adv section 3 and 9).
**Links:** ENV-1090, ENV-1091
**Trap:** A PBR is an input, not proof of benefit sharing.
**Mains hook:** Reform priority is PBR completion and ABS consistency (qualified).
**Back:** *"Register on paper is not benefit on the ground."*

### Flashcard ENV-1096 — Kunming-Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework and 30x30
**Front:** What is the 30x30 target and where does it come from?
**Definition (learn):** The Kunming-Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework (KMGBF), adopted at CBD COP15 in December 2022, sets a 30x30 target: conserve 30 percent of terrestrial and marine areas by 2030, alongside 22 other 2030 targets and four 2050 goals.
**Breakdown:**
- Origin: CBD COP15, Montreal, December 2022
- 30x30: 30 percent terrestrial and marine by 2030
- Counts OECMs as well as protected areas (ENV-1097)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv section 5 and 10; ✅ owner 04 adv Must-Know). Live fetch not made; date from owner.
**Links:** ENV-1097, ENV-1146
**Trap:** 30x30 is not an Indian domestic invention; it is a global commitment India is party to.
**Mains hook:** Connect hotspot conservation to the global area-based target, not as separate tracks (qualified).
**Back:** *"Thirty by thirty: land and sea, by 2030, from Montreal."*

### Flashcard ENV-1097 — OECMs and 30x30
**Front:** What is an OECM, and how does it count towards 30x30?
**Definition (learn):** OECM stands for "other effective area-based conservation measure": areas delivering conservation outcomes outside the formal protected-area system. Under KMGBF they count toward 30x30 without being legally protected areas.
**Breakdown:**
- Examples (owner): sacred groves, community forests, well-managed working landscapes
- Counts toward 30x30
- Not a legal protected area
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 04 adv Must-Know; ✅ owner 06 basic section 2).
**Links:** ENV-1096, ENV-1134
**Trap:** An OECM is not a notified protected area.
**Mains hook:** OECMs can bridge terrestrial gaps; marine legal tools are thinner (qualified).
**Back:** *"Other effective areas count; they need not be notified parks."*

### Flashcard ENV-1098 — Cali Fund and the Article 8(j) body
**Front:** What two structural outcomes did CBD COP16 produce, according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** COP16 (Cali, 2024; resumed in Rome, February 2025) created the Cali Fund for sharing benefits from digital sequence information and a permanent subsidiary body on Article 8(j) for indigenous peoples and local communities.
**Breakdown:**
- Cali Fund: benefit sharing from DSI
- Article 8(j) body: permanent subsidiary body
- Status: established mechanism, contributions still developing
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-stated (owner 04 adv section 8 and 10); not fetched. Do not claim disbursements without a dated figure.
**Links:** ENV-1093, ENV-1096
**Trap:** Established does not mean compensating countries now.
**Mains hook:** Benefit sharing from sequence data is the new genetic frontier (qualified).
**Back:** *"Cali funds the sequence, Rome resumed the talks."*

### Flashcard ENV-1099 — EU Nature Restoration Law as a comparator
**Front:** Why is the EU Nature Restoration Law not an Indian conservation law?
**Definition (learn):** The EU Nature Restoration Law is an external comparator. It is not an Indian law and must not be imported into an India answer.
**Breakdown:**
- Status: EU measure
- PYQ: 2025 GS-I Q90 routed to this topic (owner)
- Use: comparison only
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PYQ ledger (✅ owner 04 basic PYQ integration; owner 04 adv section 13). Official key is held locally but not recorded in owner; no answer inferred.
**Links:** ENV-1096, ENV-1100
**Trap:** Do not present EU targets as Indian targets.
**Mains hook:** Use only as comparative evidence for restoration governance (qualified).
**Back:** *"Europe restores its nature; India's rules are its own."*

### Flashcard ENV-1100 — PYQ route: sixth mass extinction and the Invasive Species Specialist Group
**Front:** Which Prelims demands route to this topic, and what is the Invasive Species Specialist Group's parent?
**Definition (learn):** The 2018 GS-I Q60 demand concerns sixth mass extinction causes and human impact. The 2023 GS-I Q13 demand concerns the Invasive Species Specialist Group and its parent organisation, which the owner identifies as an IUCN specialist group (IUCN Species Survival Commission).
**Breakdown:**
- 2018 Q60: sixth mass extinction, human activity
- 2023 Q13: ISSG is an IUCN specialist group, not an Indian statutory body
- Parent: IUCN Species Survival Commission (⚠️ standard fact, not fetched)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PYQ ledger (✅ owner 04 basic PYQ integration; owner 04 adv section 13). Keys are not held locally in the owner; no answer inferred.
**Links:** ENV-1102, ENV-1099
**Trap:** An IUCN specialist group is not an Indian legal body.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand; statement elimination only.
**Back:** *"Sixth extinction on 2018's paper; the invasive group sits under IUCN."*

### Flashcard ENV-1101 — Core theme: biodiversity and hotspot triage
**Front:** What is the answer thesis for biodiversity levels and hotspots?
**Definition (learn):** Distinguish a level of diversity (genetic, species, ecosystem), a scientific priority label (hotspot), and a legal mechanism (Biological Diversity Act). Hotspot triage focuses resources, but durable governance must also protect genetic resources, connectivity and community knowledge beyond hotspots.
**Breakdown:**
- Level: genetic, species, ecosystem
- Label: hotspot (scientific)
- Mechanism: Biological Diversity Act (legal)
- Beyond hotspots: non-hotspot ecosystems also matter
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Answer thesis (✅ owner 04 basic section 13).
**Links:** ENV-1076, ENV-1081, ENV-1088, ENV-1090, ENV-1096
**Trap:** Do not use hotspot to mean "any species-rich area."
**Mains hook:** Hotspot-based conservation is necessary but not sufficient (qualified).
**Back:** *"Levels, labels, laws: keep the three apart."*

### GROUP — IUCN Red List and Endemism

### Flashcard ENV-1102 — IUCN Red List: advisory scientific inventory
**Front:** What is the IUCN Red List, and is it law?
**Definition (learn):** The IUCN Red List of Threatened Species is a standardised, criteria-based global inventory of extinction risk, maintained by IUCN (International Union for Conservation of Nature). It is a scientific and advisory assessment, not a law.
**Breakdown:**
- Criteria-based categories
- Maintained with IUCN Species Survival Commission specialist groups (owner)
- Binding trade regulation for threatened species comes from CITES (Topic 09)
- Indian legal protection comes from Wildlife Protection Act Schedules, which are separate
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2 and 4; ✅ Wikipedia: IUCN Red List, fetched 1 Nov 2026, category list).
**Links:** ENV-1103, ENV-1119, ENV-1128
**Trap:** Red List status does not create a legal obligation in India by itself.
**Mains hook:** Treat the Red List as an advisory risk scale; law and courts act separately (qualified).
**Back:** *"Red List is the science, not the statute."*

### Flashcard ENV-1103 — Red List category order
**Front:** Arrange the IUCN Red List categories from least to most at risk.
**Definition (learn):** The order is: Least Concern, Near Threatened, Vulnerable, Endangered, Critically Endangered, Extinct in the Wild, Extinct. Not Evaluated and Data Deficient sit outside the risk scale.
**Breakdown:**
- Least Concern (LC) → Near Threatened (NT) → Vulnerable (VU) → Endangered (EN) → Critically Endangered (CR) → Extinct in the Wild (EW) → Extinct (EX)
- Outside the scale: Not Evaluated (NE), Data Deficient (DD)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 6; ✅ Wikipedia: IUCN Red List, fetched 1 Nov 2026, nine categories).
**Links:** ENV-1104, ENV-1105, ENV-1106, ENV-1107, ENV-1111
**Trap:** Vulnerable is not the most severe threatened category; Critically Endangered is.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to explain why CR is the last stop before extinction in the wild (qualified).
**Back:** *"Least Near Vulnerable Endangered Critical, then wild-extinct, then extinct."*

### Flashcard ENV-1104 — "Threatened" means VU, EN and CR
**Front:** Which Red List categories together form "threatened"?
**Definition (learn):** "Threatened" is a collective term for Vulnerable, Endangered and Critically Endangered.
**Breakdown:**
- VU + EN + CR = threatened
- NT is not threatened; it is close to qualifying
- DD, NE and LC are not threatened
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2 and 6; ✅ Wikipedia: IUCN Red List, fetched 1 Nov 2026).
**Links:** ENV-1103, ENV-1105, ENV-1106, ENV-1107, ENV-1108
**Trap:** Do not count NT or DD as threatened.
**Mains hook:** Threatened status is a risk category, not a legal Schedule (qualified).
**Back:** *"Vulnerable, Endangered, Critical: the threatened three."*

### Flashcard ENV-1105 — Vulnerable (VU)
**Front:** Define Vulnerable under the IUCN Red List.
**Definition (learn):** A taxon is Vulnerable when it faces a high risk of extinction in the wild. It is the least severe of the three threatened categories.
**Breakdown:**
- Position: third from the least-concern end of the ladder
- Threatened: yes
- Lower than Endangered and Critically Endangered
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2).
**Links:** ENV-1104, ENV-1106
**Trap:** Vulnerable is not the most severe threatened category.
**Mains hook:** Early warning category; trends matter more than the label (qualified).
**Back:** *"Vulnerable: high risk, lowest of the threatened three."*

### Flashcard ENV-1106 — Endangered (EN)
**Front:** Define Endangered under the IUCN Red List.
**Definition (learn):** A taxon is Endangered when it faces a very high risk of extinction in the wild.
**Breakdown:**
- Position: between Vulnerable and Critically Endangered
- Threatened: yes
- Owner example: Western hoolock gibbon global EN (ENV-1122)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2).
**Links:** ENV-1104, ENV-1105, ENV-1107, ENV-1122
**Trap:** Endangered is not the same as Critically Endangered.
**Mains hook:** Use the category with its assessment year and scale (qualified).
**Back:** *"Endangered: very high risk, middle of the threatened three."*

### Flashcard ENV-1107 — Critically Endangered (CR)
**Front:** Define Critically Endangered and name its owner-cited Indian example.
**Definition (learn):** A taxon is Critically Endangered when it faces an extremely high risk of extinction in the wild. It is the most severe threatened category. The owner cites the Great Indian Bustard (ENV-1120).
**Breakdown:**
- Most severe threatened category
- Next stage: Extinct in the Wild if no wild population remains
- Owner example: Great Indian Bustard, assessed globally CR on 17 August 2018
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2 and 5).
**Links:** ENV-1104, ENV-1120, ENV-1123
**Trap:** Critically Endangered is not the same as extinct; EW is next.
**Mains hook:** CR status justifies habitat-level action, not only single-species fixes (qualified).
**Back:** *"Critical: extremely high risk, last step before the wild goes silent."*

### Flashcard ENV-1108 — Near Threatened (NT)
**Front:** Define Near Threatened.
**Definition (learn):** A taxon is Near Threatened when it is close to qualifying for a threatened category in the near future.
**Breakdown:**
- Position: between LC and VU
- Not threatened yet
- Signal: monitoring needed
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2).
**Links:** ENV-1103, ENV-1104
**Trap:** NT is not threatened; it is a watch category.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains item; use for ordering.
**Back:** *"Near Threatened: almost threatened, not yet."*

### Flashcard ENV-1109 — Least Concern (LC)
**Front:** Define Least Concern, and state the trap.
**Definition (learn):** Least Concern covers widespread and abundant taxa assessed as low risk. A species can be LC globally yet locally significant or legally protected in India.
**Breakdown:**
- Position: lowest risk on the ladder
- Global vs domestic: LC does not mean protected in every country
- Example type: a widespread species with a national Schedule (owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2 and 5).
**Links:** ENV-1103, ENV-1119
**Trap:** Least Concern does not mean legally unprotected in India.
**Mains hook:** Global and domestic assessments are not interchangeable (qualified).
**Back:** *"Widespread and abundant: low risk, but local rules still apply."*

### Flashcard ENV-1110 — Extinct in the Wild versus Extinct
**Front:** Distinguish Extinct in the Wild from Extinct.
**Definition (learn):** Extinct in the Wild (EW) means the taxon survives only in cultivation, captivity or as a naturalised population well outside its historic range. Extinct (EX) means there is no reasonable doubt that the last individual has died.
**Breakdown:**
- EW: captivity or cultivation or distant naturalised population
- EX: last individual dead
- Recovery is possible for EW through reintroduction
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2).
**Links:** ENV-1103, ENV-1107
**Trap:** EW is not EX; the species still survives in captivity or cultivation.
**Mains hook:** EW justifies reintroduction planning (qualified).
**Back:** *"Extinct in the wild lives in a zoo or garden; plain Extinct is gone."*

### Flashcard ENV-1111 — Data Deficient versus Not Evaluated
**Front:** Distinguish Data Deficient from Not Evaluated.
**Definition (learn):** Not Evaluated (NE) means the taxon has not been assessed against the criteria at all. Data Deficient (DD) means an assessment was attempted but information was inadequate to judge extinction risk.
**Breakdown:**
- NE: never assessed
- DD: assessed, evidence insufficient
- Neither is a threatened category
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2 and 6).
**Links:** ENV-1112, ENV-1103
**Trap:** NE and DD are not interchangeable.
**Mains hook:** DD can hide high risk (see ENV-1112).
**Back:** *"Not Evaluated: never checked. Data Deficient: checked, not enough data."*

### Flashcard ENV-1112 — Data Deficient is not reassurance
**Front:** Does Data Deficient mean a species is safe?
**Definition (learn):** No. Data Deficient means insufficient information to judge risk. It can itself be a conservation-priority signal, and in many invertebrate, fungal and aquatic groups DD reflects survey gaps.
**Breakdown:**
- DD is not LC
- DD can conceal high risk
- Survey gaps drive DD in many groups
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 6; ✅ owner 05 adv section 5).
**Links:** ENV-1111, ENV-1103
**Trap:** Data Deficient does not equal Least Concern.
**Mains hook:** Treating DD species as low priority is a documented critique of resource allocation (qualified).
**Back:** *"Data Deficient: unknown, not unharmed."*

### Flashcard ENV-1113 — IUCN Criteria A to E
**Front:** Summarise the five IUCN Red List criteria A to E.
**Definition (learn):** The five quantitative criteria place a taxon in a threatened category. A: population size reduction. B: restricted geographic range (extent of occurrence or area of occupancy) with fragmentation, decline or fluctuation. C: small population size with continuing decline. D: very small or very restricted population. E: quantitative analysis of extinction probability.
**Breakdown:**
- A: population reduction over time
- B: range restriction plus fragmentation or decline
- C: small population plus decline
- D: very small or very restricted population
- E: population viability analysis
- One qualifying criterion at the threshold is enough
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 2 and 6, adv section 2). Wikipedia confirms five criteria A to E (fetched 1 Nov 2026) but not the letter-by-letter meanings.
**Links:** ENV-1114, ENV-1120
**Trap:** Do not require a population decline for every threatened category.
**Mains hook:** Use the criterion route to explain a species' risk pathway (qualified).
**Back:** *"A falls, B bounded, C crashing-small, D dwindling-tiny, E extinction model."*

### Flashcard ENV-1114 — Critically Endangered without documented decline
**Front:** How can a species be Critically Endangered without a documented population decline?
**Definition (learn):** A species qualifies through Criterion B (extreme range restriction with fragmentation) or Criterion D (very small population) without demonstrated decline. A numerically large population confined to a tiny range can still be CR.
**Breakdown:**
- Criterion B: restricted range and fragmentation
- Criterion D: very small population
- Decline is not always required
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 adv sections 2 and 8).
**Links:** ENV-1113, ENV-1116
**Trap:** A species must show decline to be CR is false.
**Mains hook:** Range restriction, not only headcount, can drive urgent categories (qualified).
**Back:** *"Tiny range or tiny population can be enough: no decline needed."*

### Flashcard ENV-1115 — Endemism versus native, rare, threatened, flagship and keystone
**Front:** Which status words must not be confused with endemism?
**Definition (learn):** Endemism means restricted natural range. Native means naturally occurring in a place (not necessarily restricted). Rare means low abundance. Threatened is a Red List risk category. Flagship and keystone are conservation roles, not measures of range or risk.
**Breakdown:**
- Endemic: restricted to one region
- Native: occurs naturally in a region, possibly widely
- Rare: low numbers
- Flagship: public-appeal species
- Keystone: disproportionate ecological effect
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic semantic-completeness control).
**Links:** ENV-1080, ENV-1025, ENV-1116
**Trap:** Endemic is not rare, and flagship is not keystone.
**Mains hook:** Name which measure a conservation argument is using (qualified).
**Back:** *"Endemic is where; rare is how many; flagship is who; keystone is what it does."*

### Flashcard ENV-1116 — Endemism as a risk multiplier and island recolonisation
**Front:** Why does endemism compound extinction risk, and why are island endemics harder to recover?
**Definition (learn):** An endemic confined to a hotspot fragment faces habitat loss with no alternative range to retreat to. Island endemics face the same problem, and isolation also means no natural recolonisation from elsewhere after a collapse.
**Breakdown:**
- Risk multiplier: habitat loss plus no alternative range
- Hotspot link: ≤30 percent habitat remaining (ENV-1081)
- Island: recolonisation impossible without reintroduction
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 3; ✅ owner 05 adv section 3 and 8).
**Links:** ENV-1080, ENV-1087, ENV-1114
**Trap:** Island endemics do not have the same recovery prospects as mainland endemics.
**Mains hook:** Endemism explains why hotspots produce disproportionate Red-Listed species (qualified).
**Back:** *"One home, no spare home: endemics fall together with their patch."*

### Flashcard ENV-1117 — IUCN Green Status of Species
**Front:** What does IUCN's Green Status of Species measure?
**Definition (learn):** The Green Status of Species is a separate IUCN assessment of species recovery and conservation impact. The Red List answers "how close to extinction?"; the Green Status answers "how fully recovered?"
**Breakdown:**
- Red List: extinction risk
- Green Status: recovery and conservation impact
- Complements, does not replace, the Red List
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-stated (owner 05 basic section 6 and adv section 7); not confirmed by the fetched Wikipedia page.
**Links:** ENV-1102, ENV-1103
**Trap:** Green Status is not a second Red List category.
**Mains hook:** Recovery metrics pair with risk metrics in conservation outcome reporting (qualified).
**Back:** *"Red Listing counts the danger; Green Status counts the recovery."*

### Flashcard ENV-1118 — Global versus regional IUCN assessment
**Front:** Why must a Red List category always state its geographic scale?
**Definition (learn):** IUCN assessments can be global or regional or national. The same species can carry different categories at different scales. Always state which scale a category refers to.
**Breakdown:**
- Global: worldwide
- Regional or national: country-scale assessment
- Divergence: possible
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 6 and 7).
**Links:** ENV-1119, ENV-1124
**Trap:** A single global category does not apply everywhere.
**Mains hook:** State scale and year in every species claim (qualified).
**Back:** *"Say the scale: global, regional or national."*

### Flashcard ENV-1119 — Red List versus Wildlife Protection Act Schedule
**Front:** Why are a species' Red List category and its WPA Schedule not the same thing?
**Definition (learn):** The Red List is a global or regional scientific risk assessment. The Wildlife Protection Act Schedules (e.g., Schedule I) are domestic legal protection calibrated to national priorities, enforcement and trade. The two systems can diverge in either direction.
**Breakdown:**
- Red List: scientific, advisory
- WPA Schedule: legal, domestic
- Divergence: both directions possible
- Reassessment cycles are not synchronised
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 3 and 7; ✅ owner 05 adv section 1 and 4).
**Links:** ENV-1102, ENV-1108, ENV-1109
**Trap:** Red-Listed and Schedule I protected are not synonyms.
**Mains hook:** Use both as complementary evidence (qualified; detailed Schedule content belongs to Topic 08).
**Back:** *"Red List judges risk; the Schedule commands law."*

### Flashcard ENV-1120 — Great Indian Bustard: global Critically Endangered (2018)
**Front:** What is the Great Indian Bustard's global IUCN category and assessment date, as stated by the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The Great Indian Bustard (Ardeotis nigriceps) was assessed globally as Critically Endangered on 17 August 2018, under criteria A2a+4acd; C1 (owner-stated).
**Breakdown:**
- Drivers: decline, very small population, fragmentation across a shrinking grassland range
- Threat pathway: collision with high-voltage overhead transmission lines
- Scale: global; assessment year 2018
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-cited (owner 05 basic section 5 and 8; owner 05 adv section 3). IUCN assessment PDF (iucnredlist.org/species/pdf/134188105) returned HTTP 404 on fetch in this session; criteria string not independently confirmed.
**Links:** ENV-1107, ENV-1121, ENV-1067
**Trap:** Do not present the 2018 category as a fresh 2026 assessment.
**Mains hook:** Link science (IUCN), law (WPA) and judicial remedy (Supreme Court) in one chain (qualified).
**Back:** *"Bustard: critical since 2018, the power lines are the enemy."*

### Flashcard ENV-1121 — M.K. Ranjitsinh v. Union of India (21 March 2024)
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court's 21 March 2024 order do in the Great Indian Bustard case?
**Definition (learn):** The owner states that in M.K. Ranjitsinh and Ors. v. Union of India (order dated 21 March 2024) the Supreme Court modified its earlier blanket direction on undergrounding transmission lines across bustard habitat. It constituted an expert committee for area-by-area feasibility and retained species-protection review.
**Breakdown:**
- Earlier: blanket undergrounding direction (April 2021, per owner)
- 2024: expert committee, area-sensitive feasibility
- Not proof that mitigation is complete
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-cited (owner 05 basic section 8; owner 05 adv section 9). Not fetched from a court source in this session.
**Links:** ENV-1120, ENV-1067
**Trap:** A court direction is not evidence that mitigation was installed.
**Mains hook:** Judicial remedy can redesign a species protection measure but does not guarantee recovery (qualified).
**Back:** *"Court redrew the wire rule; the wire still needs checking."*

### Flashcard ENV-1122 — Western hoolock gibbon: global Endangered (2017; published 2019)
**Front:** What is the Western hoolock gibbon's global category and assessment date?
**Definition (learn):** The Western hoolock gibbon (Hoolock hoolock) was assessed globally as Endangered, assessed 12 September 2017 and published in 2019 (owner-stated). It is a forest-canopy brachiating ape of north-eastern India with a transboundary range.
**Breakdown:**
- Category: EN (global)
- Assessment: 12 September 2017
- Publication: 2019
- Threats: habitat fragmentation, hunting or capture (owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-cited (owner 05 basic section 9 and adv section 9). IUCN PDF (iucnredlist.org/species/pdf/17968083) returned HTTP 404 on fetch.
**Links:** ENV-1106, ENV-1125
**Trap:** A retrieval date or census is not the assessment year, and does not show current abundance.
**Mains hook:** Canopy bridges or sanctuary censuses do not prove recovery (qualified).
**Back:** *"Hoolock: endangered in 2017, published 2019, still needs canopies."*

### Flashcard ENV-1123 — Peacock or Gooty tarantula: global Critically Endangered (2008)
**Front:** What is the owner's cited category and date for the Peacock or Gooty tarantula?
**Definition (learn):** The owner cites a global Critically Endangered assessment dated 1 January 2008 for the Peacock or Gooty tarantula.
**Breakdown:**
- Category: CR (global)
- Assessment date: 1 January 2008
- Lesson: an old assessment remains an old assessment
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-cited (owner 05 basic section 8 and adv section 9). IUCN PDF (iucnredlist.org/species/pdf/12681959) not fetched in this session.
**Links:** ENV-1107, ENV-1124
**Trap:** A recent PYQ does not make an old assessment new.
**Mains hook:** Pair category with year and current range evidence (qualified).
**Back:** *"Tarantula critical since 2008: old year, still a warning."*

### Flashcard ENV-1124 — Assessment-year discipline
**Front:** Why is the assessment year not the retrieval date?
**Definition (learn):** Every species-specific IUCN statement must give the geographic scale, the category, the assessment date or year and, where useful, the criteria. A page-access or retrieval date and a publication date are separate metadata.
**Breakdown:**
- Assessment date: when status was assessed
- Publication date: when published
- Retrieval date: when checked online
- Current abundance requires separate evidence
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 05 basic section 8 and adv section 9).
**Links:** ENV-1118, ENV-1122, ENV-1123
**Trap:** A retrieval date is not the assessment date.
**Mains hook:** Cite the assessment year before the category in any answer (qualified).
**Back:** *"Assessed, published, retrieved: three different dates."*

### Flashcard ENV-1125 — PYQ route: Western hoolock gibbon (2026 GS-I Q22)
**Front:** Which 2026 Prelims demand routes to the Red List topic, and what is the key status?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 GS-I Q22 demand concerns the Western hoolock gibbon's conservation status, habitat and arboreal adaptation. The owner notes the 2026 Set-A key held locally is provisional and records no answer letter.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: conservation status, habitat, arboreal adaptation
- Key status: provisional; no answer letter recorded
- Owner ownership: this topic (Topic 05), not Topic 28
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 2026 PYQ ledger (✅ owner 05 basic section 2026 block). No answer is inferred.
**Links:** ENV-1122, ENV-1124
**Trap:** A provisional key is not final; check the official key before relying on it.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand; use for statement elimination.
**Back:** *"2026 gibbon question: know the status, check the final key."*

### Flashcard ENV-1126 — PYQ route: country-animal habitat pairs (2024 GS-I Q23)
**Front:** Which 2024 Prelims demand pairs countries with animals, and what facts should a candidate check?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 GS-I Q23 demand matches country-animal natural habitat pairs (Brazil-Indri, Indonesia-Elk, Madagascar-Bonobo). The owner records the key as held locally but does not record the answer.
**Breakdown:**
- Indri: lemur endemic to Madagascar (⚠️ standard fact, not fetched)
- Bonobo: great ape of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (⚠️ standard fact)
- Elk: Eurasia and North America (⚠️ standard fact)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PYQ ledger (✅ owner 05 basic 2024-2025 block). Key held locally, answer not recorded.
**Links:** ENV-1080, ENV-1127
**Trap:** Do not claim a country-animal pair from memory; check the official key.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand; use for statement elimination.
**Back:** *"Match the animal to its native home, then check the key."*

### Flashcard ENV-1127 — PYQ routes: wildlife endemic to India and species-habitat matching
**Front:** Which other endemism and species demands route to this topic?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes four demands: 2019 GS-I Q22 (wildlife species naturally found only in India), 2019 Q29 (species and natural Indian habitats matching), 2022 Q47 (identification of Golden Mahseer, Nightjar, Spoonbill and Ibis), and 2023 Q12 (marsupials' natural habitat and distribution in India).
**Breakdown:**
- Endemic-to-India species: check the species' range
- Species-habitat matching: check habitat type
- Identification: match morphology and habitat
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PYQ ledgers (✅ owner 05 basic 2018-2023 block). Official keys are not held locally in the owner; no answer inferred.
**Links:** ENV-1080, ENV-1115, ENV-1126
**Trap:** Endemic to India is a range claim, not an abundance claim.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand; statement elimination only.
**Back:** *"Range, habitat, identity: three checks for species questions."*

### Flashcard ENV-1128 — Core theme: IUCN risk, law and endemism
**Front:** What three-layer chain should a species answer follow?
**Definition (learn):** Separate the scientific assessment layer (IUCN Red List), the domestic legal layer (Wildlife Protection Act, Schedules), and the judicial or enforcement layer (courts, mitigation). Endemism is the key variable explaining disproportionate risk.
**Breakdown:**
- Science: IUCN category, criteria, assessment year and scale
- Law: WPA Schedules and protected-area law
- Enforcement: courts, mitigation, habitat action
- Endemism: removes spatial buffer
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner answer thesis (✅ owner 05 basic section 13 and adv section 11).
**Links:** ENV-1102, ENV-1116, ENV-1119, ENV-1120
**Trap:** Red List, law and court orders are complementary layers, not synonyms.
**Mains hook:** Conclude with a landscape-level, not single-species, recommendation (qualified).
**Back:** *"Science rates it, law lists it, courts enforce it."*

### GROUP — Protected Area Network India

### Flashcard ENV-1129 — Four legal categories under the Wildlife Protection Act, 1972
**Front:** Name the four protected-area categories under the Wildlife Protection Act, 1972, from strictest to least strict.
**Definition (learn):** The WPA 1972 provides National Park, Wildlife Sanctuary, Conservation Reserve and Community Reserve, each with a different permitted-activity regime and declaration procedure. Tiger and Elephant Reserves are species-specific overlays, not separate land categories.
**Breakdown:**
- National Park: strictest, no human activity except as allowed
- Wildlife Sanctuary: regulated human activity, some existing rights
- Conservation Reserve: government land buffer or connector, consulted communities
- Community Reserve: private or community land, consent-based, community keeps management role
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic sections 1, 2 and 3).
**Links:** ENV-1130, ENV-1131, ENV-1133, ENV-1134, ENV-1136
**Trap:** Four categories are not all created in 1972; the reserve categories came by amendment (see ENV-1133).
**Mains hook:** A graded hierarchy suits a densely populated country better than one rigid model (qualified).
**Back:** *"Park, Sanctuary, Conservation, Community: strict to shared."*

### Flashcard ENV-1130 — National Park
**Front:** What defines a National Park under the WPA, and what are its boundary safeguards?
**Definition (learn):** A National Park is an area declared for protecting wildlife, habitat and landscape, in which no human activity is permitted except as the Act allows. Grazing and private rights are not normally permitted unless specifically allowed.
**Breakdown:**
- Strictest regime in the hierarchy
- Boundary alteration needs a State Legislature resolution (owner)
- De-notification needs NBWL recommendation (owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic sections 2, 6 and 7; boundary safeguard ⚠️ owner-stated, not confirmed by the fetched Wikipedia page).
**Links:** ENV-1129, ENV-1132, ENV-1140
**Trap:** A state government cannot shrink a National Park administratively.
**Mains hook:** Strict regimes need rights-respecting relocation (qualified; see ENV-1143).
**Back:** *"No human use, legislature alone changes the lines."*

### Flashcard ENV-1131 — Wildlife Sanctuary
**Front:** How does a Wildlife Sanctuary differ from a National Park in permitted activity?
**Definition (learn):** A Wildlife Sanctuary is an area for protecting wildlife with more permitted, regulated human activity than a National Park, including certain existing rights.
**Breakdown:**
- Permitted: certain existing rights and regulated use
- Declaration: state government under the WPA
- Less strict than a National Park
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic section 2).
**Links:** ENV-1129, ENV-1130, ENV-1132
**Trap:** National Parks and Sanctuaries do not have identical legal restrictions.
**Mains hook:** Sanctuary rights can conflict with habitat inviolability (qualified; see ENV-1143).
**Back:** *"Sanctuary allows some rights; park allows almost none."*

### Flashcard ENV-1132 — National Park versus Wildlife Sanctuary
**Front:** Give the core difference between a National Park and a Wildlife Sanctuary.
**Definition (learn):** The National Park has the stricter regime, with rights and grazing not normally permitted. The Sanctuary permits more regulated activity, including certain existing rights.
**Breakdown:**
- NP: strict exclusion
- WLS: regulated coexistence
- Declaration by state government in both cases
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic sections 6 and 7).
**Links:** ENV-1130, ENV-1131
**Trap:** Do not treat a Sanctuary as a National Park.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains item; use for comparison.
**Back:** *"Park says no, Sanctuary says rules apply."*

### Flashcard ENV-1133 — Conservation Reserve
**Front:** What is a Conservation Reserve, and when were reserve categories added?
**Definition (learn):** A Conservation Reserve is a buffer or connector area, usually government land next to protected areas, declared after consulting local communities. The owner states the reserve categories were added by a 2002 amendment to the WPA.
**Breakdown:**
- Land: usually government land
- Function: buffer and corridor
- Declaration: after consulting communities
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic sections 2 and 6). ⚠️ The fetched Wikipedia page on the WPA confirms the 2002 Amendment Act (in force January 2003) but does not mention reserve categories, so the 2002 link is not independently confirmed here.
**Links:** ENV-1129, ENV-1135, ENV-1145
**Trap:** Reserves were not created in 1972.
**Mains hook:** Conservation Reserves can link larger protected areas (qualified).
**Back:** *"Government buffer, consulted villages, linked parks."*

### Flashcard ENV-1134 — Community Reserve
**Front:** What is a Community Reserve, and does the state need to acquire land?
**Definition (learn):** A Community Reserve is an area on private or community land declared for conservation with the consent of the community or individual, who retain a management role. No land acquisition is needed.
**Breakdown:**
- Land: private or community
- Consent: community or individual
- Management: community retains role
- Examples in owner: sacred groves, community forests
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic sections 2, 5 and 7; ✅ owner 06 adv section 7).
**Links:** ENV-1129, ENV-1097, ENV-1135
**Trap:** Community Reserves do not require the state to acquire private land.
**Mains hook:** Community reserves recognise that many well-conserved landscapes are community-managed (qualified).
**Back:** *"Community land, community consent, community keeps the keys."*

### Flashcard ENV-1135 — Conservation Reserve versus Community Reserve
**Front:** Distinguish Conservation Reserves from Community Reserves by land and consent.
**Definition (learn):** Conservation Reserves are usually government land declared after consulting communities. Community Reserves are private or community land declared with the consent of the community or individual.
**Breakdown:**
- Conservation: government land, consultation
- Community: private or community land, consent
- Both extend protection without acquisition
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic sections 2 and 7).
**Links:** ENV-1133, ENV-1134
**Trap:** The consent axis separates the two; do not reverse them.
**Mains hook:** Consent-based models are central to rights-respecting conservation (qualified).
**Back:** *"Government land consults; community land consents."*

### Flashcard ENV-1136 — Tiger Reserve as a species overlay
**Front:** How is a Tiger Reserve structured, and who declares it?
**Definition (learn):** A Tiger Reserve is declared by the state government on NTCA recommendation. It comprises a core or critical tiger habitat, kept largely inviolate, and a buffer zone permitting regulated coexistence. It is typically declared over an existing National Park or Sanctuary.
**Breakdown:**
- Core: critical tiger habitat, relocation on voluntary informed consent (ENV-1143)
- Buffer: regulated coexistence and research
- Authority: NTCA (statutory body)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic sections 2, 3 and 4).
**Links:** ENV-1137, ENV-1138, ENV-1140, ENV-1143
**Trap:** A Tiger Reserve is not a National Park by default.
**Mains hook:** Overlays add focus without replacing the legal category (qualified).
**Back:** *"Core for the tiger, buffer for the people, NTCA for the paperwork."*

### Flashcard ENV-1137 — Tiger Reserve is not a fifth legal category
**Front:** Is a Tiger Reserve a fifth legal category alongside the four WPA categories?
**Definition (learn):** No. It is a management overlay with core and buffer zones under NTCA, usually declared over an existing National Park or Sanctuary, not a separate land category.
**Breakdown:**
- Overlay: management system
- Underlying category: NP or WLS
- Co-existing layers
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic section 7).
**Links:** ENV-1129, ENV-1136
**Trap:** Do not count the Tiger Reserve as a fifth WPA category.
**Mains hook:** Layered designations are typical in India (qualified).
**Back:** *"Tiger Reserve sits on top, not beside."*

### Flashcard ENV-1138 — National Tiger Conservation Authority (NTCA)
**Front:** What is the NTCA's role, and what is its legal basis?
**Definition (learn):** The National Tiger Conservation Authority oversees Project Tiger and Tiger Reserve management, approves core and buffer demarcation and runs the tiger census. The owner states it is a statutory body under the WPA, as amended in 2006.
**Breakdown:**
- Oversight: Project Tiger
- Approvals: core and buffer demarcation
- Monitoring: tiger census
- Statutory basis: WPA (2006 amendment, owner-stated)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic section 4 and adv section 4). ⚠️ The 2006 amendment year is owner-stated; the fetched Wikipedia WPA page does not name the NTCA's statutory year.
**Links:** ENV-1136, ENV-1139, ENV-1144
**Trap:** NTCA is not the same body as the National Board for Wildlife.
**Mains hook:** Statutory authority gives NTCA stronger exclusion leverage than advisory elephant schemes (qualified).
**Back:** *"NTCA guards the tiger's core on statute."*

### Flashcard ENV-1139 — National Board for Wildlife (NBWL)
**Front:** Who chairs the National Board for Wildlife, and what does its Standing Committee do?
**Definition (learn):** The NBWL is chaired by the Prime Minister (owner-stated). Its Standing Committee, chaired by the Environment Minister, does most clearance work in practice.
**Breakdown:**
- NBWL: chaired by the Prime Minister
- Standing Committee: chaired by Environment Minister; clearance work
- Role in boundary changes: recommendation for de-notification; approval for tiger reserve boundary changes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-stated (owner 06 basic section 6); not fetched.
**Links:** ENV-1138, ENV-1140
**Trap:** The NBWL is not NTCA.
**Mains hook:** Apex clearance bodies create approval bottlenecks; monitor transparency (qualified).
**Back:** *"PM chairs the board; the Minister's committee does the work."*

### Flashcard ENV-1140 — Boundary and de-notification safeguards
**Front:** What safeguards govern alteration of National Park, Sanctuary and Tiger Reserve boundaries?
**Definition (learn):** For a National Park or Sanctuary, boundaries cannot be altered except by a State Legislature resolution, and de-notification requires NBWL recommendation (owner). For a Tiger Reserve, boundary alteration needs NTCA recommendation and NBWL approval; de-notification needs approval of both bodies in the public interest.
**Breakdown:**
- NP or WLS: State Legislature resolution; NBWL for de-notification
- Tiger Reserve: NTCA recommendation plus NBWL approval
- Owner: a stricter double lock for Tiger Reserves
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic section 6 and 7). Wikipedia WPA page (fetched 1 Nov 2026) says altering boundaries of a sanctuary or National Park is an offence carrying enhanced penalties under the 2002 amendment (min three years, up to seven years, fine at least Rs 10,000), which is consistent with the safeguard. The specific legislature rule is not confirmed by that page.
**Links:** ENV-1130, ENV-1136, ENV-1139
**Trap:** A state government cannot shrink a National Park administratively.
**Mains hook:** Boundary safeguards are the statutory answer to administrative shrinkage (qualified).
**Back:** *"Legislature changes parks, NTCA and the board double-lock tigers."*

### Flashcard ENV-1141 — Eco-Sensitive Zone: parent statute and extent
**Front:** Under which Act are Eco-Sensitive Zones notified, and is there a uniform width?
**Definition (learn):** Eco-Sensitive Zones (ESZs) are notified under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986, not under the Wildlife Protection Act. ESZ extent is site-specific and fixed by the individual notification. There is no automatic uniform 10 km rule.
**Breakdown:**
- Parent statute: EP Act 1986
- Function: regulate, not prohibit, activities such as mining, industry and construction
- Extent: set per notification
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic sections 2, 6 and 7).
**Links:** ENV-1142, ENV-1129, ENV-1146
**Trap:** Every protected area does not have a uniform 10 km ESZ.
**Mains hook:** Buffer design should be evidence-based and zone-specific (qualified).
**Back:** *"EP Act draws the ESZ; each notification sets its width."*

### Flashcard ENV-1142 — 1 km ESZ direction (2022) and the 2023 modification
**Front:** What was the Supreme Court's 2022 ESZ direction and its 2023 modification?
**Definition (learn):** The owner states that in 2022, in the T.N. Godavarman Thirumulpad proceedings, the Supreme Court directed a minimum 1 km eco-sensitive zone as a default buffer around every protected area. In 2023 the Court modified that direction to accept that a uniform blanket buffer could not be applied mechanically where settlements, existing ESZ notifications or essential infrastructure exist.
**Breakdown:**
- 2022: default minimum 1 km buffer
- 2023: site-specific, notification-led approach
- Reading: a proportionality test, not dilution
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-stated judicial sequence (owner 06 adv section 3); the owner says verify exact order dates before citing. Not fetched.
**Links:** ENV-1141, ENV-1143
**Trap:** The 2023 change is not a repeal of buffer principle.
**Mains hook:** Use both orders to show a proportionality test (qualified; check the exact orders).
**Back:** *"One kilometre in 2022, then local reality in 2023."*

### Flashcard ENV-1143 — Core relocation: voluntary and informed consent
**Front:** What must relocation from critical tiger habitat satisfy under NTCA norms?
**Definition (learn):** NTCA guidelines require relocation of families from critical tiger habitat to be voluntary, based on informed consent, and accompanied by a legally mandated rehabilitation package.
**Breakdown:**
- Consent: voluntary and informed
- Package: land or cash compensation under NTCA norms (owner)
- Conflict: implementation gaps and overlap with the Forest Rights Act, 2006
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 adv section 2 for the NTCA guideline; implementation critique ⚠️).
**Links:** ENV-1136, ENV-1138
**Trap:** NTCA relocation is not mandatory and involuntary.
**Mains hook:** Two statutes with different goals: habitat inviolability and tenure recognition (qualified).
**Back:** *"Consent first, package next, tenure by the Forest Rights Act."*

### Flashcard ENV-1144 — Project Elephant versus NTCA
**Front:** Compare the statutory basis of the Tiger Reserve system and the Elephant Reserve system.
**Definition (learn):** NTCA is a statutory authority with specific core-habitat backing. Project Elephant historically operated as a centrally sponsored scheme without an NTCA-equivalent statutory authority, relying on advisory notification and landscape coordination.
**Breakdown:**
- Tiger: statutory core habitat and relocation procedure
- Elephant: advisory notification and corridor coordination
- Implication: landscape management suits wide-ranging elephants
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 adv section 4 for the statutory contrast; ✅ NTCA statutory basis per owner ENV-1138).
**Links:** ENV-1138, ENV-1145
**Trap:** Elephant Reserves do not have the same statutory core-buffer structure as Tiger Reserves.
**Mains hook:** Bounded reserves suit tigers, corridors suit elephants (qualified).
**Back:** *"Tigers get a statutory core; elephants get a corridor."*

### Flashcard ENV-1145 — Elephant corridors and landscape management
**Front:** Why is a bounded core-buffer model less suited to elephants?
**Definition (learn):** Elephants range over much larger and often multi-state landscapes than a tiger's territory, so corridor-based landscape management fits better than a rigid bounded reserve.
**Breakdown:**
- Range: vast, multi-state
- Model: corridors and multi-stakeholder coordination
- Contrast with tiger core-buffer logic
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner analytical point (owner 06 adv section 4).
**Links:** ENV-1144, ENV-1133
**Trap:** Do not apply a tiger-style core-buffer model to elephants without considering corridors.
**Mains hook:** Use species ranging to choose reserve design (qualified).
**Back:** *"Wide range, wide corridor."*

### Flashcard ENV-1146 — Marine blind spot in the protected-area network
**Front:** What does the owner call the marine blind spot, and why does it matter for 30x30?
**Definition (learn):** India's protected-area vocabulary is terrestrial, while the KMGBF 30x30 target covers terrestrial and marine areas. Marine protected areas are mostly Marine National Parks or Sanctuaries under WPA categories designed for bounded terrestrial habitat. Protection is split across statutes and ministries, without a single core-buffer analogue.
**Breakdown:**
- Marine statutes: WPA, CRZ notifications under EP Act (Topic 24), fisheries regulation
- Terrestrial arithmetic: OECMs and conservation reserves can bridge the gap
- Marine arithmetic: legal toolkit thinner
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner analytical point (owner 06 adv section 3A).
**Links:** ENV-1096, ENV-1097, ENV-1129
**Trap:** Do not assume OECMs solve the marine gap in the same way as on land.
**Mains hook:** Separate terrestrial from marine arithmetic in any 30x30 answer (qualified).
**Back:** *"Land counts easily; sea needs a different legal toolkit."*

### Flashcard ENV-1147 — Designation count versus management versus verified protection
**Front:** Name the three things a protected-area "count" can mean.
**Definition (learn):** A count can mean areas notified, areas with an approved management plan, or areas with verified on-ground protection. Only the last shows protection in practice.
**Breakdown:**
- Notified: legal designation
- Managed: approved management plan
- Verified: on-ground protection
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 06 basic section 8).
**Links:** ENV-1148, ENV-1149, ENV-1124
**Trap:** Notification is not protection.
**Mains hook:** Measure outcomes, not only designations (qualified).
**Back:** *"Notified, managed, verified: count the last one for protection."*

### Flashcard ENV-1148 — India's protected-area counts (WII ENVIS, March 2025)
**Front:** What counts does the owner give for India's protected-area network, and what date?
**Definition (learn):** The owner cites the WII ENVIS Protected Area Database (as of March 2025): 106 National Parks, 573 Wildlife Sanctuaries, 115 Conservation Reserves and 220 Community Reserves.
**Breakdown:**
- National Parks: 106
- Wildlife Sanctuaries: 573
- Conservation Reserves: 115
- Community Reserves: 220
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-cited (owner 06 basic section 8); the WII ENVIS database was not fetched in this session. Always cite the database date, as counts change.
**Links:** ENV-1147, ENV-1129
**Trap:** Counts change with notifications; do not quote them undated.
**Mains hook:** Expansion is not outcome (qualified; see ENV-1149).
**Back:** *"106, 573, 115, 220: March 2025, owner-cited, re-check."*

### Flashcard ENV-1149 — Madhav and Ratapani Tiger Reserves (2024-2025)
**Front:** Which Tiger Reserves does the owner cite as recent additions, and what is India's count?
**Definition (learn):** The owner states Ratapani Tiger Reserve (Madhya Pradesh) was added in December 2024 and Madhav Tiger Reserve (Madhya Pradesh) in March 2025, bringing the count to 58 at that dated milestone.
**Breakdown:**
- Ratapani: Madhya Pradesh, December 2024 (owner)
- Madhav: Madhya Pradesh, March 2025 (owner)
- Count: 58 (owner, as of March 2025)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-cited (owner 06 basic section 8 and adv section 9); not verified against the NTCA or a dated source in this session. Verify the latest count before citing.
**Links:** ENV-1150, ENV-1136, ENV-1148
**Trap:** Reserve count growth does not confirm better core-habitat inviolability.
**Mains hook:** Expansion needs management-effectiveness evidence (qualified).
**Back:** *"Ratapani then Madhav, Madhya Pradesh twice, fifty-eight by owner count."*

### Flashcard ENV-1150 — PYQ route: Madhav National Park, Madhav Tiger Reserve and Sakhya Sagar (2026 Q32)
**Front:** Which 2026 Prelims demand routes to this topic, and how should the three names be separated?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 GS-I Q32 demand concerns Madhav National Park, its tiger reserve status and Sakhya Sagar. Keep the three designations distinct: a National Park (WPA category), a Tiger Reserve overlay (NTCA), and a Ramsar Site (international wetland listing).
**Breakdown:**
- Madhav National Park: WPA category
- Tiger Reserve: overlay, March 2025 (owner)
- Sakhya Sagar: Ramsar Site, designated 2022 (owner-stated; see ENV-1185)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 2026 block (✅ owner 06 basic 2026 PYQ integration). Key is provisional and no answer is inferred.
**Links:** ENV-1136, ENV-1149, ENV-1185
**Trap:** A Ramsar designation is not a National Park or Tiger Reserve.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand; statement elimination only.
**Back:** *"Park, overlay, Ramsar: three legal layers for one landscape."*

### Flashcard ENV-1151 — PYQ route: Pakhui Wildlife Sanctuary (2018 Q94)
**Front:** Which 2018 Prelims demand routes here, and what is the location fact?
**Definition (learn):** The 2018 GS-I Q94 demand concerns the state location of Pakhui Wildlife Sanctuary. The owner does not record the state. Standard knowledge places Pakhui Wildlife Sanctuary in Arunachal Pradesh.
**Breakdown:**
- Pakhui: a Wildlife Sanctuary (WPA category)
- State: Arunachal Pradesh (⚠️ standard knowledge, not verified here)
- Key: official 2018 key not held locally
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PYQ ledger (✅ owner 06 basic 2018-2023 block).
**Links:** ENV-1131, ENV-1129
**Trap:** Do not guess the state for a sanctuary without checking the official key or a dated source.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand; statement elimination only.
**Back:** *"Pakhui: a sanctuary, check the state in the key."*

### Flashcard ENV-1152 — PYQ route: Desert National Park and the Great Indian Bustard (2020 Q95)
**Front:** Which 2020 Prelims demand links a national park to the bustard, and what must be checked?
**Definition (learn):** The 2020 GS-I Q95 demand asks about Desert National Park districts, habitation and Great Indian Bustard habitat. Standard knowledge places Desert National Park in Jaisalmer district, Rajasthan.
**Breakdown:**
- Desert NP: WPA National Park (Rajasthan, ⚠️ standard knowledge)
- Bustard habitat link: grassland species (ENV-1067)
- Check: district names and habitation claims against the official key
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PYQ ledger (✅ owner 06 basic 2018-2023 block). Key not held locally in the owner.
**Links:** ENV-1130, ENV-1067, ENV-1120
**Trap:** Habitation inside a national park is a separate fact from bustard habitat.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand; statement elimination only.
**Back:** *"Desert park, bustard grass: check districts and habitation separately."*

### Flashcard ENV-1153 — PYQ routes: largest critical tiger habitat and community reserve governance
**Front:** Which other 2020 and 2023 Prelims demands route to this topic?
**Definition (learn):** Routed demands include 2020 GS-I Q100 (Tiger Reserves with the largest Critical Tiger Habitat area), 2020 Q73 (protected areas in the Cauvery basin), 2020 Q75 (protected area for hard-ground Barasingha swamp deer), 2019 Q18 (National Parks in the temperate alpine zone), and 2023 Q38 (Community Reserve governance: hunting, NTFP and agricultural rights).
**Breakdown:**
- Tiger Reserves: CTH area comparisons (check the official key)
- Cauvery basin protected areas
- Barasingha and hard-ground habitat
- Community Reserve: consent-based governance, use rights
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PYQ ledgers (✅ owner 06 basic 2018-2023 block). No answers inferred.
**Links:** ENV-1136, ENV-1134, ENV-1135
**Trap:** Do not answer CTH-size or basin questions from memory without a dated figure.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand except the 2023 GS-I Q15 rain-forest sanctuaries demand (routed to this owner).
**Back:** *"Tigers by core area, rivers by basin, communities by consent."*

### Flashcard ENV-1154 — Core theme: protected areas as a graded legal network
**Front:** What is the answer thesis for India's protected-area network?
**Definition (learn):** India's protected-area network is a graded, multi-category legal hierarchy, overlaid by species-specific reserves. Durable outcomes depend on connectivity and community partnership across the hierarchy, not on the strictness of one category.
**Breakdown:**
- Classify first: NP, WLS, CR, CommR, TR overlay, ESZ, or international designation
- Then match rights, connectivity and management to that category
- Test: notification versus implementation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Answer thesis (✅ owner 06 basic section 10 and 13).
**Links:** ENV-1129, ENV-1137, ENV-1141, ENV-1143, ENV-1147
**Trap:** Expansion of reserve count is not an outcome measure.
**Mains hook:** Rights-respecting, connectivity-oriented conservation beats an expand-more-reserves answer (qualified).
**Back:** *"Classify the layer, match the rights, test the implementation."*

### GROUP — Biosphere Reserves and Ramsar Sites

### Flashcard ENV-1155 — Biosphere reserve: national declaration versus UNESCO recognition
**Front:** What is a biosphere reserve, and is every Indian biosphere reserve in the UNESCO network?
**Definition (learn):** A biosphere reserve is a large area declared by the national government, following UNESCO's Man and Biosphere (MAB) Programme criteria, zoned into core, buffer and transition areas. Not every domestic reserve is in UNESCO's World Network of Biosphere Reserves (WNBR); that needs separate international recognition.
**Breakdown:**
- Declaration: national (MoEFCC nominates)
- International: WNBR inclusion under MAB
- Criteria: representative ecosystem, significant biodiversity, sustainable-development demonstration
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic sections 2 and 3). MAB criteria per owner.
**Links:** ENV-1156, ENV-1158, ENV-1177
**Trap:** Domestic declaration is not UNESCO recognition.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish domestic designation from international recognition in any answer (qualified).
**Back:** *"Nation declares, UNESCO networks: two steps, two bodies."*

### Flashcard ENV-1156 — The three-zone biosphere model
**Front:** Name the three zones of a biosphere reserve in order from centre outward and state each function.
**Definition (learn):** Core zone: strict protection with minimal disturbance. Buffer zone: research, monitoring, education, tourism and limited resource use around the core. Transition zone: outermost zone for settlements, agriculture and sustainable economic activity.
**Breakdown:**
- Core: strict protection; often overlaps a National Park or Sanctuary
- Buffer: regulated research and limited use
- Transition: sustainable human use and livelihoods
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic sections 1 and 2).
**Links:** ENV-1155, ENV-1157, ENV-1129
**Trap:** Core, buffer and transition are a zoning model, not three legal categories.
**Mains hook:** The model allows conservation and livelihood in one landscape (qualified).
**Back:** *"Core guards, buffer studies, transition farms."*

### Flashcard ENV-1157 — Legal enforceability by biosphere zone
**Front:** Which biosphere zones carry independent statutory protection, and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** Usually only the core zone has independent enforceable protection, via pre-existing WPA or forest law. Buffer and transition zones depend more on planning and cooperative frameworks, so outcomes vary.
**Breakdown:**
- Core: enforceable under existing law
- Buffer: regulated, but planning-based
- Transition: planning aspirations, least legal teeth
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 adv sections 3 and 7).
**Links:** ENV-1156, ENV-1129
**Trap:** The three zones do not carry equal enforceability.
**Mains hook:** Effectiveness of the biosphere model depends on resourced transition planning (qualified).
**Back:** *"Only the core has teeth by default."*

### Flashcard ENV-1158 — India's biosphere reserves: 18 notified, 13 in the UNESCO network
**Front:** How many biosphere reserves does India have, and how many are in the UNESCO World Network?
**Definition (learn):** India has 18 notified biosphere reserves, of which 13 are in UNESCO's World Network of Biosphere Reserves, after Cold Desert joined in 2025 (owner; also consistent with the fetched Wikipedia table).
**Breakdown:**
- Notified: 18
- WNBR: 13
- Not in WNBR: Manas, Dibru-Saikhowa, Dihang-Dibang, Great Rann of Kutch, Seshachalam Hills
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic section 8; ✅ Wikipedia: Biosphere reserves of India, fetched 1 Nov 2026, 18 reserves and 13 in WNBR).
**Links:** ENV-1155, ENV-1177, ENV-1174
**Trap:** Do not equate the domestic count with the UNESCO count.
**Mains hook:** Keep the two counts distinct and dated (qualified).
**Back:** *"Eighteen declared, thirteen networked."*

### Flashcard ENV-1178 — Ramsar Convention (1971)
**Front:** What is the Ramsar Convention, where was it signed, and where is its Secretariat?
**Definition (learn):** The Ramsar Convention (1971) is an intergovernmental treaty on the conservation and wise use of wetlands, named after the Iranian city where it was signed. Its Secretariat is housed at IUCN in Gland, Switzerland (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Treaty: 1971, Ramsar, Iran
- Scope: wetlands only
- Secretariat: IUCN, Gland, Switzerland
- Not a UN treaty administered by UNEP (owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic sections 2, 4 and 7; ✅ owner 07 adv Montreux section; Ramsar founding year 1971 standard).
**Links:** ENV-1179, ENV-1181, ENV-1191
**Trap:** Ramsar is not a UNEP treaty; it is an independent intergovernmental treaty with its own Secretariat.
**Mains hook:** Use the treaty's wise-use logic in wetland answers (qualified).
**Back:** *"Ramsar, Iran, 1971: wetlands treaty, Gland secretariat."*

### Flashcard ENV-1179 — Ramsar Site and the nine criteria
**Front:** What is a Ramsar Site, and how many listing criteria apply?
**Definition (learn):** A Ramsar Site is a wetland designated under the Ramsar Convention as a Wetland of International Importance. The owner says there are nine listing criteria covering representative or rare wetland types, support for threatened species, biodiversity value, and waterbird or fish thresholds. Meeting any one criterion suffices.
**Breakdown:**
- Nine criteria (owner)
- Waterbird thresholds: 20,000 or more waterbirds, or 1 percent of a biogeographic population (⚠️ standard Ramsar criterion, not fetched)
- Any one criterion is enough
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic section 3; threshold figures ⚠️ standard knowledge).
**Links:** ENV-1178, ENV-1180, ENV-1193
**Trap:** Ramsar designation does not itself create domestic legal power.
**Mains hook:** Designation creates reporting duties, not an automatic protection regime (qualified).
**Back:** *"Nine criteria, any one passes: waterbirds count."*

### Flashcard ENV-1180 — Wise use
**Front:** What does the Ramsar "wise use" principle permit, and what does it not mean?
**Definition (learn):** Wise use is maintaining a wetland's ecological character while allowing sustainable human use. It is not strict no-use preservation.
**Breakdown:**
- Permitted: sustainable use compatible with ecological character
- Not required: strict no-use
- Reporting: periodic national reports (owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic sections 2, 5 and 7).
**Links:** ENV-1178, ENV-1179, ENV-1190
**Trap:** Ramsar status does not mean no human use.
**Mains hook:** Use wise use to argue against a false binary between wetland conservation and livelihood use (qualified).
**Back:** *"Wise use means sustainable, not sealed."*

### Flashcard ENV-1181 — Montreux Record
**Front:** What is the Montreux Record, and is it a penalty?
**Definition (learn):** The Montreux Record is a register within the Ramsar List of wetland sites where ecological character has changed, is changing or is likely to change because of technological development, pollution or other human interference. It is a watch-list, not a penalty, and it is not a list of the best sites.
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: flag adverse change and trigger advisory help
- Established: 1990, COP4 at Montreux, Switzerland (fetched Wikipedia, Recommendation 4.8)
- Size: 48 sites as of August 2021 (fetched Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia: Montreux Record, fetched 1 Nov 2026).
**Links:** ENV-1182, ENV-1183, ENV-1184, ENV-1178
**Trap:** Montreux Record is not a prestige list and does not mean loss of Ramsar status.
**Mains hook:** Designation includes accountability through the Montreux mechanism (qualified).
**Back:** *"Montreux flags the worried wetlands, not the proud ones."*

- Additions after June 2026 are consistent with the later count
- Total area on fetched page: 1,387,981 ha
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia: List of Ramsar sites in India, fetched 1 Nov 2026; the 100 milestone ⚠️ owner-cited).
**Links:** ENV-1186, ENV-1178, ENV-1188
**Trap:** Quote the count with its exact date; never say "India has 100 Ramsar sites" as a present fact.
**Mains hook:** Designation count is a dated snapshot, not outcome (qualified).
**Back:** *"Hundred on June 5, one-oh-three by October: date the count."*

### Flashcard ENV-1188 — Wetlands (Conservation and Management) Rules, 2017
**Front:** What domestic instrument governs wetlands in India, and who implements it?
**Definition (learn):** The Wetlands (Conservation and Management) Rules, 2017, notified under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986, establish State and UT Wetland Authorities that identify, notify and regulate activities in wetlands, independent of Ramsar status.
**Breakdown:**
- Parent Act: EP Act 1986
- Implementers: State and UT Wetland Authorities
- Independent of Ramsar listing
- Implementation: uneven state capacity (owner critique)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic section 3 and 4; ✅ owner 07 adv section 4; year 2017 per owner).
**Links:** ENV-1178, ENV-1189, ENV-1193
**Trap:** The Ramsar label creates no new Indian legal power.
**Mains hook:** Domestic wetland law decides real protection (qualified).
**Back:** *"Rules 2017: state authorities do the protecting."*

### Flashcard ENV-1189 — NPCA and the merged wetland programmes (2013)
**Front:** Which programmes did the NPCA absorb, and in which year, according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The owner states the National Wetland Conservation Programme (NWCP) and the National Lake Conservation Plan were merged into the National Plan for Conservation of Aquatic Ecosystems (NPCA) in 2013. NPCA funds the protected wetlands.
**Breakdown:**
- Earlier: NWCP and National Lake Conservation Plan
- Merged: NPCA in 2013
- Economic Survey 2025-26 credits NPCA with flood-moderation and water-security wetlands (owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-stated (owner 07 basic section 6 and 13.4; owner 07 adv Must-Know); not fetched. Economic Survey claim ⚠️.
