**Links:** ENV-1188, ENV-1190, ENV-1194
**Trap:** Do not present an NWCP result as a current national outcome.
**Mains hook:** Frame wetlands as adaptation infrastructure, not only bird habitat (qualified).
**Back:** *"Two lake plans merged into NPCA in 2013."*

### Flashcard ENV-1190 — Amrit Dharohar and MISHTI as wetland and mangrove counterparts
**Front:** What are Amrit Dharohar and MISHTI, and how do they pair?
**Definition (learn):** The owner states Amrit Dharohar, announced in the Union Budget 2023-24, is the scheme for community-led management of Ramsar sites, covering wetland ecotourism, carbon stock and livelihoods. Its mangrove counterpart announced in the same Budget is MISHTI (see ENV-1021).
**Breakdown:**
- Amrit Dharohar: inland wetlands and Ramsar sites
- MISHTI: coastal mangroves
- Pairing: inland and coastal halves of the wetland-as-infrastructure agenda
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner-stated (owner 07 adv section 9); not fetched.
**Links:** ENV-1021, ENV-1189, ENV-1180
**Trap:** Scheme labels are not outcome evidence.
**Mains hook:** Use the pair to show systemic knowledge of wetland policy (qualified).
**Back:** *"Amrit for inland wetlands, MISHTI for mangroves."*

### Flashcard ENV-1191 — World Wetlands Day
**Front:** On which date is World Wetlands Day, and what does it commemorate?
**Definition (learn):** World Wetlands Day is 2 February, the anniversary of the Ramsar Convention's signature at Ramsar, Iran, in 1971.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 2 February
- Commemorates: signature of the Convention, 1971
- Distinct from World Environment Day (5 June)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic section 6).
**Links:** ENV-1178, ENV-1186
**Trap:** Do not confuse 2 February (wetlands) with 5 June (environment).
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains item.
**Back:** *"Two February: the wetlands treaty's birthday."*

### Flashcard ENV-1192 — Biosphere reserve versus Ramsar Site
**Front:** Distinguish a biosphere reserve from a Ramsar Site.
**Definition (learn):** A biosphere reserve is a zoned, landscape-scale UNESCO MAB designation with core, buffer and transition zones. A Ramsar Site is a wetland-specific international listing under the Ramsar Convention. They are distinct systems that may overlap.
**Breakdown:**
- Biosphere: landscape, UNESCO MAB, zoned
- Ramsar: wetland, treaty, wise use
- Overlap: Sundarbans and others (ENV-1163)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic section 7 and 13.1).
**Links:** ENV-1155, ENV-1178, ENV-1163
**Trap:** Biosphere and Ramsar are not the same type of designation.
**Mains hook:** Never collapse the two (qualified).
**Back:** *"Biosphere is a zoned landscape; Ramsar is a wetland label."*

### Flashcard ENV-1193 — A Ramsar label creates no domestic legal power
**Front:** Does Ramsar designation give an Indian wetland legal protection?
**Definition (learn):** No. Ramsar designation raises visibility and reporting obligations. Actual protection depends on Indian law, principally the Wetlands (Conservation and Management) Rules, 2017, and State Wetland Authorities.
**Breakdown:**
- Ramsar: international recognition
- Domestic protection: 2017 Rules, State Wetland Authorities
- Three statuses: notified, designated, funded management plan
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 07 basic section 6 and 8; ✅ owner 07 adv section 1 and 4).
**Links:** ENV-1188, ENV-1178, ENV-1147
**Trap:** Ramsar status does not mean the wetland is protected by Ramsar law in India.
**Mains hook:** Designation-versus-implementation verdict (qualified).
**Back:** *"Ramsar names it; Indian rules protect it."*

### Flashcard ENV-1194 — PYQ routes: wetlands, water bodies and NWCP
**Front:** Which wetland-related PYQs route to this topic?
**Definition (learn):** Routed demands: 2018 GS-III Q7 (wetlands and Ramsar wise use with Indian examples); 2019 GS-I Q27 (Agasthyamala components) and Q40 (Ramsar obligations and India Wetlands Rules); 2021 GS-I Q6 (environmental implications of reclaiming water bodies); 2022 GS-I Q30 (wetlands location and state matching); 2023 GS-III Q17 (National Wetland Conservation Programme and India Ramsar sites).
**Breakdown:**
- Mains: 2018 Q7 (10 marks), 2021 Q6 (10 marks), 2023 Q17 (15 marks)
- Prelims: 2019 Q27, Q40; 2022 Q30 (keys not held locally)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PYQ ledgers (✅ owner 07 basic and adv 2018-2023 block). No answers inferred.
**Links:** ENV-1178, ENV-1180, ENV-1188, ENV-1189
**Trap:** Do not answer a Prelims wetland location question from memory without the official key.
**Mains hook:** Mains answers on urban water bodies need lost storage, flood risk and zoning (qualified; see owner 13.3).
**Back:** *"Wetland wise use, water body reclamation, NWCP: know the 2018, 2021, 2023 routes."*

### Flashcard ENV-1195 — Core theme: designations are layers, not protection
**Front:** What is the answer thesis for biosphere reserves and Ramsar sites?
**Definition (learn):** Biosphere Reserves and Ramsar Sites are complementary international-recognition layers. The first zones space to balance protection and livelihood; the second flags wetlands for wise use. Judge real conservation value by domestic implementation, not by designation count.
**Breakdown:**
- Biosphere: zoning and livelihoods
- Ramsar: wise use and reporting
- Domestic: Wildlife Protection Act for core zones; 2017 Rules for wetlands
- Status ladder: notified, designated, funded and implemented
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Answer thesis (✅ owner 07 basic section 10 and adv section 11).
**Links:** ENV-1155, ENV-1178, ENV-1147, ENV-1193
**Trap:** A count of designations is not evidence of protection.
**Mains hook:** Recommend stronger State Wetland Authority capacity and transition-zone livelihood planning (qualified).
**Back:** *"Designation shows the map; implementation shows the protection."*

## ENVIRONMENT AND ECOLOGY — Part 2: Wildlife Law, Forests, Pollution and Rivers

Sources: owners `upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/{basic,advanced}/08_` to `14_`. Fetched-source checks (Wikipedia, fetched 2026-11-01): Wildlife Protection Act page, CITES page, CMS page, Forest Rights Act page. Namami Gange, NCAP and the CAQM pages returned 404 and were not confirmed. Card-level tags: (✅ owner NN) owner-only; (✅ Wikipedia) fetched; ⚠️ unverified or conflicting.

### GROUP — Wildlife Protection Act and Schedules (owner 08)

### Flashcard ENV-2001 — Wildlife Protection Act, 1972: the core domestic wildlife statute
**Front:** Which Act is India's principal law for protecting wild animals, birds and plants and regulating hunting and wildlife trade, and when was it enacted?
**Definition (learn):** The Wild Life (Protection) Act, 1972 is India's main domestic species-protection statute. It uses a graded Schedule system to set protection levels and penalties, and empowers governments to declare protected areas and regulate trade in wild animals, parts and derivatives.
**Breakdown:**
- Protects animals, birds and specified plants.
- Operates through graded Schedules, with differentiated penalties.
- Governs protected areas, hunting permissions and trade controls.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Act No. 53 of 1972, enacted 9 September 1972 (✅ Wikipedia). Amended in 1982, 1986, 1991, 1993, 2002, 2006 and 2022 (Act No. 18 of 2022) (✅ Wikipedia). The 2022 amendment is the current four-Schedule structure (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** ENV-2002 (2022 restructure); ENV-2021 (CITES alignment); ENV-2014 (WCCB).
**Trap:** Do not treat the 1972 Act as a CITES-only or trade-only statute. It is a domestic protection law with its own offences and protected-area powers.
**Mains hook:** Explain how a 1972 statute was restructured in 2022 to meet current CITES and enforcement needs. Qualify: legal design alone does not secure enforcement.
**Back:** *"1972 is the root, 2022 is the restructure: protect, schedule, trade-control."*

### Flashcard ENV-2002 — Wildlife (Protection) Amendment Act, 2022: six schedules to four
**Front:** How did the 2022 amendment change the Schedule structure of the Wildlife Protection Act?
**Definition (learn):** The 2022 amendment reduced the Act's six Schedules to four. It moved plants from the old Schedule VI to a new Schedule III, created a CITES-mirroring Schedule IV, and omitted the old vermin Schedule V.
**Breakdown:**
- Schedules I and II: animals (greatest and lesser protection).
- Schedule III: plants (formerly VI).
- Schedule IV: CITES-listed scheduled specimens (trade).
- Old Schedule V (vermin): omitted.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Six schedules pre-2022; four post-2022 (✅ owner 08). ⚠️ The Wikipedia snapshot still lists six schedules and describes Schedule V, and does not confirm the 2022 restructure. Verify against India Code.
**Links:** ENV-2003, ENV-2004, ENV-2005, ENV-2006, ENV-2007.
**Trap:** Pre-2022 and post-2022 schedule numbers differ. A question about a plant species protected under the old Schedule VI must be read in its historical time frame.
**Mains hook:** Argue that the restructure aligns domestic law with CITES Appendices. Qualify that alignment requires notification and enforcement capacity.
**Back:** *"Six became four: I-II animals, III plants, IV trade, V gone."*

### Flashcard ENV-2003 — Schedule I: highest protection for animals
**Front:** Which Schedule gives animals the greatest degree of protection under the Wildlife Protection Act?
**Definition (learn):** Schedule I lists animals given the greatest degree of protection, with the most severe penalties for offences. Hunting is near-totally prohibited; the narrow exceptions require the Chief Wildlife Warden's permission.
**Breakdown:**
- Animal schedule (not plants).
- Highest penalty tier.
- Vermin declaration can never apply to a Schedule I animal (⚠️ owner-stated rule).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tiger and elephant are flagship Schedule I species (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ current species mapping must be checked against the latest notification).
**Links:** ENV-2004 (Schedule II); ENV-2009 (hunting exceptions); ENV-2016 (S.O. 2360(E)).
**Trap:** Schedule IV is not a higher protection tier than Schedule I. Schedule IV is a trade-control schedule.
**Mains hook:** Use Schedule I to show how a grading system sets enforcement priority. Qualify: a status on paper does not prevent poaching.
**Back:** *"One is the top rung for animals: the strictest penalty."*

### Flashcard ENV-2004 — Schedule II: lesser protection for animals
**Front:** What is the difference between Schedule I and Schedule II for animals?
**Definition (learn):** Schedule II lists animals given a lesser degree of protection than Schedule I. The penalties are lower and the Schedule sits below Schedule I on the protection ladder.
**Breakdown:**
- Animal schedule.
- Second protection tier.
- Amended in 2025 alongside Schedule I and Schedule IV entries (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Post-2022 structure: Schedules I and II cover animals. Gazette S.O. 2360(E) dated 27 May 2025 amended entries in Schedules I, II and IV (✅ owner 08; ✅ Gazette reference in owner).
**Links:** ENV-2003; ENV-2002; ENV-2017.
**Trap:** Do not place Schedule II above Schedule I. Do not place Schedule IV in the protection ladder.
**Mains hook:** Use the animal ladder (I > II) to show graded protection. Qualify: the ladder only tells the penalty level, not the actual population status.
**Back:** *"Two is the lesser animal shield, one is the strongest."*

### Flashcard ENV-2005 — Schedule III: plants under the post-2022 Act
**Front:** Which Schedule lists protected plant species under the post-2022 Wildlife Protection Act?
**Definition (learn):** Schedule III lists specified plant species protected under the Act. It replaced the old Schedule VI for plants in the 2022 restructure.
**Breakdown:**
- Plants only.
- Formerly Schedule VI.
- Protects specified endemic plants from cultivation and planting (✅ Wikipedia describes the old Schedule VI rule).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Plant schedule moved to III in 2022 (✅ owner 08). The old Schedule VI rule barred cultivation of specified endemic plants (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ENV-2002; ENV-2019 (time boundary; 2020 Q81 routed to owner 08).
**Trap:** Schedule III is not a third animal tier. Only Schedules I and II grade animal protection.
**Mains hook:** Use Schedule III to show that the Act covers plants as well as animals. Qualify: plant protection depends on species listing and monitoring.
**Back:** *"Three is the plant shelf: the Act protects flora too."*

### Flashcard ENV-2006 — Schedule IV: CITES-listed scheduled specimens
**Front:** What does Schedule IV of the post-2022 Wildlife Protection Act cover?
**Definition (learn):** Schedule IV lists scheduled specimens, meaning species listed in CITES Appendices I, II and III. It is a trade-regulation schedule that aligns domestic law with the international treaty.
**Breakdown:**
- Parts mirror CITES Appendices I, II and III.
- Linked to the 2022 Chapter VB on trade in scheduled specimens.
- Not a protection tier.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Created in 2022 (✅ owner 08). Amended by S.O. 2360(E) on 27 May 2025 (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** ENV-2010 (Chapter VB); ENV-2021 (CITES); ENV-2026 (Appendix III; parts mirror all three).
**Trap:** A CITES Appendix change does not instantly amend Schedule IV. A domestic notification is required (⚠️ owner 08 advanced).
**Mains hook:** Explain Schedule IV as the bridge from CITES listing to domestic trade control. Qualify: the bridge needs notification and enforcement.
**Back:** *"Four is the treaty bridge: CITES in, trade control out."*

### Flashcard ENV-2007 — Vermin: the omitted Schedule V
**Front:** What happened to the old Schedule V (vermin) under the 2022 amendment?
**Definition (learn):** The old Schedule V vermin list was omitted in 2022. A wild animal can still be declared vermin for a specified area and period, but the power now operates through a central-government notification.
**Breakdown:**
- Old Schedule V: animals declared vermin, hunted freely.
- 2022: Schedule V omitted.
- Current power: central-government notification for an area and period.
- A Schedule I animal can never be declared vermin (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Schedule V omitted in 2022 (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ not confirmed by the Wikipedia snapshot, which still describes Schedule V). Section 62 notification mechanism named in owner 08 (⚠️ section number unverified).
**Links:** ENV-2002; ENV-2008 (Indian Flying Fox PYQ); ENV-2019 (time boundary).
**Trap:** Do not state that the vermin schedule still exists in post-2022 law. Do not apply the old list to a current question.
**Mains hook:** Argue that removing the standing vermin list reduces blanket culling risk. Qualify: a notification power still exists.
**Back:** *"Five is gone: vermin by notice, never by list, never for Schedule I."*

### Flashcard ENV-2008 — Indian Flying Fox and the 'vermin' PYQ (2024 Q20)
**Front:** Which PYQ concerns the Indian Flying Fox and the vermin category, and what is the right way to answer it?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 Prelims GS-I Q20 asked about the Indian Flying Fox in the vermin category under the Wild Life (Protection) Act, 1972. The owner routes the demand but does not record an answer letter.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: the vermin category.
- Source status: official Set-A key held locally; answer not recorded in owner 08.
- Required skill: time-sensitive reading (old Schedule V vs post-2022 notification power).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Prelims GS-I Q20 (✅ owner 08). No answer letter recorded.
**Links:** ENV-2007 (vermin omission); ENV-2019 (time boundary).
**Trap:** Do not retrofit post-2022 law to an older classification. Do not infer the answer from this card.
**Mains hook:** Not a direct Mains demand. Use the vermin shift to show how legal time frames change answers.
**Back:** *"Flying fox asks: which law, which year, which list?"*

### Flashcard ENV-2009 — Hunting permitted only in narrow circumstances
**Front:** In what narrow circumstances may an animal be hunted under the Wildlife Protection Act?
**Definition (learn):** Hunting is generally prohibited. The narrow exceptions include an animal that has become dangerous to human life, or is disabled or diseased beyond recovery. For a Schedule I animal, the Chief Wildlife Warden's written permission is required.
**Breakdown:**
- Exception 1: dangerous to human life.
- Exception 2: disabled or diseased beyond recovery.
- Permission: Chief Wildlife Warden (Schedule I) or an authorised officer.
- Permitted scientific, education or collection purposes under permit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** ENV-2003 (Schedule I); ENV-2007 (vermin).
**Trap:** Do not treat hunting as allowed for sport or crop protection under the Act. The vermin route is a notification mechanism, not a general hunting licence.
**Mains hook:** Discuss the human-wildlife conflict dilemma. Qualify: the narrow exception protects people, but permission must be transparent and time-bound.
**Back:** *"Hunt only if dangerous, dying, or with the Warden's pen."*

### Flashcard ENV-2010 — Chapter VB: regulating international trade in scheduled specimens
**Front:** What did the 2022 amendment add to regulate international trade in scheduled specimens?
**Definition (learn):** The 2022 amendment inserted Chapter VB, which regulates international trade in scheduled specimens. It requires the central government to designate a Management Authority (permits and certificates) and a Scientific Authority (trade-impact advice).
**Breakdown:**
- Chapter VB: trade regulation.
- Management Authority: permits and certificates.
- Scientific Authority: advice on trade impact.
- Mirrors CITES design requirements.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Inserted by the 2022 amendment (✅ owner 08). Institutional design required by CITES (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** ENV-2006 (Schedule IV); ENV-2021 (CITES core); ENV-2024 (Management and Scientific Authorities).
**Trap:** The Scientific Authority does not issue permits. The Management Authority issues permits (✅ owner 09).
**Mains hook:** Show how domestic institutions turn treaty duties into enforceable permits. Qualify: a permit system fails if species identification or population data is weak.
**Back:** *"Chapter VB: Management permits, Scientific advice."*

### Flashcard ENV-2011 — Invasive alien species: new power under the 2022 amendment
**Front:** What new power did the 2022 amendment give the central government over invasive alien species?
**Definition (learn):** The central government may regulate or prohibit the import, trade, possession or proliferation of invasive alien species. The provision extends wildlife law beyond native-species protection into proactive ecosystem control.
**Breakdown:**
- Powers: regulate or prohibit import, trade, possession, proliferation.
- Scope: broad, not plants-only (⚠️ owner 08 advanced trap).
- Trade-off: ecological risk versus existing economic use.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 amendment (✅ owner 08). Economic dependence on certain ornamental and agricultural species is flagged as a trade-off (⚠️ owner 08 advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2002; ENV-2012 (management plans).
**Trap:** The invasive-species provision is not limited to plants. Do not assume invasive species control is administratively simple.
**Mains hook:** Argue that invasive-species control is a proactive ecosystem tool. Qualify: economic dependence makes enforcement contested.
**Back:** *"Invasive in, ecosystem out: regulate, don't just react."*

### Flashcard ENV-2012 — Management plans for sanctuaries and national parks (2022)
**Front:** What did the 2022 amendment add on sanctuaries and national parks?
**Definition (learn):** The 2022 amendment gave statutory backing to management plans for sanctuaries and national parks, making the plan the operative instrument rather than ad hoc administrative decisions.
**Breakdown:**
- Instrument: management plan.
- Places: sanctuaries and national parks.
- Effect: strengthens the protected-area backbone.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 amendment (✅ owner 08; ✅ owner 06 cross-reference).
**Links:** ENV-2011; ENV-2006.
**Trap:** A management plan is not proof of protection. The plan must be implemented and monitored.
**Mains hook:** Argue that plan-based management strengthens protected areas. Qualify: plans need funding and monitoring.
**Back:** *"Plan the park, don't just pray for it."*

### Flashcard ENV-2013 — Voluntary surrender of captive animals (2022)
**Front:** What route did the 2022 amendment open for owners of undeclared captive wildlife?
**Definition (learn):** The 2022 amendment allows voluntary surrender of captive animals or derivatives to the Chief Wildlife Warden, after which they vest in the government. It acts as a pragmatic amnesty route for undeclared holdings.
**Breakdown:**
- Surrender to the Chief Wildlife Warden.
- Animals vest in the government.
- Purpose: bring holdings into the legal system.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** ENV-2010 (trade chapter); ENV-2009 (Chief Wildlife Warden).
**Trap:** Surrender is not a licence to keep. It transfers the animal to the government.
**Mains hook:** Discuss the amnesty as a tool that encourages disclosure. Qualify: it works only if the state can house and care for the animals.
**Back:** *"Surrender, don't hide: the Warden takes it."*

### Flashcard ENV-2014 — Wildlife Crime Control Bureau (WCCB)
**Front:** What is the WCCB and where does it sit institutionally?
**Definition (learn):** The Wildlife Crime Control Bureau is a statutory, central, multi-disciplinary body under MoEFCC. It combats organised wildlife crime, including trafficking and international smuggling, and coordinates intelligence and liaison.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: central, statutory, multi-disciplinary.
- Parent ministry: MoEFCC.
- Functions: intelligence, investigation support, cross-border trafficking, international liaison.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 08; ✅ owner 09 on enforcement role). Establishment year not in owner text (⚠️ memory: 2007).
**Links:** ENV-2001; ENV-2021; ENV-2022 (enforcement chain).
**Trap:** WCCB is not a state-level body. Enforcement on the ground involves state forest departments.
**Mains hook:** Explain why a central body is needed for organised wildlife crime. Qualify: prosecution capacity, forensic identification and inter-state coordination are weak points.
**Back:** *"WCCB: central, statutory, multi-disciplinary, trafficking-hunter."*

### Flashcard ENV-2015 — National Tiger Conservation Authority and NBWL
**Front:** Which two wildlife bodies named in the owner advise or regulate under the Act, and what is each one's role?
**Definition (learn):** NTCA is a statutory authority under the Act for tiger conservation. The National Board for Wildlife (NBWL) and State Boards for Wildlife are advisory or approving bodies on wildlife matters, including projects affecting protected areas.
**Breakdown:**
- NTCA: statutory tiger-conservation authority.
- NBWL: national advisory and approving body.
- State Boards for Wildlife: state-level counterparts.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 08; cross-reference owner 06). The statutory basis for NTCA (2006 amendment) is ⚠️ memory-only.
**Links:** ENV-2014; owner 06 cross-reference (not carded).
**Trap:** NBWL is not an enforcement agency. It advises on projects and protected areas.
**Mains hook:** Use NBWL clearances to discuss the trade-off between development and protected areas. Qualify: clearance quality depends on the independence of the process.
**Back:** *"NTCA guards tigers; NBWL advises on the wild."*

### Flashcard ENV-2016 — Schedule amendment S.O. 2360(E) (May 2025)
**Front:** What does the 2025 Gazette amendment S.O. 2360(E) change, and does it restore the old six-schedule system?
**Definition (learn):** S.O. 2360(E) amends entries in Schedules I, II and IV. It does not restore the old six-Schedule system. It is a notification within the 2022 four-Schedule architecture.
**Breakdown:**
- Amends: Schedules I, II and IV.
- Does not: restore six Schedules.
- Date: 27 May 2025 (notification); Gazette issue 28 May 2025.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Notification dated 27 May 2025; Gazette issue 28 May 2025 (✅ owner 08 advanced; ✅ Gazette link cited in owner).
**Links:** ENV-2002; ENV-2003; ENV-2004; ENV-2006.
**Trap:** Do not use a portal upload date as the operative legal date. Distinguish notification date, Gazette date and any commencement date (✅ owner 08 advanced).
**Mains hook:** Use this amendment to show legal-date discipline in environment law. Qualify: a current species claim needs the Gazette entry.
**Back:** *"Twenty-seven May notice, twenty-eight Gazette: inside four, not back to six."*

### Flashcard ENV-2017 — Tiger and elephant as Schedule I flagship species
**Front:** Which two flagship species are commonly cited as Schedule I animals under the Wildlife Protection Act?
**Definition (learn):** Tiger and elephant are the commonly cited flagship examples of Schedule I animals. They illustrate the highest protection level and enforcement priority.
**Breakdown:**
- Tiger: Schedule I flagship.
- Elephant: Schedule I flagship.
- Use: illustration, not a current list.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ verify against the latest MoEFCC notification before citing).
**Links:** ENV-2003; ENV-2016.
**Trap:** Do not treat a flagship example as a complete species-to-Schedule mapping. Mappings change by notification.
**Mains hook:** Use flagship species to explain enforcement priority. Qualify: a Schedule entry is a legal instrument, not a population estimate.
**Back:** *"Tiger and tusker top the animal ladder."*

### Flashcard ENV-2018 — Concept: WPA as a graded statute with a trade bridge
**Front:** What is the core theme of the Wildlife Protection Act and how does its structure answer it?
**Definition (learn):** The Act is a graded domestic protection statute. Its Schedule system sets protection tiers and penalties, and the 2022 structure adds a trade-control bridge to CITES. Enforcement, not drafting, is the real test.
**Breakdown:**
- Tier layer: Schedules I and II.
- Plant layer: Schedule III.
- Trade layer: Schedule IV and Chapter VB.
- Enforcement layer: WCCB, state departments, courts.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** ENV-2002; ENV-2006; ENV-2010; ENV-2014.
**Trap:** Legal restructuring is necessary but not sufficient. Do not claim that 2022 solved wildlife crime.
**Mains hook:** Conclude that enforcement capacity matters more than schedule count. Qualify: prosecution data and forensic capacity are not centrally published.
**Back:** *"Tier, plant, trade, enforce: four layers, one test."*

### Flashcard ENV-2019 — Pre-2022 vs post-2022 time boundary (concept trap)
**Front:** Why must a wildlife-law answer state the legal time point before giving schedule numbers?
**Definition (learn):** Historical questions may test the pre-2022 six-Schedule structure. Current answers must use the post-2022 four-Schedule structure. Mixing the two periods is a common error.
**Breakdown:**
- Pre-2022: six Schedules (I–VI).
- Post-2022: four Schedules (I–IV).
- Check: later Gazette amendments such as S.O. 2360(E).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** ENV-2002; ENV-2007; ENV-2008.
**Trap:** A plant protected under old Schedule VI is now in Schedule III. Do not treat the old numbering as current.
**Mains hook:** Show command of legal time frames. Qualify that the answer must cite the Gazette date for any current mapping.
**Back:** *"Old six, new four: date the law before you number it."*

### Flashcard ENV-2020 — Wikipedia snapshot conflict on the WPA schedule structure
**Front:** What conflicts exist between the owner and the fetched Wikipedia page on the Wildlife Protection Act's schedules?
**Definition (learn):** The owner says the 2022 amendment reduced the Schedules to four. The fetched Wikipedia snapshot still describes six Schedules, Schedule V vermin and Schedule VI plants, and does not mention a 2022 restructure in detail.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: four Schedules after 2022.
- Wikipedia (fetched): six Schedules, vermin Schedule V present.
- Resolution: verify against the India Code text and Gazette.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Conflict flagged; owner 08 is preferred for the structure. Wikipedia lists Act No. 18 of 2022 as an amendment (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ENV-2002; ENV-2007.
**Trap:** Do not cite a Wikipedia schedule count as current law.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable. This is a source-discipline card.
**Back:** *"Wiki says six, owner says four: check India Code first."*

### GROUP — CITES and Wildlife Trade (owner 09)

### Flashcard ENV-2021 — CITES: the trade-regulation treaty (core concept)
**Front:** What does CITES regulate, and through what mechanism?
**Definition (learn):** CITES (Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora) regulates cross-border trade in listed wild fauna and flora. It works through a three-tier Appendix system and graded permit requirements at import and export, not through habitat or domestic hunting controls.
**Breakdown:**
- Scope: international trade in listed species (animals and plants).
- Mechanism: Appendices I, II, III with permits.
- Limit: no direct domestic habitat or hunting jurisdiction.
- India's domestic link: Wildlife Protection Act Schedule IV (ENV-2006).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Washington Convention; full name in card ENV-2029 (✅ owner 09). Trade-only jurisdiction (✅ owner 09 and advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2022; ENV-2023; ENV-2024; ENV-2025; ENV-2026; ENV-2036; ENV-2006; ENV-2010.
**Trap:** CITES is not a habitat-protection treaty. A species can be CITES-listed and still decline from habitat loss or local demand.
**Mains hook:** Argue that trade regulation must pair with domestic habitat and anti-poaching law. Qualify: CITES cannot reach local, non-trade drivers.
**Back:** *"CITES watches the border, not the forest."*

### Flashcard ENV-2022 — Permit-verification chain: where CITES succeeds or fails
**Front:** What are the three stages of CITES's permit-verification chain, and which is its weakest link in practice?
**Definition (learn):** The chain runs from export permit issuance (Management Authority), through the non-detriment finding (Scientific Authority), to border or customs inspection. Each stage can fail: permit fraud, weak population data, or weak species identification.
**Breakdown:**
- Stage 1: Management Authority issues export permit (legal sourcing).
- Stage 2: Scientific Authority non-detriment finding (no harm to survival).
- Stage 3: customs or border inspection against the permit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2024; ENV-2027; ENV-2038.
**Trap:** The elegance of the Appendix system does not determine outcomes. The weakest link (often species identification or data-poor findings) does.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate CITES effectiveness through the weakest link. Qualify: a permit system fails if identification or population data is weak.
**Back:** *"Permit, finding, inspection: the weakest link decides."*

### Flashcard ENV-2023 — Appendix I: near-prohibition on commercial trade
**Front:** What is the trade rule for CITES Appendix I species?
**Definition (learn):** Appendix I lists species threatened with extinction. Commercial international trade is generally prohibited, except in exceptional circumstances such as non-commercial scientific research under strict permits.
**Breakdown:**
- Threat status: threatened with extinction.
- Commercial trade: generally prohibited.
- Exceptions: narrow, permit-based.
- Amendment: two-thirds majority at CoP (ENV-2028).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tiger, Asian elephant and Indian Star Tortoise are cited Appendix I examples (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** ENV-2021; ENV-2025; ENV-2033; ENV-2034.
**Trap:** Appendix I does not allow regulated commercial trade with permits as Appendix II does. Also it does not automatically bind every Party if a reservation exists (ENV-2030).
**Mains hook:** Discuss whether a total commercial ban helps species or drives black markets. Qualify: the answer depends on enforcement and demand.
**Back:** *"One is the ban: threatened, commerce shut, science exception only."*

### Flashcard ENV-2024 — Management Authority and Scientific Authority
**Front:** What are the roles of CITES Management Authority and Scientific Authority?
**Definition (learn):** Every Party designates both bodies. The Management Authority issues export and import permits. The Scientific Authority provides scientific advice, including the non-detriment finding that gates export.
**Breakdown:**
- Management Authority: permits and certificates.
- Scientific Authority: non-detriment finding and trade-impact advice.
- India: MoEFCC acts as Management Authority, with a wildlife-wing function (✅ owner 09).
- India: statutory footing given by the 2022 amendment (ENV-2010).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ENV-2010; ENV-2022; ENV-2027.
**Trap:** The Scientific Authority does not issue permits. Do not swap their roles.
**Mains hook:** Argue that two-body design separates legal sourcing from science. Qualify: both bodies need data and staff.
**Back:** *"Management grants the permit, Science gives the finding."*

### Flashcard ENV-2025 — Appendix II: regulated trade
**Front:** What is the rule for Appendix II species, and what permit do they need?
**Definition (learn):** Appendix II lists species not necessarily threatened now, but whose trade must be regulated to avoid them becoming threatened. Trade is permitted with an export permit issued by the Management Authority.
**Breakdown:**
- Threat status: not necessarily threatened now.
- Trade: allowed with export permits.
- Also covers look-alike species (⚠️ from memory of CITES text, cited by Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Red Sanders is an Appendix II example (✅ owner 09; ENV-2032).
**Links:** ENV-2021; ENV-2023; ENV-2032.
**Trap:** Appendix II is not a prohibition. Do not treat it as Appendix I.
**Mains hook:** Use Appendix II to show that regulated trade can be sustainable only if the non-detriment finding is credible.
**Back:** *"Two is regulated: permit in, permit out."*

### Flashcard ENV-2026 — Appendix III: unilateral listing by a single Party
**Front:** How is a species added to CITES Appendix III, and what does it require?
**Definition (learn):** Appendix III lists species protected in at least one country that has asked other Parties to help control trade. Any single Party can list unilaterally, without a CoP vote, and it requires a certificate of origin.
**Breakdown:**
- Who lists: a single Party, unilaterally.
- Vote: none needed (unlike Appendices I and II).
- Trade document: certificate of origin (or export permit, per Wikipedia).
- Withdrawal: also unilateral (✅ owner 09 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09; ✅ Wikipedia on Appendix III text)
**Links:** ENV-2028; ENV-2021.
**Trap:** "Adding to Appendix III requires a CoP vote" is wrong. Only I and II need two-thirds at CoP.
**Mains hook:** Show how Appendix III helps a country seek cooperation. Qualify: cooperation depends on other Parties' response.
**Back:** *"Three is solo: one Party lists, no vote needed."*

### Flashcard ENV-2027 — Non-detriment finding (NDF)
**Front:** What is a non-detriment finding and why does it matter for export permits?
**Definition (learn):** A non-detriment finding is the Scientific Authority's determination that a proposed export will not be detrimental to the survival of the species. It is the scientific gate that must be cleared before the Management Authority issues an export permit for Appendix I or II specimens.
**Breakdown:**
- Issuer: Scientific Authority of the exporting State.
- Gate: before export permit issued.
- Basis: population and trade data.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ENV-2022; ENV-2024; ENV-2025.
**Trap:** An export permit is not a purely administrative formality. It must be preceded by an NDF.
**Mains hook:** Argue that CITES is science-gated. Qualify: NDF quality varies, especially for data-poor plants and lesser-studied fauna.
**Back:** *"NDF: science says no harm before paper says go."*

### Flashcard ENV-2028 — CoP voting threshold for Appendix I and II
**Front:** What voting threshold applies to amending Appendix I and II at a CoP?
**Definition (learn):** Amendments to Appendix I and II require a two-thirds majority of Parties present and voting at a Conference of the Parties. Listing outcomes are therefore shaped by diplomacy as well as science.
**Breakdown:**
- Forum: Conference of the Parties (CoP).
- Threshold: two-thirds of Parties present and voting.
- Appendix III: no vote needed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09; ✅ Wikipedia "2/3 majority").
**Links:** ENV-2026; ENV-2023; ENV-2035.
**Trap:** The two-thirds rule does not apply to Appendix III. Do not state it as "every Appendix listing".
**Mains hook:** Use the CoP vote to show that listing is political economy. Qualify: science still anchors the proposals.
**Back:** *"Two-thirds for I and II; zero votes for III."*

### Flashcard ENV-2029 — CITES signing and entry into force
**Front:** When and where was CITES signed, and when did it enter into force?
**Definition (learn):** CITES was concluded at Washington D.C. on 3 March 1973 and entered into force on 1 July 1975, after the tenth ratification. It is therefore also called the Washington Convention. The UN marks 3 March as World Wildlife Day.
**Breakdown:**
- Signed: 3 March 1973, Washington D.C.
- In force: 1 July 1975.
- Nickname: Washington Convention.
- Observance: World Wildlife Day, 3 March.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09; ✅ Wikipedia body text for Washington and 1 July 1975)
**Links:** ENV-2021; ENV-2031.
**Trap:** ⚠️ The Wikipedia infobox gives Geneva as the signing place, while its body says Washington D.C. Owner and body text agree on Washington; prefer that. Do not confuse 1973 signing with 1975 entry into force.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains demand alone. Use as chronology for India's treaty commitments.
**Back:** *"Three March seventy-three signed; July seventy-five in force."*

### Flashcard ENV-2030 — Reservation: the escape hatch in CITES
**Front:** What is a CITES reservation and why does it matter for an Appendix I listing?
**Definition (learn):** A Party may enter a reservation against a specific listing. For trade in that taxon it is then treated as a non-Party. This is the structural leak that explains why an Appendix I listing does not guarantee a global trade halt.
**Breakdown:**
- Who: a Party entering a reservation.
- Effect: treated as non-Party for that taxon.
- Consequence: a possible trade channel despite Appendix I.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2023; ENV-2035.
**Trap:** Once a species is Appendix I, not every Party is automatically bound if a reservation applies.
**Mains hook:** Argue that reservations weaken the regime. Qualify: reservations are legal options, not violations.
**Back:** *"Reserve it and you're outside for that taxon."*

### Flashcard ENV-2031 — CITES Secretariat under UNEP
**Front:** Which body administers the CITES Secretariat, and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** The CITES Secretariat is administered under UNEP and coordinates the Convention's global implementation and the Conference of the Parties. Owner 09 places its headquarters in Geneva.
**Breakdown:**
- Administrator: UNEP.
- Function: coordination and CoP.
- Headquarters: Geneva (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09; ✅ Wikipedia: administered by UNEP).
**Links:** ENV-2029; ENV-2035.
**Trap:** The Secretariat does not issue national trade permits. National Management Authorities do.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone demand.
**Back:** *"UNEP runs the secretariat; nations issue the permits."*

### Flashcard ENV-2032 — Red Sanders: Appendix II under CITES
**Front:** What is the current CITES listing for Red Sanders (Pterocarpus santalinus), and why is it a useful Indian example?
**Definition (learn):** Red Sanders is listed in CITES Appendix II, effective 13 September 2007, with annotation #7 covering specified timber products. It is a classic Indian example of persistent trafficking despite listing.
**Breakdown:**
- Appendix: II.
- Effective date: 13 September 2007.
- Annotation: #7 (specified timber products).
- Endemism, Appendix status and Indian legality are separate claims.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09; ✅ Species+ record cited in owner). Check the live Checklist before any species-level claim.
**Links:** ENV-2025; ENV-2036; ENV-2038.
**Trap:** Do not state Red Sanders is Appendix I. Do not assume a trafficking seizure proves a trend.
**Mains hook:** Use Red Sanders to argue that listing alone cannot stop trafficking. Qualify: demand reduction and domestic enforcement are needed.
**Back:** *"Red Sanders: Appendix Two, seven annotated, still smuggled."*

### Flashcard ENV-2033 — Indian Star Tortoise: Appendix I
**Front:** What is the current CITES listing for the Indian Star Tortoise, and since when?
**Definition (learn):** The Indian Star Tortoise (Geochelone elegans) is listed in CITES Appendix I, effective 26 November 2019. It is a persistent trafficking example.
**Breakdown:**
- Appendix: I.
- Effective date: 26 November 2019.
- Use: Indian trafficking example.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09; ✅ Species+ record cited in owner advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2023; ENV-2036.
**Trap:** Do not use an older trafficking article to decide the current Appendix. The current entry controls.
**Mains hook:** Use it to argue for border enforcement. Qualify: Appendix I status does not stop demand-driven trade.
**Back:** *"Star Tortoise: One since 2019, still trafficked."*

### Flashcard ENV-2034 — Tiger and Asian elephant: CITES Appendix I examples
**Front:** Which two Indian flagship species are current CITES Appendix I examples?
**Definition (learn):** The tiger (Panthera tigris) and the Asian elephant (Elephas maximus) are current Appendix I examples. Species+ also records a 2019 tiger taxonomic note, showing that the accepted taxon and listing history must be read together.
**Breakdown:**
- Tiger: Appendix I.
- Asian elephant: Appendix I.
- Note: 2019 taxonomic lumping note for tiger.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09; ✅ Species+ record in owner).
**Links:** ENV-2023; ENV-2017.
**Trap:** Do not treat the tiger's Schedule I status under the WPA and its CITES Appendix I status as the same legal test.
**Mains hook:** Show that domestic and international listings operate separately. Qualify: each needs its own record.
**Back:** *"Tiger and tusker: CITES One, WPA One, separate laws."*

### Flashcard ENV-2035 — CoP20 (Samarkand) and Appendices valid from 5 March 2026
**Front:** What was the outcome of CITES CoP20 and when did the Appendices valid from 5 March 2026 take effect?
**Definition (learn):** CITES CoP20 closed in Samarkand on 5 December 2025. The CITES Secretariat's outcome record (published 16 December 2025) reports 82 new listings. The Appendices edition valid from 5 March 2026 is the operative listing record, subject to reservations and annotations.
**Breakdown:**
- CoP20 venue: Samarkand.
- CoP20 closed: 5 December 2025.
- Outcome record: 16 December 2025, 82 new listings.
- Appendices valid from: 5 March 2026.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 📰 (✅ owner 09 cites CITES Secretariat pages; date values as recorded in owner).
**Links:** ENV-2028; ENV-2030; ENV-2033.
**Trap:** A CoP adoption date is not the operative date. A tabled proposal is not an adopted listing. An IUCN category is not a CITES Appendix.
**Mains hook:** Cite the operative date and the entry record. Qualify: a press release shows what was adopted, not what is operative for each Party.
**Back:** *"Samarkand decides, March 2026 operates, the Checklist confirms."*

### Flashcard ENV-2036 — Trade regulation vs domestic habitat: CITES trap
**Front:** Can CITES directly penalise a country for habitat destruction affecting a listed species?
**Definition (learn):** No. CITES's authority is limited to international trade. Habitat loss, land use and hunting for local consumption are domestic matters governed by national law.
**Breakdown:**
- Inside CITES: export, import, re-export of listed specimens.
- Outside CITES: habitat, land use, domestic hunting, local demand.
- Domestic tool in India: Wildlife Protection Act.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2021; ENV-2022; ENV-2018 (WPA theme card).
**Trap:** CITES is not the domestic habitat law. Do not describe CITES as a comprehensive species-protection tool.
**Mains hook:** Argue that domestic habitat and anti-poaching law must complement CITES. Qualify: even a fully listed species can decline locally.
**Back:** *"CITES stops the crossing, not the clearing."*

### Flashcard ENV-2037 — Listing is not IUCN category: CITES trap
**Front:** Is a CITES Appendix II listing the same as an IUCN Red List threatened category?
**Definition (learn):** No. CITES Appendix listing reflects trade-risk criteria. IUCN categories reflect extinction-risk assessment. The two are legally and scientifically distinct, and a CITES listing cannot be inferred from an IUCN status or vice versa.
**Breakdown:**
- CITES: trade-risk and permit regime.
- IUCN: extinction-risk assessment (see Topic 05 owner).
- Indian Schedule: a third, domestic legal record.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 09; ✅ owner 05 cross-ref)
**Links:** ENV-2021; ENV-2032.
**Trap:** Do not convert an IUCN "Critically Endangered" into a CITES Appendix I. Do not convert a WPA Schedule into a CITES Appendix.
**Mains hook:** Argue that three records serve three purposes. Qualify: each needs its own source and date.
**Back:** *"IUCN measures extinction, CITES measures trade, Schedule measures law."*

### Flashcard ENV-2038 — Customs, DRI and WCCB: border enforcement of CITES
**Front:** Which agencies coordinate border enforcement of CITES obligations in India?
**Definition (learn):** The MoEFCC Management and Scientific Authorities, customs and the Wildlife Crime Control Bureau coordinate to intercept illegal wildlife trade at ports and borders. Customs and the Directorate of Revenue Intelligence also play an enforcement role for interceptions.
**Breakdown:**
- MoEFCC Management and Scientific Authorities: permits and findings.
- Customs: port and airport inspection.
- WCCB: intelligence and coordination.
- DRI: enforcement role (⚠️ owner 09 notes this as an enforcement role).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Illegal items cited in owner 09: ivory, pangolin scales, star tortoises, red sanders (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** ENV-2014; ENV-2022; ENV-2032; ENV-2033.
**Trap:** A seizure establishes interception, not a trafficking trend, prosecution or biological recovery.
**Mains hook:** Argue for customs-wildlife coordination at ports. Qualify: species identification and prosecution capacity are the limits.
**Back:** *"Customs catches, WCCB connects, Authorities permit."*

### GROUP — CMS Bonn Convention Migratory Species (owner 10)

### Flashcard ENV-2039 — CMS (Bonn Convention): range-state cooperation for migratory species
**Front:** What is the CMS and what problem does it address that a single national law cannot?
**Definition (learn):** The Convention on the Conservation of Migratory Species of Wild Animals (CMS, Bonn Convention) is a UNEP-administered multilateral treaty. It addresses species whose survival depends on protection across an entire migratory range spanning several countries. Its logic is range-state coordination, not trade regulation.
**Breakdown:**
- Subject: migratory wild animals (birds, mammals, fish, insects, reptiles).
- Mechanism: Appendix I and II listings, Agreements, MOUs, Concerted Actions.
- Coordinating unit: range states.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10; ✅ Wikipedia: CMS is the Bonn Convention)
**Links:** ENV-2040; ENV-2041; ENV-2042; ENV-2043; ENV-2054.
**Trap:** CMS does not regulate trade. CITES does (ENV-2054).
**Mains hook:** Argue that migratory species need transboundary cooperation. Qualify: an MOU is not evidence of protection on every route.
**Back:** *"Migratory species cross borders; CMS coordinates the route."*

### Flashcard ENV-2040 — Range state: the definition that matters
**Front:** Who counts as a range state under CMS?
**Definition (learn):** A range state is any country whose jurisdiction a migratory species' range passes through at any point in its life cycle. It includes breeding, passage, stopover, moulting and wintering jurisdictions, not only the breeding or final destination country.
**Breakdown:**
- Breeding ground: a range state.
- Stopover: a range state.
- Wintering ground: a range state.
- Transit or moulting: a range state.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10)
**Links:** ENV-2039; ENV-2052 (Amur Falcon).
**Trap:** Do not say only the breeding country is a range state.
**Mains hook:** Use range-state logic to show why one country cannot protect a migrant alone. Qualify: presence at one stopover does not prove breeding or safety across the route.
**Back:** *"Every country on the route is a range state."*

### Flashcard ENV-2041 — CMS signed at Bonn, in force 1 November 1983
**Front:** When and where was the CMS signed, and when did it enter into force?
**Definition (learn):** The CMS was signed at Bonn, then West Germany, on 23 June 1979. It entered into force on 1 November 1983, after 15 states had ratified.
**Breakdown:**
- Signed: 23 June 1979, Bonn (West Germany).
- In force: 1 November 1983.
- Trigger: 15 ratifications.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia for 23 June 1979, 1 November 1983 and 15 ratifications; owner 10 gives the year only in general).
**Links:** ENV-2039; ENV-2047.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the 1979 signing with the 1983 entry into force. Do not confuse CMS (1979) with CITES (1973/1975).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains demand. Use for chronology.
**Back:** *"Seventy-nine signed in Bonn; eighty-three in force."*

### Flashcard ENV-2042 — CMS Appendix I: strict protection
**Front:** What obligation does CMS Appendix I impose on range states?
**Definition (learn):** Appendix I lists endangered migratory species. Range states must strictly protect them, prohibit taking (except narrow exceptions) and conserve or restore critical habitat. Special attention goes to removing obstacles, such as structures, that impede migration.
**Breakdown:**
- Listing: endangered migratory species.
- Duty: prohibit taking (narrow exceptions).
- Duty: conserve and restore habitat.
- Duty: remove or mitigate barriers to migration.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10)
**Links:** ENV-2043; ENV-2044; ENV-2053.
**Trap:** Appendix I is binding on range states. Do not call Appendix II equivalent to it.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a strict-protection duty needs habitat and infrastructure action. Qualify: strict duty on paper does not stop power-line collisions.
**Back:** *"One is strict: no taking, restore the habitat, clear the way."*

### Flashcard ENV-2043 — CMS Appendix II: cooperation encouraged
**Front:** What does a CMS Appendix II listing require and what does it encourage?
**Definition (learn):** Appendix II lists species with unfavourable status, or species that would benefit significantly from international cooperation. Range states are encouraged, rather than strictly required, to conclude Agreements or MOUs for coordinated conservation across the range.
**Breakdown:**
- Listing: unfavourable status or benefit from cooperation.
- Duty: encouraged, not mandated.
- Route: Agreements and MOUs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10)
**Links:** ENV-2042; ENV-2045; ENV-2046.
**Trap:** Appendix II is not the stricter tier. Do not say Appendix II carries the same mandatory protection duty as Appendix I.
**Mains hook:** Argue that Appendix II is a flexible route to tailored cooperation. Qualify: flexibility depends on voluntary compliance.
**Back:** *"Two invites cooperation; One orders protection."*

### Flashcard ENV-2044 — Dual listing: Appendix I and II together
**Front:** Can a migratory species be listed on both CMS Appendix I and Appendix II?
**Definition (learn):** Yes. A species may be on both. Appendix I imposes protection duties; Appendix II opens the route to a cooperative Agreement or MOU. The two serve different functions.
**Breakdown:**
- Appendix I: protection duty.
- Appendix II: cooperation route.
- Both: deliberate, not contradictory.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10)
**Links:** ENV-2042; ENV-2043.
**Trap:** Do not assume a species must be in only one Appendix.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone demand. Use to explain layered protection.
**Back:** *"Both appendices can hold one species: duty plus route."*

### Flashcard ENV-2045 — CMS Agreement vs MOU: legal weight
**Front:** What is the legal difference between a CMS Agreement and a CMS MOU?
**Definition (learn):** A CMS Agreement is legally binding on the range states that sign it. A Memorandum of Understanding is non-binding and voluntary, and is faster to conclude. CMS supports both instruments.
**Breakdown:**
- Agreement: binding on signatories.
- MOU: non-binding, voluntary.
- Trade-off: binding instruments are harder to negotiate; MOUs can mature into stronger ones.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2043; ENV-2048.
**Trap:** Not all CMS instruments are binding. All four of India's CMS instruments are MOUs (owner 10 basic).
**Mains hook:** Discuss the trade-off between binding and voluntary instruments. Qualify: MOUs help broad buy-in but depend on compliance.
**Back:** *"Agreement binds signatories; MOU invites them."*

### Flashcard ENV-2046 — Concerted Actions: the bridge between listing and field work
**Front:** What are CMS Concerted Actions?
**Definition (learn):** Concerted Actions designate priority species or populations for focused, time-bound cooperative measures between COPs. They operationalise an Appendix listing on the ground.
**Breakdown:**
- Designation: priority species or population.
- Duration: time-bound.
- Between: COP meetings.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10)
**Links:** ENV-2043; ENV-2049.
**Trap:** A Concerted Action is not an Agreement. It is an operational cooperation tool.
**Mains hook:** Not standalone. Use to show implementation between COPs.
**Back:** *"Concerted Action: focus the flock, time-bound, between COPs."*

### Flashcard ENV-2047 — India and CMS: party since 1983
**Front:** Since when has India been a Party to CMS?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 10 states that India has been a Party to CMS since 1983. The fetched Wikipedia page lists India among parties but did not show an accession year.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: Party since 1983.
- Wikipedia: list of parties visible, no year.
- Status: ⚠️ year not independently confirmed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ 1983 (owner 10); the CMS entry into force was also 1 November 1983 (✅ Wikipedia). Verify the accession date on the CMS Party list.
**Links:** ENV-2041; ENV-2048.
**Trap:** Do not conflate India's year of accession with the Convention's entry into force.
**Mains hook:** Not standalone. Use for India's commitment chronology.
**Back:** *"India in since eighty-three: check the party list."*

### Flashcard ENV-2048 — India's four CMS MOUs
**Front:** Which four CMS instruments has India signed, and what is their legal character?
**Definition (learn):** India has signed four CMS species-specific MOUs: Siberian Crane, Marine Turtles (Indian Ocean-South-East Asian region), Dugong, and Raptors (Birds of Prey). All are non-legally-binding MOUs.
**Breakdown:**
- Siberian Crane MOU.
- Marine Turtles MOU (Indian Ocean-South-East Asia).
- Dugong MOU.
- Raptors (Birds of Prey) MOU.
- All: non-binding.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10 basic). ⚠️ Not verified against the CMS MOU list in this session.
**Links:** ENV-2045; ENV-2047; ENV-2049.
**Trap:** Do not say India has signed a binding CMS Agreement under these four. They are MOUs.
**Mains hook:** Use the MOUs to show India's cooperative role. Qualify: MOUs rely on voluntary implementation.
**Back:** *"Crane, turtle, dugong, raptor: four MOUs, none binding."*

### Flashcard ENV-2049 — CMS COP13 Gandhinagar 2020 and India's Presidency
**Front:** Where and when did India host CMS COP13, and what did it produce?
**Definition (learn):** India hosted the 13th CMS Conference of the Parties in Gandhinagar, Gujarat, in 2020. India then held the CMS Presidency for three years. The meeting produced the Gandhinagar Declaration on migratory species and ecological connectivity.
**Breakdown:**
- Venue: Gandhinagar, Gujarat.
- Year: 2020.
- Presidency: three-year term afterward.
- Outcome: Gandhinagar Declaration.
- Mascot: "Gibi" (owner-stated, ⚠️ not independently verified).
- Theme: "Migratory species connect the planet and we welcome them home" (owner-stated).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10; ⚠️ mascot and theme wording from owner only)
**Links:** ENV-2046; ENV-2050; ENV-2054.
**Trap:** COP13 is historical context, not the latest completed COP. The latest completed COP is COP15 (ENV-2051).
**Mains hook:** Argue that India used its Presidency to push Central Asian Flyway coordination. Qualify: hosting is not the same as institutional delivery.
**Back:** *"Gandhinagar twenty-twenty: India hosts, presides, declares."*

### Flashcard ENV-2050 — CMS COP14 Samarkand 2024 and the Strategic Plan 2024-2032
**Front:** What did CMS COP14 (Samarkand, February 2024) produce?
**Definition (learn):** COP14 at Samarkand in February 2024 produced the Samarkand Strategic Plan for Migratory Species 2024-2032 and the first State of the World's Migratory Species report.
**Breakdown:**
- Venue: Samarkand.
- Date: February 2024.
- Outputs: Strategic Plan 2024-2032; first State of the World's Migratory Species report.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10)
**Links:** ENV-2049; ENV-2051.
**Trap:** COP14 is not the latest completed COP. COP15 is later.
**Mains hook:** Not standalone. Use as the strategy anchor before COP15.
**Back:** *"Samarkand twenty-four: strategy to 2032, first world report."*

### Flashcard ENV-2051 — CMS COP15 Campo Grande 2026 and the 27 June 2026 Appendices
**Front:** What is the latest completed CMS COP, and when did its revised Appendices enter into force?
**Definition (learn):** CMS COP15 met at Campo Grande, Brazil, from 23 to 29 March 2026. Notification 2026/006 (dated 22 April 2026) records 40 taxa added and taxonomic changes. Notification 2026/012 (dated 29 June 2026) confirms that the revised Appendices entered into force on 27 June 2026, except for a Party making a reservation.
**Breakdown:**
- Venue: Campo Grande, Brazil.
- Dates: 23 to 29 March 2026.
- Taxa added: 40 (Notification 2026/006, 22 April 2026).
- Entry into force: 27 June 2026 (Notification 2026/012, 29 June 2026), subject to reservation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 📰 (✅ owner 10 and 10 advanced with CMS notification links).
**Links:** ENV-2050; ENV-2053; ENV-2052.
**Trap:** Adoption is not entry into force. Reservations can alter the legal result for a Party.
**Mains hook:** Cite COP15 as the current baseline. Qualify: a COP entry does not prove range-wide implementation or recovery.
**Back:** *"Campo Grande decides, twenty-seven June operates."*

### Flashcard ENV-2052 — Amur Falcon and Doyang/Pangti stopover (2026 Prelims demand)
**Front:** Which 2026 Prelims GS-I question concerns the Amur Falcon, and what is the right analytical route?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 Prelims GS-I Q27 asks about Amur Falcon migration to Doyang Lake and community-based conservation. Community protection at this Indian stopover in Nagaland secures one route segment. It does not establish breeding in India or safety across the whole flyway.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: Amur Falcon migration; Doyang Lake; community conservation.
- Route segment: Indian stopover (Pangti/Doyang, Nagaland).
- Limit: not breeding, not full-range safety.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q27 (✅ owner 10). Provisional 2026 Set-A key present locally; answer not recorded or inferred here.
**Links:** ENV-2040; ENV-2046; ENV-2054.
**Trap:** Do not state the answer from this card. Do not treat one community success as range-wide proof.
**Mains hook:** Use Amur Falcon to show that local stopover protection can repair one vulnerable link. Qualify: a route is only as strong as its weakest stage.
**Back:** *"Amur Falcon: community saves one stop, not the whole flyway."*

### Flashcard ENV-2053 — Great Indian Bustard: three legal layers
**Front:** How do the Great Indian Bustard's IUCN, Indian and CMS listings differ?
**Definition (learn):** The Great Indian Bustard has three distinct records: IUCN Critically Endangered (assessed 17 August 2018); Wildlife Protection Act Schedule I; and CMS Appendix I in the revised Appendices effective 27 June 2026. These are separate records, not one merged highest-protection label.
**Breakdown:**
- IUCN: Critically Endangered, 17 August 2018.
- WPA: Schedule I.
- CMS: Appendix I (revised, effective 27 June 2026).
- Main threat: collision with high-voltage transmission lines (domestic infrastructure problem).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10 advanced; ✅ owner 05 cross-ref for IUCN; ✅ owner 08 for Schedule I).
**Links:** ENV-2037; ENV-2003; ENV-2051; ENV-2055.
**Trap:** Do not merge IUCN, WPA and CMS into one "highest protection" claim.
**Mains hook:** Use the bustard to separate range-state failures from domestic implementation failures. Qualify: power-line mitigation is a national fix.
**Back:** *"Bustard: IUCN red, Schedule I, CMS one, three separate records."*

### Flashcard ENV-2054 — CMS vs CITES: range and habitat vs trade
**Front:** How does CMS differ from CITES in purpose and tools?
**Definition (learn):** CMS focuses on migratory species conservation across their range, through habitat protection and range-state cooperation. CITES focuses on regulating international trade through Appendices and permits. CMS listing and CITES listing are never interchangeable.
**Breakdown:**
- CMS: range, habitat, cooperation (Appendices, Agreements, MOUs).
- CITES: trade, permits (Appendices, Management and Scientific Authorities).
- Ramsar: wetland-specific (not owned here).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10 and owner 09)
**Links:** ENV-2021; ENV-2039; ENV-2036.
**Trap:** Do not say CMS regulates trade in migratory species. Do not say CITES covers migratory-route cooperation.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a migratory species needs both trade control and range cooperation where relevant. Qualify: each tool covers a different risk.
**Back:** *"CMS guards the route; CITES guards the trade."*

### Flashcard ENV-2055 — Central Asian Flyway: an under-institutionalised route
**Front:** Why is the Central Asian Flyway a frequently cited example of the CMS weakest-link problem?
**Definition (learn):** The Central Asian Flyway runs from Arctic Russia and Central Asia through South Asia to the Indian Ocean, passing directly through India. It has historically had a less formalised institutional architecture than some other major flyways, despite CMS-linked efforts, including a CMS Action Plan framework.
**Breakdown:**
- Route: Arctic Russia and Central Asia to the Indian Ocean.
- India: a passage country and range state.
- Gap: less formal coordination than some global flyways.
- India's response: CoP13 Presidency pushed coordination (ENV-2049).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 10 advanced; ⚠️ qualitative comparison, no ranking claimed)
**Links:** ENV-2040; ENV-2049; ENV-2052.
**Trap:** Do not assert that the Central Asian Flyway has an equally formal partnership to other flyways. Do not rank flyways without a cited assessment.
**Mains hook:** Argue that flyway protection needs institutional strengthening alongside local action. Qualify: MOUs and action plans are not yet a formal flyway partnership.
**Back:** *"Central Asian Flyway: India's route, a loose institution."*

### GROUP — Forest Types and Forest Rights Act (owner 11)

### Flashcard ENV-2056 — Champion and Seth forest classification (1968)
**Front:** What is the standard framework for classifying India's forest types, and how many groups does it recognise?
**Definition (learn):** The Champion and Seth (1968) revised survey is the standard reference framework. It groups India's forests into 16 forest type groups under five broad classes: tropical moist, tropical dry, montane subtropical, montane temperate and alpine.
**Breakdown:**
- Classes: tropical moist, tropical dry, montane subtropical, montane temperate, alpine.
- Groups: 16.
- Driver: rainfall gradient, with altitude in the Himalaya.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11, citing NCERT biogeography). ⚠️ The 16-group and 5-class figures are owner-stated; not checked against the 1968 text in this session.
**Links:** ENV-2057; ENV-2058; ENV-2059; ENV-2060; ENV-2063.
**Trap:** Forest type is ecological and climatic. Canopy density is a separate measurement (ENV-2062).
**Mains hook:** Explain forest types by rainfall and altitude. Qualify: local soils, fire and grazing shape real boundaries.
**Back:** *"Champion-Seth: sixteen types, five classes, rainfall first."*

### Flashcard ENV-2057 — Tropical wet evergreen forest
**Front:** Where and under what rainfall does tropical wet evergreen forest occur in India?
**Definition (learn):** Tropical wet evergreen forest is dense and multi-storeyed, found in very high-rainfall zones such as the Western Ghats windward slopes and parts of the Northeast.
**Breakdown:**
- Rainfall: very high (the wettest end of the gradient).
- Structure: dense, multi-storeyed.
- Location: Western Ghats windward slopes; parts of Northeast India.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2056; ENV-2058.
**Trap:** Wet evergreen is not thorn forest. Thorn forest is the drought-adapted end.
**Mains hook:** Use rainfall to explain biodiversity hotspots. Qualify: forest type alone does not measure biodiversity status.
**Back:** *"Wettest zone, tallest storeys: wet evergreen."*

### Flashcard ENV-2058 — Tropical moist and dry deciduous: the largest share
**Front:** Which forest types cover the largest share of India's forested area?
**Definition (learn):** Tropical moist and dry deciduous forests together cover the largest share of India's forested area. Their trees shed leaves seasonally, and they are found across much of the peninsula and the Himalayan foothills.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: moist and dry deciduous.
- Share: largest share of India's forest.
- Location: peninsula and Himalayan foothills.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2056; ENV-2059; ENV-2079 (PYQ 2023 Q3 demand).
**Trap:** Do not say tropical thorn forest covers the largest area. Thorn forest is a smaller, drier type.
**Mains hook:** Discuss why deciduous forests dominate and what that means for fire and grazing pressures. Qualify: management depends on local conditions.
**Back:** *"Deciduous dominates: moist and dry together lead."*

### Flashcard ENV-2059 — Tropical thorn forest: drought-adapted
**Front:** Where is tropical thorn forest found and what is its key feature?
**Definition (learn):** Tropical thorn forest is sparse, thorny and drought-adapted vegetation found in low-rainfall or arid zones, such as parts of Rajasthan, Gujarat and the Deccan interior.
**Breakdown:**
- Rainfall: low (the driest forest end).
- Structure: sparse, thorny.
- Location: Rajasthan, Gujarat, Deccan interior (owner examples).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2056; ENV-2058.
**Trap:** Thorn forest is not the largest share of Indian forest.
**Mains hook:** Not standalone. Use as the dry end of the rainfall gradient.
**Back:** *"Driest end, thorns and sparse cover: thorn forest."*

### Flashcard ENV-2060 — Montane and alpine forests: the altitude belt
**Front:** What drives forest zonation in the Himalaya, and how does it differ from peninsular types?
**Definition (learn):** In the Himalaya, altitude drives forest zonation. Vegetation transitions from temperate to sub-alpine to alpine as elevation rises. Rainfall is the primary driver across most of the rest of India.
**Breakdown:**
- Driver: altitude in the Himalaya.
- Sequence: temperate, sub-alpine, alpine with rising elevation.
- Contrast: rainfall gradient in most of India.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2056; ENV-2058.
**Trap:** Altitude is not the primary determinant across most of India. It is the key factor specifically in the Himalayan zone.
**Mains hook:** Use altitude zonation to explain why Himalayan forests differ from peninsular forests. Qualify: local slope and aspect also matter.
**Back:** *"Himalaya climbs by altitude; the rest of India by rain."*

### Flashcard ENV-2061 — Legal forest categories under the Indian Forest Act, 1927
**Front:** What are the three legal forest categories under the Indian Forest Act, 1927?
**Definition (learn):** Reserved Forest is the most restrictive: everything is prohibited unless expressly permitted. Protected Forest allows everything unless expressly prohibited. Village or Unclassed Forest is the third category. These are legal categories, not measurement classes.
**Breakdown:**
- Reserved Forest: prohibited unless permitted.
- Protected Forest: permitted unless prohibited.
- Village or Unclassed Forest: third legal category.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2062; ENV-2077 (reversal trap); ENV-2078.
**Trap:** Reserved and Protected forests are not the same with different names. Their permission logic is reversed.
**Mains hook:** Use legal categories to show that legal status and ecology differ. Qualify: a degraded notified forest may still be legally protected.
**Back:** *"Reserved: nothing unless allowed. Protected: anything unless banned."*

### Flashcard ENV-2062 — FSI canopy-density classes
**Front:** What are the four FSI canopy-density classes and their thresholds?
**Definition (learn):** FSI measures canopy density in four classes: Very Dense Forest (canopy 70% or more), Moderately Dense Forest (40% to 70%), Open Forest (10% to 40%) and Scrub (below 10%).
**Breakdown:**
- Very Dense: ≥70%.
- Moderately Dense: 40% to 70%.
- Open: 10% to 40%.
- Scrub: <10%.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11, owner 12 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2063; ENV-2061; ENV-2075.
**Trap:** Canopy density classes are not legal categories. They are administrative measurement classes.
**Mains hook:** Use canopy classes to show measurement versus law. Qualify: canopy does not show ecological quality.
**Back:** *"Seventy, forty, ten: very dense, moderate, open, scrub."*

### Flashcard ENV-2063 — ISFR forest cover: ≥1 ha and above 10% canopy
**Front:** How does the ISFR define forest cover, and does ownership matter?
**Definition (learn):** Forest cover in the India State of Forest Report is all land of at least 1 hectare with tree canopy density above 10%, irrespective of ownership or legal status. Plantations and orchards can therefore count.
**Breakdown:**
- Minimum area: 1 ha.
- Canopy: above 10%.
- Ownership: irrelevant.
- Legal status: irrelevant.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2062; ENV-2076; ENV-2075.
**Trap:** Forest cover is not recorded forest area. Do not use cover data for tenure claims.
**Mains hook:** Argue that cover data cannot answer legal or tenure questions. Qualify: cover shows canopy, not ecological integrity.
**Back:** *"One hectare, ten percent canopy, any owner: forest cover."*

### Flashcard ENV-2064 — FRA: full title and enactment
**Front:** What is the full title of the Forest Rights Act and when was it enacted?
**Definition (learn):** The full title is the Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act, 2006. It recognises the rights of forest-dwelling Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers over forest land they occupied.
**Breakdown:**
- Full title: as above.
- Enactment: 2006 (Wikipedia gives notification dates of 18 and 29 December 2006).
- Operative: Wikipedia states it took effect on 31 December 2007.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11; ✅ Wikipedia for 2006 and 31 December 2007). ⚠️ Wikipedia's enactment date (18/29 December) and owner's silence on the in-force date should be checked on India Code.
**Links:** ENV-2065; ENV-2066; ENV-2080.
**Trap:** Do not state the 13 December 2005 cut-off as the Act's date. It is a pre-enactment cut-off.
**Mains hook:** Explain the FRA as a rights-restoration statute. Qualify: its design is progressive, and its implementation is the contested part.
**Back:** *"Two thousand six makes it law; two thousand five is the cut-off."*

### Flashcard ENV-2065 — The 13 December 2005 cut-off
**Front:** What is the FRA cut-off date for occupation, and what does it mean?
**Definition (learn):** Rights are recognised for occupation before 13 December 2005. This is a statutory cut-off that predates the Act's enactment. Occupation after this date does not qualify.
**Breakdown:**
- Cut-off: 13 December 2005.
- Applies to: both STs and OTFDs.
- Effect: evidence of occupation before the cut-off.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11; ✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** ENV-2064; ENV-2066.
**Trap:** The cut-off is not the date the Act came into force. It is earlier.
**Mains hook:** Discuss how a cut-off date can exclude people with recent occupation. Qualify: evidence burdens fall heaviest on oral tenure communities.
**Back:** *"Thirteen December two thousand five: prior occupation or nothing."*

### Flashcard ENV-2066 — OTFD test: three generations, 75 years
**Front:** What extra test applies to Other Traditional Forest Dwellers under the FRA?
**Definition (learn):** OTFDs must show primary residence in forest land and dependence on the forest for at least three generations, equated to 75 years, before the 13 December 2005 cut-off. Scheduled Tribes face no such generational test.
**Breakdown:**
- OTFD: three generations (75 years) of residence and dependence.
- ST: no generational test.
- Cut-off: 13 December 2005 for both.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11; ✅ Wikipedia for three generations/75 years)
**Links:** ENV-2065; ENV-2064.
**Trap:** Do not say the FRA applies only to Scheduled Tribes. It also covers OTFDs who meet the test.
**Mains hook:** Argue that the generational test can exclude genuine forest dwellers. Qualify: documentary proof is hard for oral histories.
**Back:** *"OTFD: seventy-five years of forest home before the cut-off."*

### Flashcard ENV-2067 — Individual Forest Rights (IFR) and the 4-hectare ceiling
**Front:** What do Individual Forest Rights cover and what is the area limit?
**Definition (learn):** IFR vest the right to hold and live in forest land under individual or family occupation, for habitation or self-cultivation. The area is capped at the land under actual occupation, subject to a statutory maximum of 4 hectares.
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: habitation or self-cultivation.
- Holder: individual or family.
- Cap: actual occupation, maximum 4 ha.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11; ✅ Wikipedia confirms the 4-hectare ceiling on title rights).
**Links:** ENV-2068; ENV-2069; ENV-2072.
**Trap:** IFR is not a community right. Do not confuse the 4 ha cap with the 1 ha diversion limit (ENV-2071).
**Mains hook:** Discuss individual titles as tenure security. Qualify: caps and evidence standards limit the benefit.
**Back:** *"IFR: my home, my field, four hectares at most."*

### Flashcard ENV-2068 — Community Rights: grazing, water and minor forest produce
**Front:** What uses do Community Rights under the FRA cover?
**Definition (learn):** Community Rights cover uses such as grazing, access to water bodies, and minor forest produce for own use. They are traditionally exercised by the community as a whole, not by individuals.
**Breakdown:**
- Grazing.
- Access to water bodies.
- Minor forest produce (own use).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2067; ENV-2069; ENV-2073.
**Trap:** Community Rights are not the same as CFR rights. CFR gives a management role, not only use.
**Mains hook:** Show how use rights differ from management rights. Qualify: use rights alone do not secure conservation.
**Back:** *"Community use: graze, drink, gather, all together."*

### Flashcard ENV-2069 — Community Forest Resource (CFR) rights
**Front:** What do CFR rights give a community, and how do they differ from individual rights?
**Definition (learn):** CFR rights vest the community with the right and responsibility to protect, regenerate, conserve and manage a community forest resource it has traditionally protected and used. They create a formal statutory forest-governance role, the most conservation-relevant FRA category.
**Breakdown:**
- Right: protect, regenerate, conserve, manage.
- Holder: the community.
- Effect: formal governance role.
- Limit: needs technical and financial support to work.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11; ✅ owner 12 for NAPCC linkage)
**Links:** ENV-2067; ENV-2068; ENV-2073.
**Trap:** CFR is not the same as individual rights. ⚠️ Wikipedia's rights summary lists four categories (title, use, relief and development, management) rather than the three in the owner. Use the owner's three-category framing and flag the difference.
**Mains hook:** Argue that CFR shifts forest governance from restriction to community empowerment. Qualify: a title without support does not guarantee outcomes.
**Back:** *"CFR: protect, regrow, conserve, manage: the community governs."*

### Flashcard ENV-2070 — Section 4(5): the eviction bar
**Front:** What does FRA Section 4(5) provide, and why is it the key legal pivot?
**Definition (learn):** Section 4(5) provides that no member of a forest-dwelling Scheduled Tribe or OTFD shall be evicted or removed from forest land until the recognition and verification procedure is complete. It is the Act's strongest procedural safeguard.
**Breakdown:**
- Protects: members of forest-dwelling STs and OTFDs.
- Bars: eviction or removal.
- Until: recognition and verification complete.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11 basic and advanced). ⚠️ Wikipedia did not confirm the section number in this session.
**Links:** ENV-2072; ENV-2071; ENV-2078.
**Trap:** Section 4(5) does not erase every lawful conservation or diversion process. Sequencing and facts matter.
**Mains hook:** Argue that relocation without settled claims breaches the Section 4(5) logic. Qualify: a lawful process can still proceed if sequencing is respected.
**Back:** *"Four-five: no eviction until the claim is verified."*

### Flashcard ENV-2071 — Section 3(2): diversion for 13 community facilities
**Front:** What does FRA Section 3(2) permit, and what are its limits?
**Definition (learn):** Section 3(2) permits diversion of forest land for 13 categories of community development facilities. It is limited to 1 hectare per project, with felling of not more than 75 trees per hectare, and needs a Gram Sabha recommendation.
**Breakdown:**
- Limit: up to 1 ha.
- Felling: not more than 75 trees per ha.
- Recommendation: Gram Sabha.
- Facilities (13): schools, hospitals, anganwadis, fair-price shops, roads, drinking water, minor irrigation, electric and telecom lines, tanks, vocational centres, community centres, rainwater harvesting, non-conventional energy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11 basic). ⚠️ Wikipedia did not confirm the Section number or the 1 ha limit.
**Links:** ENV-2070; ENV-2067.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the 1 ha diversion limit with the 4 ha IFR cap.
