**Mains hook:** Discuss how small-scale diversion meets local needs. Qualify: the Gram Sabha must actually decide.
**Back:** *"Three-two: one hectare, seventy-five trees, thirteen local facilities."*

### Flashcard ENV-2072 — The claims process: Gram Sabha, SDLC, DLC
**Front:** What are the stages of the FRA claims process, in order?
**Definition (learn):** Claims are first verified by the Gram Sabha (village assembly). They go next to the Sub-Divisional Level Committee (SDLC), which examines resolutions and may ask for evidence. The District Level Committee (DLC) gives final approval or rejection and title issuance.
**Breakdown:**
- Stage 1: Gram Sabha resolution.
- Stage 2: SDLC review.
- Stage 3: DLC final approval.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2070; ENV-2073; ENV-2074.
**Trap:** The Gram Sabha starts the process but does not grant titles. The DLC is the final approving body.
**Mains hook:** Trace the process and locate its friction points. Qualify: high rejection rates reflect evidence standards more than statutory design.
**Back:** *"Gram Sabha starts, SDLC reviews, DLC decides."*

### Flashcard ENV-2073 — Minor forest produce and PVTG rights
**Front:** What rights does the FRA give over minor forest produce and to Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Groups?
**Definition (learn):** FRA recognises ownership, access, use and disposal rights over minor forest produce (MFP). It also grants rights to Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Groups (PVTGs) and pre-agricultural communities over habitat.
**Breakdown:**
- MFP: ownership, access, use, disposal.
- PVTGs and pre-agricultural communities: habitat rights.
- Example: bamboo as MFP (2019 PYQ demand).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2068; ENV-2069; ENV-2079.
**Trap:** MFP ownership is not a plantation or timber-logging right. Do not confuse MFP with timber.
**Mains hook:** Argue that MFP rights support livelihoods. Qualify: markets and processing decide returns.
**Back:** *"MFP is theirs to own; PVTG habitat is theirs to keep."*

### Flashcard ENV-2074 — Institutions: Ministry of Tribal Affairs, FSI, Gram Sabha
**Front:** Which institutions are central to FRA implementation and forest-cover measurement?
**Definition (learn):** The Ministry of Tribal Affairs is the nodal ministry for FRA implementation. The Gram Sabha is the foundational unit for claims. The Forest Survey of India (FSI) classifies forest types and monitors cover through the biennial State of Forest Report. State Forest Departments retain forest management and often overlap with FRA work.
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry of Tribal Affairs: nodal FRA ministry.
- Gram Sabha: claim starting point.
- FSI: forest cover and type monitoring (ISFR).
- State Forest Departments: management and overlap.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2072; ENV-2075; ENV-2063.
**Trap:** FSI cover data does not measure FRA claims. Use Tribal Affairs reports for claim statistics.
**Mains hook:** Argue for better coordination between Tribal Affairs and the Forest Department. Qualify: coordination gaps persist.
**Back:** *"Tribal Affairs runs FRA, FSI counts canopy, Gram Sabha starts claims."*

### Flashcard ENV-2075 — ISFR 2023: 8,27,357 sq km and 25.17%
**Front:** What do the 18th ISFR figures say about India's total forest and tree cover?
**Definition (learn):** The 18th ISFR (2023 assessment, released December 2024) reports total forest and tree cover at 8,27,357 sq km, or 25.17% of the geographical area. It is the dated baseline for forest-extent discussion.
**Breakdown:**
- Figure: 8,27,357 sq km.
- Share: 25.17% of geographical area.
- Assessment year: 2023; released December 2024.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 📰 (✅ owner 11 and owner 11 advanced). Cite the report year and do not apply it to claim statistics.
**Links:** ENV-2063; ENV-2074; ENV-2076.
**Trap:** Forest and tree cover is not forest quality or recorded forest area. Do not use this figure for FRA claims.
**Mains hook:** Use the ISFR as dated extent evidence. Qualify: canopy and stock differ from ecological health.
**Back:** *"Two-three-two-seven: twenty-five percent of land, canopy counted."*

### Flashcard ENV-2076 — Forest cover vs recorded forest area: the category trap
**Front:** Why are forest cover and recorded forest area different numbers?
**Definition (learn):** Forest cover is a canopy-density measurement (at least 1 ha, above 10% canopy, any ownership). Recorded forest area is the legally notified forest land in government records. The two rarely coincide.
**Breakdown:**
- Cover: canopy-based.
- Recorded forest area: legal and record-based.
- Plantation: can count as cover, not as recorded forest.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11; ✅ owner 11 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2063; ENV-2061; ENV-2078.
**Trap:** Do not use them interchangeably. Do not make legal claims from cover numbers.
**Mains hook:** Argue that category discipline matters for tenure and diversion decisions. Qualify: the 2023 Act and the 2024 order complicate the picture.
**Back:** *"Cover counts canopy; record counts law."*

### Flashcard ENV-2077 — Reserved vs Protected Forest: the reversed permission logic
**Front:** Why are Reserved and Protected Forests not just different names?
**Definition (learn):** In a Reserved Forest, everything is prohibited unless expressly permitted. In a Protected Forest, everything is permitted unless expressly prohibited. The permission logic is inverted.
**Breakdown:**
- Reserved: prohibited unless permitted.
- Protected: permitted unless prohibited.
- Both: under the Indian Forest Act, 1927.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2061; ENV-2076.
**Trap:** A reversal pair is a close-option trap. Do not state both allow the same activity by default.
**Mains hook:** Use the permission logic to show how legal status shapes local use. Qualify: enforcement and notification matter.
**Back:** *"Reserved says no unless yes; Protected says yes unless no."*

### Flashcard ENV-2078 — Three legal orders on one hectare
**Front:** Why are FRA-linked forest questions hard, and what are the three layers that apply to one hectare?
**Definition (learn):** One hectare of Indian forest can sit inside three systems at once: the legal-tenure layer (Indian Forest Act, 1927, and the Van Adhiniyam, 1980), the measurement layer (FSI cover), and the rights layer (FRA 2006). Each has its own authority, standard and remedy.
**Breakdown:**
- Legal-tenure: Indian Forest Act 1927; Van (Sanrakshan Evam Samvardhan) Adhiniyam 1980 (renamed 2023).
- Measurement: FSI ISFR cover.
- Rights: FRA 2006; Gram Sabha, SDLC, DLC.
- Court: SC February 2024 interim direction on the Godavarman definition.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11 advanced; ✅ owner 12 advanced for 2023 and 2024)
**Links:** ENV-2086; ENV-2089; ENV-2076; ENV-2070.
**Trap:** Do not treat one layer as the whole answer. The statute alone overstates the narrowing; the order alone understates it.
**Mains hook:** Show the sequence: statute narrowed, court preserved breadth, state records pending. Qualify: outcomes depend on records.
**Back:** *"One hectare, three laws: legal, measured, claimed."*

### Flashcard ENV-2079 — PYQ routing: forest types, cover and FRA demands
**Front:** Which PYQs route to the forest-types and FRA owner, and what do they test?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 11 routes these demands: 2018 Q98 (FRA Critical Wildlife Habitat and Baiga rights); 2019 Q33 (states ranked by forest cover); 2019 Q55 (FRA bamboo as MFP); 2020 GS-I Q17 (status of forest resources and climate, 15 marks); 2022 Q50 (Miyawaki method); 2023 Q3 (deciduous trees classification); 2024 Q30 (native tree species: cashew, papaya, red sanders). The 2025 owner list is not in this block.
**Breakdown:**
- Prelims: forest-type classification, FRA rights, MFP, Miyawaki, native trees.
- Mains: forest resources and climate (2020).
- Answer keys: not recorded in owner 11.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11 routing ledger; keys not recorded)
**Links:** ENV-2056; ENV-2058; ENV-2068; ENV-2073; ENV-2075.
**Trap:** Do not infer answer letters from these demand summaries.
**Mains hook:** The 2020 forest and climate question needs ISFR as dated evidence plus a counterpoint on forest quality.
**Back:** *"Seven PYQ routes: types, cover, rights, MFP, Miyawaki, native trees, climate."*

### Flashcard ENV-2080 — Concept: FRA as a rights-restoration statute
**Front:** What is the core theme of the Forest Rights Act and how should an answer frame it?
**Definition (learn):** The FRA is a rights-restoration statute. It corrects the historical exclusion of forest-dwelling communities from land they occupied before state forest law formalised ownership. It is not primarily a forest-management statute, although its CFR provisions carry conservation implications.
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: restore rights (historical injustice).
- Rights: IFR, Community Rights, CFR.
- Process: Gram Sabha, SDLC, DLC.
- Conservation: a secondary effect through CFR.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 11)
**Links:** ENV-2064; ENV-2069; ENV-2070; ENV-2072.
**Trap:** Do not treat the FRA as a conservation law. Do not treat tenure recognition as proof of conservation.
**Mains hook:** Argue that secure tenure can support stewardship. Qualify: a title alone does not prove ecological improvement.
**Back:** *"FRA restores rights first; conservation follows only with support."*

### GROUP — Forest Governance, CAMPA and Green India Mission (owner 12)

### Flashcard ENV-2081 — CAMPA: the compensatory mechanism for diverted forest land
**Front:** What is CAMPA and what is its trigger?
**Definition (learn):** CAMPA (Compensatory Afforestation Fund Management and Planning Authority) manages funds collected for compensatory afforestation and related forest and wildlife activities. Its trigger is the diversion of forest land for non-forest use, paid for by the user agency.
**Breakdown:**
- Trigger: forest-land diversion under the Van Adhiniyam, 1980.
- Payer: user agency (mining, roads, industry).
- Use: afforestation, regeneration, wildlife management, forest infrastructure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ENV-2082; ENV-2083; ENV-2084; ENV-2086; ENV-2090.
**Trap:** CAMPA is not a general greening programme. It is diversion-triggered.
**Mains hook:** Explain CAMPA as a compensatory finance instrument. Qualify: money collected is not restoration achieved.
**Back:** *"CAMPA: diversion in, compensation funded, use monitored."*

### Flashcard ENV-2082 — Compensatory Afforestation Fund Act, 2016
**Front:** Which Act gives CAMPA its statutory basis, and what does it establish?
**Definition (learn):** The Compensatory Afforestation Fund Act, 2016 establishes the National and State Compensatory Afforestation Funds (CAF) and their governance and utilisation framework.
**Breakdown:**
- Establishes: National CAF and State CAFs.
- Governs: utilisation and planning.
- Rules: CAF Rules, 2018 (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2081; ENV-2084.
**Trap:** The 2016 Act is different from the 1980 Van Adhiniyam, which is the diversion-approval trigger.
**Mains hook:** Argue that statutory funds allow stable compensation. Qualify: utilisation is the bottleneck.
**Back:** *"Sixteen made the fund; eighty made the trigger."*

### Flashcard ENV-2083 — NPV plus compensatory afforestation cost: the payment trigger
**Front:** What must a user agency pay when forest land is diverted?
**Definition (learn):** The user agency pays the Net Present Value (NPV) of the diverted forest land, which values the ecosystem services lost, plus the cost of compensatory afforestation. Both flow into the Compensatory Afforestation Fund.
**Breakdown:**
- Payment 1: NPV of diverted forest land.
- Payment 2: compensatory afforestation cost.
- Destination: CAF.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ENV-2081; ENV-2084; ENV-2085.
**Trap:** NPV is an ecosystem-service value, not a plantation cost alone.
**Mains hook:** Explain the payment chain. Qualify: NPV valuation methods shape adequacy.
**Back:** *"Pay NPV and planting cost: forest out, fund in."*

### Flashcard ENV-2084 — National CAF vs State CAF: the share split
**Front:** How are CAMPA funds split between the National CAF and State CAFs?
**Definition (learn):** A smaller share is retained in the National CAF for national priorities. The larger share goes to State CAFs for compensatory afforestation, forest regeneration, wildlife management and permitted forest infrastructure in each state.
**Breakdown:**
- National CAF: smaller retained share.
- State CAFs: larger share.
- State use: afforestation, regeneration, wildlife, infrastructure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12). Exact percentages are not stated in the owner and are not carded here (⚠️ check the CAF Act).
**Links:** ENV-2082; ENV-2105.
**Trap:** CAMPA funds are not entirely central. The bulk goes to states.
**Mains hook:** Discuss why state-level utilisation is the bottleneck. Qualify: state planning capacity varies.
**Back:** *"National keeps a little; the states get most."*

### Flashcard ENV-2085 — Where compensatory afforestation must occur
**Front:** Where must compensatory afforestation be done for a diverted forest area?
**Definition (learn):** Compensatory afforestation is done on non-forest land equal in extent to the diverted area. If non-forest land is not available, it is done on degraded forest land twice the extent of the diverted area.
**Breakdown:**
- Primary: non-forest land, equal extent.
- Fallback: degraded forest land, twice the extent.
- Purpose: offset diversion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic). ⚠️ The twice-the-extent fallback is owner-stated and should be checked against the Forest (Conservation) Rules.
**Links:** ENV-2081; ENV-2099; ENV-2103.
**Trap:** Compensatory afforestation is not always on forest land. Its primary site is non-forest land.
**Mains hook:** Argue that site choice drives the ecological quality of replacement. Qualify: degraded land may already carry community rights.
**Back:** *"Equal non-forest land first; degraded forest twice over as fallback."*

### Flashcard ENV-2086 — Van (Sanrakshan Evam Samvardhan) Adhiniyam, 1980
**Front:** What is the current name of the Forest (Conservation) Act, 1980, and what changed in 2023?
**Definition (learn):** The Forest (Conservation) Act, 1980 was renamed the Van (Sanrakshan Evam Samvardhan) Adhiniyam, 1980 by the Forest (Conservation) Amendment Act, 2023. The Act was not repealed. It remains the diversion-approval trigger for CAMPA payments.
**Breakdown:**
- Old name: Forest (Conservation) Act, 1980.
- New name: Van (Sanrakshan Evam Samvardhan) Adhiniyam, 1980.
- Change: renamed and scope redefined in 2023.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced; ✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2087; ENV-2088; ENV-2089; ENV-2078.
**Trap:** The Act was not repealed. Do not describe it as repealed.
**Mains hook:** Argue that renaming signals a change in purpose and scope. Qualify: the scope change is contested.
**Back:** *"Seventy-nine-eighty-three: renamed in twenty-three, still in force."*

### Flashcard ENV-2087 — The 2023 amendment: which land is covered
**Front:** Which land does the Van Adhiniyam, as amended in 2023, cover?
**Definition (learn):** The Act applies to land notified as forest under the Indian Forest Act, 1927 or any other law, and to land recorded as forest in government records on or after 25 October 1980.
**Breakdown:**
- Covered: land notified as forest.
- Covered: land recorded as forest on or after 25 October 1980.
- Effect: narrower than the earlier ecological reading.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 October 1980 record date (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2086; ENV-2089; ENV-2076.
**Trap:** Do not assume the 2023 coverage is the same as the Godavarman dictionary meaning. The Court preserved a broader test in the interim (ENV-2089).
**Mains hook:** Argue that statutory certainty can narrow ecological coverage. Qualify: the cost is coverage of unrecorded forest-like land.
**Back:** *"Notified or recorded since twenty-five October eighty: that's covered."*

### Flashcard ENV-2088 — 2023 exemptions from prior approval
**Front:** Which categories does the 2023 amendment exempt from prior central approval?
**Definition (learn):** The amendment exempts land within 100 km of international borders, LoC and LAC for strategic linear projects of national importance and security. It also exempts small tracts for security infrastructure and defence projects, and land alongside rail lines and public roads for access facilities, subject to conditions.
**Breakdown:**
- Border belt: 100 km of borders, LoC, LAC (strategic linear projects).
- Security infrastructure: small tracts.
- Defence projects.
- Rail and road access: land alongside rail lines and public roads.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced; ✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2087; ENV-2089.
**Trap:** The 100 km border exemption falls largely on the Himalaya and the North-East, where forest and FRA claims are densest.
**Mains hook:** Discuss the security-biodiversity trade-off in border regions. Qualify: exemptions need transparent conditions.
**Back:** *"Hundred kilometres at the border, plus defence, plus roadside access."*

### Flashcard ENV-2089 — Supreme Court, February 2024: the Godavarman interim direction
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court direct in February 2024 on the meaning of forest?
**Definition (learn):** In the Ashok Kumar Sharma v. Union of India proceedings, the Supreme Court directed that the broad dictionary meaning of forest from T.N. Godavarman Thirumulpad (1996) continue to apply pending compilation of State expert-committee records of forest-like land.
**Breakdown:**
- Case: Ashok Kumar Sharma v. Union of India proceedings.
- Date: February 2024.
- Effect: Godavarman (1996) definition preserved in the interim.
- Condition: pending State records.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** February 2024 (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced; ✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2086; ENV-2087; ENV-2078.
**Trap:** Do not say Godavarman has been abolished or permanently entrenched. It is an interim direction tied to record compilation.
**Mains hook:** Present the sequence: statute narrowed, court preserved breadth, records pending. Qualify: the outcome depends on State records.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four: Godavarman stays until the state records land."*

### Flashcard ENV-2090 — Green India Mission: a NAPCC mission
**Front:** What is the Green India Mission and how many NAPCC missions was it one of?
**Definition (learn):** The Green India Mission (GIM) is one of the original NAPCC missions. It focuses on protecting, restoring and enhancing forest and tree cover, ecosystem services and forest-dependent livelihoods. The original NAPCC had eight missions; the Government now describes NAPCC as nine missions with health added.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: NAPCC national mission.
- Original NAPCC: eight missions.
- Current description: nine (health added).
- GIM: broader than area replacement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced). ⚠️ The nine-mission description is owner-stated; verify on the NAPCC portal.
**Links:** ENV-2081; ENV-2091; ENV-2092; ENV-2099.
**Trap:** Do not say the historical eight-mission list has been legally replaced by a new statute.
**Mains hook:** Argue that GIM links forest cover to climate and livelihoods. Qualify: mission scope is not an achievement claim.
**Back:** *"Eight original, nine now: GIM is a mission, not a tax."*

### Flashcard ENV-2091 — Original GIM targets: 5 million hectares plus 5 million for quality
**Front:** What were the original GIM framework's area targets?
**Definition (learn):** The original framework sought 5 million hectares of additional forest and tree cover, and quality improvement over another 5 million hectares, making 10 million hectares in total. It also set an additional annual sequestration of 50 to 60 million tonnes by 2020 and improved livelihood income for about 3 million households.
**Breakdown:**
- Additional cover: 5 million ha.
- Quality improvement: 5 million ha.
- Combined: 10 million ha.
- Sequestration: 50 to 60 million tonnes per year by 2020.
- Livelihoods: about 3 million households.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic). These are objectives, not verified achievements.
**Links:** ENV-2090; ENV-2092; ENV-2100.
**Trap:** Do not report these as achieved. Do not add them to the revised 2021-2030 figures.
**Mains hook:** Use the original targets to show how mission targets evolve. Qualify: targets without dated progress are not evidence.
**Back:** *"Five and five, fifty million tonnes by twenty: objectives, not results."*

### Flashcard ENV-2092 — Revised GIM 2021-2030: three sub-missions and micro-ecosystems
**Front:** What is the architecture of the revised GIM mission document for 2021-2030?
**Definition (learn):** The revised document reorganises GIM around a micro-ecosystem, landscape-saturation approach with three sub-missions: enhanced forest quality and ecosystem services; increased forest and tree cover with restoration; and enhanced household incomes and livelihoods for forest-dependent communities.
**Breakdown:**
- Sub-mission 1: forest quality and ecosystem services.
- Sub-mission 2: cover increase and restoration.
- Sub-mission 3: household incomes and livelihoods.
- Approach: micro-ecosystem and landscape saturation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Official document available on the NAPCC portal in September 2026 (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced). Document pages cited in owner 12.
**Links:** ENV-2093; ENV-2094; ENV-2090.
**Trap:** The revised document does not claim any target has been achieved.
**Mains hook:** Show that the revised GIM emphasises quality and livelihoods, not only area. Qualify: no universal model-wise target is fixed.
**Back:** *"Quality, cover, livelihoods: three sub-missions in micro-ecosystems."*

### Flashcard ENV-2093 — 24 million hectares through convergence vs 1 million hectares financed by GIM
**Front:** How does the revised GIM separate its 24 million hectares from its 1 million hectares?
**Definition (learn):** The revised framework separates 24 million hectares of afforestation through convergence with Central and State schemes and CAMPA (12 million in 2021-25 and 12 million in 2025-30) from 1 million hectares of intervention specifically financed under GIM.
**Breakdown:**
- 24 million ha: convergence (12 in 2021-25; 12 in 2025-30).
- 1 million ha: financed directly under GIM.
- Status: targets and programme boundaries, not achievements.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2092; ENV-2100; ENV-2101.
**Trap:** Do not say the revised mission directly finances all 24 million hectares.
**Mains hook:** Use the nested boundaries to demand dated progress evidence. Qualify: convergence depends on other schemes.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four converge, one is ours."*

### Flashcard ENV-2094 — GIM monitoring architecture
**Front:** What monitoring tools does the revised GIM use?
**Definition (learn):** The revised framework uses JFMC and Gram Sabha social audit, State Forest Development Agency planning, GIS and remote sensing, a web-based MIS, third-party monitoring and selected long-term ecosystem-service monitoring.
**Breakdown:**
- Community: JFMC and Gram Sabha social audit.
- State: SFDA planning.
- Technology: GIS and remote sensing; web-MIS.
- Independent: third-party monitoring.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2092; ENV-2095; ENV-2098.
**Trap:** A monitoring architecture is not evidence that outcomes have been delivered.
**Mains hook:** Argue for outcome-based monitoring. Qualify: monitoring quality varies across states.
**Back:** *"Community audits, satellites, third parties: outcomes verified, not assumed."*

### Flashcard ENV-2095 — National Afforestation Programme (NAP)
**Front:** What is the National Afforestation Programme and how does it differ from CAMPA?
**Definition (learn):** The National Afforestation Programme is an older centrally sponsored scheme for afforestation of degraded forest land, involving Joint Forest Management Committees. It is distinct from CAMPA's funding and implementation mechanism, though both fund afforestation.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: centrally sponsored scheme.
- Land: degraded forest land.
- Implementation: JFMCs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2081; ENV-2094.
**Trap:** NAP and CAMPA are not identical schemes.
**Mains hook:** Use NAP to show the layered restoration landscape. Qualify: overlapping schemes need convergence.
**Back:** *"NAP: the old scheme, JFMC-led, degraded land."*

### Flashcard ENV-2096 — Green Credit Rules, 2023: not carbon credits
**Front:** What are green credits under the Green Credit Rules, 2023, and how do they differ from carbon credits?
**Definition (learn):** Green credits are tradable credits earned under the Green Credit Rules, 2023 (under the Environment (Protection) Act) for specified voluntary environmental actions, most prominently tree plantation on degraded forest land. They are not denominated in tonnes of CO2-equivalent.
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: Environment (Protection) Act, Green Credit Rules 2023.
- Earned: voluntary environmental actions.
- Example: plantation on degraded forest land.
- Not: carbon credits.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2097; ENV-2101; ENV-2081.
**Trap:** A green credit is not a carbon credit. Carbon credits under CCTS are measured in tCO2e (cross-ref owner 21, not carded here).
**Mains hook:** Argue that credits reward inputs, not outcomes. Qualify: plantation counts need verification.
**Back:** *"Green credit counts actions; carbon credit counts tonnes."*

### Flashcard ENV-2097 — Afforestation and Green Credit Programme (Economic Survey 2025-26)
**Front:** What is the Afforestation and Green Credit Programme, as described in the Economic Survey 2025-26?
**Definition (learn):** The Economic Survey 2025-26 refers to the combined Afforestation and Green Credit Programme as the vehicle for incentivising public and private participation in compensatory afforestation and degraded-forest restoration.
**Breakdown:**
- Vehicle: Afforestation and Green Credit Programme.
- Actors: public and private.
- Land: degraded forest restoration and compensatory afforestation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 📰 Economic Survey 2025-26 (✅ owner 12 advanced; ✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2096; ENV-2072.
**Trap:** Credits earned per plantation are input metrics. Do not treat them as restoration achieved.
**Mains hook:** Ask whose consent is required before degraded forest is committed to a credit plantation. Qualify: where CFR rights are recognised or claims pending, the Gram Sabha is a rights-holder.
**Back:** *"Credits for planting: ask the Gram Sabha first."*

### Flashcard ENV-2098 — State Forest Development Agencies (SFDAs)
**Front:** What role do State Forest Development Agencies play in the revised GIM?
**Definition (learn):** SFDAs are the nodal state-level bodies for preparing State Afforestation Perspective Plans and approving or overseeing GIM implementation plans.
**Breakdown:**
- Level: state.
- Functions: perspective plans; approve and oversee GIM plans.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2092; ENV-2094.
**Trap:** SFDAs are not a national statutory authority like CAMPA.
**Mains hook:** Discuss state capacity in planning. Qualify: planning quality varies.
**Back:** *"SFDA: state plans, state approvals."*

### Flashcard ENV-2099 — CAMPA and GIM are not the same programme (trap)
**Front:** Are CAMPA and the Green India Mission the same programme?
**Definition (learn):** No. CAMPA is a compensatory, diversion-triggered financial mechanism. GIM is a broader NAPCC climate-policy mission with its own objectives and funding streams.
**Breakdown:**
- CAMPA: trigger is diversion; funded by user agencies.
- GIM: broader NAPCC mission; multi-scheme funding.
- Link: convergence in revised GIM.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ENV-2081; ENV-2090; ENV-2093.
**Trap:** Do not merge the two in an answer. Show where they converge.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the two instruments in a 10-mark answer. Qualify: convergence is not automatic.
**Back:** *"CAMPA compensates diversion; GIM restores the nation's green."*

### Flashcard ENV-2100 — The 5+5 million and 24 million hectare frameworks are not additive (trap)
**Front:** Can the original 5+5 million hectare objectives and the revised 24 million hectare figure be added together?
**Definition (learn):** No. They are successive planning frameworks with different definitions and financing boundaries. Neither should be reported as an achieved total without dated implementation evidence.
**Breakdown:**
- Original: 5 + 5 million ha.
- Revised: 24 million ha through convergence, 1 million ha GIM-financed.
- Rule: do not add or claim achievement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2091; ENV-2093; ENV-2101.
**Trap:** The revised mission does not simply add 24 million to the legacy objectives.
**Mains hook:** Demand source-dated outcome evidence. Qualify: targets are planning, not results.
**Back:** *"Successive frameworks never add up to achievements."*

### Flashcard ENV-2101 — Announced vs achieved: money, actions and canopy are not restoration
**Front:** What do CAMPA fund balances, green credits and ISFR canopy measure, and what do they not measure?
**Definition (learn):** CAMPA corpus measures money accrued. Green credits measure actions registered. ISFR measures canopy detected. None of the three measures ecological restoration achieved.
**Breakdown:**
- Money: CAF accrual.
- Actions: green credits registered.
- Canopy: ISFR detected cover.
- Not measured: restored ecological function.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 advanced; ✅ owner 11 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2096; ENV-2075; ENV-2105.
**Trap:** A large corpus is not evidence of successful restoration.
**Mains hook:** Say explicitly which metric a claim uses. Qualify: none independently proves restoration.
**Back:** *"Money accrued, actions registered, canopy counted: none is restored."*

### Flashcard ENV-2102 — Khasi Hills Community REDD+ Project, Meghalaya (Plan Vivo)
**Front:** What is India's first Plan Vivo-certified REDD+ project, and where is it?
**Definition (learn):** The Khasi Hills Community REDD+ Project in East Khasi Hills, Meghalaya is India's first Plan Vivo-certified REDD+ project. It illustrates community-led forest-carbon conservation, traditional governance and benefit-sharing.
**Breakdown:**
- Location: East Khasi Hills, Meghalaya.
- Certification: Plan Vivo; owner states 2013.
- Model: community-led carbon conservation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 advanced micro-card). ⚠️ The 2013 certification year is owner-stated; not verified here.
**Links:** ENV-2104; ENV-2081.
**Trap:** Do not infer a current carbon-credit volume or income share without monitoring data.
**Mains hook:** Use community carbon projects to show benefit-sharing. Qualify: outcomes need verified monitoring.
**Back:** *"Meghalaya's Khasi Hills: first Plan Vivo, community carbon."*

### Flashcard ENV-2103 — Ecological commensurability: the 'no net loss' critique
**Front:** Why do ecologists challenge CAMPA's compensation premise?
**Definition (learn):** CAMPA assumes a mature forest diverted for mining or infrastructure can be compensated by planting an equal area elsewhere. Ecological succession shows that new plantations cannot replicate a mature forest's structure, species composition or services within a policy timeframe.
**Breakdown:**
- Premise: equal-area replacement.
- Critique: succession, species composition, services.
- Result: "no net loss" is contested as incommensurable.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 advanced; cross-ref owner 03)
**Links:** ENV-2085; ENV-2081; ENV-2092.
**Trap:** Compensatory afforestation is not an ecologically equivalent substitute by consensus.
**Mains hook:** Argue that compensation can finance restoration but not replace maturity. Qualify: restoration finance is still needed.
**Back:** *"Equal area is not equal forest."*

### Flashcard ENV-2104 — PYQ routing: CAMPA, NY Declaration, Tree City and REDD+ demands
**Front:** Which PYQs route to the forest governance owner, and what do they test?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 12 routes: 2019 Q68 (Compensatory Afforestation Fund Management Planning Authority); 2021 Q24 (New York Declaration on Forests); 2021 Q97 (Hyderabad recognised as a Tree City of the World); 2026 Q40 (Plan Vivo certified REDD+ projects and community carbon conservation).
**Breakdown:**
- 2019 Q68: CAMPA's full name and role.
- 2021 Q24: NY Declaration on Forests.
- 2021 Q97: Tree City recognition.
- 2026 Q40: Plan Vivo REDD+ and community carbon.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 routing ledgers). The 2026 key is provisional; no answer letter recorded.
**Links:** ENV-2081; ENV-2102.
**Trap:** Do not infer answer letters from these routing summaries.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains demand.
**Back:** *"CAMPA name, NY forests, Tree City, Plan Vivo: four routes."*

### Flashcard ENV-2105 — The CAMPA fund-utilisation gap
**Front:** What is the fund-utilisation gap in CAMPA, and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** CAG and parliamentary committee reviews have repeatedly flagged that a large accrued State CAF corpus has been spent slowly or unevenly, while spending quality also raises concerns. The gap delays restoration benefits even where compensation design is sound.
**Breakdown:**
- Problem: accrued corpus vs spending.
- Source: CAG and parliamentary committee reviews.
- Compounds: the ecological-commensurability critique.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12 advanced). Latest figures not carded; cite the report and date (📰).
**Links:** ENV-2084; ENV-2101; ENV-2103.
**Trap:** Do not claim utilisation has always been rapid or fully matched the corpus.
**Mains hook:** Argue that disbursal pace and monitoring are the bottleneck. Qualify: the fund-collection design is sound.
**Back:** *"Corpus grows, spending lags, restoration waits."*

### Flashcard ENV-2106 — Concept: forest governance from diversion to restoration
**Front:** What is the core theme of forest governance under CAMPA and GIM?
**Definition (learn):** Forest governance in this owner runs from a diversion trigger, through payment and compensation, to a national restoration mission judged by outcomes. The fund and plan are inputs; restoration quality is the output that counts.
**Breakdown:**
- Trigger: forest diversion.
- Payment: NPV and compensation.
- Fund: CAF.
- Mission: GIM 2021-2030.
- Test: ecological outcomes, monitored and verified.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ENV-2081; ENV-2090; ENV-2101; ENV-2103.
**Trap:** Do not judge CAMPA or GIM by fund size or area planted alone.
**Mains hook:** Conclude that restoration quality, not money or hectares, is the test. Qualify: monitoring capacity decides.
**Back:** *"Divert, pay, plant, verify: the outcome is the test."*

### GROUP — Air Pollution and CPCB Standards (owner 13)

### Flashcard ENV-2107 — Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1981 and Article 253
**Front:** Under which constitutional route was the Air Act, 1981 enacted, and what did it give effect to?
**Definition (learn):** The Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1981 was enacted by Parliament under Article 253, the power to legislate to implement international agreements. It gave effect to decisions of the 1972 Stockholm Conference on the Human Environment.
**Breakdown:**
- Act: Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1981.
- Route: Article 253 (international agreements).
- Background: 1972 Stockholm Conference.
- Other Article 253 law: Environment (Protection) Act, 1986.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 advanced; ✅ owner 13 basic for the Act year)
**Links:** ENV-2108; ENV-2124; ENV-2131.
**Trap:** The Air Act is not enacted under the Concurrent List public-health entry. It is Article 253.
**Mains hook:** Explain how a treaty-linked power supports central environmental law. Qualify: federal implementation still depends on the states.
**Back:** *"Eighty-one, Article 253, Stockholm's child."*

### Flashcard ENV-2108 — CPCB: apex statutory body for air and water
**Front:** What is CPCB and under which Acts does it operate?
**Definition (learn):** The Central Pollution Control Board is the apex statutory body under the Air Act, 1981 and the Water Act, 1974. It sets national standards and coordinates pollution control, and guides State Pollution Control Board enforcement.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: apex statutory body.
- Acts: Air Act 1981; Water Act 1974.
- Functions: standards, national monitoring, guidance.
- Enforcement: mostly SPCBs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic and owner 14 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2107; ENV-2109; ENV-2132.
**Trap:** CPCB sets standards; SPCBs enforce on the ground. Do not give CPCB sole enforcement.
**Mains hook:** Discuss the central-state split in pollution control. Qualify: SPCB capacity varies.
**Back:** *"CPCB sets, SPCB enforces: two boards, one air, one water."*

### Flashcard ENV-2109 — NAAQS: 2009 standards for 12 pollutants
**Front:** Which 12 pollutants do the current NAAQS cover, and when were they notified?
**Definition (learn):** The National Ambient Air Quality Standards are CPCB-notified permissible concentration limits in outdoor air. The current standards were notified in 2009 and cover 12 pollutants: PM10, PM2.5, SO2, NO2, CO, ozone, lead, ammonia, benzene, benzo(a)pyrene, arsenic and nickel.
**Breakdown:**
- Standard body: CPCB.
- Year: 2009.
- Pollutants: 12 (listed in definition).
- Legal nature: binding ambient limits.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic). ⚠️ Current numeric limits should be sourced to the latest CPCB notification (owner 13 advanced).
**Links:** ENV-2110; ENV-2111; ENV-2108.
**Trap:** NAAQS are not only PM2.5 and PM10. They cover 12 pollutants including lead, ammonia and nickel.
**Mains hook:** Argue that binding standards need credible monitoring. Qualify: limits are periodically reviewed.
**Back:** *"Two-oh-nine, twelve pollutants, one legal limit each."*

### Flashcard ENV-2110 — NAAQS vs AQI: standard and communication
**Front:** How do NAAQS and AQI differ?
**Definition (learn):** NAAQS are legally notified permissible concentration limits. AQI is a public-communication scale (0 to 500) that translates measured concentrations into one severity category.
**Breakdown:**
- NAAQS: legal limit.
- AQI: communication tool.
- AQI input: measured concentrations.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2109; ENV-2111.
**Trap:** NAAQS and AQI are not the same thing.
**Mains hook:** Argue that AQI communication should be paired with enforceable standards. Qualify: AQI is not a legal limit.
**Back:** *"NAAQS is the law; AQI is the message."*

### Flashcard ENV-2111 — AQI: eight pollutants, six categories
**Front:** How many pollutants and categories does India's AQI use, and what are the bands?
**Definition (learn):** India's AQI, launched in 2014-15, uses eight pollutants: PM10, PM2.5, NO2, SO2, CO, ozone, ammonia and lead. It has six categories: Good (0-50), Satisfactory (51-100), Moderate (101-200), Poor (201-300), Very Poor (301-400) and Severe (401-500).
**Breakdown:**
- Pollutants: 8.
- Categories: 6.
- Bands: 0-50, 51-100, 101-200, 201-300, 301-400, 401-500.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2110; ENV-2112; ENV-2113.
**Trap:** Do not describe the AQI as an average of pollutants. It is the highest sub-index on a 0-500 scale.
**Mains hook:** Use the bands to explain public health messaging. Qualify: sparse monitoring networks reduce representativeness.
**Back:** *"Eight pollutants, six bands, zero to five hundred."*

### Flashcard ENV-2112 — AQI is the worst sub-index, not the average
**Front:** How is the reported AQI calculated from several pollutants?
**Definition (learn):** The reported AQI is the worst, or maximum, sub-index across the monitored pollutants, not an average. One pollutant can therefore drive a city's whole reported category.
**Breakdown:**
- Method: maximum sub-index.
- Effect: one pollutant sets the category.
- Monitoring: station density affects representativeness.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2111; ENV-2110.
**Trap:** Do not assume the AQI averages all pollutants.
**Mains hook:** Explain why the category can mislead about source mix. Qualify: the maximum sub-index is a communication choice.
**Back:** *"Worst pollutant wins the AQI."*

### Flashcard ENV-2113 — PM2.5 vs PM10
**Front:** What is the difference between PM2.5 and PM10?
**Definition (learn):** PM2.5 is particulate matter of diameter 2.5 micrometres or less. PM10 is 10 micrometres or less. PM2.5 penetrates deeper into the respiratory system and is considered a more direct health hazard.
**Breakdown:**
- PM2.5: 2.5 micrometres or less; deeper penetration.
- PM10: 10 micrometres or less.
- Health: PM2.5 more hazardous.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2111; ENV-2114.
**Trap:** PM10 is not more hazardous than PM2.5. Do not reverse the health order.
**Mains hook:** Argue that PM2.5 targets matter more for health. Qualify: PM10 remains an NAAQS pollutant.
**Back:** *"Two-point-five goes deeper than ten."*

### Flashcard ENV-2114 — NCAP: launch, target and revision
**Front:** What is the National Clean Air Programme, when was it launched, and what target did it revise to?
**Definition (learn):** NCAP was launched in 2019 as a national framework setting city-specific particulate-matter reduction targets for identified non-attainment cities. The original target was 20-30% reduction by 2024 against a 2017 base. It was revised to a 40% reduction in PM10 concentration, or attainment of NAAQS, by 2025-26, against a 2017-18 base.
**Breakdown:**
- Launch: 2019.
- Original target: 20-30% by 2024 (base 2017).
- Revised target: 40% PM10 or NAAQS attainment by 2025-26 (base 2017-18).
- Structure: city clean-air action plans.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 📰 (✅ owner 13 basic and advanced; NCAP page not fetched (404)).
**Links:** ENV-2115; ENV-2116; ENV-2118.
**Trap:** An announced target is not city-level attainment. The revision date matters.
**Mains hook:** Argue that NCAP is a structural source-reduction programme. Qualify: city implementation capacity is the gap.
**Back:** *"Nineteen launched; forty percent by twenty-five-twenty-six."*

### Flashcard ENV-2115 — Non-attainment city
**Front:** What makes a city a non-attainment city under NCAP?
**Definition (learn):** A non-attainment city is one that failed to meet NAAQS for PM10 or NO2 over a specified five-year monitoring period. NCAP cities were identified by this criterion.
**Breakdown:**
- Criterion: fails NAAQS for PM10 or NO2.
- Period: a specified five-year monitoring window (owner-stated).
- Use: NCAP city list.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic). ⚠️ The five-year window is owner-stated; verify against the NCAP document before citing a city list.
**Links:** ENV-2114; ENV-2118.
**Trap:** Do not assume the city list is static. Counts are revised.
**Mains hook:** Discuss why non-attainment spans multiple states. Qualify: city lists change with monitoring data.
**Back:** *"Fails PM10 or NO2 over the window: non-attainment."*

### Flashcard ENV-2116 — GRAP: the Delhi-NCR emergency response
**Front:** What is GRAP and where does it apply?
**Definition (learn):** The Graded Response Action Plan is a staged emergency air-pollution response, primarily for Delhi-NCR. It escalates restrictions as AQI severity worsens, from poor toward severe.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: staged, AQI-triggered.
- Area: Delhi-NCR (not all India).
- Escalation: poor to severe.
- Examples: construction and vehicle restrictions (owner examples).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2117; ENV-2129.
**Trap:** GRAP is not a uniform national scheme.
**Mains hook:** Argue that GRAP is reactive crisis management. Qualify: it cannot replace source reduction.
**Back:** *"GRAP: staged, Delhi-NCR, AQI-triggered, reactive."*

### Flashcard ENV-2117 — CAQM: statutory body for Delhi-NCR and adjoining areas
**Front:** What is CAQM, when was it created, and which states does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** The Commission for Air Quality Management in NCR and Adjoining Areas is a statutory body coordinating air-quality management across Delhi-NCR and adjoining states. It was created by Ordinance in 2020 and placed on a permanent statutory footing by the CAQM in NCR and Adjoining Areas Act, 2021. Its directions override those of individual SPCBs on air quality in the region.
**Breakdown:**
- Created: Ordinance 2020; Act 2021.
- Covers: NCT of Delhi and NCR areas of Haryana, Punjab, Rajasthan and Uttar Pradesh.
- Power: directions override SPCBs on air quality in the region.
- Purpose: cross-state coordination.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2116; ENV-2118; ENV-2119.
**Trap:** CAQM does not cover all of India. Its statute names specific states, not the whole country.
**Mains hook:** Argue that cross-jurisdictional pollution needs statutory coordination. Qualify: political coordination and resources still decide results.
**Back:** *"Twenty-twenty ordinance, twenty-one Act: Delhi, Haryana, Punjab, Rajasthan, UP."*

### Flashcard ENV-2118 — CAQM is not NCAP (trap)
**Front:** Does NCAP have an enforcement body equivalent to CAQM's cross-state statutory power?
**Definition (learn):** No. NCAP relies on city and state administrative capacity. It has no NCAP-specific cross-jurisdictional enforcement body equivalent to CAQM.
**Breakdown:**
- NCAP: national programme, targets and plans.
- CAQM: statutory, cross-state, directions override SPCBs.
- Gap: NCAP's teeth are weaker for the NCR problem.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2114; ENV-2117.
**Trap:** Do not treat NCAP and CAQM as one mechanism.
**Mains hook:** Argue that structure, not targets, is the implementation gap. Qualify: NCAP's target-setting method is sound.
**Back:** *"NCAP plans; CAQM commands the region."*

### Flashcard ENV-2119 — Source apportionment: crop-residue burning is seasonal
**Front:** Is crop-residue burning the year-round dominant cause of Delhi-NCR's pollution?
**Definition (learn):** No. Source-apportionment studies show that crop-residue burning's contribution is strongly seasonal, concentrated around harvest windows and shaped by meteorology. Vehicular and local industrial and construction sources contribute substantially through the rest of the year.
**Breakdown:**
- Crop-residue burning: seasonal peak.
- Vehicles and construction: year-round.
- Method: city- and season-specific source apportionment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2117; ENV-2116; ENV-2125.
**Trap:** Do not assign a fixed single blame to crop-residue burning.
**Mains hook:** Argue that season-specific source data should drive policy. Qualify: economic alternatives for farmers are also needed.
**Back:** *"Burning peaks in season; vehicles run all year."*

### Flashcard ENV-2120 — CPCB industry categories: Red, Orange, Green, White and Blue
**Front:** What are CPCB's industry pollution categories, and what is the new Blue category?
**Definition (learn):** CPCB classifies industries by Pollution Index into Red, Orange, Green and White, and now also Blue. Blue covers industries providing essential environmental services for managing domestic waste, with an extended consent validity of two years.
**Breakdown:**
- Red, Orange, Green, White: existing categories.
- Blue: new category for essential environmental services.
- Blue consent validity: two years.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic; Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10 cited in owner).
**Links:** ENV-2108; ENV-2121.
**Trap:** Blue is not a polluting-industry category. It covers essential waste-management services.
**Mains hook:** Argue that graded categories allow risk-based regulation. Qualify: category shifts need monitoring.
**Back:** *"Red, Orange, Green, White, and Blue for essential services."*

### Flashcard ENV-2121 — Surat particulate-matter emissions trading scheme
**Front:** What is the Surat PM emissions trading scheme and what did its evaluation find?
**Definition (learn):** Reported in the Economic Survey 2025-26, Surat's scheme is described as the world's first particulate-matter emissions market. It covered 317 industrial plants, was underpinned by mandatory Continuous Emissions Monitoring Systems (CEMS), and replaced a command-and-control regime. The experimental evaluation found near-universal compliance, 20-30% lower particulate emissions and 11-14% lower abatement costs.
**Breakdown:**
- Plants: 317.
- Monitoring: mandatory CEMS.
- Regime: replaced command-and-control.
- Evaluation: near-universal compliance; 20-30% lower PM; 11-14% lower costs.
- Evaluation source: Greenstone, Pande, Ryan and Sudarshan, QJE 2025 (owner-cited).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 📰 (✅ owner 13 advanced; Economic Survey 2025-26 Box X.9 cited by owner). ⚠️ Evaluation figures are owner-cited and not independently fetched.
**Links:** ENV-2120; ENV-2119; ENV-2122.
**Trap:** Do not generalise a single scheme into proof that every market works. Monitoring credibility is the binding constraint.
**Mains hook:** Argue that credible measurement can enable market instruments in low-capacity settings. Qualify: MRV needs to be credible and independent.
**Back:** *"Surat: three-seventeen plants, sensors on, costs down."*

### Flashcard ENV-2122 — October 2025 green-belt rationalisation
**Front:** What did the October 2025 green-belt change replace?
**Definition (learn):** In October 2025, MoEFCC rationalised green-belt requirements by pollution potential. It replaced the blanket 33% green-cover norm for industrial estates.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: October 2025.
- Replaced: blanket 33% green-cover norm.
- New basis: pollution potential.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic and advanced; Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10 cited by owner).
**Links:** ENV-2121; ENV-2120.
**Trap:** Do not state the 33% norm still applies everywhere.
**Mains hook:** Discuss risk-based green belts. Qualify: green cover still needs enforcement.
**Back:** *"October twenty-five: thirty-three percent goes, pollution potential decides."*

### Flashcard ENV-2123 — Jan Vishwas: decriminalisation of minor Air Act offences
**Front:** How were minor offences under the Air Act, 1981 changed, and through which route?
**Definition (learn):** Following amendments through the Jan Vishwas route, minor offences under the Air Act, 1981 were decriminalised. Monetary penalties replaced imprisonment for these offences, according to the Economic Survey 2025-26.
**Breakdown:**
- Route: Jan Vishwas amendments.
- Change: monetary penalty replaces imprisonment for minor offences.
- Also covered: EPA 1986 and Indian Forest Act 1927 (owner-stated).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic; Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10 cited).
**Links:** ENV-2124; ENV-2121.
**Trap:** Decriminalisation is not the same as legalisation. Penalties still apply.
**Mains hook:** Discuss the ease-of-doing-business gain against deterrence. Qualify: small penalties may not deter.
**Back:** *"Jan Vishwas: minor air offences pay fines, not jail."*

### Flashcard ENV-2124 — Article 252(1) vs 253: the Air and Water route trap
**Front:** Why did the Water Act need a different constitutional route from the Air Act?
**Definition (learn):** The Air Act was enacted under Article 253 to implement international agreements. The Water Act, 1974 needed the Article 252 consent route, because water is a State List subject. Its criminal provisions were amended using Article 252(1), legislation for two or more consenting States.
**Breakdown:**
- Air Act: Article 253 (Stockholm).
- Water Act: Article 252 consent route (State List).
- Water decriminalisation: Article 252(1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic; ✅ owner 14 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2107; ENV-2131; ENV-2141.
**Trap:** Do not state that the Water Act was enacted under Article 253. Do not state the Air Act used Article 252.
**Mains hook:** Use the federal route to show the Centre-State balance in environmental law. Qualify: consent-based route depends on state participation.
**Back:** *"Air takes two-five-three; water asks two-five-two."*

### Flashcard ENV-2125 — Photochemical smog and the Gothenburg Protocol
**Front:** How does photochemical smog form, and what does the Gothenburg Protocol do?
**Definition (learn):** Photochemical smog forms from NOx and VOCs in sunlight, producing ground-level ozone and secondary oxidants. It causes respiratory irritation and crop and material damage. The Gothenburg Protocol is a regional transboundary air-pollution instrument, not an Indian statute.
**Breakdown:**
- Precursors: NOx and VOCs.
- Energy source: sunlight.
- Products: ground-level ozone; secondary oxidants.
- Gothenburg Protocol: regional, not Indian law.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 advanced; 2022 GS-III Q7 demand routed to owner 13).
**Links:** ENV-2119; ENV-2126; ENV-2128.
**Trap:** Do not write the Gothenburg Protocol as India's governing air law.
**Mains hook:** Argue for precursor reduction from transport, fuels and industry. Qualify: regional protocols are comparative tools.
**Back:** *"NOx and VOCs plus sun make ozone smog."*

### Flashcard ENV-2126 — Acid rain: SO2 and NOx precursors; thermal power as SO2 source
**Front:** Which pollutants drive acid rain, and what is a major SO2 source?
**Definition (learn):** Acid rain is driven principally by SO2 and NOx precursors. Fossil-fuel combustion, including thermal power, is a major SO2 source. Owner routing includes 2024 Q90 (EPA's largest SO2 source: fossil-fuel power plants) and 2022 Q100 (acid rain pollutants).
**Breakdown:**
- Precursors: SO2, NOx.
- Major SO2 source: fossil-fuel power, including thermal power.
- PYQs: 2024 Q90; 2022 Q100.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 basic and advanced). Answer keys not recorded in the owner.
**Links:** ENV-2125; ENV-2128.
**Trap:** Source shares are place- and time-specific. Do not assert unsupported rankings.
**Mains hook:** Not standalone. Use for source-pathway diagnosis.
**Back:** *"SO2 and NOx make acid rain; power plants burn the sulphur."*

### Flashcard ENV-2127 — WHO Air Quality Guidelines vs NAAQS
**Front:** How should WHO Air Quality Guidelines be used in an Indian answer?
**Definition (learn):** WHO Air Quality Guidelines are health-based reference values. NAAQS are India's notified ambient standards with legal force. An answer must not present a WHO guideline as an Indian legal limit, or quote an old value without its edition.
**Breakdown:**
- WHO: health-based reference.
- NAAQS: India's legal ambient standard.
- Edition: must be stated when quoting a WHO value.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 advanced; 2021 Mains (WHO revised AQG and NCAP) and 2022 Prelims Q44 routed).
**Links:** ENV-2109; ENV-2114; ENV-2128.
**Trap:** A WHO guideline is not an Indian law.
**Mains hook:** Discuss the gap between WHO health values and NAAQS. Qualify: NCAP's target is a separate policy layer.
**Back:** *"WHO advises health, NAAQS commands law."*

### Flashcard ENV-2128 — PYQ routing: air-pollution demands 2019 to 2025
**Front:** Which air-pollution PYQs route to the owner, and what do they test?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 13 routes: 2019 Q35 (pollutants from crop and biomass burning); 2020 GS-III Q17 (NCAP features and implementation); 2020 Q48 (benzene exposure sources); 2020 Q79 (coal ash toxins); 2021 GS-III Q7 (WHO AQG and NCAP); 2021 Q17 (copper smelting); 2021 Q18 (furnace oil and sulphur); 2021 Q25 (magnetite particles); 2022 GS-III Q7 (photochemical smog and Gothenburg); 2022 Q44 (WHO PM2.5 and ozone); 2022 Q100 (acid rain pollutants); 2024 Q90 (SO2 largest source); 2025 Q85 (artificial rainfall or cloud seeding).
**Breakdown:**
- Prelims: crop burning, benzene, coal ash, copper, furnace oil, magnetite, WHO values, acid rain, SO2, cloud seeding.
- Mains: NCAP (2020), WHO and NCAP (2021), smog (2022).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 routing ledgers). Keys: 2024 and 2025 keys are held locally but not recorded in the owner.
**Links:** ENV-2116; ENV-2114; ENV-2125; ENV-2126; ENV-2127.
**Trap:** Cloud seeding is at most an episodic particulate-washout intervention. It is not a substitute for source control.
**Mains hook:** Not standalone. Use the routing as a checklist.
**Back:** *"Thirteen air routes: sources, standards, NCAP, smog, cloud seeding."*

### Flashcard ENV-2129 — GRAP vs NCAP: reactive vs structural (trap)
**Front:** Do GRAP and NCAP serve the same function and timeframe?
**Definition (learn):** No. GRAP is reactive, short-term emergency response triggered by AQI severity. NCAP is a structural, multi-year source-reduction programme. The two are complementary, not interchangeable.
**Breakdown:**
- GRAP: acute, AQI-triggered, Delhi-NCR.
- NCAP: multi-year, city plans, national.
- Use: both are needed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13 advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2116; ENV-2114; ENV-2119.
**Trap:** Emergency restrictions cannot replace long-run source reduction.
**Mains hook:** Argue that integrated tools need both. Qualify: neither substitutes for the other.
**Back:** *"GRAP treats the fever; NCAP treats the cause."*

### Flashcard ENV-2130 — Concept: three-layer air governance
**Front:** What is the core theme of India's air-quality governance and how should an answer frame it?
**Definition (learn):** India's air governance rests on three linked layers: legally binding ambient standards (NAAQS), a national structural programme (NCAP), and severity-triggered emergency response (GRAP), with CAQM providing cross-state coordination for Delhi-NCR. Effectiveness depends on accurate source apportionment and coordinated governance.
**Breakdown:**
- Standards: NAAQS (CPCB).
- Structure: NCAP.
- Emergency: GRAP.
- Coordination: CAQM.
- Information: AQI.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ENV-2109; ENV-2114; ENV-2116; ENV-2117; ENV-2119.
**Trap:** Do not judge the system by AQI numbers alone.
**Mains hook:** Conclude that standards, monitoring and source-specific action must operate together. Qualify: enforcement and political coordination are the limits.
**Back:** *"Standards, structure, emergency, coordination: four layers, one air."*

### GROUP — Water Pollution and River Cleaning Missions (owner 14)

### Flashcard ENV-2131 — Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974: the regulatory core
**Front:** What does the Water Act, 1974 establish, and who runs it on the ground?
**Definition (learn):** The Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974 established CPCB at the national level and State Pollution Control Boards at the state level. They set effluent standards, grant or refuse consents, and take enforcement action. Water is a State List subject, so the Act was adopted through the Article 252 consent route.
**Breakdown:**
- National body: CPCB.
- State bodies: SPCBs (on-ground enforcement).
- Tools: effluent standards, consents, enforcement.
- Constitutional route: Article 252 consent.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced). Act year 1974 (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** ENV-2108; ENV-2132; ENV-2124.
**Trap:** CPCB does not enforce on the ground everywhere. SPCBs do.
**Mains hook:** Explain why general water regulation alone proved insufficient for the Ganga. Qualify: state capacity and funding are the constraints.
**Back:** *"Seventy-four: CPCB sets, SPCBs enforce, states consent."*

### Flashcard ENV-2132 — Consent to Establish and Consent to Operate
**Front:** What are Consent to Establish and Consent to Operate, and who grants them?
**Definition (learn):** These are statutory approvals under the Water Act, 1974 (and the Air Act, 1981) that industries and projects must obtain from SPCBs before establishing or operating a polluting activity.
**Breakdown:**
- Consent to Establish: before setting up.
- Consent to Operate: before running.
- Issuer: SPCB.
- Effect: a conditional licence to discharge within standards.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2131; ENV-2142; ENV-2143.
**Trap:** Consent or installed treatment is not proof of continuous compliance.
**Mains hook:** Argue that consent is a prevention tool at source. Qualify: monitoring must verify operation.
**Back:** *"Establish first, operate second: both need the SPCB."*

### Flashcard ENV-2133 — BOD, DO and faecal coliform: the sewage signature
**Front:** What water-quality pattern signals classic sewage or organic pollution?
**Definition (learn):** High BOD combined with low dissolved oxygen (DO) and high faecal coliform count is the classic signature of sewage or organic pollution. BOD measures oxygen consumed by microbes decomposing organic matter. DO is oxygen dissolved in water for aquatic life.
**Breakdown:**
- BOD: oxygen consumed by decomposers; high = more organic pollution.
- DO: oxygen available; low = depletion.
- Faecal coliform: sewage or waste indicator.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2134; ENV-2143.
**Trap:** High BOD does not mean clean, oxygen-rich water. It means heavy organic load.
**Mains hook:** Use the signature to diagnose the pollution source. Qualify: other sources need different indicators.
**Back:** *"High BOD, low DO, high coliform: sewage talks."*

### Flashcard ENV-2134 — Eutrophication vs sewage pollution
**Front:** How does eutrophication differ from organic sewage pollution?
**Definition (learn):** Eutrophication is nutrient (nitrogen and phosphorus) over-enrichment of a water body, causing algal blooms and then oxygen depletion. Sewage pollution is broader organic-matter and pathogen contamination. The two can co-occur.
**Breakdown:**
- Eutrophication: N and P enrichment; algal blooms.
- Sewage: organic matter and pathogens.
- Link: sewage can add nutrients.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic; cross-ref owner 02)
**Links:** ENV-2133; ENV-2145.
**Trap:** Do not treat eutrophication and sewage as one phenomenon.
**Mains hook:** Argue that nutrient runoff needs different tools from sewage. Qualify: runoff is diffuse.
**Back:** *"Nutrients bloom algae; sewage carries waste and germs."*

### Flashcard ENV-2135 — Namami Gange Programme and NMCG
**Front:** What is the Namami Gange Programme, who implements it, and what components does it include?
**Definition (learn):** Namami Gange is India's flagship integrated river-conservation programme for the Ganga. It is implemented by the National Mission for Clean Ganga (NMCG) under the Ministry of Jal Shakti. Components include sewage treatment infrastructure, industrial effluent monitoring, river-front development, afforestation, biodiversity and public participation.
**Breakdown:**
- Implementer: NMCG (MoJS).
- Components: STPs; industrial monitoring; river-front; afforestation; biodiversity; public participation.
- Launch: 2014-15 (owner advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced). Launch year from owner 14 advanced.
**Links:** ENV-2136; ENV-2137; ENV-2143; ENV-2144.
**Trap:** Namami Gange is not run by MoEFCC. It is implemented by NMCG under MoJS.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate the integrated multi-component design. Qualify: infrastructure without operation and sewer connection underdelivers.
**Back:** *"Namami Gange: NMCG implements, Jal Shakti oversees, many parts."*

### Flashcard ENV-2136 — National Ganga Council: chaired by the Prime Minister
**Front:** Who chairs the National Ganga Council, and under which law was it constituted?
**Definition (learn):** The National Ganga Council is the apex body for Ganga rejuvenation. It is chaired by the Prime Minister. It was created by the River Ganga (Rejuvenation, Protection and Management) Authorities Order, 2016, issued under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986. An Empowered Task Force under the Jal Shakti Minister sits below it, with State and District Ganga Committees.
**Breakdown:**
- Chair: Prime Minister.
- Legal basis: 2016 Authorities Order under EP Act 1986.
- Below: Empowered Task Force (Jal Shakti Minister).
- Below that: State and District Ganga Committees.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2135; ENV-2137.
**Trap:** The Council is not chaired by the Jal Shakti Minister. The Task Force is.
**Mains hook:** Discuss why river governance was built under environmental law. Qualify: the chain's coordination depends on the PM's engagement and state participation.
**Back:** *"Council under the PM; Task Force under Jal Shakti; Order of 2016."*

### Flashcard ENV-2137 — NMCG: a registered society, not a statutory board (trap)
**Front:** Is NMCG a statutory board like CPCB?
**Definition (learn):** No. NMCG is a registered society vested with the powers of an Authority under the 2016 Order issued under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986. It is the implementation arm, not a statutory regulator like CPCB.
**Breakdown:**
- NMCG: registered society.
- Powers: vested Authority powers under the 2016 Order.
- CPCB: statutory board.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2136; ENV-2135; ENV-2108.
**Trap:** Do not describe NMCG as a statutory board.
**Mains hook:** Discuss the implications of a society-based implementer for accountability. Qualify: its Authority powers are delegated by Order.
**Back:** *"NMCG: society with Authority powers, not a board."*

### Flashcard ENV-2138 — Ganga declared a National River in 2008
**Front:** When was the Ganga declared India's National River, and what does that status legally do?
**Definition (learn):** The Ganga was declared India's National River in 2008. This declaration raised priority and funding for the river. It was not a new legal protection category.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2008.
- Status: declaratory, priority-signalling.
- Legal regime: unchanged.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2136; ENV-2135.
**Trap:** Do not claim the 2008 status created a new legal regime.
**Mains hook:** Not standalone. Use as chronology for river governance.
**Back:** *"Two-oh-oh-eight: Ganga becomes National River; law unchanged."*

### Flashcard ENV-2139 — Arth Ganga: six verticals
**Front:** What are the six verticals of Arth Ganga, the economic model extension of Namami Gange?
**Definition (learn):** Arth Ganga is the economic-model extension of Namami Gange. Its six verticals are zero-budget natural farming, monetisation and reuse of treated water and sludge, livelihood generation, increased public participation, cultural heritage and tourism, and institution building.
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Zero-budget natural farming.
- 2. Monetisation and reuse of treated water and sludge.
- 3. Livelihood generation.
- 4. Public participation.
- 5. Cultural heritage and tourism.
- 6. Institution building.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2135; ENV-2140.
**Trap:** Arth Ganga is an extension, not a separate Act.
**Mains hook:** Argue that reuse of treated water and sludge can fund O&M. Qualify: market and reuse demand decide returns.
**Back:** *"Six verticals: farm, reuse, livelihoods, people, heritage, institutions."*

### Flashcard ENV-2140 — Hybrid Annuity Model: pay for performance
**Front:** What is the Hybrid Annuity Model, and why was it used for Namami Gange STPs?
**Definition (learn):** The HAM is a PPP contracting model. A share of capital cost is paid during construction and the balance as performance-linked annuities over a long operation period. It targets the historic failure of built-but-not-operated STPs. Owner 14 notes a 'one city, one operator' approach tied to sustained performance.
**Breakdown:**
- Capital: share during construction.
- Balance: annuities tied to performance.
- Aim: operation, not just construction.
- Approach: one city, one operator.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2144; ENV-2135.
**Trap:** HAM does not guarantee sewer connectivity. Last-mile gaps can remain.
**Mains hook:** Argue that payment for performance fixes O&M gaps. Qualify: sewer networks must also be complete.
**Back:** *"Pay for operation, not just the concrete."*

### Flashcard ENV-2141 — Water decriminalisation: Article 252(1) and the federal route
**Front:** How were criminal provisions of the Water Act decriminalised, and why through that route?
**Definition (learn):** Criminal provisions of the Water Act, 1974 were amended using Article 252(1), legislation by Parliament for two or more consenting States. Water being a State List subject, a central amendment alone could not do it. The Air Act, EPA and Indian Forest Act were decriminalised through the Jan Vishwas route.
**Breakdown:**
- Water: Article 252(1) consent route.
- Air, EPA, IFA: Jan Vishwas route.
- Reason: State List subject.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic; ✅ owner 14 advanced citing Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10).
**Links:** ENV-2124; ENV-2123; ENV-2142.
**Trap:** Do not say Parliament used Article 253 for the Water Act decriminalisation.
**Mains hook:** Use the route to show the federal constraint on environmental law. Qualify: consent by states is needed.
**Back:** *"Water needs the states' consent: two-five-two, one-zero."*

### Flashcard ENV-2142 — Uniform consent guidelines and the inter-state variability critique
**Front:** What do MoEFCC's uniform consent guidelines aim to fix?
**Definition (learn):** MoEFCC has issued uniform guidelines for grant of consent to establish and operate across all SPCBs. They target inter-state variability, the standing critique that the same industry gets different outcomes depending on its SPCB.
**Breakdown:**
- Issuer: MoEFCC.
- Scope: all SPCBs.
- Target: inter-state variability.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 📰 (✅ owner 14 advanced; Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10 cited in owner). Date of guideline not carded.
**Links:** ENV-2132; ENV-2141; ENV-2152.
**Trap:** Uniform guidelines do not remove the SPCB's enforcement role.
**Mains hook:** Argue that uniform rules reduce regulatory arbitrage. Qualify: monitoring capacity still differs by state.
**Back:** *"One consent guideline for all states: less arbitrage."*

### Flashcard ENV-2143 — Untreated municipal sewage: the largest documented Ganga load (trap)
**Front:** Is industrial effluent the largest pollution load on the Ganga?
**Definition (learn):** No. Untreated municipal sewage has historically been documented as the largest single pollution-load contributor to the Ganga. Industrial effluent, such as from tanneries, is a distinct and important source, but not the largest.
**Breakdown:**
- Largest documented: untreated municipal sewage.
- Important: industrial effluent (tanneries, distilleries).
- Non-point: agricultural runoff.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic)
**Links:** ENV-2133; ENV-2135; ENV-2144.
**Trap:** Do not reverse the largest-source order.
**Mains hook:** Argue for sewage treatment investment as the primary priority. Qualify: industrial point sources still need consent and monitoring.
**Back:** *"Sewage first, industry second, runoff hardest to see."*

### Flashcard ENV-2144 — The last-mile gap: capacity created vs utilised
**Front:** What is the 'last-mile' gap in river cleaning?
**Definition (learn):** STP capacity creation does not guarantee full sewer-network connection or continuous operation at design load. The gap between treatment capacity created, capacity operated, and river-stretch quality is the central implementation critique of river-cleaning missions.
**Breakdown:**
- Capacity created: installed STP (MLD).
- Capacity used: operated at design load.
- Outcome: river-stretch quality.
- Gap: sewer connection and O&M.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 advanced; ✅ owner 14 advanced 'capacity-created vs capacity-used' discipline)
**Links:** ENV-2135; ENV-2140; ENV-2146.
**Trap:** Created capacity does not equal proportionate pollution-load reduction.
**Mains hook:** Demand sewer completion and O&M funding. Qualify: monitored reduction must be shown by stretch.
**Back:** *"Built is not connected; connected is not running."*

### Flashcard ENV-2145 — Point sources vs non-point sources
**Front:** Why are industrial and municipal pollution easier to regulate than agricultural runoff?
**Definition (learn):** Industrial and municipal discharges come from identifiable pipes or outfalls, so consent and monitoring apply. Agricultural runoff of fertiliser and pesticide residues is diffuse across a catchment, harder to measure and attribute, and has no equivalent consent mechanism.
**Breakdown:**
- Point: outfall, pipe, consent.
- Non-point: diffuse runoff.
- Tools: buffers, fertiliser-use practices, less mature in India.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2134; ENV-2132; ENV-2143.
**Trap:** Agricultural runoff is not regulated through the industrial consent mechanism.
**Mains hook:** Argue that different pollution types need different tools. Qualify: measurement is harder for diffuse sources.
**Back:** *"Pipes are regulated; fields are not, yet."*

### Flashcard ENV-2146 — Water-quality claims must name stretch, indicator and period
**Front:** Why is a national claim that 'the Ganga is clean' an overstatement?
**Definition (learn):** Water quality varies by season (dry vs monsoon dilution) and by river stretch (industrialised vs upstream). A precise claim must state the stretch, the indicator and the reporting period, and cite the CPCB or NMCG report.
**Breakdown:**
- Variables: stretch, season, indicator, period.
- Source: CPCB or NMCG report with date.
- Phrase: 'fit for bathing' needs the report.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 advanced; ✅ owner 14 basic current-anchor caution)
**Links:** ENV-2144; ENV-2135.
**Trap:** A blanket national claim of cleanliness is not supported by stretch-specific data.
**Mains hook:** Demand disaggregated monitoring. Qualify: some stretches improved while others did not.
**Back:** *"Name the stretch, the indicator, the date, and the report."*

### Flashcard ENV-2147 — From regulation to mission-mode: why Namami Gange
**Front:** Why did India move from general pollution law to a dedicated mission for the Ganga?
**Definition (learn):** General pollution law proved insufficient because responsibility was fragmented across states and urban local bodies, sewage infrastructure was chronically under-funded against growing sewage, SPCB enforcement was weak in several states, and no single adequately funded coordinating authority existed before the mission.
**Breakdown:**
- Fragmentation: many states and ULBs.
- Under-investment: sewage infrastructure.
- Enforcement: SPCB capacity and independence.
- Coordination: no single authority before the mission.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 advanced; launch 2014-15 owner-stated)
**Links:** ENV-2131; ENV-2135; ENV-2144.
**Trap:** Namami Gange is not simply a strengthened SPCB. It is a mission-mode response.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate why law alone failed. Qualify: a mission also needs O&M funding and state capacity.
**Back:** *"Law alone failed the Ganga; mission-mode followed."*

### Flashcard ENV-2148 — PYQ 2024 GS-III: industrial river pollution and mitigation
**Front:** What does the 2024 GS-III question on industrial river pollution ask for?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 GS-III question states that industrial pollution of river water is a significant issue in India. It asks for mitigation measures and government initiatives, as a 10-mark answer of 150 words.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: industrial pollution of rivers.
- Ask: mitigation and government initiatives.
- Marks: 10; words: 150.
- Route: source segregation, consent and monitoring, treatment, liability, basin governance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and PYQ integration (GS-III 2024 Q7)).
**Links:** ENV-2132; ENV-2135; ENV-2145.
**Trap:** Do not present river-front work as a substitute for source control.
**Mains hook:** Build the source-to-river chain: prevent, enforce, treat, verify.
**Back:** *"Prevent at source, consent, treat, verify the river."*

### Flashcard ENV-2149 — PYQ 2024 GS-III: freshwater technologies (three technologies)
**Front:** What does the 2024 GS-III freshwater question demand?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 GS-III question asks for any three alternative technologies that can solve the freshwater crisis, citing key merits and demerits, in 250 words, 15 marks. Desalination, wastewater recycling and reuse, and managed aquifer recharge or rainwater harvesting are the standard blocks.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: three technologies; merits and demerits each.
- Desalination: climate-independent; energy and brine.
- Recycling: reliable; needs sewers, power and trust.
- MAR and rainwater harvesting: stores wet-season water; needs clean recharge.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced; PYQ 2024 GS-III Q15)
**Links:** ENV-2150; ENV-2144.
**Trap:** Do not write a general water-scarcity essay. Give three structured blocks.
**Mains hook:** Conclude with a diversified portfolio rather than a silver bullet.
**Back:** *"Three techs: desalinate, reuse, recharge: merits and demerits each."*

### Flashcard ENV-2150 — PYQ 2025 GS-III: groundwater depletion and government steps
**Front:** What does the 2025 GS-III groundwater question demand, and which government steps should an answer use?
**Definition (learn):** The 2025 GS-III question asks for factors depleting groundwater in India and steps taken to mitigate it, in 250 words, 15 marks. It is a quantity question, distinct from surface-water quality. Steps include Atal Bhujal Yojana, Jal Shakti Abhiyan (Catch the Rain), CGWA regulation of notified over-exploited blocks, and the Master Plan for Artificial Recharge.
**Breakdown:**
- Causes: irrigation demand, cheap pumping, urban abstraction, sealed recharge zones.
- Steps: Atal Bhujal Yojana; Catch the Rain; CGWA; Master Plan for Artificial Recharge.
- Condition: aquifer-level data and enforceable extraction discipline.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ENV-2149; ENV-2151.
**Trap:** Groundwater quantity is not the same as Namami Gange's river-quality framing.
**Mains hook:** Argue that depletion needs demand management plus recharge. Qualify: enforcement of notified blocks is the bottleneck.
**Back:** *"Pump less, recharge more, notify blocks, measure aquifers."*

### Flashcard ENV-2151 — Water PYQ routing: 2018 to 2025 Prelims demands
**Front:** Which Prelims water demands route to this owner, and what do they test?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 14 routes: 2018 Q81 (heavy sand mining of riverbeds and groundwater consequences); 2019 Q30 (microbeads in water); 2024 Q17 (PFAS in consumer products); 2024 Q39 (membrane bioreactors in wastewater); 2025 Q50 (activated carbon for effluent pollutants).
**Breakdown:**
- 2018 Q81: sand mining.
- 2019 Q30: microbeads.
- 2024 Q17: PFAS.
- 2024 Q39: membrane bioreactors.
- 2025 Q50: activated carbon.
