**Trap:** Statement-level distinctions among these items; learn the named concept, not a guessed key.
**Mains hook:** Geoengineering and methane hydrates are frontier options; state their uncertainty.
**Back:** *"Plants feed on carbon, Earth gets geoengineered, hydrates hold methane."*

### Flashcard ENV-3074 — PYQ routing 2022–2025: Kyoto, crops, food security and cement
**Front:** Which 2022–2025 items route to climate science?
**Definition (learn):** 2022 GS-III Q17 (15 marks: GHG effects and Kyoto measures); 2022 Prelims Q21 (crops as anthropogenic methane and N₂O sources); 2022 Prelims Q22 (SRI methane reduction); 2023 GS-I Q4 (climate change and food security in tropical countries, 10 marks); 2025 Prelims Q31 (cement-industry emissions; limestone and clinker); 2025 Prelims Q38 (India's CO₂ per capita and largest sources).
**Breakdown:**
- Mains: 2022 Q17 (15 marks), 2023 Q4 (10 marks).
- Prelims: 2022 Q21, Q22; 2025 Q31, Q38.
- Keys: 2025 Set-A key held locally (answers not recorded here).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 17 basic PYQ blocks; keys as stated).
**Links:** ENV-3071, ENV-3072, ENV-3069
**Trap:** Cement emissions are process as well as fuel emissions; treat the 2025 Q31 concept as a statement-level distinction.
**Mains hook:** Tropical food security needs heat, rainfall and sea-level mechanisms linked to crops and freshwater.
**Back:** *"Crops, rice, cement, per capita, food: six routes."*

### GROUP — IPCC Assessment Reports

### Flashcard ENV-3075 — IPCC as a scientific assessment body, not a research or negotiating body
**Front:** What does the IPCC do, and what does it not do?
**Definition (learn):** The IPCC assesses and synthesises existing peer-reviewed climate science into policy-relevant but not policy-prescriptive reports. It does no primary research and does not negotiate treaties; negotiation happens under the UNFCCC.
**Breakdown:**
- Does: assess, synthesise, publish assessment reports, Special Reports, methodology reports.
- Does not: run experiments, collect primary data, recommend a specific national policy, negotiate treaties.
- Downstream: UNFCCC negotiations draw on IPCC science.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 basic §1, §3; ✅ owner 18 advanced §4).
**Links:** ENV-3076, ENV-3086, ENV-3092
**Trap:** Treating the IPCC as a COP-style negotiating body (ENV-3092).
**Mains hook:** Assessment-without-prescription is the source of its credibility across divided governments.
**Back:** *"Assess, synthesise, never prescribe, never negotiate."*

### Flashcard ENV-3076 — IPCC founding and secretariat
**Front:** When and by whom was the IPCC established, and where is its secretariat?
**Definition (learn):** The IPCC was set up in 1988 by the World Meteorological Organization (WMO) and the UN Environment Programme (UNEP), with UN endorsement later that year. Its secretariat is hosted by the WMO in Geneva.
**Breakdown:**
- Founders: WMO and UNEP.
- Year: 1988 (✅ Wikipedia: IPCC).
- Secretariat: Geneva, hosted by WMO.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1988; WMO and UNEP; Geneva (✅ Wikipedia: Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change).
**Links:** ENV-3075, ENV-3077
**Trap:** Founding bodies are WMO and UNEP; not UNFCCC (created 1992).
**Mains hook:** The founding organisations explain its UN-linked, science-first credibility.
**Back:** *"Eighty-eight, WMO and UNEP, housed in Geneva."*

### Flashcard ENV-3077 — Working Group I: the physical science basis
**Front:** What does IPCC Working Group I assess?
**Definition (learn):** Working Group I assesses the physical scientific basis of climate change: the climate system, observed warming, GHG concentrations and projections.
**Breakdown:**
- Focus: physical science.
- Output: physical-science assessment reports.
- Relevance: the basis for ENV-3051 to ENV-3072.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia: IPCC WG I "scientific aspects of the climate system and climate change"; ✅ owner 18 basic §2).
**Links:** ENV-3075, ENV-3078, ENV-3079
**Trap:** Mixing WG I (physics) with WG II (impacts).
**Mains hook:** WG I sets the evidence; WG II and III turn it into impacts and mitigation.
**Back:** *"Working Group One: the physics."*

### Flashcard ENV-3078 — Working Group II: impacts, adaptation and vulnerability
**Front:** What does IPCC Working Group II assess?
**Definition (learn):** Working Group II assesses the impacts of climate change on human and natural systems, vulnerability and adaptation options.
**Breakdown:**
- Focus: impacts, vulnerability, adaptation.
- Owner-cited example: small-island chapter (AR6 WG-II Ch. 15).
- Relevance: coastal, water and food risks.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia: WG II "effects on human and natural systems, plus adaptation options"; ✅ owner 18 basic §2).
**Links:** ENV-3077, ENV-3079, ENV-3065
**Trap:** Adaptation is WG II's remit, not WG III's.
**Mains hook:** Adaptation-finance debates rest on WG II vulnerability findings.
**Back:** *"Working Group Two: harm and how to live with it."*

### Flashcard ENV-3079 — Working Group III: mitigation
**Front:** What does IPCC Working Group III assess?
**Definition (learn):** Working Group III assesses options and pathways for limiting greenhouse-gas emissions, i.e., mitigation.
**Breakdown:**
- Focus: mitigation options, pathways, costs.
- Contrast: WG I (cause), WG II (impact), WG III (response).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia: WG III "limiting greenhouse gas emissions"; ✅ owner 18 basic §2).
**Links:** ENV-3077, ENV-3078, ENV-3080
**Trap:** Mixing mitigation with adaptation.
**Mains hook:** Structure any climate answer in three layers: science, impacts, mitigation.
**Back:** *"Working Group Three: cut the emissions."*

### Flashcard ENV-3080 — Task Force on National Greenhouse Gas Inventories (the fourth body)
**Front:** What is the IPCC's fourth body and what does it produce?
**Definition (learn):** The Task Force on National Greenhouse Gas Inventories (TFI) develops and refines the methodologies countries use to estimate GHG emissions and removals, the technical rulebook behind UNFCCC reporting.
**Breakdown:**
- Role: inventory methodology.
- Managing the national inventories programme since 1998 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Technical support unit hosted by Japan's Institute for Global Environmental Strategies (✅ Wikipedia).
- Outputs: methodology reports (e.g., 2027 SLCF and CDR/CCUS reports, owner 18).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Since 1998 (✅ Wikipedia: IPCC).
**Links:** ENV-3088, ENV-3076, ENV-3079
**Trap:** "IPCC has only three bodies" is wrong (✅ owner 18 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Methodology decides how removals and methane are counted, often more consequential than headline findings (✅ owner 18 advanced §9).
**Back:** *"TFI writes the accounting rules."*

### Flashcard ENV-3081 — Assessment cycles: AR1 to AR6 years
**Front:** List the IPCC assessment reports with years and the AR6 Synthesis Report date.
**Definition (learn):** The IPCC produces numbered assessment reports roughly every five to seven years. Each report comes with three Working Group volumes and a Synthesis Report.
**Breakdown:**
- AR1: 1990 (updated 1992).
- AR2: 1995 (Synthesis 1996).
- AR3: 2001 (Synthesis 2001).
- AR4: 2007 (Synthesis 2007).
- AR5: 2013–2014 (Synthesis 2014).
- AR6: 2021–2022 (Synthesis March 2023).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia: IPCC assessment reports).
**Links:** ENV-3082, ENV-3087
**Trap:** AR6 is the sixth, not the first; AR1 was not 2001 (✅ owner 18 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Cite the specific assessment; the cycle lag matters for recent science.
**Back:** *"Ninety, ninety-five, oh-one, oh-seven, fourteen, twenty-three."*

### Flashcard ENV-3082 — AR6 Synthesis Report (March 2023): "unequivocally" and 1.1°C
**Front:** What does the AR6 Synthesis Report say about human-caused warming?
**Definition (learn):** The AR6 Synthesis Report (March 2023) states that human activities have unequivocally caused global warming, with global surface temperature at about 1.1°C above 1850–1900 in 2011–2020.
**Breakdown:**
- Headline: "unequivocally" human-caused.
- Figure: about 1.1°C above 1850–1900 (2011–2020 average).
- Risk: escalates with each increment of warming.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** March 2023 (✅ Wikipedia: AR6 Synthesis completed March 2023). The 1.1°C figure is owner-stated (✅ owner 18 basic §3); the fetched page did not confirm it.
**Links:** ENV-3081, ENV-3084, ENV-3068
**Trap:** "Unequivocally" is the headline wording, not a synonym for "high confidence"; separate findings keep their own qualifiers (✅ owner 18 basic §3).
**Mains hook:** Cite the year and the baseline period when quoting warming.
**Back:** *"Unequivocal headline, one point one, against 1850–1900."*

### Flashcard ENV-3083 — Summary for Policymakers: line-by-line approval and the acceptance split
**Front:** How are the SPM and the underlying report approved?
**Definition (learn):** Working Group reports are accepted without line-by-line agreement. Each Summary for Policymakers is approved line by line by government representatives with the scientist-authors. The Synthesis Report SPM is approved line by line, and the longer report is adopted section by section.
**Breakdown:**
- WG report: accepted (balanced, comprehensive).
- WG SPM: approved line by line.
- Synthesis SPM: line by line; longer report: section by section.
- Trade-off: consensus can favour cautious wording.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 advanced §1; ✅ owner 18 basic §3).
**Links:** ENV-3075, ENV-3084, ENV-3094
**Trap:** Governments do not sign off every sentence of the underlying report.
**Mains hook:** The SPM process gives findings both scientific and political authority, with a cautious-wording cost.
**Back:** *"Accepted report, approved summary, line by line."*

### Flashcard ENV-3084 — Calibrated language: confidence versus likelihood
**Front:** Distinguish confidence terms from likelihood terms and give the probability bands.
**Definition (learn):** IPCC uses two calibration systems. Confidence (very low to very high) reflects strength of evidence and expert agreement. Likelihood gives a probability range for a specific outcome.
**Breakdown:**
- Confidence: high, medium, low (evidence and agreement).
- Likelihood: virtually certain ≥ 99%; extremely likely ≥ 95%; likely ≥ 66%.
- Distinct dimensions: "high confidence" is not "likely".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Bands (✅ owner 18 advanced §2; AR6 calibration as stated in owner).
**Links:** ENV-3082, ENV-3083
**Trap:** Treating "high confidence" and "likely" as synonyms (✅ owner 18 advanced §8).
**Mains hook:** Using the calibrated terms precisely shows you are not presenting probabilistic findings as proven facts.
**Back:** *"Confidence is evidence; likelihood is a percentage."*

### Flashcard ENV-3085 — IPCC Special Reports: SR1.5, SROCC and SRCCL
**Front:** Which three Special Reports are most cited, and what does each cover?
**Definition (learn):** Special Reports are targeted assessments between full cycles. SR1.5 (2018) covers impacts and pathways for 1.5°C versus 2°C, requested after COP21. SROCC (2019) covers ocean and cryosphere. SRCCL (2019) covers land, desertification and food.
**Breakdown:**
- SR1.5 (2018): 1.5°C vs 2°C; invited after the 2015 Paris Agreement.
- SROCC (2019): ocean warming, sea level, glaciers, permafrost.
- SRCCL (2019): land degradation, desertification, food security.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 advanced §3; SR1.5 accepted 2018 (owner)).
**Links:** ENV-3067, ENV-3090, ENV-3091
**Trap:** Special Reports are not shorter versions of the regular cycle (✅ owner 18 advanced §8).
**Mains hook:** SR1.5 became a reference point for NDC ambition; SROCC anchors Indian Ocean and coastal risk.
**Back:** *"1.5 for warming, ocean for sea, land for food."*

### Flashcard ENV-3086 — Trap: policy-relevant but not policy-prescriptive
**Front:** What is the IPCC's mandate on policy, and what is the trap?
**Definition (learn):** The IPCC is policy-relevant but not policy-prescriptive. It assesses options and costs; it does not recommend which policy a government should adopt.
**Breakdown:**
- Allowed: assess pathways, costs and trade-offs.
- Not allowed: recommend a particular national policy.
- Why: keeps it a common reference for divergent states.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 advanced §4).
**Links:** ENV-3075, ENV-3083
**Trap:** "IPCC recommends specific national policies" is wrong (✅ owner 18 advanced §8).
**Mains hook:** Non-prescriptiveness is what allows fossil exporters and vulnerable islands to accept the same science.
**Back:** *"It shows the road, not which car to drive."*

### Flashcard ENV-3087 — AR7 cycle timeline (as of 2 August 2026, per owner)
**Front:** What are the AR7 milestones and their dates?
**Definition (learn):** AR7 is the seventh cycle. Its milestones are process steps, not findings.
**Breakdown:**
- IPCC-60, Istanbul (Jan 2024): agreed to produce three WG contributions.
- IPCC-61, Sofia (27 Jul–2 Aug 2024): outline of Special Report on Climate Change and Cities.
- IPCC-62, Hangzhou (Feb 2025): outlines of three WG contributions.
- IPCC-63, Lima (Oct 2025): scientific content of 2027 CDR/CCUS methodology report; 2026 workplan.
- AR7 Synthesis Report: by late 2029.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Per owner 18 basic §8 (✅ owner, stated as verified from ipcc.ch on 2 Aug 2026). Not re-fetched here.
**Links:** ENV-3081, ENV-3088, ENV-3093
**Trap:** An agreed outline is not a published finding.
**Mains hook:** A late-2029 synthesis sits awkwardly against the five-yearly Global Stocktake (✅ owner 18 advanced §9, analytical).
**Back:** *"Istanbul, Sofia, Hangzhou, Lima, then 2029."*

### Flashcard ENV-3088 — AR7 methodology reports (2027) and the process-not-findings rule
**Front:** Which two 2027 methodology reports are in the AR7 cycle, and why do they matter?
**Definition (learn):** The 2027 Methodology Report on Inventories for Short-Lived Climate Forcers and the 2027 Methodology Report on Carbon Dioxide Removal Technologies, CCUS decide how methane and removals are counted, which shapes what countries can claim.
**Breakdown:**
- SLCF inventories methodology (2027).
- CDR/CCUS methodology (2027), scientific content agreed at IPCC-63 (Oct 2025).
- Rule: decide counting, not headline conclusions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 basic §6, §8; owner 18 advanced §9).
**Links:** ENV-3080, ENV-3087, ENV-3093
**Trap:** Citing an AR7 finding before its Summary for Policymakers is approved.
**Mains hook:** Accounting rules often matter more than headlines (ENV-3080).
**Back:** *"Methodology counts the methane and the carbon removed."*

### Flashcard ENV-3089 — Literature cut-off lag and regional-projection uncertainty
**Front:** What two structural limits does the owner identify for IPCC findings?
**Definition (learn):** IPCC synthesises published literature up to a cut-off, so newer research waits for the next cycle or a Special Report. Regional projections carry more uncertainty than global averages.
**Breakdown:**
- Lag: literature cut-off → assessed consensus.
- Regional: monsoon-shift magnitude is less certain than global warming.
- Use: cite the confidence level when using regional projections.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 advanced §5; ⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** ENV-3084, ENV-3085
**Trap:** Regional projections carry the same confidence as global averages (✅ owner 18 advanced §8).
**Mains hook:** Qualify a South Asian monsoon claim with its confidence band.
**Back:** *"Old literature, local uncertainty: say so."*

### Flashcard ENV-3090 — PYQ 2023 GS-III Q18: IPCC sea-level rise and the Indian Ocean (15 marks)
**Front:** What does the 2023 question on IPCC sea-level prediction and the Indian Ocean require?
**Definition (learn):** The 2023 GS-III Q18 (15 marks, 250 words) asks for IPCC sea-level predictions and their impact on the Indian Ocean. The owner routes it here, using SROCC and AR6 as anchors.
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism: ocean thermal expansion plus glacier and ice-sheet mass loss (ENV-3091).
- Anchors: SROCC (2019) and AR6.
- Impacts: low-lying coasts, islands, salinity, ports, livelihoods.
- Response: mitigation plus risk-sensitive coastal planning.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 basic PYQ block; ✅ owner 18 advanced PYQ block).
**Links:** ENV-3085, ENV-3091, ENV-3067
**Trap:** Do not invent a single India-wide sea-level figure or present a global projection as a local forecast (✅ owner 18 basic §13.4).
**Mains hook:** Close on coastal adaptation plus emissions mitigation, not on either alone.
**Back:** *"Expansion plus melt, anchored in SROCC, India's coasts at risk."*

### Flashcard ENV-3091 — Sea-level mechanism for an Indian Ocean answer
**Front:** What two mechanisms drive sea-level rise in the owner's answer spine?
**Definition (learn):** Warming ocean water expands, and glacier and ice-sheet mass loss adds water. Regional sea level and impacts vary with ocean dynamics, land motion and local exposure.
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism 1: thermal expansion.
- Mechanism 2: glacier and ice-sheet melt.
- Modifiers: ocean dynamics, land subsidence or uplift, local exposure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 basic §13.4).
**Links:** ENV-3090, ENV-3065, ENV-3067
**Trap:** Sea level rises uniformly everywhere (ENV-3065).
**Mains hook:** Define the mechanism first, then the regional modifiers.
**Back:** *"Warm water swells, melt adds, locations differ."*

### Flashcard ENV-3092 — Trap: IPCC research versus UNFCCC negotiation
**Front:** Which two confusions does the owner flag between the IPCC and the UNFCCC COP?
**Definition (learn):** The IPCC is a scientific assessment body. The UNFCCC COP is the negotiating body where treaty outcomes are decided. Policy negotiation sits outside the IPCC.
**Breakdown:**
- IPCC: assessment, no primary research.
- COP: negotiation and decisions.
- Link: IPCC evidence feeds COP negotiations.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 basic §7).
**Links:** ENV-3075, ENV-3096, ENV-3086
**Trap:** IPCC reports are "written by scientists with no government involvement" is also wrong; the SPM is government-approved (✅ owner 18 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Evidence informs but cannot compel a treaty outcome.
**Back:** *"IPCC informs; COP decides."*

### Flashcard ENV-3093 — Trap: AR7 is not yet the baseline
**Front:** Has AR7 replaced AR6 as the citable baseline, and what is the status as of 2 August 2026?
**Definition (learn):** AR6 (2023) remains the latest completed full assessment. AR7 Working Group reports are in preparation, with the Synthesis Report due by late 2029.
**Breakdown:**
- Latest completed: AR6.
- In progress: AR7.
- Rule: cite the report name and year.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As of 2 August 2026 (✅ owner 18 basic §7–8, as-of date per owner).
**Links:** ENV-3081, ENV-3087
**Trap:** "AR7 has replaced AR6" is wrong (✅ owner 18 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Use AR6 as the current reference and flag the literature cut-off.
**Back:** *"Six is the latest done; seven is still in progress."*

### Flashcard ENV-3094 — Mains hook: IPCC's dual authority
**Front:** Why does the IPCC's design give its findings both scientific and political authority?
**Definition (learn):** The IPCC combines scientist-authored assessment with a government-approved SPM. Scientific credibility and political buy-in reinforce each other.
**Breakdown:**
- Scientific: synthesis of peer-reviewed literature with calibrated language.
- Political: government approval of the SPM.
- Trade-off: consensus can soften wording.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 18 basic §10; ✅ owner 18 advanced §11).
**Links:** ENV-3083, ENV-3084, ENV-3092
**Trap:** Dual authority is not the same as government authorship of the science.
**Mains hook:** Argue that dual authority makes IPCC findings the common reference for negotiations, while the translation to action remains a separate political task.
**Back:** *"Scientists write, governments approve, the world negotiates from it."*

### GROUP — UNFCCC COP, Kyoto Protocol and Paris Agreement

### Flashcard ENV-3095 — Architecture shift: framework, then Annex B targets, then nationally determined contributions
**Front:** Summarise the three-stage evolution of global climate treaty architecture.
**Definition (learn):** Global climate governance moved from a framework convention with no numeric caps (UNFCCC, 1992), to binding but narrow quantified commitments for Kyoto Parties listed in Annex B (1997), to a universal but nationally self-determined process of NDCs (Paris, 2015).
**Breakdown:**
- 1992: framework; goal; COP created; no numeric targets.
- 1997: Kyoto; Annex B quantified commitments for Parties bound in the period.
- 2015: Paris; all Parties submit NDCs; procedural duties binding; substantive level nationally determined.
- 2024–25: COP29 finance goal; COP30 implementation and adaptation finance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §1; dates verified in ENV-3096, ENV-3098, ENV-3103).
**Links:** ENV-3096, ENV-3098, ENV-3103, ENV-3106
**Trap:** Dates alone are not the answer; the architecture is the test.
**Mains hook:** Trace the shift as a sovereignty-versus-ambition trade-off, not as a chronology (ENV-3122).
**Back:** *"Framework, Annex B, NDCs: from goal to quota to pledge."*

### Flashcard ENV-3096 — UNFCCC: adoption, signing, entry into force and secretariat
**Front:** Give the UNFCCC's adoption date, signing opening, entry-into-force date, secretariat and first COP.
**Definition (learn):** The UN Framework Convention on Climate Change sets the overarching goal of preventing dangerous anthropogenic interference with the climate system. It created the COP as the annual governing forum.
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted: 9 May 1992 (negotiated in New York; ✅ Wikipedia: UNFCCC).
- Opened for signature: 4 June 1992 at the Rio Earth Summit (UNCED); 154 signatures on 12 June 1992 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Entry into force: 21 March 1994 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Secretariat: Bonn, Germany (✅ Wikipedia).
- COP1: Berlin, 1995 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed; ✅ Wikipedia: UNFCCC.
**Links:** ENV-3095, ENV-3097, ENV-3110
**Trap:** The UNFCCC contains no binding numeric emission target (ENV-3124).
**Mains hook:** The framework treaty secured universal membership (154 signatories) but no numbers; numbers came later.
**Back:** *"Nine May adopted, four June signed at Rio, twenty-one March 1994 in force."*

### Flashcard ENV-3097 — COP, CMP and CMA: three governing bodies
**Front:** What do COP, CMP and CMA stand for, and what does each govern?
**Definition (learn):** The COP is the UNFCCC's supreme body. The CMP governs the Kyoto Protocol. The CMA governs the Paris Agreement. They can meet at the same annual conference but their membership, mandate and decisions are legally distinct.
**Breakdown:**
- COP: Convention.
- CMP: Kyoto Protocol.
- CMA: Paris Agreement.
- Example: decision 1/CMA.6 (Baku, COP29) is a CMA decision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §2).
**Links:** ENV-3096, ENV-3113
**Trap:** A COP decision is not automatically a CMA or CMP decision.
**Mains hook:** Identify the governing body before treating an outcome as binding on a given treaty.
**Back:** *"COP for the Convention, CMP for Kyoto, CMA for Paris."*

### Flashcard ENV-3098 — Kyoto Protocol: adoption, entry into force and the first commitment period
**Front:** When and where was the Kyoto Protocol adopted, when did it enter into force, and what was the first commitment period?
**Definition (learn):** The first treaty with legally binding quantified emission commitments, adopted at COP3 in Kyoto. It entered into force only after Russia's ratification met the 55% emissions condition.
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted: 11 December 1997, Kyoto, Japan (COP3) (✅ Wikipedia: Kyoto Protocol).
- Entry into force: 16 February 2005, 90 days after Russia's ratification on 18 November 2004 (✅ Wikipedia).
- First commitment period: 2008–2012 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Second commitment period: 2013–2020, agreed 2012 (owner; ENV-3102).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ENV-3099, ENV-3100, ENV-3101, ENV-3102
**Trap:** Adoption (1997) is not entry into force (2005).
**Mains hook:** Kyoto took eight years from adoption to entry into force, showing how ratification thresholds delay treaties.
**Back:** *"Ninety-seven adopted, oh-five in force, eight to twelve first."*

### Flashcard ENV-3099 — Annex I, Annex II, non-Annex I and Kyoto Annex B
**Front:** Distinguish Convention Annex I, Annex II, non-Annex I and Kyoto Annex B.
**Definition (learn):** Annex I lists industrialised countries and economies in transition with additional Convention commitments. Annex II is a subset of Annex I carrying extra finance and technology-transfer duties. Non-Annex I is the developing group, including India. Annex B is the Kyoto schedule of quantified commitments and only partly overlaps Annex I.
**Breakdown:**
- Annex I: industrialised plus economies in transition.
- Annex II: subset of Annex I; finance and tech transfer.
- Non-Annex I: developing countries, including India.
- Kyoto Annex B: quantified commitments for listed Parties.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §2, §6).
**Links:** ENV-3098, ENV-3125
**Trap:** Annex I and Annex II are not alternative names; Annex I and Annex B are not synonyms.
**Mains hook:** Annex classifications explain why India sits outside Kyoto's binding list.
**Back:** *"Annex II sits inside Annex I; India is non-Annex I."*

### Flashcard ENV-3100 — Kyoto flexibility mechanisms: CDM, JI, international emissions trading
**Front:** Name the three Kyoto flexibility mechanisms and what each does.
**Definition (learn):** The Kyoto Protocol's market mechanisms are the Clean Development Mechanism (CDM) for developing-country projects generating credits for Annex I buyers; Joint Implementation (JI) for projects between Annex I countries; and International Emissions Trading.
**Breakdown:**
- CDM: developing-country projects → credits to Annex I buyers.
- JI: Annex I to Annex I projects.
- IET: trading of assigned emission amounts.
- India: one of the largest CDM host countries (✅ owner 19 basic §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §2).
**Links:** ENV-3108, ENV-3098
**Trap:** Paris Article 6.4 succeeds the CDM in function, not as its legal copy (ENV-3108).
**Mains hook:** CDM showed both the promise and the integrity risk of offset credits.
**Back:** *"CDM for developing, JI for Annex I pairs, trading for the rest."*

### Flashcard ENV-3101 — Kyoto participation failure: United States and Canada
**Front:** How did the United States and Canada affect Kyoto's participation?
**Definition (learn):** The US signed Kyoto but never ratified it; the Senate passed the Byrd–Hagel Resolution 95–0 in 1997 and the treaty was never submitted for ratification. Canada announced withdrawal, and second-period participation narrowed.
**Breakdown:**
- US: signed 12 November 1998 under Clinton; never ratified (✅ Wikipedia: Kyoto Protocol).
- Byrd–Hagel Resolution 1997, 95–0 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Canada: announced withdrawal December 2011; effective December 2012 (✅ Wikipedia; the page's dates vary between "2011" and "2012").
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ENV-3098, ENV-3102, ENV-3122
**Trap:** Annex I status did not oblige the US or Canada in practice (✅ owner 19 basic §3, §7).
**Mains hook:** Kyoto's participation problem is the design reason Paris chose nationally determined targets.
**Back:** *"Signed not ratified, withdrawn, and the Senate said ninety-five to nothing."*

### Flashcard ENV-3102 — Doha Amendment: second commitment period and late entry into force
**Front:** When was the Doha Amendment adopted and when did it enter into force, and why is that date notable?
**Definition (learn):** The Doha Amendment (2012) set the second commitment period, 2013–2020. It entered into force on 31 December 2020, the last day of that period.
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted: 8 December 2012, Doha, Qatar (✅ Wikipedia: Kyoto Protocol).
- Second period: 2013–2020.
- Entry into force: 31 December 2020, after at least 144 acceptances (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ENV-3098, ENV-3101
**Trap:** "The Doha Amendment never took effect" is wrong; it did, on the final day it governed (✅ owner 19 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Ratification lag can defeat a treaty's purpose; Doha is the standard example.
**Back:** *"Doha 2012 adopted, Doha's period ended the day it began to bind."*

### Flashcard ENV-3103 — Paris Agreement: adoption, signing, entry into force
**Front:** Give the Paris Agreement's adoption venue and date, opening for signature and entry-into-force date.
**Definition (learn):** The Paris Agreement was adopted by consensus at COP21 and is universally applicable.
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted: 12 December 2015 at COP21, Le Bourget near Paris (✅ Wikipedia: Paris Agreement).
- Opened for signature: 22 April 2016 at UN headquarters, New York (✅ Wikipedia).
- Entry into force: 4 November 2016, after EU ratification on 5 October 2016 crossed the 55 parties / 55% emissions threshold (✅ Wikipedia).
- Current parties: 194 as of January 2026 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ENV-3095, ENV-3104, ENV-3105
**Trap:** Adoption (Dec 2015) and entry into force (Nov 2016) are different dates.
**Mains hook:** Universal participation was achieved by making targets national.
**Back:** *"Twelve December adopted, twenty-two April signed, four November in force."*

### Flashcard ENV-3104 — Paris long-term goal: well below 2°C and pursue 1.5°C
**Front:** What is the Paris Agreement's long-term temperature goal?
**Definition (learn):** Hold the increase in global average temperature well below 2°C above pre-industrial levels, and pursue efforts to limit it to 1.5°C.
**Breakdown:**
- Ceiling: well below 2°C.
- Aspiration: pursue efforts for 1.5°C.
- Legal character: a treaty goal, not an enforceable national temperature share (ENV-3124).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia: Paris Agreement, Article 2 wording).
**Links:** ENV-3103, ENV-3107, ENV-3085
**Trap:** Quoting 1.5°C as a binding ceiling for each country.
**Mains hook:** SR1.5 gave the scientific basis for the 1.5°C aspiration (ENV-3085).
**Back:** *"Well below two, pursue one-point-five."*

### Flashcard ENV-3105 — NDCs: five-year cycle, progression and highest possible ambition
**Front:** What do Paris rules require of each successive NDC?
**Definition (learn):** Each Party prepares, communicates and maintains successive NDCs, communicated every five years. Each successive NDC represents progression beyond the previous one and reflects the highest possible ambition.
**Breakdown:**
- Communication: every five years (Article 4.9 per owner).
- Progression and highest possible ambition (Article 4.3 per owner).
- General NDC obligation and progression principle also appear in Article 3 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Registration with the UNFCCC Secretariat (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five-year cycle (✅ Wikipedia; article numbers per owner, ⚠️ article numbers not confirmed by fetch).
**Links:** ENV-3103, ENV-3106, ENV-3123
**Trap:** Article numbering: owner cites Article 4 for progression; the fetched page cites Article 3 as well. Check the treaty text before citing a number.
**Mains hook:** The five-year cycle is the ratchet's clock.
**Back:** *"Five years, always more ambitious than the last."*

### Flashcard ENV-3106 — Paris duties versus substantive targets (the central trap)
**Front:** What does Paris make binding, and what does each Party determine itself?
**Definition (learn):** Paris imposes binding procedural and conduct duties: prepare, communicate and maintain NDCs, pursue domestic measures, account and report. The form and level of each NDC target is nationally determined.
**Breakdown:**
- Binding: procedure (prepare, communicate, maintain, account, report).
- Nationally determined: substantive target.
- Not present: a single binding global cut assigned to each country.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §3, §6).
**Links:** ENV-3105, ENV-3095, ENV-3123
**Trap:** "Paris sets a single binding global target all countries must meet" is wrong (✅ owner 19 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Universality and durability were bought with weaker externally imposed outcomes.
**Back:** *"Duties binding, numbers national."*

### Flashcard ENV-3107 — First Global Stocktake and the UAE Consensus (COP28, 2023)
**Front:** What did the first Global Stocktake conclude at COP28, and what language did it use?
**Definition (learn):** The GST is a five-yearly collective assessment of progress toward Paris goals, designed to inform the next NDC round. The first concluded at COP28 (Dubai, 2023) in the "UAE Consensus".
**Breakdown:**
- Call: contribute to transitioning away from fossil fuels in energy systems.
- Call: triple global renewable energy capacity by 2030.
- Call: double the rate of energy-efficiency improvement by 2030.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** COP28 Dubai 2023 (✅ owner 19 basic §2, §6). Article number not confirmed (⚠️).
**Links:** ENV-3104, ENV-3105, ENV-3111, ENV-3117
**Trap:** "Transitioning away" is not "phase out"; examiners test the exact wording (ENV-3124 family).
**Mains hook:** The GST is the ratchet's engine; judge Paris by whether GST outputs raised the next NDC round.
**Back:** *"Transition away, triple renewables, double efficiency, by 2030."*

### Flashcard ENV-3108 — Article 6: 6.2, 6.4 and 6.8
**Front:** What does each sub-article of Paris Article 6 provide?
**Definition (learn):** Article 6 provides the cooperative-approaches architecture for carbon markets.
**Breakdown:**
- 6.2: cooperative approaches using Internationally Transferred Mitigation Outcomes (ITMOs).
- 6.4: centralised UN-supervised crediting mechanism (Paris Agreement Crediting Mechanism, PACM); succeeds the CDM in function, not as a legal copy.
- 6.8: non-market approaches.
- Design tension: double counting must be prevented.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §6; ✅ owner 19 advanced §3).
**Links:** ENV-3100, ENV-3125
**Trap:** Article 6.2 and 6.4 are different mechanisms (ENV-3124 family; owner advanced §9).
**Mains hook:** Carbon markets lower cost only with robust accounting (no double counting).
**Back:** *"Two-point-two bilateral, four central, eight non-market."*

### Flashcard ENV-3109 — Climate finance under Article 9 and the separate layers
**Front:** How do Article 9's treaty duty and the CMA finance goals differ?
**Definition (learn):** Article 9.1 imposes a treaty duty on developed-country Parties to provide financial resources. The CMA goals adopted at COP29 are collective goals and calls, not assessed country contributions.
**Breakdown:**
- Article 9.1: treaty duty on developed countries.
- CMA goal (COP29): at least USD 300 billion per year by 2035, developed countries leading.
- All-actor call: at least USD 1.3 trillion per year by 2035 (ENV-3113).
- Pledge is not disbursement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 9.1 (✅ owner 19 advanced §4); USD figures (✅ owner 19 basic §3, ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3113, ENV-3110, ENV-3116
**Trap:** Treating the USD 300 billion goal as a treaty receipt.
**Mains hook:** Equity claims rest on which finance layer is being discussed.
**Back:** *"Article nine is the duty; the goal is a goal."*

### Flashcard ENV-3110 — Common But Differentiated Responsibilities and Respective Capabilities (CBDR-RC)
**Front:** What does CBDR-RC mean and how does it operate after Paris?
**Definition (learn):** The UNFCCC's equity principle: all countries share responsibility for climate action, but developed countries bear greater responsibility given historical emissions and capability.
**Breakdown:**
- Foundation: UNFCCC principle.
- Kyoto: binary developed/developing split.
- Paris: universal participation with nationally differentiated ambition, not a binary.
- India's use: equity and historical cumulative emissions argument.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §2, §7).
**Links:** ENV-3096, ENV-3069, ENV-3109
**Trap:** "CBDR-RC was abandoned in Paris" is wrong (✅ owner 19 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** CBDR-RC frames India's position on finance and differentiated ambition.
**Back:** *"Shared duty, differentiated share."*

### Flashcard ENV-3111 — Loss and Damage Fund: agreed at COP27, operationalised at COP28
**Front:** When was the Loss and Damage Fund agreed and operationalised?
**Definition (learn):** The Loss and Damage Fund addresses climate harms that mitigation and adaptation cannot prevent. It is the third pillar of climate response.
**Breakdown:**
- Agreed in principle: COP27, Sharm el-Sheikh, 2022.
- Operationalised: COP28, Dubai, 2023, with initial pledges.
- Distinct from adaptation finance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §2, §6).
**Links:** ENV-3107, ENV-3109
**Trap:** Created at COP28 (wrong); the fund was agreed at COP27 (✅ owner 19 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** The existence of a third pillar reflects vulnerable countries' argument that adaptation alone cannot cover unavoidable harm.
**Back:** *"Agreed in Egypt, opened in Dubai."*

### Flashcard ENV-3112 — COP26 Glasgow: coal phase-down language and subsidy language
**Front:** What do the COP26 Glasgow outcome and India's national announcement each contain?
**Definition (learn):** COP26's Glasgow Climate Pact includes careful language on coal phase-down and inefficient fossil-fuel subsidies. India's Panchamrit is a separate national announcement (ENV-3132).
**Breakdown:**
- COP26 outcome: negotiated text (coal phase-down; inefficient fossil subsidy phase-out).
- India's Panchamrit: national announcement, not automatically an NDC.
- Keep the two blocks separate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** COP26 Glasgow 2021 (✅ owner 19 basic §13.4; ✅ owner 19 basic PYQ 2021 GS-III Q17).
**Links:** ENV-3132, ENV-3117, ENV-3121
**Trap:** Treating a national announcement as an agreed COP outcome.
**Mains hook:** Glasgow mattered for implementation pressure and coal equity, but text is not delivery.
**Back:** *"Glasgow's text is the COP outcome; Panchamrit is India's pledge."*

### Flashcard ENV-3113 — COP29 Baku: decision 1/CMA.6 and the finance figures
**Front:** What does decision 1/CMA.6 set for climate finance by 2035?
**Definition (learn):** Decision 1/CMA.6 (COP29, Baku, 2024) calls on all actors to scale finance for developing-country climate action to at least USD 1.3 trillion per year by 2035. It sets a goal, with developed countries taking the lead, of at least USD 300 billion per year by 2035.
**Breakdown:**
- All-actor call: USD 1.3 trillion/year by 2035.
- Collective goal: USD 300 billion/year by 2035, developed countries lead, wide sources.
- Developing countries encouraged to contribute voluntarily.
- Needs cited: USD 5.1–6.8 trillion to 2030 (about USD 455–584 billion/year); adaptation USD 215–387 billion/year (owner advanced §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §3, §8; ⚠️ not fetched; COP29 (2024) as stated).
**Links:** ENV-3109, ENV-3116, ENV-3097
**Trap:** The two figures are not the same commitment; neither is an assessed country contribution (✅ owner 19 advanced §4).
**Mains hook:** Critique the gap between the goal and costed needs, naming which figure is which.
**Back:** *"One-point-three trillion calls, three hundred billion goal."*

### Flashcard ENV-3114 — COP30 Belém (2025): roadmap, adaptation and no binding fossil phase-out
**Front:** What did COP30 in Belém deliver, and what did it not deliver?
**Definition (learn):** COP30 (CMA7, Belém, Brazil, 2025) continued the Baku-to-Belém Roadmap, adaptation-finance scaling and loss-and-damage implementation, without a binding fossil-fuel phase-out commitment (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Continued: Baku-to-Belém Roadmap.
- Continued: adaptation finance and L&D implementation.
- Not secured: binding fossil-fuel phase-out.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §8 and advanced §10; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3113, ENV-3111, ENV-3107
**Trap:** Do not infer an outcome from a Presidency proposal or coalition statement (✅ owner 19 advanced §10).
**Mains hook:** Evidence of incremental progress against structurally difficult issues.
**Back:** *"Belém: roadmap yes, binding phase-out no."*

### Flashcard ENV-3115 — COP31 Antalya timing and India's 2031–2035 NDC submission
**Front:** When and where is COP31, and when did India submit its NDC for 2031–2035?
**Definition (learn):** As of 5 October 2026, COP31 (CMP21, CMA8) was scheduled for 9–20 November 2026 in Antalya, Türkiye, with no negotiated outcome yet. India submitted its NDC for 2031–2035 on 24 April 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- COP31 / CMP21 / CMA8: 9–20 November 2026, Antalya.
- India NDC 2031–2035: submitted 24 April 2026 (owner).
- As-of date: 5 October 2026 (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner-only as-of; not fetched in this build; UNFCCC links cited by owner).
**Links:** ENV-3140, ENV-3141, ENV-3116
**Trap:** Quoting an outcome for COP31 before its decisions exist.
**Mains hook:** Use status dates in every current-affairs claim.
**Back:** *"Antalya in November; India's new NDC in April."*

### Flashcard ENV-3116 — Decision, pledge and disbursement: three different facts
**Front:** Distinguish a COP decision text, a pledge and verified money delivered.
**Definition (learn):** A decision text is adopted by the COP or CMA. A pledge is a voluntary commitment by a party. Money delivered is verified disbursement. Only the last is delivery.
**Breakdown:**
- Decision text: adopted outcome.
- Pledge: voluntary commitment.
- Disbursement: verified money.
- NCQG USD 300 billion: a goal, not a receipt.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §8).
**Links:** ENV-3113, ENV-3109, ENV-3115
**Trap:** Treating a goal as delivered money.
**Mains hook:** Judge finance by verified disbursement, not by headline goals.
**Back:** *"Text adopted, pledge promised, money delivered: three facts."*

### Flashcard ENV-3117 — PYQ 2025 GS-III: Paris commitments, COP26 and the 2022 NDC update
**Front:** What does the 2025 GS-III question route to this file, and what goes to topic 20?
**Definition (learn):** The 2025 GS-III question asks for India's Paris commitments, COP26 strengthening, and the 2022 NDC update. This file owns the treaty and COP architecture; domestic targets belong to the India climate-policy owner.
**Breakdown:**
- Treaty part: Paris architecture (this card set).
- Domestic part: NDC targets, Panchamrit (ENV-3132 onward).
- Keep announced, submitted and achieved separate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §9 and PYQ block; ✅ owner 20 basic §9).
**Links:** ENV-3103, ENV-3136, ENV-3132
**Trap:** Do not call a COP announcement an automatically updated NDC (✅ owner 19 advanced §13).
**Mains hook:** Build the answer as treaty architecture plus national evidence.
**Back:** *"Treaty here, targets there, evidence everywhere."*

### Flashcard ENV-3118 — PYQ 2025 Prelims GS-I Q32: COP28 Declaration on Climate and Health
**Front:** What does the 2025 Prelims question on the COP28 Declaration on Climate and Health test?
**Definition (learn):** A 2025 Prelims GS-I objective question (Q32) on the COP28 Declaration on Climate and Health and India's position.
**Breakdown:**
- Test: named declaration and its position.
- Keys: official Set-A key held locally; answer not recorded in the owner.
- Route: COP28 (Dubai, 2023).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic PYQ block 2024–2025).
**Links:** ENV-3107, ENV-3117
**Trap:** Do not infer the answer letter from memory; check the Set-A key.
**Mains hook:** Health links climate policy to public-health co-benefits (ENV-3148).
**Back:** *"Climate and health at Dubai: check the key, not memory."*

### Flashcard ENV-3119 — PYQ 2018 Prelims: PAGE, Climate Neutral Now and GACSA
**Front:** Which three initiatives were tested in 2018 Prelims GS-I?
**Definition (learn):** Q35: Global Alliance for Climate-Smart Agriculture (features and membership). Q83: Partnership for Action on Green Economy (PAGE), a UN mechanism. Q88: Momentum for Change, Climate Neutral Now initiative and its originator.
**Breakdown:**
- Q35: GACSA.
- Q83: PAGE (UN).
- Q88: Climate Neutral Now (Momentum for Change).
- Keys not held locally (✅ owner 19 basic PYQ block 2018–2023).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic PYQ block).
**Links:** ENV-3121
**Trap:** Acronym confusion; name the initiative and its sponsor.
**Mains hook:** Initiatives sit alongside UNFCCC; they are not treaty bodies.
**Back:** *"Agriculture alliance, green-economy partnership, neutral-now momentum."*

### Flashcard ENV-3120 — PYQ 2022 Prelims: Climate Action Tracker, EP100 and Under2 Coalition
**Front:** Which climate monitoring and energy initiatives appear in 2022 Prelims Q41 and Q42?
**Definition (learn):** Q41: the body that monitors emission pledges (Climate Action Tracker). Q42: Climate Group's EP100 and the Under2 Coalition energy initiatives.
**Breakdown:**
- Q41: Climate Action Tracker (pledge monitoring).
- Q42: EP100 and Under2 Coalition (energy efficiency and sub-national action).
- Keys not held locally.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic PYQ block 2018–2023).
**Links:** ENV-3119, ENV-3116
**Trap:** Monitoring bodies do not set targets.
**Mains hook:** Sub-national coalitions show ambition outside national targets.
**Back:** *"Tracker tracks pledges; EP100 and Under2 push energy."*

### Flashcard ENV-3121 — PYQ 2021 and 2022 routes: COP26 and clean energy in international fora
**Front:** What do the 2021 GS-III Q17 and 2022 GS-II Q20 questions route to?
**Definition (learn):** 2021 GS-III Q17 (15 marks): COP26 major outcomes and India's Glasgow commitments; routed to this owner. 2022 GS-II Q20 (15 marks): clean energy and India's climate policy in international fora; IR owns the diplomatic dimension.
**Breakdown:**
- 2021 GS-III Q17: COP26 outcomes and India's commitments.
- 2022 GS-II Q20: clean energy in international fora (IR core owns).
- Answer shape: context, dimensions, evidence, counterpoint, conclusion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic PYQ block 2018–2023).
**Links:** ENV-3112, ENV-3132, ENV-3117
**Trap:** Treating an IR-owned diplomatic question as a pure climate-science question.
**Mains hook:** Use Glasgow as the example, and the national announcement as the evidence, with the treaty route in the background.
**Back:** *"Glasgow for the COP, clean energy for the diplomacy."*

### Flashcard ENV-3122 — Mains hook: sovereignty versus ambition, the master framework
**Front:** What is the master analytical framework for the UNFCCC–Kyoto–Paris evolution?
**Definition (learn):** The master framework is the trade-off between universal participation and externally fixed national outcomes. Kyoto gave binding quantified targets to a narrow group; Paris gave near-universal participation with nationally determined substantive targets and a ratchet.
**Breakdown:**
- Kyoto: external targets, narrow participation (US non-ratification, Canada withdrawal).
- Paris: national targets, universal participation, progression, GST.
- Open question: whether procedural compliance delivers an adequate aggregate pathway.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 advanced §1–2; ⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** ENV-3101, ENV-3105, ENV-3123
**Trap:** Describing the evolution only as a chronology.
**Mains hook:** Use this lens for 10- and 15-mark answers on global climate governance.
**Back:** *"Kyoto bound a few; Paris binds the process, not the number."*

### Flashcard ENV-3123 — Ratchet, compliance and why Paris cannot compel a percentage
**Front:** Does Paris legally compel a specific percentage increase in each NDC, and how is compliance handled?
**Definition (learn):** No. Paris requires progression and highest possible ambition, assessed qualitatively through the GST and peer or public pressure. Article 15 compliance is expert-based, facilitative, non-adversarial and non-punitive.
**Breakdown:**
- Progression: qualitative standard.
- GST: collective review.
- Compliance: facilitative, non-punitive (Article 15 per owner).
- Gap: procedural compliance can coexist with an inadequate aggregate pathway.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 advanced §2, §9; Article 15 per owner, ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3105, ENV-3107, ENV-3122
**Trap:** "The ratchet legally compels a specific percentage" is wrong (✅ owner 19 advanced §9).
**Mains hook:** Ask whether the stocktake actually raised the next round's ambition.
**Back:** *"Ratchet pushes, stocktake reviews, compliance advises, no percentage forced."*

### Flashcard ENV-3124 — Traps: UNFCCC targets, Paris as a national ceiling, and "phase-out"
**Front:** Which three treaty-level traps does the owner flag most often?
**Definition (learn):** The UNFCCC itself has no binding numeric targets. The 1.5°C goal is not an enforceable national share. COP28's wording is "transitioning away", not phase-out.
**Breakdown:**
- UNFCCC: framework; no numeric targets.
- Paris 1.5°C: treaty goal, no national allocation.
- COP28: "transitioning away from fossil fuels in energy systems".
- Also: Kyoto did not impose targets on every Annex I country.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §7).
**Links:** ENV-3096, ENV-3104, ENV-3107, ENV-3125
**Trap:** These are close-option pairs in objective questions.
**Mains hook:** Precise wording is the examiner's test of understanding.
**Back:** *"Framework has no numbers, one-point-five is an aim, transition is not phase-out."*

### Flashcard ENV-3125 — Trap: Kyoto targets for all Annex I, and Annex II as synonym
**Front:** Why are "all Annex I countries had Kyoto targets" and "Annex II equals Annex I" wrong?
**Definition (learn):** Kyoto's quantified commitments applied through Annex B for Parties bound in the commitment period, not to every Annex I country. Annex II is a subset of Annex I with added finance and technology duties.
**Breakdown:**
- Annex B: listed Parties and period-specific participation.
- US: did not ratify; Canada: withdrew; India: no Annex B commitment.
- Annex II: subset with finance and tech duties.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 19 basic §3, §7).
**Links:** ENV-3099, ENV-3101, ENV-3124
**Trap:** Treating Annex I and Annex B as the same list.
**Mains hook:** Classification shapes who owes what; India's non-Annex I status shapes its equity argument.
**Back:** *"Annex B is a list, Annex II a subset, India outside both targets."*

### GROUP — India Climate Policy: NAPCC, Panchamrit, NDCs and LT-LEDS

### Flashcard ENV-3126 — India's layered climate-policy instruments (not interchangeable)
**Front:** Distinguish NAPCC, Panchamrit, the NDCs and the LT-LEDS.
**Definition (learn):** India's climate policy has four layers. NAPCC (2008) is the domestic missions framework. Panchamrit (COP26, 2021) is a set of political announcements. NDCs are formal Paris contributions submitted to the UNFCCC (2015, 2022 update, 2026 for 2031–2035). LT-LEDS (2022) is the long-term strategy toward net zero by 2070. They are not legally or metrically the same.
**Breakdown:**
- NAPCC: domestic, co-benefits framing, missions.
- Panchamrit: political pledges with mixed metric types.
- NDC: formal Paris contribution; a Panchamrit item counts only if it is in the NDC text.
- LT-LEDS: long-term strategy; not a binding carbon-budget law.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic §1–2).
**Links:** ENV-3127, ENV-3132, ENV-3135, ENV-3138
**Trap:** Calling LT-LEDS the NDC, or a Panchamrit pledge an NDC target.
**Mains hook:** Keep political announcements, communicated targets and achieved outcomes separate.
**Back:** *"NAPCC plans, Panchamrit pledges, NDC commits, LT-LEDS long-hauls."*

### Flashcard ENV-3127 — NAPCC (2008): eight original National Missions
**Front:** When was NAPCC launched and what are its eight original missions?
**Definition (learn):** The National Action Plan on Climate Change, launched in 2008, is India's foundational domestic climate strategy with eight national missions spanning mitigation and adaptation.
**Breakdown:**
- Solar.
- Enhanced Energy Efficiency.
- Sustainable Habitat.
- Water.
- Sustaining the Himalayan Ecosystem.
- Green India.
- Sustainable Agriculture.
- Strategic Knowledge for Climate Change.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Launched 2008 (✅ Wikipedia: Climate change in India); eight missions (✅ owner 20 basic §6).
**Links:** ENV-3128, ENV-3129, ENV-3131
**Trap:** The 2008 launch count is eight; the nine-mission description is a later government framing (ENV-3129).
**Mains hook:** Co-benefits design links adaptation and development rather than emissions alone.
**Back:** *"Eight missions in 2008: sun, energy, habitat, water, Himalaya, green, farms, knowledge."*

### Flashcard ENV-3128 — NAPCC missions and nodal institutions
**Front:** Which institution is nodal for each NAPCC mission?
**Definition (learn):** Each mission has a nodal ministry or department as listed by the owner.
**Breakdown:**
- National Solar Mission: MNRE.
- Enhanced Energy Efficiency: Ministry of Power; BEE administers PAT.
- Sustainable Habitat: MoHUA.
- Water: Department of Water Resources, Ministry of Jal Shakti.
- Himalayan Ecosystem: DST.
- Green India: MoEFCC.
- Sustainable Agriculture: Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare.
- Strategic Knowledge: DST.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic §4; ⚠️ institutional names not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3127, ENV-3129, ENV-3027
**Trap:** Green India sits with MoEFCC, not the Ministry of Agriculture.
**Mains hook:** Multi-ministry coordination is a structural implementation challenge (ENV-3149).
**Back:** *"Solar to MNRE, energy to Power, habitat to MoHUA, water to Jal Shakti, green to MoEFCC."*

### Flashcard ENV-3129 — The ninth mission: health (eight originally, nine as described now)
**Front:** How many NAPCC missions does the Government now describe, and what is the added one?
**Definition (learn):** NAPCC launched with eight missions. The Economic Survey 2025-26 describes nine, the ninth being the National Programme on Climate Change and Human Health under the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare.
**Breakdown:**
- Original: eight (2008).
- Current description: nine (adds health).
- Answer rule: state "eight originally; nine as currently described".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic §2, §4 cited to Economic Survey 2025-26; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3127, ENV-3128
**Trap:** A bare mission count with no reference period (✅ owner 20 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Health co-benefits strengthen the development-first argument.
**Back:** *"Eight in 2008, nine today with health."*

### Flashcard ENV-3130 — Mission LiFE as part of India's NDC
**Front:** What is Mission LiFE and what status does the Economic Survey give it?
**Definition (learn):** Mission LiFE (Lifestyle for Environment) links individual and community consumption to climate action. The Economic Survey 2025-26 says "LiFE is an integral part of India's NDC".
**Breakdown:**
- Nature: behaviour change.
- Status: formal part of the NDC (per Survey).
- Contrast: not merely a campaign.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic §2, §6; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3136, ENV-3148
**Trap:** "LiFE is a purely domestic campaign outside the NDC" is wrong per the Government's statement (✅ owner 20 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Lifestyle-based mitigation links individual choices to international commitments.
**Back:** *"LiFE is in the NDC, not just on posters."*

### Flashcard ENV-3131 — State Action Plans on Climate Change and the adaptation shift
**Front:** How have revised SAPCCs changed in emphasis?
**Definition (learn):** SAPCCs translate NAPCC objectives to states. The revised versions shift from mitigation emphasis toward adaptation-led development, with greater weight on district-level risk and sectoral vulnerability.
**Breakdown:**
- Earlier: mitigation emphasis.
- Revised: adaptation-led, contextual, district-level.
- Source: Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10 (✅ owner 20 basic §2; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ENV-3127, ENV-3144
**Trap:** Treating SAPCCs as federal law; they are state plans.
**Mains hook:** Federal delivery of climate adaptation runs through states.
**Back:** *"State plans turned from cutting to coping."*

### Flashcard ENV-3132 — Panchamrit at COP26 (Glasgow, 2021): the five pledges
**Front:** List the five Panchamrit pledges announced at COP26.
**Definition (learn):** Panchamrit is the five-point set of commitments announced by India at COP26 (Glasgow, 2021).
**Breakdown:**
- 500 GW non-fossil capacity by 2030.
- 50% of energy requirements from renewables by 2030.
- Reduce projected carbon emissions by one billion tonnes from now to 2030.
- Reduce carbon intensity of the economy by more than 45% by 2030.
- Net zero by 2070.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five pledges, 2021 (✅ Wikipedia: Climate change in India, which lists them; ✅ owner 20 basic §6). Wikipedia paraphrases the intensity pledge as "45%"; owner says "more than 45%". Use the owner's exact wording.
**Links:** ENV-3133, ENV-3137, ENV-3112
**Trap:** Panchamrit is a political announcement, not automatically an NDC entry.
**Mains hook:** Use the Panchamrit-to-NDC progression to argue for credibility through formal communication.
**Back:** *"Five-hundred, fifty percent, one billion, forty-five plus, zero by seventy."*

### Flashcard ENV-3133 — Panchamrit's mixed target types
**Front:** Classify each Panchamrit pledge as absolute, share, intensity or long-term.
**Definition (learn):** The five pledges use different metric types, so they must not be conflated.
**Breakdown:**
- Absolute capacity: 500 GW non-fossil capacity.
- Share: 50% of energy requirements from renewables.
- Absolute emissions reduction: one billion tonnes of projected emissions.
- Intensity: more than 45% carbon-intensity reduction (emissions per unit GDP).
- Long-term: net zero by 2070.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 advanced §3, §7).
**Links:** ENV-3132, ENV-3150, ENV-3136
**Trap:** Reading the intensity target as an absolute emissions cut (ENV-3150).
**Mains hook:** Metric discipline is what distinguishes an expert answer.
**Back:** *"Capacity, share, tonnes, intensity, long-term: five different metrics."*

### Flashcard ENV-3134 — Net zero by 2070 and the 2050 trap
**Front:** What is India's net-zero target year, and what is the trap?
**Definition (learn):** India's pledged net-zero target year is 2070 (Panchamrit, COP26). It was the first publicly announced economy-wide net-zero year and was not a separate quantified entry in the 2022 NDC.
**Breakdown:**
- Target: net zero by 2070.
- Source: Panchamrit announcement (✅ Wikipedia confirms 2070 and first carbon-neutrality date).
- Not: 2050, which is a different country-group reference point.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2070 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 20 basic §6).
**Links:** ENV-3132, ENV-3138
**Trap:** "India's net-zero year is 2050" is wrong (✅ owner 20 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Net zero is a long horizon, not a near-term statutory cap.
**Back:** *"Seventy, not fifty."*

### Flashcard ENV-3135 — India's first NDC (2015): intensity, non-fossil capacity and sink
**Front:** What did India's first NDC (2015 INDC) commit to?
**Definition (learn):** India's first Paris contribution, submitted in 2015, had three components.
**Breakdown:**
- Intensity: 33–35% reduction in emissions intensity of GDP by 2030 (from 2005 level per owner; ✅ Wikipedia gives 33–35% by 2030).
- Non-fossil capacity: about 40% cumulative installed electric-power capacity by 2030, with technology and low-cost finance (✅ Wikipedia).
- Sink: additional 2.5–3 billion tCO₂e forest and tree sink by 2030 (⚠️ owner-only; not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ENV-3136, ENV-3126
**Trap:** Original 2015 targets are not the 2022 update's numbers.
**Mains hook:** The first NDC is the baseline for judging later strengthening.
**Back:** *"Thirty-three to thirty-five, forty percent, two-and-a-half to three."*

### Flashcard ENV-3136 — Updated first NDC (August 2022): 45% and about 50%
**Front:** What quantified 2030 targets did the 2022 updated NDC formally communicate?
**Definition (learn):** The updated first NDC (August 2022) formally quantified two Panchamrit elements for 2030.
**Breakdown:**
- Emissions intensity of GDP: 45% reduction by 2030 from the 2005 level (✅ owner 20 basic; ✅ Wikipedia gives 45% by 2030).
- Cumulative non-fossil installed electric-power capacity: about 50% by 2030, with technology transfer and low-cost international finance including from the Green Climate Fund (✅ owner 20 basic §6).
- Forest/tree-cover sink retained; LiFE updated.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** August 2022 (✅ owner 20 advanced §14 URL dated 2022-08).
**Links:** ENV-3135, ENV-3137, ENV-3140
**Trap:** Treating the 2022 update as the same as the 2031–35 NDC.
**Mains hook:** Two quantified 2030 targets are the formal test of Panchamrit's translation.
**Back:** *"Forty-five by 2030, half of capacity with finance attached."*

### Flashcard ENV-3137 — Trap: only two of the five Panchamrit pledges are quantified NDC targets
**Front:** Which Panchamrit pledges became quantified NDC targets in 2022?
**Definition (learn):** Only two: the 45% emissions-intensity cut by 2030 from 2005 and about 50% non-fossil cumulative installed capacity by 2030. The 500 GW, one-billion-tonne and net-zero 2070 pledges remain announcements.
**Breakdown:**
- Quantified in NDC 2022: 45% intensity; ~50% capacity.
- Announced only: 500 GW; 1 billion tonnes; 2070 net zero; 50% energy requirements.
- Test: check the NDC text.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic §6–7).
**Links:** ENV-3132, ENV-3136
**Trap:** "All five Panchamrit pledges are quantified NDC targets" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Credibility comes from formal communication, not from announcement.
**Back:** *"Two in the NDC, three in the speech."*

### Flashcard ENV-3138 — LT-LEDS (2022): seven strategic transitions
**Front:** Name the seven LT-LEDS strategic transitions.
**Definition (learn):** The Long-Term Low-Emission Development Strategy, submitted to the UNFCCC in 2022, sets a sector-by-sector pathway toward net zero by 2070.
**Breakdown:**
- Electricity systems.
- Integrated low-carbon transport.
- Urban design, buildings and sustainable urbanisation.
- Economy-wide decoupling and low-emission industry.
- CO₂ removal and engineering solutions.
- Forest and vegetative cover.
- Economic and financial aspects.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 submission (✅ owner 20 basic §3, §6; ✅ owner 20 advanced §2; MoEFCC document cited).
**Links:** ENV-3139, ENV-3134, ENV-3126
**Trap:** Transitions are strategic directions, not binding sectoral budgets (ENV-3139).
**Mains hook:** Walk through the seven transitions and their enablers in a 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Power, transport, cities, industry, removal, forests, finance."*

### Flashcard ENV-3139 — Trap: LT-LEDS is not a binding law or the NDC
**Front:** Why is LT-LEDS neither the NDC nor a binding law?
**Definition (learn):** LT-LEDS is a long-term strategic pathway document submitted separately from the shorter-term NDC. It contains illustrative scenarios, not enforceable sector sub-targets or penalties.
**Breakdown:**
- LT-LEDS: long horizon (to 2070), strategic.
- NDC: formal Paris contribution with a dated target.
- Binding: neither carries statutory penalties.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic §7; ✅ owner 20 advanced §5, §8).
**Links:** ENV-3138, ENV-3126
**Trap:** Treating LT-LEDS as a legally enforceable carbon budget (✅ owner 20 advanced §8).
**Mains hook:** Pair the strategy with the medium-term NDC and avoid inventing sector budgets.
**Back:** *"LT-LEDS plans, NDC pledges, neither penalises."*

### Flashcard ENV-3140 — NDC for 2031–2035 (submitted April 2026)
**Front:** What did India's April 2026 NDC communicate for 2035?
**Definition (learn):** India's NDC for 2031–2035, submitted in April 2026, communicates three 2035 commitments, relative to 2005 for the first two.
**Breakdown:**
- 47% reduction in GDP emissions intensity by 2035 from 2005.
- About 60% cumulative electric-power installed capacity from non-fossil fuel-based resources by 2035, with help from technology transfer and low-cost international finance.
- 3.5–4.0 billion tCO₂e forest and tree-cover sink by 2035, relative to 2005.
- Economy-wide intensity approach; no sector-specific obligations (including agriculture).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner-only, UNFCCC PDF cited in owner 20; not fetched in this build).
**Links:** ENV-3141, ENV-3136, ENV-3115
**Trap:** These are 2035 targets; the 2030 targets remain the 2022 update's (ENV-3141).
**Mains hook:** The 2035 NDC is the next formal test, but its finance condition must be preserved in any summary.
**Back:** *"Forty-seven, about sixty, three-point-five to four: all by 2035."*

### Flashcard ENV-3141 — Trap: 2030 versus 2035 and the finance condition
**Front:** Which target years and conditions must not be mixed in the 2031–35 NDC?
**Definition (learn):** The 47%, about 60% and 3.5–4.0 billion tCO₂e commitments are 2035 targets, not new 2030 targets. The about-60% capacity commitment carries technology-transfer and low-cost-finance wording, so it is not unconditional.
**Breakdown:**
- 2030 targets: from the updated first NDC (2022).
- 2035 targets: from the 2031–35 NDC (April 2026).
- Condition: technology transfer and low-cost international finance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic §7; ✅ owner 20 advanced §3, §8).
**Links:** ENV-3140, ENV-3136, ENV-3137
**Trap:** "The approximately 60% commitment is unconditional" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Preserve the conditionality when judging the ambition of a target.
**Back:** *"2035 is not 2030, and finance is part of the promise."*

### Flashcard ENV-3142 — Installed capacity versus generation versus energy requirements
**Front:** Distinguish installed-capacity share, generation share and share of energy requirements.
**Definition (learn):** India's non-fossil share of installed power capacity was 51.93% at end-December 2025 (Ministry of Power, cited by the Survey), and 52.57% on 28 February 2026 (NDC status). This is capacity, not generation, and not the energy-requirements pledge.
**Breakdown:**
- Installed capacity: 51.93% (end-Dec 2025); 52.57% (28 Feb 2026, NDC).
- Generation share: lower, because solar and wind have lower capacity utilisation.
- Energy requirements: the Panchamrit 50% formulation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner-only, Economic Survey and NDC; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3132, ENV-3133, ENV-3143
**Trap:** "Half of India's electricity is non-fossil" is wrong; the figure is capacity (✅ owner 20 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Use as-on dates; capacity is not outcome.
**Back:** *"Capacity is not current: 51.93 in December, 52.57 in February."*

### Flashcard ENV-3143 — Renewable additions and India's global rank (2025–26)
**Front:** How much renewable capacity was added in 2025–26 to 31 December 2025, and where does India rank?
**Definition (learn):** India added 38.61 GW of renewable capacity in 2025–26 up to 31 December 2025: solar 30.16 GW, wind 4.47 GW, hydro 3.24 GW, bio-power 0.03 GW. IRENA's Renewable Energy Statistics 2025 ranks India fourth globally in installed renewable capacity, behind China, the USA and Brazil.
**Breakdown:**
- Total: 38.61 GW.
- Solar: 30.16; wind: 4.47; hydro: 3.24; bio: 0.03 (GW).
- Rank: fourth (IRENA, as cited).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner-only; Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10, not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3142, ENV-3132
**Trap:** Mixing "fourth" with other rankings; the basis is installed renewable capacity.
**Mains hook:** Capacity growth is the main vehicle for the 500 GW pledge.
**Back:** *"Thirty-eight point six one, solar leads, fourth in the world."*

### Flashcard ENV-3144 — Adaptation: 5.6% of GDP in FY22 and the ecosystem-led frame
**Front:** What adaptation-expenditure figure does India report, and what policy direction goes with it?
**Definition (learn):** India's adaptation-relevant expenditure reached 5.6% of GDP in FY22, reported in the Initial Adaptation Communication within the Third National Communication (2023). The Government's direction is "ecosystem-led, development-integrated climate resilience", operationalised through MISHTI (mangroves), the National Coastal Mission, NPCA (aquatic ecosystems) and adaptation-led SAPCCs.
**Breakdown:**
- Figure: 5.6% of GDP in FY22.
- Source: Third National Communication (2023), Initial Adaptation Communication.
- Tools: MISHTI, National Coastal Mission, NPCA, SAPCCs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner-only; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3131, ENV-3117, ENV-3148
**Trap:** Presenting adaptation as a minor add-on to mitigation; the owner treats it as the larger fiscal story (✅ owner 20 advanced §9).
**Mains hook:** Adaptation spend shows that a developing country already pays for a problem it did not cause (CBDR-RC in fiscal form).
**Back:** *"Five-point-six of GDP, mangroves and coasts first."*

### Flashcard ENV-3145 — BUR-4 versus NDC versus LT-LEDS
**Front:** What is India's Fourth Biennial Update Report, and how does it differ from the NDC and LT-LEDS?
**Definition (learn):** The Fourth Biennial Update Report (BUR-4) is a backward-looking transparency and inventory-and-mitigation report submitted to the UNFCCC (PIB PRID 2089589, cited by owner). It is neither an NDC (a forward pledge) nor LT-LEDS (a long-term strategy).
**Breakdown:**
- BUR-4: reporting, past inventory.
- NDC: forward nationally determined pledge.
- LT-LEDS: long-horizon pathway.
- Rule: quote BUR figures only with their reference year.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner-only; PIB link not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-3126, ENV-3146, ENV-3147
**Trap:** Treating a biennial update as a new target.
**Mains hook:** Transparency reports make claims checkable; they do not set ambition.
**Back:** *"BUR reports the past; NDC pledges the future; LT-LEDS charts the long road."*

### Flashcard ENV-3146 — PYQ 2025 GS-III Q18: Paris commitments, COP26 and updated NDC (15 marks)
**Front:** What three-block answer does the 2025 domestic-policy question require?
**Definition (learn):** The 2025 GS-III Q18 (15 marks, 250 words) asks for review of India's Paris commitments, COP26 and the updated NDC.
**Breakdown:**
- Block 1: first NDC (2015) targets.
- Block 2: COP26 Panchamrit (2021).
- Block 3: 2022 updated NDC (45% intensity, ~50% capacity by 2030).
- Evidence: 51.93% non-fossil installed capacity (Dec 2025).
- Close: credible but unfinished, with dated evidence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic PYQ block 2024–2025, §13.1).
**Links:** ENV-3135, ENV-3136, ENV-3142, ENV-3117
**Trap:** Do not conflate the three instruments (ENV-3126).
**Mains hook:** Separate announced, submitted and achieved.
**Back:** *"First NDC, Panchamrit, updated NDC, evidence, verdict."*

### Flashcard ENV-3147 — PYQ 2026 Prelims GS-I Q21: LT-LEDS, BUR-4, net zero and resilience
**Front:** What does the 2026 Prelims item on LT-LEDS and BUR-4 test, and what is its key status?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 Prelims GS-I Q21 covers India's LT-LEDS, BUR-4, the net-zero pathway and climate resilience. The owner records a provisional 2026 Set-A key, with no answer letter recorded or inferred.
**Breakdown:**
- Topics: LT-LEDS, BUR-4, net-zero pathway, climate resilience.
- Key status: provisional.
- Route: cards ENV-3138, ENV-3145, ENV-3134.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic PYQ block 2026).
**Links:** ENV-3138, ENV-3145, ENV-3134
**Trap:** Answering from a provisional key; check against the final key.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish reporting, pledge and strategy in any objective statement.
**Back:** *"Long strategy, past report, net zero, resilient: check the provisional key."*

### Flashcard ENV-3148 — Co-benefits versus carbon-budget logic in India's framing
**Front:** Why does India frame climate policy around development co-benefits rather than a carbon-budget allocation?
**Definition (learn):** India's architecture (NAPCC through LT-LEDS) is deliberately framed around co-benefits: energy security, air quality, rural livelihoods and water security. Mitigation is pursued as a co-benefit of development choices, not as an independent constraint on growth.
**Breakdown:**
- Priority: poverty eradication and development.
- Mitigation: co-benefit of solar for energy access and security.
- Consequence: intensity targets rather than absolute caps.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 advanced §1; ⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** ENV-3127, ENV-3130, ENV-3150
**Trap:** Judging India's targets by an absolute developed-country benchmark alone.
**Mains hook:** Use this as the interpretive lens for ambition and equity (✅ owner 20 advanced §10).
**Back:** *"Development first, carbon as a co-benefit."*

### Flashcard ENV-3149 — Coordination challenge and the financing gap
**Front:** What two implementation constraints does the owner identify for India's climate policy?
**Definition (learn):** Implementation spans many central ministries (MNRE, Ministry of Power, Heavy Industries, MoEFCC) and states, which control distribution, land and much clearance. The investment needed for 2030 targets and the 2070 pathway is large, with significant reliance on international finance and private capital.
**Breakdown:**
- Coordination: multi-ministry plus states.
- Finance: large requirement; reliance on international and private money.
- Link: domestic credibility depends on the finance debate (ENV-3109).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner analysis; cost estimates vary by study).
**Links:** ENV-3128, ENV-3109, ENV-3016
**Trap:** Quoting a single investment figure without its source and assumptions.
**Mains hook:** Multi-actor coordination and finance access decide delivery.
**Back:** *"Many ministries, states and a finance gap."*

### Flashcard ENV-3150 — Trap: intensity is not absolute emissions
**Front:** Can intensity targets reduce emissions while absolute emissions still rise?
**Definition (learn):** Yes. An intensity target measures emissions per unit of GDP. Absolute emissions can rise with economic growth even when intensity falls. Panchamrit's more-than-45% and the NDC's 45% are both intensity metrics.
**Breakdown:**
- Intensity: emissions per GDP.
- Absolute: total emissions.
- Both can move in different directions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 20 basic §7; ✅ owner 20 advanced §3).
**Links:** ENV-3133, ENV-3148, ENV-3132
**Trap:** "The carbon-intensity announcement means absolute national emissions fall" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Name the metric before judging ambition.
**Back:** *"Intensity per rupee of GDP; absolute tonnes can still rise."*

## ENVIRONMENT AND ECOLOGY — Part 4: Carbon Markets, Conventions, Desertification, Marine and Renewable Energy

### GROUP — Carbon Markets CCUS and Direct Air Capture

### Flashcard ENV-4001 — Carbon-mitigation toolkit: markets, CCUS and DAC are distinct
**Front:** Why are carbon markets, point-source CCUS and Direct Air Capture (DAC) complementary but not interchangeable?
**Definition (learn):** Carbon markets create a price signal and accounting units; point-source CCUS separates CO₂ before it leaves a flue and usually avoids a new emission; DAC separates CO₂ from ambient air. Only DAC plus durable storage and lifecycle accounting becomes a carbon-dioxide-removal (CDR) pathway.
**Breakdown:**
- Carbon market → price incentive and tradable units (compliance or voluntary)
- Point-source CCUS → capture → transport → use or storage; avoidance/reduction
- DAC → separation from dilute air; separation alone is not removal
- DACCS → DAC + transport + durable (normally geological) storage
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** CDR = intentional atmospheric CO₂ removal + durable storage + lifecycle accounting (✅ owner 21; definition consistent with IPCC AR6 WGIII CDR factsheet cited in owner 21).
**Links:** ENV-4002, ENV-4005, ENV-4006, ENV-4007; ENV-4001 is the core concept card for owner 21.
**Trap:** Treating CCUS, DAC and CDR as one technology. Each answers a different question: where the CO₂ is taken from, whether the climate outcome is avoidance or removal, and where it ends up.
**Mains hook:** Carbon markets, CCUS and CDR are complementary instruments serving price/accounting, emissions-avoidance and atmosphere-to-reservoir functions. Conclusion: direct reduction first; capture reserved for residual/process emissions; every tonne verified.
**Back:** *"Market prices it, CCUS catches it at the chimney, DAC fishes it from air, and only durable storage makes it removal."*

### Flashcard ENV-4002 — Compliance vs voluntary carbon market
**Front:** Distinguish compliance and voluntary carbon markets.
**Definition (learn):** A compliance market is created by law: covered entities must surrender eligible units against a quantified obligation. A voluntary market lets entities buy credits (often from afforestation or renewable-energy projects) to offset emissions without a regulatory mandate.
**Breakdown:**
- Compliance → mandate set by law; cap-and-trade surrenders follow covered emissions; baseline-and-credit certificates address performance versus a target
- Voluntary → buyer's own choice; no regulatory obligation
- India's CCTS has both: a compliance leg and an offset leg (ENV-4008)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 21, Section 2 definitions)
**Links:** ENV-4008, ENV-4009, ENV-4015
**Trap:** Assuming all government-backed markets are cap-and-trade. A baseline-and-credit compliance system (India's CCTS intensity leg) is not an absolute cap.
**Mains hook:** Mandatory compliance markets create demand; voluntary markets can add scale, but their climate value depends on additionality and MRV (ENV-4021).
**Back:** *"Compliance is ordered by law; voluntary is chosen by the buyer."*

### Flashcard ENV-4003 — CCUS: point-source capture chain
**Front:** What is CCUS and where is it applied?
**Definition (learn):** Carbon Capture, Utilisation and Storage separates CO₂ at a point source (power plant, cement, steel, fertiliser), conditions and transports it, then uses it or places it in storage.
**Breakdown:**
- Capture at a concentrated source (flue gas)
- Conditioning and transport
- Utilisation (fuel, product, enhanced oil recovery) or geological storage
- Standard fossil/process-source CCUS → avoidance or reduction of a new emission
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Most relevant to residual process emissions in cement, steel and fertiliser (✅ owner 21, Section 3 and 13.1).
**Links:** ENV-4004, ENV-4007, ENV-4023 (EOR), ENV-4025 (PYQ)
**Trap:** Utilisation is not automatically durable storage. CO₂ used in short-lived products can return to the atmosphere.
**Mains hook:** CCUS is most defensible for hard-to-abate process emissions after efficiency and electrification, not as a licence to defer direct mitigation.
**Back:** *"Catch at the source, carry it, then use or bury it; use alone is not burial."*

### Flashcard ENV-4004 — DAC: separation from dilute ambient air
**Front:** What is Direct Air Capture and why is it more energy-intensive than point-source CCUS?
**Definition (learn):** DAC is a separation process that captures CO₂ directly from ambient air, where CO₂ is dilute (a much lower concentration than industrial flue gas).
**Breakdown:**
- Ambient air separation → regeneration and compression → transport → use or storage → monitoring and liability → lifecycle net-removal calculation (✅ owner 21, Advanced Section 4 chain)
- High air-handling volume and solvent/sorbent regeneration need significant heat and electricity
- Net removal depends on the carbon intensity and additionality of the energy supply
