The 17 June 2026 PIB release reported 21.76 million hectares under restoration efforts and released the Second Bonn Challenge Progress Report covering 2011–2020.
**Breakdown:**
- Ambition → 26 Mha by 2030 (raised from 21 Mha)
- Reported 17 June 2026 → 21.76 Mha under restoration efforts (programme reporting)
- Second Bonn Challenge Progress Report → 2011–2020 period
- Target, not proof of LDN achievement
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 26 Mha by 2030; 21.76 Mha (17 June 2026); Bonn Challenge 2011–2020 (✅ owner 23; PIB release cited, not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-4066, ENV-4067, ENV-4076
**Trap:** Adding the ISRO 2018–19 atlas area to the PIB 2026 restoration area or to scheme-specific areas; the four records are different periods and meanings.
**Mains hook:** Judge restoration by ecological function, not hectares reported.
**Back:** *"Twenty-six by 2030, twenty-one-point-seven-six reported: area is not recovery."*

### Flashcard ENV-4069 — UNCCD COP17 (Ulaanbaatar 2026): decisions 6, 12, 16 and 20
**Front:** What did UNCCD COP17 decide on the post-2030 framework, agricultural land, drought and rangelands?
**Definition (learn):** COP17 met in Ulaanbaatar, Mongolia, 17–28 August 2026 under the theme "Restoring Land. Restoring Hope." Decision 6/COP.17 renewed the working group for a post-2030 framework for COP18; 12/COP.17 encouraged avoid-reduce-reverse on agricultural lands and soils; 16/COP.17 carried drought discussions to COP18; 20/COP.17 recognised pastoral stewardship and tenure security.
**Breakdown:**
- Venue and dates → Ulaanbaatar, Mongolia, 17–28 August 2026
- Decision 6 → post-2030 framework working group (2026–2028)
- Decision 12 → avoid-reduce-reverse for agriculture
- Decision 16 → drought (see ENV-4070)
- Decision 20 → pastoralism, commons, tenure, rangelands
- Use the final action report ICCD/COP(17)/14/Add.1 for adopted decisions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (📰 owner 23, Section 8 and Section 9; status as of 5 October 2026)
**Links:** ENV-4063, ENV-4070, ENV-4071
**Trap:** Citing an early L-document draft as an adopted decision. Use the final action report.
**Mains hook:** COP17 confirms LDN is prevention-first; rangeland governance links ecology to rights and institutions.
**Back:** *"Six renews the future, twelve prevents, sixteen postpones drought, twenty honours herders."*

### Flashcard ENV-4070 — Drought at COP17: no binding global drought protocol
**Front:** What did COP17 decide on drought, and did it adopt a binding protocol?
**Definition (learn):** Decision 16/COP.17 continued drought negotiations to COP18, made drought a stand-alone recurring COP agenda item from COP18 onward, and did not adopt a binding global drought protocol.
**Breakdown:**
- Negotiation carried to COP18
- Stand-alone agenda item from COP18 and each ordinary COP thereafter
- No binding drought protocol adopted
- Pairing tools: UNCCD Drought Initiative, Drought Toolbox (monitoring and early warning; vulnerability and risk assessment; risk mitigation), National Drought Plans
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 23, decision 16/COP.17)
**Links:** ENV-4069, ENV-4057
**Trap:** Treating the International Drought Resilience Alliance or Riyadh partnership as a binding drought protocol. They are channels, not protocols.
**Mains hook:** Proactive drought planning is a channel for early warning; a binding protocol is still under negotiation.
**Back:** *"COP17 kept drought on the table, did not sign a drought protocol."*

### Flashcard ENV-4071 — Rangelands and pastoralists: IYRP 2026 and degradation share
**Front:** Why does 2026 matter for rangelands, and what share of land is degraded according to UNCCD?
**Definition (learn):** 2026 is the UN International Year of Rangelands and Pastoralists (IYRP). Rangelands cover around half the Earth's surface and support food systems and livelihoods. UNCCD frames up to 40% of the world's land as already degraded.
**Breakdown:**
- IYRP 2026 → rangelands and pastoralists
- Rangelands → about half of Earth's surface
- UNCCD framing → up to 40% of land degraded
- Decision 20/COP.17 → pastoral stewardship, mobility, commons, tenure, inclusive governance
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** IYRP 2026; around half of Earth's surface; up to 40% degraded (✅ owner 23, FAO IYRP page cited; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-4069, ENV-4060
**Trap:** Treating rangeland degradation as only a plantation problem. Commons governance and pastoral mobility are part of the answer.
**Mains hook:** Use decision 20 to challenge plantation-only restoration and link ecology with rights.
**Back:** *"Half the Earth is range, forty percent is degraded, herders are stewards."*

### Flashcard ENV-4072 — Great Green Wall: AU-led, not a UNCCD treaty obligation
**Front:** What is the Great Green Wall and what are its 2030 ambitions?
**Definition (learn):** An African Union-led landscape-restoration and rural-development initiative launched in 2007. It is not a continuous belt of trees and not a UNCCD treaty obligation.
**Breakdown:**
- Launched 2007, AU-led
- Original spine: 11 founding countries, 156 million ha, Senegal to Djibouti (✅ owner 23)
- UNCCD describes 22 African countries
- 2024 AU strategy: continent-wide, Sahel foundation, Southern Great Green Wall expansion
- 2030 ambition: 100 Mha restored, 250 million tonnes of carbon sequestered, 10 million green jobs
- Accelerator review (as of 31 December 2025): 1.66 Mha restored, 4.6 million jobs, 24 million tonnes CO₂e (⚠️ from fewer than 90 of 389 projects with harmonised data)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (✅ owner 23; the 2026 Accelerator figures are ⚠️ owner-only and partial).
**Links:** ENV-4058, ENV-4069, ENV-4071
**Trap:** Combining the 2026 Accelerator figures with older headline estimates as if methodologies matched.
**Mains hook:** Headline ambitions are not progress; partial data requires national validation.
**Back:** *"A wall of ambition, not a wall of trees: 100 Mha by 2030 targets, partial progress."*

### Flashcard ENV-4073 — PYQ routes for desertification and land degradation
**Front:** What are the PYQ routes for this topic?
**Definition (learn):** The owner records a 2020 GS-I Q5 (10 marks, 150 words) on desertification as a process without climatic boundaries; a 2018 Prelims GS-I Q82 item on soil organic matter and salinisation (answer key held locally but omitted from the owner; no answer inferred); and the 2024 Mains Essay "Forests precede civilizations and deserts follow them" as support.
**Breakdown:**
- 2020 GS-I Q5 → desertification as a process; justify with examples
- 2018 Prelims GS-I Q82 → soil organic matter, sulphur cycle, salinisation
- 2024 Essay → forests, deserts and civilisations (owner 23, Essay primary owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 23 PYQ integration blocks)
**Links:** ENV-4055, ENV-4057, ENV-4074
**Trap:** Answering the 2018 Prelims item without the key. Use the statement-level distinction and check the locally held key.
**Mains hook:** Correct the metaphor: desertification is degradation within drylands, not an advancing sand front; civilisational land exhaustion is the essay's real claim.
**Back:** *"Desertification is not a moving sand dune: it is a process inside drylands."*

### Flashcard ENV-4074 — LDN and UNCCD Prelims traps
**Front:** Which LDN and UNCCD statements are most often tested as false?
**Definition (learn):** LDN is not a tradable-offset permission to degrade; national LDN targets are voluntary, not a uniform treaty quota; the UNCCD is a binding treaty whose LDN targets are set nationally; the UNCCD is one of three Rio Conventions.
**Breakdown:**
- "LDN permits degradation first." → False; avoid first
- "LDN targets are binding uniform hectare quotas." → False; voluntary, nationally set
- "UNCCD is not a Rio Convention." → False; it is one of three
- "India has never hosted a major UNCCD event." → False; COP14 in New Delhi, 2019
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 23, Basic Section 7)
**Links:** ENV-4060, ENV-4061, ENV-4059
**Trap:** Mixing the treaty's binding status with the voluntary nature of national targets.
**Mains hook:** LDN's flexibility is its strength and its risk; the safeguards decide which.
**Back:** *"Avoid first, voluntary targets, binding treaty, three Rio children, India hosted COP14."*

### Flashcard ENV-4075 — Desertification vs drought-driven vegetation: multi-year evidence
**Front:** Why does a single satellite image not establish desertification?
**Definition (learn):** Vegetation-index data alone cannot distinguish a temporary drought-driven vegetation dip from a longer-term degradation trend. Multi-year trend analysis, climate context and field ground-truthing (soil health, water table, land use) are needed.
**Breakdown:**
- Remote sensing → vegetation cover and land productivity trends
- Ground-truthing → soil, water table, socio-economic land use
- Recoverability varies by process; desertification is not inherently irreversible
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner 23, Advanced Section 3, analytical)
**Links:** ENV-4057, ENV-4062, ENV-4067
**Trap:** "Satellite vegetation data alone can definitively distinguish desertification from a single-year drought." It cannot.
**Mains hook:** Multi-method monitoring is the defensible way to call a land-degradation trend.
**Back:** *"One dry year is weather; a dry decade with soil loss is a trend."*

### Flashcard ENV-4076 — Causes of land degradation: multi-causal, not single-cause
**Front:** What drivers can produce desertification, and why must causation be tested site by site?
**Definition (learn):** Desertification may result from climatic variation, human activities, or their interaction. Rainfall variability, overgrazing, deforestation, unsustainable agriculture and water use are relevant drivers. Article 1 does not require a dual-cause test in every instance, but site-specific science is needed to attribute a particular case.
**Breakdown:**
- Climatic variation → rainfall variability
- Human activities → overgrazing, deforestation, unsustainable agriculture and water use
- Interaction → both may act together
- Attribution → site-specific study; no single uniform mechanism nationally
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 23, Basic Section 3.1 and Section 2; ⚠️ attribution caveat from advanced Section 5)
**Links:** ENV-4055, ENV-4065, ENV-4075
**Trap:** Insisting that desertification requires both a climatic and a human cause in every case.
**Mains hook:** Process-matched responses beat a tree-only prescription.
**Back:** *"Climate can dry it, people can push it, and both can team up: diagnose the site."*

### Flashcard ENV-4077 — UNCCD's lower visibility and India's COP14 role (⚠️)
**Front:** Why has the UNCCD historically received less attention and finance than the UNFCCC or CBD?
**Definition (learn):** Analytical observation (⚠️): land degradation is slow-onset and diffuse, and sometimes framed as a regional, developing-country agricultural issue. India's COP14 hosting sought to raise its profile and link it to food security, migration and climate resilience.
**Breakdown:**
- Slow-onset, diffuse problem → less visible than temperature or extinction metrics
- Framed as regional/agricultural → lower priority
- India's COP14 (New Delhi, 2019) → profile-raising effort
- Profile must be matched by finance to be credible
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner 23 Advanced Section 1; COP14 New Delhi 2019 (✅ owner 23))
**Links:** ENV-4059, ENV-4068, ENV-4069
**Trap:** "The UNCCD receives comparable attention and finance to the UNFCCC." It has historically had less.
**Mains hook:** A 10-mark answer should explain the visibility gap and then argue that profile must be matched by finance.
**Back:** *"Slow, diffuse, regional-looking: the UNCCD's visibility gap, COP14 tried to close it."*

### GROUP — Coastal and Marine Ecology CRZ Blue Economy

### Flashcard ENV-4078 — Coastal governance: CRZ as a precondition, Blue Economy as the growth goal
**Front:** What does CRZ compliance do, and what does the Blue Economy mean?
**Definition (learn):** Coastal Regulation Zone (CRZ) areas are regulated under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 to protect coastal ecology while allowing calibrated development. The Blue Economy is the sustainable use of ocean and coastal resources for growth, livelihoods and ecosystem health. CRZ compliance is a precondition only within the notification's mapped coastal and near-shore scope, not a licence for the EEZ or high seas.
**Breakdown:**
- CRZ → graded zonation (CRZ-I to CRZ-IV)
- Blue Economy → fisheries, shipping/ports, tourism, offshore energy, marine biotechnology
- Boundary → CRZ does not govern fisheries, shipping or maritime zones in full; those keep their own regimes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, Basic Sections 1–2)
**Links:** ENV-4079, ENV-4088, ENV-4104
**Trap:** Treating CRZ compliance as a licence for any ocean activity.
**Mains hook:** CRZ is the governance tool that tries to reconcile Blue Economy growth with coastal protection; its strength depends on enforcement.
**Back:** *"CRZ guards the coast's edge, the Blue Economy grows on the sea, and each keeps its own rules."*

### Flashcard ENV-4079 — CRZ Notification 2019: four zones
**Front:** What are the four CRZ categories under the 2019 Notification?
**Definition (learn):** The CRZ Notification, 2019 classifies India's coastline into CRZ-I (ecologically sensitive areas and the inter-tidal zone), CRZ-II (developed urban coastal areas), CRZ-III (rural or relatively undisturbed coastal areas, with a No Development Zone near the shore) and CRZ-IV (water area from the Low Tide Line to 12 nautical miles, and tidal-influenced water bodies).
**Breakdown:**
- CRZ-I → highest protection (mangroves, coral reefs, sand dunes; inter-tidal zone)
- CRZ-II → regulated urban development
- CRZ-III → NDZ buffer; IIIA and IIIB sub-categories
- CRZ-IV → aquatic zone to 12 nautical miles and tidal water bodies
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, Basic Section 1 and 6)
**Links:** ENV-4080, ENV-4081, ENV-4082, ENV-4083, ENV-4085
**Trap:** Thinking CRZ-IV is a land-based category like I to III. It covers water.
**Mains hook:** The four-zone design allocates protection by ecological sensitivity and development pressure.
**Back:** *"One sensitive, two urban, three rural with buffer, four water to twelve miles."*

### Flashcard ENV-4080 — CRZ legal history: EPA 1986 and the 1991, 2011, 2018 and 2019 notifications
**Front:** What is the legal basis of the CRZ and how did its notifications develop?
**Definition (learn):** CRZ notifications are issued under Section 3 of the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986. The first notification was issued in February 1991; later notifications came in 2011, 2018 (Cabinet clearance) and 2019.
**Breakdown:**
- Legal basis → Environment (Protection) Act, 1986, Section 3 (✅ Wikipedia)
- 1991 → first CRZ notification, February 1991 (✅ Wikipedia)
- 2011 → earlier notification replaced by the 2019 notification; froze FSI (✅ Wikipedia)
- 2018 → Cabinet cleared CRZ Notification 2018; FSI unfrozen (✅ Wikipedia)
- 2019 → Notification issued, reported 23 January 2019 (✅ Wikipedia; exact gazette date ⚠️ not verified)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed; the 2019 notification replaced the 2011 rules (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** ENV-4079, ENV-4087
**Trap:** Dating the 2019 notification to 2011 or treating the 2011 notification as current.
**Mains hook:** The sequence of liberalisation explains the two-sided debate on development versus buffer protection.
**Back:** *"Ninety-one began it, eleven froze, eighteen unfroze, nineteen reset: EPA section 3 is the spine."*

### Flashcard ENV-4081 — CRZ-I: ecologically sensitive areas and the inter-tidal zone
**Front:** What does CRZ-I cover and what activities are allowed?
**Definition (learn):** CRZ-I covers ecologically sensitive areas (mangroves, coral reefs, sand dunes and similar) and the inter-tidal zone between the low and high tide lines. It receives the highest protection. Natural gas exploration and salt extraction are allowed (✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Ecologically sensitive areas → mangroves, coral reefs, sand dunes
- Inter-tidal zone → between low tide line and high tide line
- Highest protection, minimal permissible activity
- Central clearance (MoEFCC) for CRZ-I (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24 Basic Section 1 and 2; ✅ Wikipedia for natural gas and salt extraction)
**Links:** ENV-4079, ENV-4086, ENV-4089
**Trap:** CRZ-I as the least protected zone. It is the most protected.
**Mains hook:** CRZ-I protects ecosystem services (nursery habitat, storm buffering); it is not the zone for urban housing.
**Back:** *"Sensitive ecology, between the tides, least development, most protection."*

### Flashcard ENV-4082 — CRZ-II: developed urban coastal areas
**Front:** What is CRZ-II and how is development regulated there?
**Definition (learn):** CRZ-II covers designated urban and developed coastal areas. Regulated construction is permitted within the notification's conditions. State-level clearance applies (✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Urban and developed coastal areas
- Regulated construction permitted
- Clearance delegated to states (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24 Section 1 and 2; ✅ Wikipedia for state clearance)
**Links:** ENV-4079, ENV-4086
**Trap:** Confusing CRZ-II (urban) with CRZ-III (rural).
**Mains hook:** Urban coastal development needs sea-level-rise and storm-surge buffers, not only zoning.
**Back:** *"Two is for the city shore: regulated, not banned."*

### Flashcard ENV-4083 — CRZ-III: rural coast and No Development Zone
**Front:** What is CRZ-III and what is the No Development Zone?
**Definition (learn):** CRZ-III covers rural and relatively undisturbed coastal areas outside CRZ-I and CRZ-II. A No Development Zone (NDZ) is a buffer from the shoreline in which new construction is restricted. Wikipedia describes CRZ-III as covering rural and urban localities outside CRZ-I and II, which differs from owner 24's "rural and relatively undisturbed" wording.
**Breakdown:**
- CRZ-III → rural and relatively undisturbed coast (owner 24)
- NDZ → shoreline buffer restricting new construction
- CRZ-III has sub-categories IIIA and IIIB (ENV-4084)
- Only certain agriculture-related activities and public facilities allowed (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24; ✅ Wikipedia for permitted activities; conflict flagged ⚠️ on the wording of CRZ-III's scope)
**Links:** ENV-4079, ENV-4084
**Trap:** "Every CRZ-III coast received a 50 m NDZ in 2019." Only CRZ-IIIA (dense areas) does, under conditions.
**Mains hook:** A buffer distance is the regulatory tool that trades development against sea-level and erosion risk.
**Back:** *"Three is for the rural shore: a buffer, not a bulldozer."*

### Flashcard ENV-4084 — CRZ-IIIA and IIIB: 50 m vs 200 m NDZ
**Front:** What distinguishes CRZ-IIIA from CRZ-IIIB in the 2019 Notification?
**Definition (learn):** CRZ-IIIA is densely populated CRZ-III land where the NDZ is 50 m from the high tide line, versus 200 m under the 2011 rules. CRZ-IIIB has a lower density and keeps a 200 m NDZ. Owner 24 states that the 50 m distance applies only after approval of the revised 2019 CZMP; Wikipedia gives the density threshold of 2,161 persons per square km (Census 2011).
**Breakdown:**
- CRZ-IIIA → density above 2,161 persons/sq km (Census 2011) (✅ Wikipedia); NDZ 50 m (✅ Wikipedia; owner: only after revised CZMP approval)
- CRZ-IIIB → density below threshold; NDZ 200 m (✅ owner 24 and Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 50 m (IIIA), 200 m (IIIB), density threshold 2,161 persons/sq km (⚠️ owner does not state threshold; Wikipedia figure, not cross-checked against the notification text)
**Links:** ENV-4083, ENV-4087
**Trap:** Applying the 50 m IIIA buffer to every CRZ-III coast, or citing the 2019 notification alone as enough to activate it.
**Mains hook:** The IIIA relaxation is a calibrated development-facilitation shift, and its outcome depends on plan approval and enforcement.
**Back:** *"A is dense, fifty metres; B is sparse, two hundred metres; approval first."*

### Flashcard ENV-4085 — CRZ-IV: water area from low tide to 12 nautical miles
**Front:** What is CRZ-IV and what is permitted there?
**Definition (learn):** CRZ-IV is the aquatic area from the Low Tide Line up to the territorial limit, 12 nautical miles, together with tidal-influenced water bodies. Fishing and allied activities are permitted, and solid waste may not be discharged (✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Water area: Low Tide Line to 12 nautical miles
- Tidal-influenced water bodies
- Fishing and allied activities permitted
- No solid waste discharge (✅ Wikipedia)
- Central clearance for CRZ-IV (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 nautical miles (✅ owner 24 and Wikipedia)
**Links:** ENV-4079, ENV-4104
**Trap:** Mistaking the 12-nautical-mile CRZ-IV band for the 200-nautical-mile EEZ.
**Mains hook:** CRZ-IV shows that coastal regulation reaches into water, while the EEZ is a separate resource-rights zone.
**Back:** *"Four is water to twelve miles: fish here, dump no solid waste."*

### Flashcard ENV-4086 — CRZ institutions: MoEFCC, NCZMA and state CZMAs
**Front:** Who approves, advises and enforces CRZ?
**Definition (learn):** The MoEFCC issues and amends the notification, approves Coastal Zone Management Plans (CZMPs) and decides specified central clearances. The National Coastal Zone Management Authority (NCZMA) advises and coordinates nationally. State/UT Coastal Zone Management Authorities appraise compliance, recommend cases, decide delegated categories and monitor and enforce.
**Breakdown:**
- MoEFCC → notification, CZMP approval, central oversight, specified clearances
- NCZMA → national advice and coordination; not the routine final authority for each site
- State/UT CZMAs → appraisal, recommendation, delegated decisions, monitoring, violations
- Clearance split (✅ Wikipedia): central for CRZ-I and IV; state for II and III
- Ministry of Earth Sciences (NIOT, NCCR) → scientific inputs
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, Section 4; clearance split ✅ Wikipedia, not in owner)
**Links:** ENV-4081, ENV-4082, ENV-4087, ENV-4103
**Trap:** Treating NCZMA as the routine final authority for every site clearance.
**Mains hook:** Multi-actor governance is a central enforcement problem; the state CZMA is where many decisions are made.
**Back:** *"Centre sets the rules, NCZMA advises, states decide and watch the shore."*

### Flashcard ENV-4087 — CRZ 2011 to 2019: a two-sided liberalisation
**Front:** What did the 2019 CRZ Notification liberalise, and what are the two sides of the debate?
**Definition (learn):** The 2019 notification relaxed some 2011 norms: conditional 50 m NDZ for CRZ-IIIA after CZMP approval, a reduced tidal-water-body reach after plan transition, simplified clearance routes and more flexibility for tourism. Supporters cite development and community housing needs. Critics cite exposure to erosion, storm surge and sea-level rise, and weaker protection for inter-tidal and mangrove areas if oversight is weak.
**Breakdown:**
- Development rationale → growth, tourism, small-scale housing and livelihoods
- Ecological and disaster-risk critique → erosion, storm surge, sea-level rise, mangrove and inter-tidal exposure
- Outcome depends on state CZMP rigour
- Wikipedia adds that FSI norms were unfrozen (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24 Basic Section 3 and Advanced Section 1; ⚠️ analytical)
**Links:** ENV-4084, ENV-4086, ENV-4106
**Trap:** Calling the 2019 CRZ an unambiguous improvement or an unambiguous regression.
**Mains hook:** A balanced answer presents both rationales, then makes the verdict depend on enforcement and monitoring.
**Back:** *"Liberalised for growth, tightened by nothing: judge the 2019 CRZ by its oversight."*

### Flashcard ENV-4088 — Blue Economy: sustainable ocean-resource growth
**Front:** What does the Blue Economy cover and what principle governs it?
**Definition (learn):** The sustainable use of ocean and coastal resources for economic growth, improved livelihoods and ocean-ecosystem health. Sectors include fisheries and aquaculture, shipping and ports, coastal and marine tourism, offshore renewable energy and marine biotechnology.
**Breakdown:**
- Fisheries and aquaculture
- Shipping and ports (Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways, ⚠️ implementing partner)
- Coastal and marine tourism
- Offshore renewable energy and marine biotechnology
- Sustainable, not extractive, use (owner 24)
- Fisheries implementing partner: Ministry of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying (⚠️)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24 Section 2 and 3; ⚠️ for ministry roles)
**Links:** ENV-4078, ENV-4104, ENV-4106
**Trap:** "Blue Economy means unrestricted extraction for maximum gain." It means sustainable use balancing growth and ecosystem health.
**Mains hook:** Blue Economy is viable only if coastal ecosystems are treated as productive natural capital, not as space to be developed.
**Back:** *"Blue Economy: harvest the sea without emptying it."*

### Flashcard ENV-4089 — Mangroves and blue carbon: definition and ecosystem services
**Front:** What do mangroves provide and what is blue carbon?
**Definition (learn):** Mangroves are salt-tolerant coastal forests at the land–sea interface. They provide storm-surge and wave buffering, fish-nursery habitat, livelihood support and carbon storage. Blue carbon, in the standard climate-policy sense, is carbon captured and stored by coastal vegetated ecosystems, especially mangroves, seagrasses and salt marshes, with major storage in waterlogged sediments.
**Breakdown:**
- Mangroves → wave attenuation (conditional), fish nursery, livelihoods, carbon storage
- Blue carbon → coastal vegetated ecosystems; sediment storage
- Not a synonym for all ocean carbon
- Disturbance can reverse part of stored benefit
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24 Sections 2 and 6)
**Links:** ENV-4090, ENV-4091, ENV-4092
**Trap:** "Blue carbon means all carbon stored anywhere in the ocean."
**Mains hook:** Coastal ecosystems are simultaneously climate, biodiversity and livelihood assets; this argues against paving them.
**Back:** *"Mangroves buffer, nurse fish and bank carbon in the mud."*

### Flashcard ENV-4090 — Blue-carbon accounting gap and the aquaculture trade-off (advanced)
**Front:** Why has blue carbon been under-accounted and what trade-off does mangrove conversion create?
**Definition (learn):** Blue-carbon extent, condition, sediment stocks and disturbance emissions may be incompletely represented in climate accounts. Mangrove clearing for shrimp farming or infrastructure trades short-term revenue against long-term coastal protection and carbon storage. Carbon claims need a stated method, boundary, source year and reversal treatment.
**Breakdown:**
- Accounting gap → extent, condition, sediment carbon, disturbance emissions
- Trade-off → aquaculture revenue vs coastal protection and carbon storage
- Stock vs flux and depth/boundary choices change per-area comparisons with forests
- Permanence risk → degradation releases stored carbon
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner 24 Advanced Sections 2 and 5, analytical; do not invent an Indian stock figure)
**Links:** ENV-4089, ENV-4106, ENV-4102
**Trap:** Claiming India records no coastal carbon, or treating all blue carbon as permanently stored.
**Mains hook:** Integrate blue-carbon accounting into climate policy; this is the Mains recommendation.
**Back:** *"Under-counted, under-protected: the sediment carbon is real until the mangrove is cut."*

### Flashcard ENV-4091 — Mangrove misconceptions: what mangroves do not do
**Front:** Which mangrove claims are false?
**Definition (learn):** Mangroves provide conditional wave attenuation, fish-nursery and livelihood functions. They do not store freshwater, and they do not convert saline estuarine land into freshwater paddy or aquaculture zones.
**Breakdown:**
- Do → wave attenuation (conditional), fish nursery, livelihood, blue-carbon storage
- Do not → store freshwater
- Do not → convert saline estuarine land to freshwater paddy or aquaculture
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, Section 6)
**Links:** ENV-4089, ENV-4090
**Trap:** Treating mangroves as freshwater storage or as a fix for saline land conversion.
**Mains hook:** Mangrove restoration is a coastal-buffer and carbon strategy; it is not a freshwater supply plan.
**Back:** *"Mangroves protect the shore; they do not store fresh water."*

### Flashcard ENV-4092 — MISHTI: mangrove restoration target 2023–2028
**Front:** What is MISHTI and what are its targets?
**Definition (learn):** The Mangrove Initiative for Shoreline Habitats & Tangible Incomes (MISHTI) envisages restoring about 540 sq km of mangroves across nine coastal States and four Union Territories over 2023–2028, implemented through convergence with existing schemes.
**Breakdown:**
- Area → about 540 sq km
- Coverage → 9 coastal States and 4 UTs
- Period → 2023–2028
- Estimated carbon sink → 4.5 million tonnes
- Employment → about 22.8 million person-days
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 540 sq km; 2023–2028; 4.5 Mt; 22.8 million person-days (📰 Economic Survey 2025-26, Ch. 10, as cited in owner 24; these are programme targets and estimates, not audited achievements; ⚠️)
**Links:** ENV-4089, ENV-4093, ENV-4090
**Trap:** Treating MISHTI targets as proof of restored mangrove function.
**Mains hook:** Mangroves are funded as green coastal infrastructure with a livelihood dividend, not merely as a conservation cost.
**Back:** *"Five-forty square kilometres, nine States and four UTs, four-point-five million tonnes of hope."*

### Flashcard ENV-4093 — National Coastal Mission and NPCA in India's coastal resilience
**Front:** What are the National Coastal Mission and the NPCA, and where do they sit in the coastal-resilience architecture?
**Definition (learn):** Economic Survey 2025-26, Chapter 10, describes India's coastal approach as integrated: linking ecosystem protection with livelihood security and climate adaptation through the National Coastal Mission (integrated coastal zone management and climate-resilient infrastructure), MISHTI and the NPCA.
**Breakdown:**
- National Coastal Mission → integrated coastal zone management and climate-resilient infrastructure
- MISHTI → mangrove restoration (ENV-4092)
- NPCA → part of the architecture (⚠️ full expansion not recorded in owner 24)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (📰 owner 24, Economic Survey 2025-26, Ch. 10)
**Links:** ENV-4092, ENV-4086
**Trap:** Reading the coastal mission as a substitute for CRZ enforcement. It sits alongside it.
**Mains hook:** Integrated coastal management is the institutional answer to the grey-versus-green infrastructure choice.
**Back:** *"Mission for the coast, MISHTI for mangroves, NPCA as the third leg."*

### Flashcard ENV-4094 — Coral reefs: bleaching and acidification as a dual stress
**Front:** How do sea-surface temperature and ocean acidification stress coral reefs?
**Definition (learn):** Bleaching results mainly from elevated sea-surface temperature, which makes corals expel their symbiotic algae (zooxanthellae). Ocean acidification, from oceans absorbing atmospheric CO₂, independently lowers carbonate-ion availability that corals need to build skeletons. Both trace back to rising atmospheric CO₂.
**Breakdown:**
- Temperature → symbiont expulsion → bleaching
- Acidification → lower carbonate ions → weaker skeleton building
- Common driver → rising atmospheric CO₂
- Indian sites at risk (⚠️): Gulf of Mannar, Lakshadweep, Andaman and Nicobar
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24 Section 6 and advanced Section 3)
**Links:** ENV-4095, ENV-4096, ENV-4102
**Trap:** "Bleaching and acidification are the same mechanism." They are distinct stresses.
**Mains hook:** Local protection cannot substitute for global climate mitigation as the ultimate determinant of reef survival.
**Back:** *"Heat makes corals spit their algae; acid makes their skeletons weak."*

### Flashcard ENV-4095 — Coral misconceptions: bleaching is not automatic death; acidification is not acidic seawater
**Front:** Which coral and acidification statements are false?
**Definition (learn):** Bleaching is the loss of symbionts and energy; mortality depends on the duration, severity and recurrence of stress. Average seawater remains alkaline; acidification means that seawater's pH is falling and carbonate availability is declining.
**Breakdown:**
- Bleaching ≠ dead colony (depends on severity and duration)
- Acidification ≠ acidic seawater; pH is falling but water stays alkaline
- Bleaching driven mainly by temperature; pollution is a compounding stressor
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, Section 6–7)
**Links:** ENV-4094, ENV-4096
**Trap:** Claiming that all bleached coral has died, or that the ocean has become acidic.
**Mains hook:** Precision about stress mechanisms makes reef-policy arguments credible.
**Back:** *"Bleached is not dead; alkaline is not acid, but falling pH is the warning."*

### Flashcard ENV-4096 — Biorock (mineral accretion) for coral restoration
**Front:** What is Biorock and what is its limit?
**Definition (learn):** Biorock, or Mineral Accretion Technique, passes a low-voltage direct current through a submerged metal structure so minerals precipitate on it, providing a substrate for coral restoration.
**Breakdown:**
- Low-voltage DC through submerged metal frame
- Minerals precipitate → substrate for coral
- Evidence is site- and method-dependent (PLOS ONE 2024, cited in owner 24)
- Does not remove heat, acidification, sediment or pollution stress
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mineral Accretion Technique evidence review, PLOS ONE (2024), article e0315475 (✅ owner 24 source control; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-4094, ENV-4095
**Trap:** Presenting Biorock as the solution to global reef stress.
**Mains hook:** A restoration technique is useful locally; it cannot stop the global driver.
**Back:** *"Electric minerals build a reef cradle, not a climate shield."*

### Flashcard ENV-4097 — Seawater intrusion: causal order (groundwater over-extraction first)
**Front:** What drives seawater intrusion into coastal aquifers?
**Definition (learn):** Seawater intrusion is the landward movement of seawater into a coastal freshwater aquifer. It is driven chiefly by groundwater over-extraction, which lowers the freshwater head, and is compounded by sea-level rise, reduced recharge, tidal-canal or creek modification and aquaculture ponds.
**Breakdown:**
- Primary → groundwater over-extraction (freshwater head falls)
- Compounding → sea-level rise
- Compounding → reduced recharge
- Compounding → creek and canal modification
- Compounding → aquaculture ponds (saline leakage)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, Basic Section 2 and 6)
**Links:** ENV-4098, ENV-4088
**Trap:** Blaming sea-level rise alone. Over-extraction is the primary driver.
**Mains hook:** Start the answer with the hydraulic-balance failure, then rank the compounding causes.
**Back:** *"Pump the aquifer dry, the sea moves in; rising seas just push harder."*

### Flashcard ENV-4098 — Seawater intrusion remedies and the 2025 GS-III answer spine
**Front:** What are the remedies for seawater intrusion and how should the 2025 GS-III question (10 marks, 150 words) be answered?
**Definition (learn):** Remedies are grouped as demand-side (extraction regulation, crop shifts), recharge-side (artificial recharge, check dams, rainwater harvesting) and barrier-based (recharge or injection wells, subsurface barriers). The 2025 question asks for causes and remedies.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand-side → regulate extraction, shift crops, maintain aquifer budgets
- Recharge-side → managed aquifer recharge, rainwater harvesting, protect recharge zones
- Barrier and monitoring → injection wells, subsurface barriers, salinity monitoring
- Desalination → supplies water but does not restore the aquifer
- 150-word spine: hydraulic gradient → ranked causes → three remedy groups → aquifer-scale governance (Topic 14 link)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, PYQ 2025 GS-III Q8 (owner integration block), 10 marks, 150 words)
**Links:** ENV-4097, ENV-4086
**Trap:** Presenting desalination as a complete remedy. It does not restore the aquifer.
**Mains hook:** Aquifer-scale governance, not only infrastructure, determines whether intrusion is stopped.
**Back:** *"Meter the pumps, refill the aquifer, wall the edge, watch the salt."*

### Flashcard ENV-4099 — Coastal sand mining and erosion: buffers and down-drift transfer
**Front:** How does coastal sand mining and hard protection affect beaches?
**Definition (learn):** Removing sand changes the beach–dune–nearshore sediment budget, weakening natural buffers and affecting habitat. Hard structures can transfer erosion down-drift.
**Breakdown:**
- Sand mining → sediment budget loss → weaker buffers
- Hard structures (seawalls) → down-drift erosion transfer
- Responses → zonation, sediment-budget assessment, regulated extraction, dune and mangrove restoration, site-specific engineering
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, Section 13.3; PYQs: 2019 GS-III Q7 (10 marks, analyse coastal sand mining) and 2022 GS-III Q18 (15 marks, coastal erosion causes, effects, management))
**Links:** ENV-4100, ENV-4102, ENV-4081
**Trap:** Prescribing seawalls as a universal cure.
**Mains hook:** Engineering that protects one shoreline can erode the next; sediment budget thinking comes first.
**Back:** *"Mine the sand, lose the buffer; wall the beach, shift the erosion down-drift."*

### Flashcard ENV-4100 — Marine dead zones: nutrient-driven hypoxia
**Front:** How do marine dead zones form and what is the 2018 GS-I route?
**Definition (learn):** Nutrient-rich sewage and runoff trigger algal blooms, whose microbial decomposition depletes dissolved oxygen (hypoxia), harming fisheries and the food web. Owner 24 advises not calling every low-oxygen patch a permanent "dead zone" without measured data.
**Breakdown:**
- Nutrients (sewage, runoff) → algal bloom
- Decomposition → low dissolved oxygen (hypoxia)
- Consequence → fisheries and food-web loss
- Response → wastewater treatment, nutrient stewardship, monitoring
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24 Section 13.3; PYQ 2018 GS-I Q7, 10 marks, 150 words: consequences of spreading marine dead zones)
**Links:** ENV-4099, ENV-4094
**Trap:** Calling every low-oxygen patch a permanent dead zone without measurement.
**Mains hook:** Measure oxygen and nutrients, then treat the source.
**Back:** *"Nutrients feed algae, algae rot, oxygen dies, fish leave."*

### Flashcard ENV-4101 — Oil pollution: prevention and irreversible injury
**Front:** How does oil pollution harm coastal ecosystems and what is the response priority?
**Definition (learn):** Oil coats shores and biota, harming birds and fish and damaging mangroves and coral. Response needs containment, recovery, waste handling and habitat restoration. Post-spill cleanup cannot fully reverse ecological injury.
**Breakdown:**
- Harm → shore coating, birds, fish, mangroves, coral
- Response → containment, recovery, waste handling, habitat restoration
- Priority → prevention, port and vessel compliance, contingency preparedness
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24, Section 13.3; PYQ 2023 GS-III Q8, 10 marks, 150 words: oil pollution impacts and India's vulnerability)
**Links:** ENV-4099, ENV-4102
**Trap:** Assuming clean-up restores ecology in full.
**Mains hook:** Prevention is cheaper than post-spill recovery; vessel and port compliance is the lever.
**Back:** *"Oil spreads, clean-up lags, ecology loses: prevent first."*

### Flashcard ENV-4102 — BBNJ (High Seas) Agreement, 2023: entry into force and India's status
**Front:** What is the BBNJ Agreement and what is India's status?
**Definition (learn):** The Biodiversity Beyond National Jurisdiction (BBNJ) Agreement, called the High Seas Treaty, is an implementing agreement under UNCLOS on marine biodiversity beyond national jurisdiction. It covers marine genetic resources and benefit-sharing, area-based management tools including marine protected areas, EIAs, and capacity-building and technology transfer.
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted 19 June 2023 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Opened for signature 20 September 2023, New York (✅ Wikipedia)
- 60th ratification: Morocco, 19 September 2025 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Entry into force: 17 January 2026 (✅ Wikipedia infobox; owner 24)
- India: signed 25 September 2024 (✅ owner 24); not a Party as of 5 October 2026 (✅ owner 24)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19 June 2023; 20 September 2023; 17 January 2026; India signature 25 September 2024 (owner). Party and signatory counts omitted: Wikipedia gives no as-of date.
**Links:** ENV-4104, ENV-4103
**Trap:** Calling India a Party because it signed. Signature is not ratification.
**Mains hook:** BBNJ governs biodiversity beyond national jurisdiction for its Parties; it does not extend CRZ or replace the UNCLOS Part XI regime for minerals in "the Area".
**Back:** *"Signed is not ratified: India signed in 2024, not yet a Party."*

### Flashcard ENV-4103 — Deep Ocean Mission (2021) and Samudrayaan / Matsya-6000
**Front:** What is the Deep Ocean Mission and what do Samudrayaan and Matsya-6000 refer to?
**Definition (learn):** The Deep Ocean Mission (2021) is a Ministry of Earth Sciences programme covering deep-sea mining technology, a manned submersible (Samudrayaan, with Matsya-6000 as the crewed vehicle designed for three people), ocean climate-change advisory services, deep-sea biodiversity, offshore energy and desalination, and an advanced marine station for ocean biology.
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry → Earth Sciences (MoES)
- Samudrayaan → project; Matsya-6000 → three-person submersible design
- Scope → mining technology, climate advisory, biodiversity, energy, desalination, marine station
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 (✅ owner 24); three-person crew (✅ owner 24)
**Links:** ENV-4102, ENV-4086, ENV-4104
**Trap:** Treating scientific capability or an exploration contract as ownership of deep-seabed minerals or permission for commercial mining. Mining in "the Area" runs through the International Seabed Authority under UNCLOS Part XI.
**Mains hook:** Deep-sea science is an innovation route with unknown ecological cost; its governance should be precautionary.
**Back:** *"Earth Sciences leads, Samudrayaan dives, Matsya-6000 carries three, the seabed needs an authority."*

### Flashcard ENV-4104 — Four legal orders on one coast: CRZ, protected area, Ramsar and EEZ
**Front:** Which four legal categories can apply to the same stretch of coast?
**Definition (learn):** CRZ classification is a regulatory zone under the EPA, 1986. A protected area is a Wildlife Protection Act designation. A Ramsar site is an international wetland designation. An EEZ is a UNCLOS maritime zone of 200 nautical miles where a coastal State has specified resource rights.
**Breakdown:**
- CRZ → EPA 1986 zoning (CRZ-I to IV)
- Protected area → Wildlife Protection Act, 1972 (⚠️ act name not in owner 24)
- Ramsar site → international designation
- EEZ → up to 200 nautical miles, resource rights, not full sovereignty
- CRZ-IV covers water to 12 nautical miles, not 200
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** EEZ up to 200 nm (✅ owner 24); CRZ-IV to 12 nm (✅ owner 24)
**Links:** ENV-4085, ENV-4078, ENV-4102
**Trap:** Mixing CRZ-IV's 12 nautical miles with the EEZ's 200 nautical miles, or treating them as one legal order.
**Mains hook:** Legal categories operate in parallel; a coastal project may need several clearances under different regimes.
**Back:** *"CRZ zones the edge, protected areas shield life, Ramsar honours wetlands, EEZ claims resources to 200 miles."*

### Flashcard ENV-4105 — PYQ routes for coastal and marine ecology
**Front:** What are the PYQ routes and key status for coastal and marine ecology?
**Definition (learn):** The owner integrates 2018–2023 demands on dead zones, mangrove depletion, sand mining, blue carbon, coastal erosion, Biorock and oil pollution; the 2024–2025 seawater intrusion Mains question; and 2026 Prelims items on mangroves and the Deep Ocean Mission. Prelims keys are provisional (2026) or unavailable (2021, 2022), so no answers are inferred.
**Breakdown:**
- 2018 GS-I Q7 (10 marks): marine dead zones consequences
- 2019 GS-I Q5 (10 marks): mangrove depletion causes and ecological role
- 2019 GS-III Q7 (10 marks): coastal sand mining
- 2021 Prelims GS-I Q19: blue carbon (key not available)
- 2022 GS-III Q18 (15 marks): coastal erosion causes, effects, management
- 2022 Prelims GS-I Q49: Biorock (key not available)
- 2023 GS-III Q8 (10 marks): oil pollution and India's vulnerability
- 2025 GS-III Q8 (10 marks, 150 words): seawater intrusion causes and remedies
- 2026 Prelims GS-I Q23: mangrove ecosystem services (provisional key; not recorded)
- 2026 Prelims GS-I Q50: Deep Ocean Mission, Samudrayaan, Matsya-6000, ministry (provisional key; not recorded)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 24 PYQ blocks)
**Links:** ENV-4098, ENV-4092, ENV-4103, ENV-4100, ENV-4101
**Trap:** Answering the 2026 Prelims items from the provisional key; the owner records no answer.
**Mains hook:** Use the ecosystem service → pressure → response → trade-off structure for the 15-mark coastal questions.
**Back:** *"Dead zones, mangroves, sand, blue carbon, erosion, Biorock, oil, seawater, and two 2026 items."*

### Flashcard ENV-4106 — Green versus grey coastal capital: dissolving the CRZ false binary (advanced)
**Front:** How should a Mains answer on Blue Economy versus CRZ protection be framed?
**Definition (learn):** The advanced framing is that mangroves and reefs are coastal defence infrastructure with a livelihood dividend, not merely a conservation cost. The real question is which kind of coastal capital is built: grey (seawalls, embankments) or green (mangroves, dunes, reefs).
**Breakdown:**
- Grey capital → seawalls, embankments
- Green capital → mangroves, dunes, reefs
- Livelihood dividend → fishing, tourism, storm protection
- Trade-off → short-term development vs long-term service
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner 24 Advanced Section 9 analytical use of Economic Survey 2025-26 MISHTI and National Coastal Mission)
**Links:** ENV-4087, ENV-4090, ENV-4092, ENV-4093
**Trap:** Presenting the choice as a binary between development and conservation.
**Mains hook:** Examiners reward dissolving the false binary, not a balanced list.
**Back:** *"Grey walls or green living shores: choose the capital that pays dividends."*

### GROUP — Renewable Energy and Green Hydrogen

### Flashcard ENV-4107 — Renewable electricity as the base; hydrogen as a targeted complement
**Front:** What is India's renewable-to-green-hydrogen pathway and where does hydrogen fit?
**Definition (learn):** Renewable electricity is the foundation of India's energy transition. Hydrogen complements direct electrification where a molecule, reducing agent, high-temperature service, difficult-to-transport derivative or long-duration store is genuinely needed. Eligibility as green is threshold-based and pathway-specific, not an automatic zero-emission label.
**Breakdown:**
- Renewable electricity → electrolysis → hydrogen
- Qualifying biomass → conversion → hydrogen
- Both routes → same well-to-gate emissions threshold (ENV-4111)
- Use electricity directly where practical; test hydrogen for selected uses
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Basic Sections 1 and 3)
**Links:** ENV-4108, ENV-4111, ENV-4123
**Trap:** "Electrolysis is automatically green." It must meet the threshold and use a qualifying pathway.
**Mains hook:** Hydrogen is strategically valuable in hard-to-abate sectors, not as a universal substitute for electrification.
**Back:** *"Electricity first, hydrogen where molecules are needed, and both must be measured."*

### Flashcard ENV-4108 — Renewable, non-fossil and nuclear: three categories, not synonyms
**Front:** How do renewable energy, non-fossil energy and nuclear power relate?
**Definition (learn):** Renewable energy comes from replenishing sources (solar, wind, hydro, sustainably managed biomass, geothermal, ocean). Non-fossil energy is the broader category that includes renewables and nuclear. Nuclear is non-fossil but not renewable.
**Breakdown:**
- Renewable → solar, wind, hydro, sustainable biomass, geothermal, ocean
- Non-fossil → renewable + nuclear
- Nuclear → non-fossil, not renewable; reactor and liability detail belongs to Science and Technology
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Section 2 and 4)
**Links:** ENV-4116, ENV-4107
**Trap:** Non-fossil and renewable as synonyms.
**Mains hook:** Use non-fossil when a question includes nuclear; use renewable for resource-based generation.
**Back:** *"Renewables are inside non-fossil; nuclear is outside renewables but not fossil."*

### Flashcard ENV-4109 — Grid parity
**Front:** What is grid parity?
**Definition (learn):** Grid parity is the point at which renewable-electricity generation cost equals or falls below conventional fossil-fuel generation cost without subsidy.
**Breakdown:**
- Test → cost without subsidy
- Parity → renewable cost ≤ fossil cost
- Does not automatically mean firm supply or grid integration is solved
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Section 2)
**Links:** ENV-4107, ENV-4126
**Trap:** Treating grid parity as proof that storage and transmission are unnecessary.
**Mains hook:** Cheap generation is not the same as reliable delivery.
**Back:** *"Parity means renewables cost no more than coal, without a subsidy."*

### Flashcard ENV-4110 — Electrolysis and fuel cell: opposite directions
**Front:** Distinguish electrolysis from a hydrogen fuel cell.
**Definition (learn):** Electrolysis uses electricity to split water into hydrogen and oxygen. A hydrogen fuel cell consumes hydrogen and oxygen electrochemically to produce electricity, heat and water.
**Breakdown:**
- Electrolysis → electricity + H₂O → H₂ + O₂
- Fuel cell → H₂ + O₂ → electricity + heat + water
- Opposite functional direction
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Sections 2 and 6)
**Links:** ENV-4107, ENV-4122
**Trap:** A fuel cell producing hydrogen in the same direction as electrolysis.
**Mains hook:** Storage and fuel use of hydrogen depend on which device is in the chain.
**Back:** *"Electrolysis makes hydrogen; the fuel cell burns it electrically."*

### Flashcard ENV-4111 — India's green hydrogen standard: 2 kg CO₂e per kg H₂
**Front:** What is India's green hydrogen threshold and what routes qualify?
**Definition (learn):** Under the MNRE Green Hydrogen Standard (18 August 2023, per owner 25), hydrogen qualifies as green when its well-to-gate non-biogenic emissions do not exceed 2 kg CO₂e per kg H₂, averaged over 12 months. Electrolysis-based and biomass-based pathways both qualify if they meet the threshold.
**Breakdown:**
- Ceiling → 2 kg CO₂e per kg H₂
- Averaging → 12 months
- Measurement → well-to-gate, non-biogenic
- Routes → renewable-electricity electrolysis; qualifying biomass conversion
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Standard dated 18 August 2023; threshold 2 kg CO₂e/kg H₂ averaged over 12 months (✅ owner 25; not fetched; the NGHM Wikipedia page was not reachable).
**Links:** ENV-4107, ENV-4112, ENV-4113
**Trap:** Thinking the colour label or electrolysis alone proves zero emissions.
**Mains hook:** Green eligibility is measured, so the answer must name the threshold and the boundary.
**Back:** *"Two kilograms of CO₂ per kilogram of hydrogen, measured well-to-gate over a year."*

### Flashcard ENV-4112 — Green, grey and blue hydrogen: pathway and emission profile
**Front:** Distinguish green, grey and blue hydrogen.
**Definition (learn):** The H₂ molecule is the same; the label describes the production pathway and measured emissions. Green hydrogen meets India's standard through electrolysis or qualifying biomass. Grey hydrogen is made from fossil routes without capture. Blue hydrogen is fossil-based with capture and storage, and retains methane, capture and storage risks.
**Breakdown:**
- Green → qualifying renewable electrolysis or biomass; ≤ 2 kg CO₂e/kg H₂
- Grey → fossil-based, no capture (⚠️ definition not in owner 25; standard usage)
- Blue → fossil-based with capture and storage; retains methane and storage risk
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Sections 7 and 9)
**Links:** ENV-4111, ENV-4113
**Trap:** Grey and green being different molecules; blue hydrogen being automatically zero-emission.
**Mains hook:** Colour is shorthand for a measured pathway; the emissions boundary decides the label.
**Back:** *"Same molecule, different ledger: green measured, grey fossil, blue captured with leaks."*

### Flashcard ENV-4113 — National Green Hydrogen Mission: approval and 2030 targets
**Front:** What are the NGHM's approval date, 2030 production target, renewable addition and outlay?
**Definition (learn):** The National Green Hydrogen Mission was approved in January 2023. It targets 5 million metric tonnes per annum of green-hydrogen production capacity by 2030, with about 125 GW of associated additional renewable capacity, over ₹8 lakh crore projected investment and an initial public outlay of ₹19,744 crore to 2029-30.
**Breakdown:**
- Approval → January 2023
- Production target → 5 MMT per annum by 2030
- Associated renewable addition → about 125 GW
- Projected investment → over ₹8 lakh crore (projected)
- Public outlay → ₹19,744 crore (initial, to 2029-30)
- Nodal ministry → MNRE (✅ owner 25)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (✅ owner 25, Basic Sections 2, 3, 8; Advanced Section 9; not fetched. Outlay and investment figures are projections; ⚠️ verify latest figures from MNRE before quoting.)
**Links:** ENV-4114, ENV-4120, ENV-4121
**Trap:** "The mission targets 50 million tonnes per annum by 2030." The target is 5 million tonnes per annum.
**Mains hook:** Targets are plans, not achievements; the 2026 progress report must be checked against allocation and commissioning.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three approval, five million tonnes by thirty, a hundred and twenty-five gigawatts behind it."*

### Flashcard ENV-4114 — SIGHT programme: dual incentive for manufacturing and production
**Front:** What is the SIGHT Programme and why does its design matter?
**Definition (learn):** SIGHT (Strategic Interventions for Green Hydrogen Transition) is the NGHM's financial-incentive scheme for electrolyser manufacturing and green-hydrogen production. It pairs domestic manufacturing capability with production-side incentives.
**Breakdown:**
- Incentive 1 → electrolyser manufacturing
- Incentive 2 → green-hydrogen production
- Rationale → build domestic capability and reduce import dependency (⚠️ analytical)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Section 2 and 3)
**Links:** ENV-4113, ENV-4120
**Trap:** SIGHT incentivises only hydrogen production.
**Mains hook:** Two prongs: industrial capability and end-product demand.
**Back:** *"SIGHT pays for the factory and the flow."*

### Flashcard ENV-4115 — Panchamrit renewable targets: 500 GW and 50% (not interchangeable)
**Front:** What are India's two renewable Panchamrit targets for 2030?
**Definition (learn):** India targets 500 GW of non-fossil electric-power capacity by 2030 and 50% of energy requirements from renewable energy by 2030. The two metrics are not interchangeable: one is installed capacity, the other is share of energy requirements.
**Breakdown:**
- Target 1 → 500 GW non-fossil electric-power capacity by 2030
- Target 2 → 50% of energy requirements from renewables by 2030
- Ownership of climate targets → Topic 20 (India Climate Policy)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Section 3, cross-referencing owner 20)
**Links:** ENV-4116, ENV-4113
**Trap:** Treating GW of installed capacity as proof of renewable share of energy.
**Mains hook:** Capacity and energy shares measure different things; name both.
**Back:** *"Five hundred gigawatts of capacity, fifty percent of energy: two different yardsticks."*

### Flashcard ENV-4116 — Installed capacity, not generation: the 54.88% milestone (31 Aug 2026)
**Front:** What did India's consolidated capacity data show on 31 August 2026?
**Definition (learn):** On 31 August 2026, non-fossil capacity was 304.334 GW out of total installed capacity of 554.544 GW, or 54.88%. This is installed capacity, not generation or firm supply.
**Breakdown:**
- Non-fossil → 304.334 GW
- Total installed → 554.544 GW
- Share → 54.88% (installed-capacity share)
- Solar → 168.04 GW; wind → 58.52 GW (31 Aug 2026)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 31 August 2026; 304.334 GW; 554.544 GW; 54.88%; solar 168.04 GW; wind 58.52 GW (✅ owner 25; official consolidated capacity data as cited by owner; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-4115, ENV-4117, ENV-4118
**Trap:** "More than half of India's electricity is generated from non-fossil sources." The figure is an installed-capacity share, not a generation share.
**Mains hook:** Installed GW must be converted to generation and reliability before claiming energy independence.
**Back:** *"Fifty-four point eight eight percent of capacity, not of electrons."*

### Flashcard ENV-4117 — India's rank: fourth in installed renewable capacity (IRENA 2025)
**Front:** Where does India rank in global installed renewable capacity?
**Definition (learn):** Per IRENA's Renewable Energy Statistics 2025, India ranks fourth globally in total installed renewable capacity, after China, the USA and Brazil.
**Breakdown:**
- 1 → China
- 2 → USA
- 3 → Brazil
- 4 → India
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** IRENA Renewable Energy Statistics 2025 (✅ owner 25; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-4116, ENV-4118
**Trap:** Treating a rank in installed capacity as a rank in generation or per-capita terms.
**Mains hook:** Rank is a capacity statistic; the integration gap remains.
**Back:** *"Fourth by installed renewables: China, USA, Brazil ahead."*

### Flashcard ENV-4118 — Solar capacity composition, 31 August 2026
**Front:** How was India's 168.04 GW solar capacity made up on 31 August 2026?
**Definition (learn):** Solar capacity was 168.04 GW, composed of 123.99 GW ground-mounted, 32.59 GW grid-connected rooftop, 4.83 GW solar within hybrid projects and 6.63 GW off-grid.
**Breakdown:**
- Ground-mounted → 123.99 GW
- Grid-connected rooftop → 32.59 GW
- Hybrid → 4.83 GW
- Off-grid → 6.63 GW
- Sum check → 168.04 GW
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (✅ owner 25; 31 August 2026).
**Links:** ENV-4116, ENV-4119
**Trap:** Treating rooftop capacity as ground-mounted generation.
**Mains hook:** Distributed rooftop solar is a demand-side lever; ground-mounted parks need land and transmission.
**Back:** *"One-twenty-four ground, thirty-three roof, five hybrid, seven off-grid: one hundred sixty-eight."*

### Flashcard ENV-4119 — Energy storage requirement: 336.4 GWh (2029–30) and 411.4 GWh (2031–32)
**Front:** What storage requirements does CEA planning cited in the Economic Survey estimate?
**Definition (learn):** CEA planning estimates about 336.4 GWh of total energy-storage requirement by 2029–30 and 411.4 GWh by 2031–32. The 2031–32 total combines 175.18 GWh pumped storage and 236.22 GWh battery storage.
**Breakdown:**
- 2029–30 → 336.4 GWh total
- 2031–32 → 411.4 GWh total
- Composition 2031–32 → 175.18 GWh pumped + 236.22 GWh battery
- Requirement, not commissioned stock
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (✅ owner 25; Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10 as cited in owner).
**Links:** ENV-4126, ENV-4124, ENV-4116
**Trap:** Calling the 411.4 GWh figure a battery-only target, or treating a requirement as installed storage.
**Mains hook:** Storage is the integration layer that turns installed GW into reliable supply.
**Back:** *"Three-thirty-six by 2030, four-eleven by 2032: pumped and battery, a need not a stock."*

### Flashcard ENV-4120 — NGHM progress: allocated vs commissioned
**Front:** What NGHM progress figures must be kept separate?
**Definition (learn):** The Economic Survey 2025-26 reports 862,000 tonnes per year of green-hydrogen capacity allocated to 18 companies and 3,000 MW per year of electrolyser manufacturing awarded to 15 firms. A PIB update reported only about 8,000 tonnes per annum of commissioned green-hydrogen production through February 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Allocated → 862,000 t/yr to 18 companies (contracted intent)
- Electrolyser manufacturing → 3,000 MW/yr awarded to 15 firms
- Commissioned → about 8,000 t/yr through February 2026 (PIB)
- Allocation, commissioning, certified production and delivered output are different statuses
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 862,000 t/yr; 18 companies; 3,000 MW; 15 firms; ~8,000 t/yr commissioned through February 2026 (📰 owner 25; Economic Survey 2025-26 and PIB as cited; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-4113, ENV-4114, ENV-4121
**Trap:** Treating allocated capacity as commissioned production.
**Mains hook:** Use the status ladder to judge any hydrogen claim.
**Back:** *"Allocated is a promise, commissioned is a plant: 862,000 promised, 8,000 running."*

### Flashcard ENV-4121 — Three Green Hydrogen Hubs at major ports
**Front:** Which three port authorities host the designated Green Hydrogen Hubs?
**Definition (learn):** Three Green Hydrogen Hubs have been designated at Deendayal Port Authority (Gujarat), V.O. Chidambaranar Port Authority (Tamil Nadu) and Paradip Port Authority (Odisha).
**Breakdown:**
- Deendayal Port Authority → Gujarat
- V.O. Chidambaranar Port Authority → Tamil Nadu
- Paradip Port Authority → Odisha
- Siting supports logistics, bunkering and nearby industrial demand (advanced owner 25)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (📰 owner 25; Economic Survey 2025-26 Ch. 10).
**Links:** ENV-4120, ENV-4113
**Trap:** Assuming port hubs legally restrict output to exports. They do not exclude domestic uses.
**Mains hook:** Port-based hubs show a trade and logistics orientation, but domestic demand remains open.
**Back:** *"Gujarat, Tamil Nadu, Odisha: three ports, three hubs, no export-only rule."*

### Flashcard ENV-4122 — Water and efficiency: stoichiometric minimum and HHV/LHV boundaries
**Front:** Why must water and efficiency claims for green hydrogen state their boundary?
**Definition (learn):** About 9 litres of water per kg of H₂ is the stoichiometric water incorporated into the product. Actual project withdrawal is higher and site-specific. Efficiency depends on whether HHV or LHV is used, the stack or system boundary, operating load, auxiliaries, compression and process heat.
**Breakdown:**
- Stoichiometric water → about 9 L/kg H₂
- Actual withdrawal → purification, cooling, recovery, source and site
- Efficiency → HHV vs LHV; stack vs system boundary; load; compression
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** About 9 L/kg H₂ (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** ENV-4110, ENV-4107
**Trap:** Quoting 9 L/kg as the actual water use of a project.
**Mains hook:** Specify the boundary for any water or efficiency number.
**Back:** *"Nine litres is the chemistry; the project drinks more."*

### Flashcard ENV-4123 — End-use hierarchy: electrify first, then hydrogen for selected uses
**Front:** What end-use hierarchy should guide green hydrogen policy?
**Definition (learn):** Efficiency and direct electrification come first where practical. Hydrogen is most defensible for existing fertiliser and refinery feedstock, selected steel-reduction and high-temperature processes, difficult-transport derivatives and long-duration storage. Passenger vehicles are an example of an end use better served by direct electrification.
**Breakdown:**
- First → efficiency and direct electrification
- Then → hydrogen for feedstock, reductant, high-temperature, derivative fuels, long-duration storage
- Avoid → hydrogen for uses better served by electricity (e.g. passenger vehicles, owner 25 Mains angle)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Section 3 and 10; ⚠️ analytical)
**Links:** ENV-4107, ENV-4124, ENV-4131
**Trap:** Assuming hydrogen is the optimal option for every hard-to-abate label.
**Mains hook:** Use an end-use hierarchy to explain why strategic hydrogen targets hard-to-abate sectors first.
**Back:** *"Electrify where you can, hydrogen where molecules are needed."*

### Flashcard ENV-4124 — Institutions for renewables and hydrogen: MNRE, MoP/CEA and SECI
**Front:** Which bodies handle renewable policy, grid planning and procurement?
**Definition (learn):** MNRE is the nodal ministry for renewable policy and the NGHM. The Ministry of Power and CEA handle grid integration, storage and transmission planning. SECI implements specified competitive procurement and programme functions assigned by MNRE; it does not set every tariff or renewable policy. Public-sector undertakings in oil, gas, fertiliser and steel are early pilot adopters (⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- MNRE → renewable policy and NGHM
- MoP / CEA → grid, storage, transmission
- SECI → procurement and assigned programme functions
- PSUs → early green hydrogen users (⚠️)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Section 4)
**Links:** ENV-4113, ENV-4119, ENV-4086 (for other coastal institutions)
**Trap:** Treating SECI as the body that sets every renewable tariff or policy.
**Mains hook:** Divided institutions mean grid, storage and procurement must be coordinated.
**Back:** *"MNRE makes policy, MoP plans grids, SECI buys power, PSUs pilot hydrogen."*

### Flashcard ENV-4125 — Bioenergy capacity: bagasse, waste-to-energy (31 August 2026)
**Front:** What bioenergy capacity figures did the owner record for 31 August 2026?
**Definition (learn):** Capacity figures as of 31 August 2026: bagasse cogeneration 9,821.32 MW; non-bagasse cogeneration 1,047.85 MW; grid-connected waste-to-energy 324.24 MW; off-grid waste-to-energy 554.16 MWeq. These are capacity, not generation.
**Breakdown:**
- Bagasse cogeneration → 9,821.32 MW
- Non-bagasse cogeneration → 1,047.85 MW
- Grid waste-to-energy → 324.24 MW
- Off-grid waste-to-energy → 554.16 MWeq
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (📰 owner 25; 31 August 2026).
**Links:** ENV-4116, ENV-4128
**Trap:** Reading capacity as generation.
**Mains hook:** Bioenergy adds distributed capacity; generation and feedstock integrity determine clean value.
**Back:** *"Nine thousand bagasse, one thousand non-bagasse, three hundred grid waste, five hundred off-grid."*

### Flashcard ENV-4126 — The Survey's constraint list: material, minerals, storage, land and grid
**Front:** Which constraints does the Economic Survey list for the energy transition?
**Definition (learn):** The Survey lists high capital costs, land-acquisition delays, grid availability, the material intensity of solar and wind, critical-mineral access and supply concentration, and capital-intensive storage. It adds grid-stability risk under high variable output and an "entropy effect" when buffers and institutions lag.
**Breakdown:**
- Capital cost
- Land acquisition delays
- Grid availability
- Material intensity (solar and wind)
- Critical-mineral access and supply concentration
- Storage capital intensity
- Grid stability under high variable output; "entropy effect" when institutions lag
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (📰 owner 25, Economic Survey 2025-26, Ch. 10)
**Links:** ENV-4119, ENV-4109, ENV-4127
**Trap:** Describing the transition as a generation-only challenge.
**Mains hook:** Three-layer investment: generation, transmission, storage, plus species-sensitive siting.
**Back:** *"Capital, land, grid, minerals, storage: the transition's binding constraints."*

### Flashcard ENV-4127 — Renewable siting versus Great Indian Bustard habitat: M.K. Ranjitsinh
**Front:** How has the Supreme Court handled renewable-infrastructure siting near Great Indian Bustard habitat?
**Definition (learn):** The Supreme Court-monitored Great Indian Bustard power-line mitigation proceedings, M.K. Ranjitsinh v. Union of India (21 March 2024, verify latest order date), engage the tension between transmission development and bustard habitat in Rajasthan and Gujarat. The Court considered undergrounding lines and installing bird diverters, and resolved the tension by re-scoping the remedy rather than ranking the two values.
**Breakdown:**
- Species → Great Indian Bustard, Critically Endangered
- Mitigation → bird diverters, selective undergrounding, species-sensitivity mapping
- Alternative → site selection away from critical habitat
- Verdict → mitigation, not prohibition
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** M.K. Ranjitsinh v. Union of India, 21 March 2024 (⚠️ owner 25 says verify the latest order date).
**Links:** ENV-4126, ENV-4131
**Trap:** Treating the siting problem as resolved by blanket prohibition of renewable projects.
**Mains hook:** Both renewables and species conservation are national priorities; solve through siting and mitigation.
**Back:** *"Diverters and cables, not a ban: the bustard case is about siting."*

### Flashcard ENV-4128 — National Bioenergy Programme Phase I and the biotech boundary
**Front:** What is the status of the National Bioenergy Programme Phase I, and where does biotech sit?
**Definition (learn):** The MNRE National Bioenergy Programme Phase I ran from 2021–22 to 31 March 2026, supporting biomass, biogas and waste-to-energy. The National Policy on Biofuels is owned by MoPNG. Microbial and enzymatic mechanisms belong to Science and Technology basic/13. DBT and BIRAC's BioE3 supports bio-based manufacturing and carbon capture and utilisation research as enabling inputs.
**Breakdown:**
- NBP Phase I → 2021–22 to 31 March 2026
- No continuation implied without a fresh notification
- National Policy on Biofuels → MoPNG
- BioE3 → enabling research, not energy output
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021–22 to 31 March 2026 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** ENV-4125, ENV-4107
**Trap:** Assuming NBP continues after March 2026 without an official notification.
**Mains hook:** Separate programme period, policy owner and production pathway.
**Back:** *"Phase One ended March 2026: do not assume it continues."*

### Flashcard ENV-4129 — PYQ routes for renewable energy and green hydrogen
**Front:** What are the PYQ routes for this topic, and which keys are recorded?
**Definition (learn):** The owner integrates 2018–2023 demands on solar, wind, green hydrogen, Green Grid and subsidy shifts; 2024–2025 Prelims and Mains items; and a 2026 green-hydrogen Prelims item. The 2024 and 2025 Prelims keys are available locally but no answer is recorded. The 2026 key is provisional. The 2018–2023 keys are not held locally.
**Breakdown:**
- 2018 Prelims GS-I Q67: solar silicon wafers and tariff regulation (key unavailable)
- 2020 GS-I Q16 (15 marks): solar potential and regional variations
- 2020 GS-III Q16 (15 marks): solar benefits vs conventional energy
- 2021 GS-III Q6 (10 marks): Green Grid Initiative purpose at COP26 and ISA origin
- 2022 GS-I Q7 (10 marks): wind energy potential and limited spatial spread
- 2022 GS-III Q12 (15 marks): renewable 2030 target and shift from fossil subsidies
- 2020 Prelims Q84 (biofuel raw materials) and Q88 (solar water pumps); 2022 Prelims Q84 (solar parks, floating solar): keys unavailable
- 2023 Prelims Q60 and Q99: green hydrogen applications (keys unavailable)
- 2024 Prelims Q27, Q38, Q46: distributed energy resources, pumped storage, SAF feedstock (keys available; answers not recorded)
- 2025 GS-I Q6 (10 marks): solar benefits; 2025 GS-III Q6 (10 marks): energy independence by 2047 and biotechnology
- 2025 Prelims Q70: PM Surya Ghar (key available; answer not recorded)
- 2026 Prelims Q45: green hydrogen pathways and NGHM (provisional key; not recorded)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25 PYQ integration blocks)
**Links:** ENV-4111, ENV-4113, ENV-4119, ENV-4131
**Trap:** Answering a 2024–2026 Prelims item from the card alone without checking the official key.
**Mains hook:** The 2025 energy-independence question has two demands: clean-energy system and biotechnology.
**Back:** *"Solar, wind, hydrogen, storage, subsidies, and 2047 energy independence."*

### Flashcard ENV-4130 — Green hydrogen cost-competitiveness and carbon pricing (advanced)
**Front:** Why is green hydrogen not yet cost-competitive, and what would change that?
**Definition (learn):** Electrolysis-based green hydrogen generally remains costlier than grey hydrogen in many markets. Costs depend on renewable electricity, electrolyser capital, utilisation, finance, water treatment, compression and delivery. The cost-competitiveness threshold moves with technology costs and with carbon pricing that raises grey hydrogen's effective cost, linking it to the carbon market (ENV-4008) and CBAM.
**Breakdown:**
- Cost drivers → renewable power, electrolyser capex, utilisation, finance, water, compression, delivery
- Policy lever → SIGHT (manufacturing + production)
- Carbon pricing → raises grey cost
- CBAM → affects competitiveness of Indian exports made with grey vs green hydrogen (⚠️ analytical)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner 25 Advanced Section 2, analytical; no cost figures recorded)
**Links:** ENV-4008, ENV-4019, ENV-4114
**Trap:** "Green hydrogen is already cost-competitive without policy support." It generally is not.
**Mains hook:** The SIGHT design bets on scale-driven cost declines plus carbon pricing, not present parity.
**Back:** *"Green is dearer today; carbon pricing and scale narrow the gap."*

### Flashcard ENV-4131 — Variability and three integration layers: generation, transmission, storage (advanced)
**Front:** Why is renewable intermittency as important as capacity addition?
**Definition (learn):** Solar and wind are variable and normally non-dispatchable at the resource level. Converting capacity into dependable service needs transmission, forecasting, storage (batteries and pumped storage), demand response, reserves and flexible generation. Installed GW is neither generated MWh nor firm supply.
**Breakdown:**
- Generation → capacity additions (500 GW non-fossil target)
- Transmission → move power from resource-rich regions (Rajasthan and Gujarat) to demand centres
- Storage → batteries, pumped hydro (site-constrained)
- Flexibility → forecasting, demand response, reserves
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25, Advanced Sections 1 and 4)
**Links:** ENV-4119, ENV-4115, ENV-4126
**Trap:** "India's capacity-addition challenge is solved." It still needs commissioned capacity plus integration.
**Mains hook:** Conclude with a three-layer investment recommendation: generation, transmission and storage.
**Back:** *"Generate, carry, store: three layers before capacity counts as supply."*

## ENVIRONMENT AND ECOLOGY — Part 5: Disaster Framework, Institutions and Species/Current-Affairs Tracker

### GROUP — 26 Disaster Management Framework and Sendai (Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard ENV-5001 — Disaster-management cycle (four linked phases)
**Front:** What is the disaster-management cycle, and why is it not a single event response?
**Definition (learn):** A continuous cycle of four linked phases—mitigation, preparedness, response and recovery—in which recovery feeds back into mitigation (✅ owner 26 §1).
**Breakdown:**
- Mitigation: prevention and risk reduction (proactive)
- Preparedness: early warning, training, plans, capacity
- Response: rescue and relief (reactive)
- Recovery: rehabilitation and reconstruction, feeding back into mitigation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** India's Disaster Management Act, 2005 requires the full cycle, not relief alone (✅ owner 26 §3).
**Links:** DM-010 (cycle; Risk-Management vs Crisis-Management phase); DM-009 (resilience); ENV-5002; ENV-5011.
**Trap:** "Disaster management = post-disaster relief." Wrong: prevention, mitigation, capacity-building, preparedness, response and rehabilitation/reconstruction are all inside the cycle (✅ owner 26 §7).
**Mains hook:** Argue that mitigation and preparedness deserve equal policy weight with response. Qualify: for rapid-onset events such as cyclones, response capacity still decides the immediate death toll.
**Back:** *"Mitigate, Prepare, Respond, Recover — and recovery feeds back into mitigation."*

### Flashcard ENV-5002 — Disaster risk as a function of hazard, exposure, vulnerability and capacity
**Front:** How does UNDRR define disaster risk, and what are its components?
**Definition (learn):** Potential loss in a specific period, determined probabilistically as a function of hazard, exposure, vulnerability and capacity (✅ owner 26 §2). The relation is a dynamic interaction, not a literal arithmetic formula.
**Breakdown:**
- Hazard: the potentially damaging natural or human-induced event
- Exposure: people, assets and systems in harm's way
- Vulnerability: susceptibility to damage
- Capacity: the coping and recovery strength that reduces loss
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** UNDRR terminology as recorded in owner 26 (✅ owner 26; UNDRR terminology page not fetched).
**Links:** DM-006 (risk); DM-002 (hazard); DM-003 (exposure); DM-004 (vulnerability); DM-005 (capacity); ENV-5001.
**Trap:** Writing risk as hazard × vulnerability ÷ capacity. Owner 26 says the relation is probabilistic and dynamic; a formula sentence in an answer invites a fact-check failure.
**Mains hook:** Use the components to explain why the same flood kills more people in low-capacity settlements. Qualify that hazard frequency still matters for planning.
**Back:** *"Hazard meets exposure and vulnerability; capacity is what cuts the loss."*

### Flashcard ENV-5003 — Disaster Management Act, 2005: three-tier architecture
**Front:** What three-tier institutional structure does the Disaster Management Act, 2005 create, and who chairs each tier?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA (national), SDMA (State) and DDMA (district), each responsible for disaster-management planning and coordination at its level (✅ owner 26 §2–3).
**Breakdown:**
- NDMA: national apex, chaired by the Prime Minister (✅ owner 26)
- SDMA: State level, chaired by the Chief Minister (✅ owner 26)
- DDMA: district level, chaired by the District Magistrate/Collector (✅ owner 26)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** The Act followed the 2004 Indian Ocean tsunami, which exposed gaps in India's disaster-response coordination (✅ owner 26 §3). Exact composition and powers are owned by Disaster Management Topic 02.
**Links:** DM-030 (Act); DM-033 (NDMA); DM-035 (SDMA); DM-037 (DDMA); ENV-5016; ENV-5029.
**Trap:** Do not transfer the NDMA's Prime Minister chair to the district tier. DM-030 notes an elected co-chair at the district tier; the exact DDMA composition sits in DM-037.
**Mains hook:** Show the cascade from national policy to district operations. Qualify that the cascade is only as strong as its weakest tier (ENV-5027).
**Back:** *"Prime Minister, Chief Minister, Collector: three tiers, one cascade."*

### Flashcard ENV-5004 — Hyogo Framework for Action (2005–2015) and its five priorities
**Front:** What did the Hyogo Framework for Action (2005–2015) establish, and what were its five priorities?
**Definition (learn):** The global DRR framework adopted at Kobe, Japan, in 2005. It already centred DRR and resilience of nations and communities; it was not a response-only framework (✅ owner 26 adv §1).
**Breakdown:** Five priorities (✅ owner 26 adv §1):
- (1) institutional foundations
- (2) risk assessment and early warning
- (3) knowledge and education
- (4) reducing underlying risk factors
- (5) preparedness for effective response
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Period 2005–2015. Kobe launched an International Early Warning Programme (✅ owner 26 §6).
**Links:** DM-012 (Hyogo); ENV-5005; ENV-5009; ENV-5010.
**Trap:** Describing Hyogo as response-only. Owner 26 says it covered risk assessment, underlying risk, resilience and preparedness.
**Mains hook:** Use Hyogo to show continuity, not novelty: Sendai built on an existing DRR foundation.
