**Back:** *"Kobe's five: Institutions, Assess-and-warn, Know, Underlying risk, Prepare."*

### Flashcard ENV-5005 — Sendai Framework 2015–2030: adoption and legal status
**Front:** When and where was the Sendai Framework for Disaster Risk Reduction adopted, and is it legally binding?
**Definition (learn):** The UN framework for DRR for 2015–2030, the successor to Hyogo, adopted at the Third UN World Conference on Disaster Risk Reduction in Sendai, Japan, on 18 March 2015 (✅ UNDRR fetched; ✅ owner 26). It is voluntary and non-binding (✅ owner 26; the fetched UNDRR page does not state this).
**Breakdown:**
- Period: 2015–2030 ("the next 15 years", per UNDRR)
- Successor to Hyogo (2005–2015)
- First major agreement of the post-2015 development agenda (✅ owner 26 §6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Adopted 18 March 2015, Sendai, Japan (✅ UNDRR).
**Links:** DM-013; DM-014 (non-binding status); DM-024 (monitor); ENV-5004; ENV-5006.
**Trap:** Calling Sendai a binding treaty. Its targets are measurable goals with reporting and political accountability, not judicial enforcement.
**Mains hook:** Explain the accountability gap: measurable targets with no enforcement. Qualify that voluntary frameworks still shape domestic plans, as NDMP alignment shows.
**Back:** *"Sendai speaks, it does not sue: voluntary, non-binding, 2015 to 2030."*

### Flashcard ENV-5006 — Sendai's four priorities for action
**Front:** What are the four priorities for action in the Sendai Framework?
**Definition (learn):** The four categories of action under which Sendai organises DRR (✅ UNDRR fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- P1: Understanding disaster risk
- P2: Strengthening disaster risk governance to manage risk
- P3: Investing in disaster risk reduction for resilience
- P4: Enhancing preparedness for effective response, and to "Build Back Better" in recovery, rehabilitation and reconstruction
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Four priorities; seven global targets are a separate list (ENV-5007).
**Links:** DM-013; ENV-5007; ENV-5008; ENV-5030 (first priority trap).
**Trap:** Priorities and targets are different lists. A question asking for "global targets" is asking for A–G.
**Mains hook:** Use the four priorities as a diagnostic: which one is the weakest in a given State's policy?
**Back:** *"Know the risk, Govern it, Invest in it, Prepare and Build Back Better."*

### Flashcard ENV-5007 — Sendai's seven global targets (A–G)
**Front:** What are the seven global targets of the Sendai Framework, and which four reduce loss while which three increase means?
**Definition (learn):** Seven measurable targets (✅ owner 26 §6). The fetched UNDRR page confirms seven targets but does not list them; the list below is owner-grounded and should be verified against the full UNDRR PDF.
**Breakdown:**
- A: substantially reduce global disaster mortality
- B: substantially reduce the number of affected people
- C: substantially reduce direct economic loss in relation to global GDP
- D: substantially reduce damage to critical infrastructure and disruption of basic services
- E: substantially increase the number of countries with national and local DRR strategies (deadline 2020)
- F: substantially enhance international cooperation to developing countries
- G: substantially increase availability of and access to multi-hazard early warning systems and disaster risk information
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** A–D are loss targets; E–G are means targets (✅ owner 26 adv §9). Target E is the only one with an earlier deadline (2020).
**Links:** DM-015 to DM-021 (one card per target); DM-022 (A–D versus E–G asymmetry); ENV-5008.
**Trap:** Target E's 2020 deadline is earlier than the 2030 end-date; do not treat all seven targets as having a 2020 deadline.
**Mains hook:** Present the targets as a measurable baseline and name the measurement gap (ENV-5028).
**Back:** *"A to D cut the losses; E to G build the means."*

### Flashcard ENV-5008 — Priorities versus targets: the Sendai counting trap
**Front:** Why does the "four priorities" versus "seven targets" distinction recur as an exam trap?
**Definition (learn):** Sendai has four priorities for action and seven global targets (A–G). They are different lists; the 2024 GS-III PYQ on resilience asked for the targets (✅ owner 26 §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Hyogo: five priorities (2005–2015)
- Sendai: four priorities (action categories)
- Sendai: seven targets (measurable, A–G)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Three different counts: 5 (Hyogo), 4 (Sendai priorities), 7 (Sendai targets).
**Links:** ENV-5004; ENV-5006; ENV-5007; DM-022.
**Trap:** Swapping the lists or the counts. The question's noun ("priorities" or "targets") decides the answer.
**Mains hook:** In a 15-mark answer, label each list explicitly and cite the target letter when measuring progress.
**Back:** *"Four to act, seven to measure, five was Hyogo."*

### Flashcard ENV-5009 — Sendai deepened, not originated (advanced)
**Front:** How did Sendai deepen, rather than originate, the DRR and resilience agenda established under Hyogo?
**Definition (learn):** Sendai kept the DRR and resilience foundation and sharpened its scope and accountability: explicit prevention of new risk and reduction of existing risk; four priorities; seven measurable targets with baseline logic; wider attention to health, livelihoods, critical infrastructure and systemic risk; and Build Back Better placed in recovery (⚠️ owner 26 adv §1, analytical synthesis).
**Breakdown:**
- Continuity: DRR and resilience (Hyogo)
- Deepening: explicit prevention, measurement, investment, accountability
- Recovery: Build Back Better in recovery, rehabilitation and reconstruction
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Hyogo 2005–2015 (five priorities); Sendai 2015–2030 (four priorities, seven targets).
**Links:** DM-012; DM-013; DM-022; ENV-5004; ENV-5005.
**Trap:** Claiming DRR was invented in 2015, or that Hyogo ignored prevention, early warning or resilience.
**Mains hook:** The 10-mark route: "continuity plus sharper scope, measurement and accountability", with one Sendai example.
**Back:** *"Hyogo laid the floor; Sendai added the measuring tape."*

### Flashcard ENV-5010 — The three World Conferences on DRR: Yokohama, Kobe, Sendai
**Front:** Where were the three World Conferences on Disaster Risk Reduction held, and what did each produce?
**Definition (learn):** Japan hosted all three (✅ owner 26 §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Yokohama 1994: Yokohama Strategy for a Safer World (10 principles; from the mid-term review of the International Decade for Natural Disaster Reduction)
- Kobe 2005: Hyogo Framework for Action 2005–2015 (five priorities; International Early Warning Programme)
- Sendai 2015: Sendai Framework 2015–2030 (first major agreement of the post-2015 development agenda)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1994, 2005, 2015 (✅ owner 26 §6).
**Links:** ENV-5004; ENV-5005; DM-012.
**Trap:** Attributing the Hyogo Framework to Sendai, or the Yokohama Strategy to 2005.
**Mains hook:** The three conferences show the shift from response-led disaster policy toward risk governance, which is the Mains storyline.
**Back:** *"Yokohama's Strategy, Kobe's Hyogo, Sendai's Framework: Japan hosted all three."*

### Flashcard ENV-5011 — Build Back Better
**Front:** What does "Build Back Better" mean in the Sendai Framework?
**Definition (learn):** A Sendai principle that post-disaster recovery, rehabilitation and reconstruction should reduce future risk and improve resilience, not merely restore pre-disaster conditions (✅ owner 26 §2; ✅ UNDRR priority 4 wording).
**Breakdown:**
- Placed within Priority 4 (preparedness and recovery)
- Rebuild infrastructure to higher resilience standards, not to the old vulnerability
- Recovery becomes an investment opportunity
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Priority 4 wording: "Build Back Better" in recovery, rehabilitation and reconstruction (✅ UNDRR fetched).
**Links:** ENV-5006; ENV-5012; DM-013; ENV-5024 (urban-flood reconstruction).
**Trap:** Equating BBB with rebuilding as fast as possible. The principle is about lower future vulnerability.
**Mains hook:** Argue that post-flood reconstruction should carry resilience investment; qualify that BBB needs finance and land-use enforcement to work.
**Back:** *"Rebuild stronger, not just rebuild."*

### Flashcard ENV-5012 — Trap: Build Back Better is not restoration
**Front:** What is the trap in answering "Build Back Better" as restoring the pre-disaster state?
**Definition (learn):** BBB means reducing future vulnerability during reconstruction; it is not exact restoration of the pre-disaster state (✅ owner 26 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Restoration: returns the old state, possibly with the old risk
- BBB: rebuilds to resilience standards, reducing future risk
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 §7 records this as a UPSC trap.
**Links:** ENV-5011; ENV-5009.
**Trap:** The phrase "rebuild as before" is the wrong answer to a BBB question.
**Mains hook:** Use a flood example: a rebuilt embankment that ignores floodplain encroachment restores, but does not build back better.
**Back:** *"Restore is the old risk; Better is the lower risk."*

### Flashcard ENV-5013 — National Disaster Management Plan 2016: five thematic areas
**Front:** What are the five thematic areas of India's National Disaster Management Plan 2016, and what is its documented critique?
**Definition (learn):** India's first National Disaster Management Plan, aligned with the Sendai Framework, the SDGs and the Paris Agreement (✅ owner 26 §6).
**Breakdown:** Five thematic areas:
- Understanding risk
- Inter-agency coordination
- Investing in DRR through structural measures
- Investing in DRR through non-structural measures
- Capacity development
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NDMP 2016 is the first national plan. ⚠️ Documented critique: unlike Sendai or the SDGs, it sets no goals, targets, timeframes or funding projections of its own (owner 26 §6, flagged ⚠️).
**Links:** DM-055 (NDMP 2016); DM-056 (NDMP 2019); ENV-5014; ENV-5005.
**Trap:** Saying the NDMP sets its own quantified targets like Sendai does. It does not, per the critique.
**Mains hook:** Argue that India's plan needs measurable targets to match its Sendai alignment; qualify with the coordination gains of the five-area structure.
**Back:** *"Five areas, no own targets: understand, coordinate, build structurally, build softly, develop capacity."*

### Flashcard ENV-5014 — Revised NDMP 2019 and the current-plan status
**Front:** Which National Disaster Management Plan was the latest published national plan as at the owner's currency date, and what must be re-checked?
**Definition (learn):** The revised NDMP 2019 remained the latest published national plan located as at 5 October 2026 (📰 owner 26 §8). Any later edition or hazard-plan status needs a fresh official check.
**Breakdown:**
- NDMP 2016: first plan (ENV-5013)
- Revised NDMP 2019: latest published as at 5 October 2026 (owner)
- Later edition: unverified; check official sources
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As-of date 5 October 2026 (owner 26). Not checked against an official site (NDMI fetch not attempted).
**Links:** DM-056 (NDMP 2019); ENV-5013; ENV-5019.
**Trap:** Treating 2016 as the current plan, or assuming a later edition exists without a source.
**Mains hook:** Use the plan's Sendai alignment as evidence of policy intent; qualify with implementation evidence.
**Back:** *"2016 started it, 2019 revised it, verify anything later."*

### Flashcard ENV-5015 — Prime Minister's Ten-Point Agenda on DRR (AMCDRR, New Delhi, 2016)
**Front:** Where and when was the Prime Minister's Ten-Point Agenda on Disaster Risk Reduction announced, and how does it relate to Sendai?
**Definition (learn):** Announced at the Asian Ministerial Conference on Disaster Risk Reduction (AMCDRR), New Delhi, 2016; each point maps to one or more Sendai priorities (✅ owner 26 §6).
**Breakdown:**
- A policy agenda, not a statute
- Mapped onto Sendai's four priorities
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** AMCDRR, New Delhi, 2016 (✅ owner 26). DM-025 gives the November 2016 date.
**Links:** DM-025 (ten-point agenda); DM-014 (non-binding trap); ENV-5006.
**Trap:** Calling the ten-point agenda legally binding. It is a policy agenda.
**Mains hook:** Use it as an example of India translating Sendai into national policy language.
**Back:** *"Ten points, one Delhi conference, zero statute."*

### Flashcard ENV-5016 — NIDM, NDRF and IMD as operational supports
**Front:** What do NIDM, NDRF and IMD contribute to disaster preparedness, according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** NIDM provides training, capacity-building and research for disaster-management professionals; NDRF is the specialised trained force for disaster response; IMD provides early-warning data (cyclone and extreme-weather forecasting) central to preparedness (✅ owner 26 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- NIDM: training, capacity-building, research
- NDRF: specialised response force
- IMD: early-warning and forecasting
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only; exact statutory basis in Disaster Management owners.
**Links:** DM-038 (NIDM); DM-039 (NDRF); DM-004 (warning topic routes); ENV-5022.
**Trap:** Treating IMD or NIDM as a response force. NDRF is the operational force; IMD forecasts; NIDM trains.
**Mains hook:** Show that early warning (IMD) and response (NDRF) are different links in one chain; qualify that the chain needs state-level action.
**Back:** *"NIDM teaches, NDRF responds, IMD warns."*

### Flashcard ENV-5017 — Disaster Management (Amendment) Act, 2025 (Act 10 of 2025)
**Front:** What is the Disaster Management (Amendment) Act, 2025, when did it come into force, and what does it update?
**Definition (learn):** Act 10 of 2025 updated the statutory architecture, including disaster databases and provisions on national crisis and high-level bodies and urban authorities (📰 owner 26 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Act 10 of 2025
- In force: 9 April 2025 (📰 owner)
- Covers: disaster databases; national crisis/high-level bodies; urban authorities
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** In force 9 April 2025 (owner 26). Official MHA/NDMI law page cited by owner; not fetched.
**Links:** DM-045 (amendment timeline); DM-046 (crisis and high-level committees); DM-047 (urban authority); DM-053 (current law stack).
**Trap:** Citing the 2025 amendment as having redesigned the three-tier institutional architecture. The owner records it as an update, not a redesign.
**Mains hook:** Use it to show statutory modernisation (databases, urban authorities); qualify that laws do not deliver outcomes without state capacity.
**Back:** *"Act 10 of 2025: databases, crisis bodies, urban authorities; in force 9 April."*

### Flashcard ENV-5018 — Jan Vishwas (Amendment of Provisions) Act, 2026 (Act 8 of 2026)
**Front:** What did the Jan Vishwas (Amendment of Provisions) Act, 2026 change in the Disaster Management Act, and from when?
**Definition (learn):** It amended only the maximum imprisonment terms in DM Act ss. 51–53; it did not redesign the institutional architecture (📰 owner 26 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Act 8 of 2026
- Sections: 51–53 (DM Act)
- Change: maximum imprisonment terms only
- Effective: 15 May 2026 under S.O. 2181(E) (📰 owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 May 2026; S.O. 2181(E) (owner 26). Gazette not fetched.
**Links:** DM-052 (Jan Vishwas); DM-053 (law as at 5 October 2026); ENV-5017.
**Trap:** Thinking Jan Vishwas 2026 is the 2025 amendment. They are separate Acts with separate dates.
**Mains hook:** Use it only as a penal-provision change, not an institutional reform.
**Back:** *"Jan Vishwas 2026 trimmed the jail terms; it left the institutions alone."*

### Flashcard ENV-5019 — Heatwaves and lightning in the notified natural-calamity list (2026–31 guidelines)
**Front:** What did MHA confirm on 4 August 2026 about heatwaves and lightning in India's disaster-relief framework?
**Definition (learn):** MHA confirmed that heatwaves and lightning were included in the nationally notified natural-calamity list for the 2026–31 SDRF/NDRF operational guidelines (📰 owner 26 adv §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Date of confirmation: 4 August 2026
- Scope: the 2026–31 SDRF/NDRF operational guidelines
- Environment owns the climate-risk and vulnerability linkage; eligibility and fund architecture belong to DM Topics 02 and 16
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4 August 2026 (owner 26). Not checked against an MHA page.
**Links:** DM-053 (current law); DM-059 (finance awards); ENV-5022 (heatwave gap); ENV-5017.
**Trap:** Reading notified status as proof of prevention or relief outcomes. Owner 26 says it is not proof of outcomes.
**Mains hook:** Show that heat is now a formal calamity in finance rules, while heat-action capacity is still developing (ENV-5022).
**Back:** *"Heat and lightning joined the calamity list in 2026; listing is not the same as protection."*

### Flashcard ENV-5020 — Ecosystem-based disaster-risk reduction (mangroves, wetlands, forests)
**Front:** How do mangroves, wetlands and forests function as disaster-risk reduction infrastructure?
**Definition (learn):** Healthy ecosystems act as DRR infrastructure: mangroves buffer cyclone storm surge (Topic 24), intact wetlands absorb flood peaks (Topic 07), and stable forest cover reduces landslide and erosion risk (Topics 03, 11–12) (⚠️ owner 26 adv §3, analytical).
**Breakdown:**
- Mangroves: storm-surge buffer
- Wetlands: flood-peak absorption
- Forests: slope stability; landslide and erosion control
- Degrading a mangrove for coastal development removes a regulating service, so it raises risk
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Quantification needs site-specific studies, not one universal figure (⚠️ owner 26 adv §5).
**Links:** ENV-5021; 24_Coastal-and-Marine-Ecology (Topic 24); 07_Biosphere-Reserves-and-Ramsar-Sites (Topic 07); 11 and 12 (forests); ENV-5009.
**Trap:** Treating ecosystem-based measures as a separate environmental silo. Owner 26 says they belong inside DRR planning.
**Mains hook:** Argue for "green plus grey": ecosystem buffers alongside engineered structures; qualify that buffers do not replace enforcement.
**Back:** *"Mangroves hold the surge, wetlands hold the flood, forests hold the slope."*

### Flashcard ENV-5021 — MISHTI, NPCA wetlands and the National Coastal Mission (Economic Survey 2025–26)
**Front:** How does the Economic Survey 2025–26 (Chapter 10) treat ecosystem-based DRR?
**Definition (learn):** Ecosystem-based DRR is now explicit in Indian fiscal policy: MISHTI (mangroves as surge buffers), NPCA-expanded wetlands as flood-moderation buffers, and the National Coastal Mission (📰 owner 26 adv §9).
**Breakdown:**
- MISHTI: mangroves as surge buffers
- NPCA-expanded wetlands: flood moderation
- National Coastal Mission: coastal ecosystem role
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Economic Survey 2025–26, Chapter 10 (owner-cited; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-5020; DM-056 (NDMP alignment); ENV-5024.
**Trap:** Assuming the Survey's acknowledgement of ecosystem buffers means on-ground implementation is automatic.
**Mains hook:** The Government's own budget documents now say intact ecosystems are cheaper disaster infrastructure. Qualify with local capacity (ENV-5027).
**Back:** *"Mangroves, wetlands, coast: the budget now calls nature a DRR asset."*

### Flashcard ENV-5022 — Early-warning maturity gap: cyclones versus heatwaves and urban floods (advanced)
**Front:** Why is India's early-warning capability strongest for cyclones and weaker for heatwaves and urban flooding?
**Definition (learn):** Cyclone early warning and evacuation (IMD forecasting with NDMA/State evacuation protocols) has matured over decades and is credited with lowering casualties in recent major events (✅ owner 26 adv §4). Flash floods, urban flooding, landslides and heatwave action have comparatively newer, less mature institutional attention (⚠️ owner 26 adv §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Mature: cyclones, seasonal floods (predictable hazards)
- Newer: heatwave action plans, urban flooding, flash floods, landslides
- Odisha's cyclone-preparedness improvements are a documented example (⚠️ owner 26 §5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Event-level casualty figures must be cited to their specific source and event (⚠️ owner 26 adv §5).
**Links:** ENV-5016 (IMD); ENV-5019 (heatwave listing); ENV-5024 (urban floods); ENV-5028 (data limits).
**Trap:** Claiming India's preparedness is uniformly mature across hazards. Owner 26 says it is uneven.
**Mains hook:** Compare the cyclone route (decades of practice) with newer heat and urban-flood instruments; qualify that newer does not mean ineffective.
**Back:** *"Cyclones matured over decades; heat and urban floods are the newer frontier."*

### Flashcard ENV-5023 — NDMA Guidelines on Management of Urban Flooding (2010)
**Front:** Which NDMA guidelines frame India's urban-flood management, and what do they cover?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA Guidelines on Management of Urban Flooding (2010), cited by the owner as the core framework for urban flood policy (✅ owner 26 §13.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Floodplain zoning and drainage-catchment design
- CWC–IMD warning and nowcasting
- ULB and EOC response
- Urban Flood Risk Management Programme
- Wetland restoration and risk-sensitive urban planning
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2010 (owner 26 §13.2). Not fetched.
**Links:** ENV-5024; ENV-5026 (PYQ 2024); Disaster-Management owner 08 (flood detail); DM-030 (Act).
**Trap:** Thinking urban flooding is only a drainage problem. The guidelines cover land use and wetlands too.
**Mains hook:** Use the guidelines to argue for storage and land-use governance before emergency pumping.
**Back:** *"Urban floods: zone the plain, design the drains, restore the wetland, warn early."*

### Flashcard ENV-5024 — Urban floods: Mumbai 2005 and Chennai 2015 as causal cases
**Front:** What features made Mumbai 2005 and Chennai 2015 useful as examples of urban flooding?
**Definition (learn):** Two causal cases for urban flood answers (✅ owner 26 §13.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Mumbai 2005: exceptionally intense rainfall met drainage, low-lying and reclaimed-land, and waste-blockage vulnerabilities
- Chennai 2015: northeast-monsoon extreme rainfall combined with altered wetlands and waterways, plus urban drainage and land-use stress
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2005 and 2015 (owner 26). Use as causal features, not decorative names.
**Links:** ENV-5023; ENV-5026; ENV-5022; Disaster-Management owner 08 (flood evidence).
**Trap:** Naming the two cities without their causal features. The 2024 PYQ demands features of two floods.
**Mains hook:** Each case shows a different cause mix: intensity-plus-drainage (Mumbai) and wetland-loss-plus-rain (Chennai).
**Back:** *"Mumbai: rain met drains; Chennai: rain met lost wetlands."*

### Flashcard ENV-5025 — PYQ 2024 GS-III (250 words): disaster resilience and Sendai targets
**Front:** How should a 2024 GS-III answer on disaster resilience and Sendai targets be built?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 PYQ asked three things: what disaster resilience is, how it is determined, and the elements of a resilience framework, plus the global targets of the Sendai Framework (✅ owner 26 §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Definition and determinants: capacity to anticipate, absorb, adapt and recover (exposure, vulnerability, coping capacity, institutions, information) (⚠️ owner 26 §13.1)
- Framework elements: policy and legal framework; risk assessment; risk awareness; implementation through environmental management and urban planning; early warning; use of knowledge (⚠️ standard DRR fields per owner)
- Sendai: list the seven targets A–G, not the priorities
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ wording as recorded in owner 26 §9 (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** ENV-5002; ENV-5007; ENV-5008; DM-027 (PYQ 2024 Q17); DM-013.
**Trap:** Answering the targets part with the four priorities.
**Mains hook:** Close with the local-implementation verdict (ENV-5027).
**Back:** *"Define resilience, list elements, then count to G."*

### Flashcard ENV-5026 — PYQ 2024 GS-III (250 words): urban floods
**Front:** What must a 2024 GS-III answer on urban floods cover?
**Definition (learn):** The PYQ asked for causes, features of two major Indian floods in the last two decades, and policies and frameworks (✅ owner 26 §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Causes: extreme rainfall, sealed surfaces, encroached wetlands and floodplains, drainage blockage, land-use decisions
- Two named floods with features (Mumbai 2005, Chennai 2015: ENV-5024)
- Policies: NDMA urban-flood guidelines (ENV-5023); urban flood risk programme
- Documented challenges: no comprehensive urban-flood risk assessment; weak coordination; poor information sharing; no stakeholder consultation (✅ owner 26 §9)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** The question demands two named floods with features; most answers skip this (owner 26 §9).
**Links:** ENV-5023; ENV-5024; ENV-5025; DM-027.
**Trap:** Describing urban floods without naming two real events.
**Mains hook:** Close on restoring storage and governing land use before relying on emergency pumping (owner 26 §13.2).
**Back:** *"Causes, two floods, frameworks, and the four gaps in risk planning."*

### Flashcard ENV-5027 — Local implementation constraint (advanced)
**Front:** Why do ecosystem buffers and risk-sensitive land use fail even when national policy supports them?
**Definition (learn):** Ecosystem-based and risk-sensitive measures depend on local technical capacity, land-use enforcement, maintenance, participation and data. Without them, national ambition does not translate locally (⚠️ owner 26 adv §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Local technical capacity
- Land-use enforcement
- Maintenance of structures and buffers
- Participation and data
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Exact DDMA/UDMA composition, powers and finance are owned by Disaster Management Topics 02 and 16 (✅ owner 26 adv §2).
**Links:** ENV-5003; ENV-5020; ENV-5029; ENV-5063 (SPCB last-mile logic, Environment file 27).
**Trap:** Treating a national DRR policy's recognition of ecosystems as automatic local implementation.
**Mains hook:** Conclude with a locally executable resilience verdict; route the legal proof to Disaster Management.
**Back:** *"Policy travels, enforcement stops at the district gate."*

### Flashcard ENV-5028 — Data and conceptual limits on disaster-loss comparisons (advanced)
**Front:** What data limits should an answer state before comparing disaster casualties or measuring ecosystem DRR value?
**Definition (learn):** Casualty comparisons across events can vary in reporting completeness and definition. District capacity data is not uniformly published. The DRR value of a mangrove belt needs site-specific science (⚠️ owner 26 adv §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Cite the specific event and its official casualty figure
- Attribute district capacity claims to the parliamentary committee, NDMA assessment or academic study
- Quantify ecosystem DRR value only with site studies
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No single universal figure exists (⚠️ owner).
**Links:** ENV-5022; ENV-5020; ENV-5027.
**Trap:** Quoting "X deaths fewer than in year Y" without the event, source and definition.
**Mains hook:** Qualify every casualty comparison and cite the source.
**Back:** *"Name the event, the source, and the definition before comparing deaths."*

### Flashcard ENV-5029 — Ownership boundary: Environment Topic 26 versus the Disaster Management owners
**Front:** Which disaster-management content does the Environment owner 26 route to other owners rather than teach?
**Definition (learn):** Environment owner 26 covers the environment-facing interface only. Direct risk concepts, law, institutions, plan cycles, finance, Incident Response System and warning architecture belong to the Disaster Management owners (✅ owner 26 header).
**Breakdown:**
- Owner 26 keeps: Sendai's environmental implications, priority/target distinctions, ecosystem DRR, local ecosystem implementation
- Routed out: NDMA/SDMA/DDMA law and composition (DM Topic 02); warning (Topic 04); finance (Topic 16); IRS; international governance (Topic 18)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ownership map as recorded in owner 26.
**Links:** DM-030; DM-054 (IRS); ENV-5003; ENV-5027.
**Trap:** Answering a DDMA composition question from the Environment owner. Route it to the Disaster Management card.
**Mains hook:** Show disciplined ownership: cite the environmental link, then route the legal proof.
**Back:** *"Environment supplies the ecosystem; Disaster Management supplies the law."*

### Flashcard ENV-5030 — Trap: Sendai's first priority is understanding risk, not response (advanced)
**Front:** Which Sendai priority comes first, and why does it matter for answer framing?
**Definition (learn):** Priority 1 is "understanding disaster risk". It reflects the shift from response-first framing to risk reduction and prevention (✅ owner 26 adv §8; ✅ UNDRR priority list).
**Breakdown:**
- P1 understanding risk
- P2 governance
- P3 investment
- P4 preparedness and BBB
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Priority order from UNDRR fetch.
**Links:** ENV-5006; ENV-5009; DM-013.
**Trap:** Naming "effective response" as the first priority.
**Mains hook:** Start a DRR answer with risk understanding, then governance and investment.
**Back:** *"Understand first, respond fourth."*

### GROUP — 27 Environmental Institutions MoEFCC CPCB NBA WII (Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard ENV-5031 — India's environmental-institution map by primary function
**Front:** How is India's environmental-governance architecture divided by primary function?
**Definition (learn):** Governance is distributed across a differentiated ecosystem, not one regulator: policy (MoEFCC), pollution regulation (CPCB/SPCBs/PCCs), biodiversity access (NBA/SBBs/BMCs), wildlife enforcement (WCCB, NTCA), science (WII, NCSCM, GBPNIHE, ICFRE, FSI/BSI/ZSI) and adjudication (NGT) (✅ owner 27 §1–2).
**Breakdown:**
- Policy/coordination: MoEFCC
- Pollution regulation: CPCB (national) plus SPCBs/PCCs (site-level)
- Biodiversity access: NBA, SBBs, BMCs
- Wildlife enforcement: WCCB; NTCA (tiger)
- Science: WII, NCSCM, GBPNIHE, ICFRE; FSI, BSI, ZSI
- Adjudication: NGT
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Each body has a distinct legal source and bounded mandate (✅ owner 27).
**Links:** ENV-5058 (statutory-basis pairing); ENV-5036 (overlap trap); ENV-5062 (coordination cost); ENV-5057 (NGT).
**Trap:** Treating environmental bodies as interchangeable because their names overlap. Match each to its founding Act.
**Mains hook:** Argue that India's problem is coordination and capacity more than missing bodies (ENV-5062).
**Back:** *"Ministry makes policy, boards regulate, institutes advise, tribunal decides."*

### Flashcard ENV-5032 — MoEFCC: executive ministry under the Allocation of Business Rules
**Front:** What is the legal basis of the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change, and what is its limit as a regulator?
**Definition (learn):** The Union executive ministry whose subjects are allocated under the Government of India (Allocation of Business) Rules, 1961, made under Article 77(3). It is not a constitutional body or an all-purpose statutory regulator (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: Allocation of Business Rules, 1961 (✅ owner 27)
- Renamed to its present title in May 2014 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Lineage: Department of Environment (1980), Ministry of Environment and Forests (1985) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Statutory powers usually vest in the Central Government and are exercised through the Ministry, notified authorities or delegated bodies (✅ owner 27)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Constitutional context (Art. 48A, added 1977 per Wikipedia; Art. 51A(g); Art. 21 jurisprudence) supplies context but does not create the Ministry (✅ owner 27 §6; Art. 48A year ✅ Wiki).
**Links:** ENV-5031; ENV-5036; ENV-5069 (legal-form ladder).
**Trap:** Calling MoEFCC a statutory regulator or the universal consent authority. It frames policy and delegates.
**Mains hook:** Use the allocation-of-business basis to show why policy and enforcement sit in different bodies; qualify that the Ministry's policy role is substantial.
**Back:** *"Allocated by rules, not created by statute: MoEFCC steers, it does not sign every consent."*

### Flashcard ENV-5033 — CPCB: constituted under the Water Act, 1974, with Air Act functions
**Front:** How was the Central Pollution Control Board constituted, and what powers does it carry under the Air Act?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory Central Board constituted under the Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974, and entrusted with Central-Board powers and functions under the Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1981 (✅ owner 27; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Constituted: 1974 (formation date 22 September 1974, per Wikipedia infobox)
- Air Act 1981: Central-Board functions
- Coordinates State Boards, provides technical guidance and settles disputes among them (✅ Wikipedia)
- Runs NAMP and water-quality monitoring networks (✅ Wikipedia)
- Computes CEPI and identifies Critically Polluted Areas (score 70 or more) (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1974 (✅ Wiki); 1981 Air Act (✅ owner 27); CEPI threshold ≥70 (✅ Wiki).
**Links:** ENV-5034; ENV-5035; ENV-5036; ENV-5063.
**Trap:** Saying CPCB is constituted under the Air Act. Its base is the Water Act; the Air Act gives it Central-Board functions.
**Mains hook:** Use CEPI to show a national monitoring role without site-level consent power.
**Back:** *"Water Act made it, Air Act empowered it, CEPI names the critical areas."*

### Flashcard ENV-5034 — CPCB and the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986: technical and delegated role
**Front:** What role does CPCB play under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986?
**Definition (learn):** Under the EPA framework CPCB performs technical or field-formation roles and specifically delegated functions. General EPA standards are notified by the Central Government (✅ owner 27 §3; ✅ Wikipedia for technical services to the Ministry under the EPA).
**Breakdown:**
- EPA powers ordinarily remain with the Central Government unless specifically delegated
- CPCB: technical services to the Ministry (✅ Wiki)
- Not a universal site-level enforcer under the EPA
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** EPA 1986 (✅ owner 27).
**Links:** ENV-5033; ENV-5032; ENV-5036.
**Trap:** "CPCB notifies all EPA standards." The owner says the Central Government notifies general standards.
**Mains hook:** Use it to show the boundary between national standard-setting and state enforcement.
**Back:** *"CPCB advises under EPA; the Centre notifies the standards."*

### Flashcard ENV-5035 — SPCBs and PCCs: site-level consent, monitoring and enforcement
**Front:** Which bodies ordinarily grant Consent to Establish and Consent to Operate, and what happens in Union Territories?
**Definition (learn):** State Pollution Control Boards (SPCBs) under the Water and Air Acts ordinarily handle site-specific consent, inspection, monitoring and enforcement. In UTs, CPCB exercises State-Board functions and may delegate them to a Pollution Control Committee (PCC) (✅ owner 27 §2–3).
**Breakdown:**
- SPCB: State Boards under Water/Air framework
- PCC: UT body receiving delegated State-Board functions
- Functions: consent (CTE/CTO), inspection, monitoring, directions, enforcement
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 §2 and §3.
**Links:** ENV-5033; ENV-5036; ENV-5063 (last-mile capacity).
**Trap:** Assuming MoEFCC or CPCB grants industrial consents everywhere.
**Mains hook:** The consent-enforcement gap at State level is the "last mile" of pollution governance (ENV-5063).
**Back:** *"States consent and enforce; the Centre sets the frame."*

### Flashcard ENV-5036 — Trap: CPCB, NBA and MoEFCC are not interchangeable regulators
**Front:** Why does "CPCB and NBA have overlapping regulatory functions" fail as a statement?
**Definition (learn):** CPCB performs pollution-control functions under the Water and Air framework. NBA exercises only the biodiversity functions assigned by the Biological Diversity Act, 2002. MoEFCC frames policy, rules and directions (✅ owner 27 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- CPCB: pollution (Water Act 1974, Air Act 1981)
- NBA: biodiversity access (Biological Diversity Act 2002)
- MoEFCC: policy and administrative ministry
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Statutory pairing: ENV-5058.
**Links:** ENV-5033; ENV-5037; ENV-5058.
**Trap:** The owner lists both "CPCB and NBA are interchangeable" and "MoEFCC grants Water/Air consents everywhere" as traps.
**Mains hook:** Use the Act-to-body mapping to open a multi-body governance answer.
**Back:** *"Pollution is CPCB's Act, biodiversity is NBA's Act, policy is MoEFCC's allocation."*

### Flashcard ENV-5037 — National Biodiversity Authority (NBA): Biological Diversity Act, 2002
**Front:** What is the National Biodiversity Authority, when was it established, where is it headquartered, and under which Act?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory autonomous body under the Ministry of Environment, Forests and Climate Change, set up to carry out the Biological Diversity Act, 2002 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 27 for Act and statutory role).
**Breakdown:**
- Established: 2003, formation date 1 October 2003 (✅ Wikipedia; owner 27 does not state the year)
- Headquarters: Chennai (✅ Wikipedia)
- Mandate: conservation, sustainable use and fair and equitable benefit-sharing from biological resources; regulates access to biological resources and associated knowledge (✅ Wikipedia)
- Advises State governments on biodiversity-rich heritage sites (✅ Wikipedia)
- Regulates access under section 3(2) and advises on section 7 route (ENV-5038)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2003 (✅ Wiki); 2002 Act (✅ owner 27 and Wiki).
**Links:** ENV-5038; ENV-5039; ENV-5040; ENV-5041; ENV-5036.
**Trap:** Thinking NBA approves all domestic biodiversity research. Its prior approval is confined to section 3(2) persons and covered activity.
**Mains hook:** Use NBA as the national access-and-benefit-sharing body, with the State biodiversity boards handling the section 7 route.
**Back:** *"NBA: Chennai-based, 2003-born, child of the 2002 Biodiversity Act."*

### Flashcard ENV-5038 — NBA prior approval (section 3(2)) versus SBB prior intimation (section 7)
**Front:** Which persons need NBA prior approval, and which must give prior intimation to a State Biodiversity Board?
**Definition (learn):** Section 3(2) persons, including non-citizens, NRIs and covered foreign-controlled or non-Indian entities, need prior NBA approval for covered access. Other persons give prior intimation to the SBB under section 7 only for commercial utilisation, subject to exemptions (✅ owner 27 §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Section 3(2): NBA prior approval for covered access
- Section 7: prior intimation to the SBB for commercial utilisation
- Exemptions: codified traditional knowledge; qualifying cultivated medicinal plants or products; local people and communities; growers and cultivators; specified traditional-medicine practitioners (✅ owner 27)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Route is applicant- and activity-specific (✅ owner 27 §6).
**Links:** ENV-5037; ENV-5039; ENV-5040; ENV-5036.
**Trap:** "Every Indian or every research activity needs NBA approval." The owner says this is an obsolete formula.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish applicant, purpose, exemption, documentation and benefit delivery in any access question.
**Back:** *"Section 3(2) asks NBA first; section 7 tells the SBB after."*

### Flashcard ENV-5039 — State Biodiversity Boards and Biodiversity Management Committees (BMCs)
**Front:** What do State Biodiversity Boards (SBBs) and Biodiversity Management Committees (BMCs) do?
**Definition (learn):** The three-level biodiversity architecture is NBA (national), SBBs (State), and BMCs (local). BMCs support local conservation, documentation, consultation, People's Biodiversity Registers and collection fees (✅ owner 27 §3).
**Breakdown:**
- NBA: national regulation and approval (section 3(2))
- SBB: receives section 7 prior intimation
- BMC: local conservation, documentation, People's Biodiversity Registers, collection fees
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 §3 and §4.
**Links:** ENV-5037; ENV-5038; ENV-5040.
**Trap:** Treating BMCs as national regulators. Their functions are local.
**Mains hook:** Use the three-level design to argue for community-level benefit-sharing; qualify with documentation capacity.
**Back:** *"NBA national, SBB State, BMC village: a three-level biodiversity ladder."*

### Flashcard ENV-5040 — Biological Diversity Rules, 2024 and the 2025 Amendment Rules
**Front:** What do the Biological Diversity Rules, 2024 and the Biological Diversity (Amendment) Rules, 2025 provide, and from when?
**Definition (learn):** The Biological Diversity Rules, 2024 (G.S.R. 665(E), notified 22 October 2024) superseded the 2004 Rules and came into force on 21 December 2024. The Biological Diversity (Amendment) Rules, 2025, effective 1 November 2025, revised the Rule 19 certificate-of-origin procedure for cultivated medicinal plants (📰 owner 27 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- 2024 Rules: G.S.R. 665(E), notified 22 October 2024; in force 21 December 2024; supersede the 2004 Rules
- 2025 Amendment: effective 1 November 2025; Rule 19 certificate-of-origin for cultivated medicinal plants
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dates as in owner 27 §8. Gazette not fetched.
**Links:** ENV-5037; ENV-5038; ENV-5041.
**Trap:** Citing the 2004 Rules as current, or dating the 2025 amendment to 2024.
**Mains hook:** Show that rules are updated on different dates; use the rule number when citing.
**Back:** *"2024 Rules replaced 2004; 2025 amendment changed Rule 19 from 1 November."*

### Flashcard ENV-5041 — ABS Regulations, 2025 and digital sequence information (DSI)
**Front:** What do the Biological Diversity (Access ... and Fair and Equitable Sharing of Benefits) Regulations, 2025 do?
**Definition (learn):** The 2025 ABS Regulations superseded the 2014 ABS Regulations and prescribe benefit-sharing modes for access, including digital sequence information (DSI) under the applicable statutory route (📰 owner 27 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Supersede: 2014 ABS Regulations
- Benefit-sharing modes for access
- DSI covered under the applicable route
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 §8 (not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-5038; ENV-5040; ENV-5037.
**Trap:** Assuming DSI coverage makes every domestic research activity an NBA prior-approval case. Owner says it does not.
**Mains hook:** Use DSI as an example of benefit-sharing extending to digital data; qualify with route-specific rules.
**Back:** *"2025 ABS replaced 2014; DSI joins the benefit-sharing list, not every lab."*

### Flashcard ENV-5042 — Wildlife Institute of India (WII): research and training, not regulation
**Front:** What is the Wildlife Institute of India, where is it based, and what is its legal form?
**Definition (learn):** An autonomous institution under MoEFCC for wildlife research, training and advice. It is not a statutory regulator (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Location: Dehradun, Uttarakhand (✅ owner 27; ✅ Wikipedia)
- Established: 1982 (✅ Wikipedia; owner does not state year)
- Work: population, habitat and connectivity studies; advice on tiger monitoring and wildlife management
- Not: consent, prosecution or clearance authority
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Autonomous MoEFCC institution (✅ owner 27). Wikipedia calls it a research institution and does not state legal form.
**Links:** ENV-5043 (NTCA); ENV-5044 (WCCB); ENV-5046 (FSI); ENV-5064.
**Trap:** Calling WII a regulatory or enforcement body. Owner 27 lists this as a trap.
**Mains hook:** Use the WII-to-NTCA science-to-policy pipeline as an evidence model; qualify that science informs but does not decide.
**Back:** *"WII studies the wild and advises; it does not permit or prosecute."*

### Flashcard ENV-5043 — National Tiger Conservation Authority (NTCA)
**Front:** What is the NTCA, and under which Act does it operate?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory body overseeing Project Tiger and Tiger Reserve management, operating under the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972 framework (✅ owner 27 §2, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory: yes (✅ owner 27)
- Scope: tiger-specific conservation and Tiger Reserves
- Works with State forest and wildlife departments
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** WPA 1972 framework (✅ owner 27 §3; WPA year ✅ owner).
**Links:** ENV-5044; ENV-5045 (NTCA vs WCCB trap); ENV-5042; 06_Protected-Area-Network-India.
**Trap:** Calling NTCA the all-species wildlife-crime body. That is WCCB.
**Mains hook:** Use NTCA with WII research to show species-specific governance.
**Back:** *"NTCA guards the tiger; WCCB guards the wildlife trade."*

### Flashcard ENV-5044 — Wildlife Crime Control Bureau (WCCB)
**Front:** What is the Wildlife Crime Control Bureau, and what does it coordinate?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory, multi-disciplinary body coordinating action against organised wildlife crime across species, under the Wildlife (Protection) Act framework (✅ owner 27 §2, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory: yes
- Multi-disciplinary coordination
- Works alongside State forest and wildlife departments and enforcement agencies
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 §2.
**Links:** ENV-5043; ENV-5045; 09_CITES-and-Wildlife-Trade (Topic 09).
**Trap:** Calling WCCB the tiger body. It covers all species.
**Mains hook:** Link WCCB to CITES enforcement for trafficked species.
**Back:** *"WCCB coordinates the crime; it covers all species."*

### Flashcard ENV-5045 — Trap: NTCA and WCCB are different statutory bodies
**Front:** Why are NTCA and WCCB not the same body with different names?
**Definition (learn):** NTCA is specifically for tiger conservation and Project Tiger. WCCB is a broader wildlife-crime-coordination body covering all species (✅ owner 27 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- NTCA: tiger (species-specific)
- WCCB: crime coordination (all species)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27.
**Links:** ENV-5043; ENV-5044.
**Trap:** Matching "tiger crime" to NTCA alone. Trafficking coordination is WCCB's mandate.
**Mains hook:** Separate conservation management (NTCA) from enforcement coordination (WCCB).
**Back:** *"Tiger's home is NTCA; the crime desk is WCCB."*

### Flashcard ENV-5046 — Forest Survey of India (FSI) and the biennial ISFR
**Front:** What does the Forest Survey of India do, and what is its key publication?
**Definition (learn):** A MoEFCC government survey organisation assessing forest resources and publishing the biennial India State of Forest Report (ISFR) (✅ owner 27 §3, §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Forest-cover and resource assessment
- Output: ISFR (biennial)
- Evidence, not permissions or sanctions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Biennial ISFR (✅ owner 27).
**Links:** ENV-5047 (BSI); ENV-5048 (ZSI); 12_Forest-Governance-CAMPA-and-Green-India-Mission (Topic 12).
**Trap:** Treating canopy density as proof of legal forest status, native ecology or restoration. Owner 27 §5 says canopy indicators alone cannot prove these.
**Mains hook:** Use ISFR data for policy while flagging its canopy-only limit.
**Back:** *"FSI counts the canopy; it does not certify the forest."*

### Flashcard ENV-5047 — Botanical Survey of India (BSI)
**Front:** What does the Botanical Survey of India do?
**Definition (learn):** A MoEFCC government survey organisation that surveys and documents India's flora and provides taxonomic and distribution evidence (✅ owner 27 §2; ✅ owner 28 §4 for taxonomy role).
**Breakdown:**
- Survey and document flora
- Taxonomic and distribution records
- Evidence, not regulatory decisions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Government survey organisation (✅ owner 27).
**Links:** ENV-5046 (FSI); ENV-5048 (ZSI); ENV-5088 (Foxtail orchid taxonomy); ENV-5059 (NIRANTAR lead).
**Trap:** Treating a BSI record as proof of recovery or of a legal listing.
**Mains hook:** BSI records feed taxonomy-based species cases (Species file 28).
**Back:** *"BSI names the plants; it does not license them."*

### Flashcard ENV-5048 — Zoological Survey of India (ZSI)
**Front:** What does the Zoological Survey of India do?
**Definition (learn):** A MoEFCC government survey organisation that surveys and documents India's fauna and provides taxonomic and distribution evidence (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Survey and document fauna
- Taxonomic and distribution records
- Evidence, not sanctions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Government survey organisation (✅ owner 27).
**Links:** ENV-5047 (BSI); ENV-5046 (FSI); ENV-5089 (Gooty tarantula taxonomy).
**Trap:** A ZSI record is not a legal listing or a conservation outcome.
**Mains hook:** Use ZSI taxonomic records as the identity step in species governance.
**Back:** *"ZSI documents the animals; the law comes from elsewhere."*

### Flashcard ENV-5049 — Indian Council of Forestry Research and Education (ICFRE)
**Front:** What is the ICFRE, and what is its legal form?
**Definition (learn):** An autonomous council under MoEFCC and a registered society, coordinating forestry research, education and extension (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Legal form: autonomous council and registered society
- Work: research, education, extension
- Autonomy does not create regulatory power
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27.
**Links:** ENV-5050; ENV-5059 (NIRANTAR: ICFRE leads ecosystem-service vertical); ENV-5031.
**Trap:** Calling ICFRE an autonomous institute only. Owner: it is an autonomous council and registered society.
**Mains hook:** Research advice needs field adoption and outcome monitoring (owner 27 adv §3).
**Back:** *"ICFRE researches the forest; the forest still needs adoption."*

### Flashcard ENV-5050 — NCSCM and GBPNIHE: coastal and Himalayan research institutes
**Front:** What do the National Centre for Sustainable Coastal Management (NCSCM) and the G.B. Pant National Institute of Himalayan Environment (GBPNIHE) research?
**Definition (learn):** Both are autonomous MoEFCC institutions. NCSCM covers coastal and marine management; GBPNIHE covers the Himalayan environment (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- NCSCM: coastal and marine research; leads the Climate Change Support vertical of NIRANTAR (✅ owner 27 §13.4)
- GBPNIHE: Himalayan environment research; in the same NIRANTAR vertical (✅ owner 27)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NIRANTAR page uses a variant label for GBPNIHE; the institute's own site controls the name (✅ owner 27 §13.4).
**Links:** ENV-5049; ENV-5059; 24_Coastal-and-Marine-Ecology (Topic 24).
**Trap:** Assuming research status confers consent or sanction power. Owner 27 says NIRANTAR coordination confers none.
**Mains hook:** Use these institutes as evidence sources for coastal and Himalayan policy.
**Back:** *"NCSCM watches the coast, GBPNIHE watches the Himalaya; neither permits."*

### Flashcard ENV-5051 — CAQM: statutory commission for the NCR (CAQM Act, 2021)
**Front:** What is the Commission for Air Quality Management (CAQM), under which Act, and over what area does it operate?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory regional commission under the CAQM Act, 2021 (preceded by a 2020 Ordinance), for NCR and Adjoining Areas. Within its statutory field, its directions prevail over inconsistent directions of covered authorities, including CPCB, SPCBs and PCCs (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Act: CAQM Act, 2021; preceded by a 2020 Ordinance
- Area: NCR and adjoining areas (not nationwide)
- Precedence: CAQM directions prevail within its field
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Ordinance; 2021 Act (✅ owner 27).
**Links:** ENV-5035 (SPCBs); ENV-5036; ENV-5062 (coordination example); 13_Air-Pollution-and-CPCB-Standards (Topic 13).
**Trap:** Calling CAQM an all-India pollution board. Owner 27 says it is not.
**Mains hook:** Use CAQM as a designed coordination mechanism for transboundary air pollution.
**Back:** *"CAQM rules the NCR, not India: its directions outrank in its field."*

### Flashcard ENV-5052 — Bureau of Energy Efficiency (BEE): Energy Conservation Act, 2001
**Front:** What is the Bureau of Energy Efficiency, under which Ministry and Act, and what is its role in climate policy?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory body of the Ministry of Power under the Energy Conservation Act, 2001. It administers PAT and CCTS and develops or finalises recommendations for entity-level GHG emission-intensity targets (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry: Power
- Act: Energy Conservation Act, 2001
- Work: PAT, CCTS, target recommendations
- Not the pollution-consent authority and not the final MoEFCC target-notifying authority (owner 27)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 §2.
**Links:** 21_Carbon-Markets-CCUS-and-Direct-Air-Capture (Topic 21); ENV-5036.
**Trap:** Treating BEE as MoEFCC's carbon-target notifier, or as a pollution-consent body.
**Mains hook:** Use BEE to show that climate-market design spans ministries.
**Back:** *"BEE sets energy efficiency and carbon-market recommendations; it does not consent pollution."*

### Flashcard ENV-5053 — National and State Coastal Zone Management Authorities (NCZMA, SCZMA)
**Front:** Under which Act were the NCZMA and SCZMAs constituted, and what do they do?
**Definition (learn):** Constituted under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986, to appraise and monitor CRZ compliance (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Act: EPA 1986
- Function: appraise and monitor CRZ compliance
- Levels: national (NCZMA) and State (SCZMA)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 §2.
**Links:** 24_Coastal-and-Marine-Ecology (Topic 24); ENV-5020 (mangroves); ENV-5034.
**Trap:** Assuming the CRZ regime is a separate statute. It runs through EPA notifications and authorities.
**Mains hook:** Use CRZ monitoring to link coastal development with storm-surge risk.
**Back:** *"CRZ is watched by NCZMA nationally and SCZMA by State."*

### Flashcard ENV-5054 — NMCG: registered society under the Ganga order, under the National Ganga Council
**Front:** What is the National Mission for Clean Ganga (NMCG), and what is its legal form?
**Definition (learn):** A registered society vested with the powers of an Authority under the 2016 Order issued under the EPA 1986, working under the National Ganga Council chaired by the Prime Minister (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Legal form: registered society (not a statutory board like CPCB)
- Powers: Authority powers under the 2016 Order
- Oversight: National Ganga Council, chaired by PM
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2016 Order; National Ganga Council chaired by PM (✅ owner 27).
**Links:** 14_Water-Pollution-and-River-Cleaning-Missions (Topic 14); ENV-5056 (PM-chaired set); ENV-5033.
**Trap:** Calling NMCG a statutory board like CPCB. Owner 27 flags this.
**Mains hook:** Use NMCG to show how a society with delegated powers can run a river mission.
**Back:** *"NMCG: a registered society with Ganga powers, reporting to a PM-chaired Council."*

### Flashcard ENV-5055 — National Board for Wildlife (NBWL)
**Front:** What is the National Board for Wildlife, who chairs it, and what does its Standing Committee do?
**Definition (learn):** The NBWL is chaired by the Prime Minister; a Standing Committee is chaired by the Environment Minister. It performs statutory wildlife-policy functions and specified protected-area recommendations, not routine field enforcement (✅ owner 27 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Chair: Prime Minister
- Standing Committee: chaired by the Environment Minister
- Functions: wildlife policy; protected-area recommendations
- Not: routine enforcement
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 §2.
**Links:** ENV-5056; 06_Protected-Area-Network-India (Topic 06); ENV-5043.
**Trap:** Treating NBWL as an enforcement agency.
**Mains hook:** Use NBWL in protected-area clearance questions.
**Back:** *"NBWL: PM chairs the policy, the Minister runs the committee, the field is someone else's."*

### Flashcard ENV-5056 — Bodies chaired by the Prime Minister: NBWL, NDMA and National Ganga Council
**Front:** Which three bodies in this family are chaired by the Prime Minister?
**Definition (learn):** The NBWL, the NDMA and the National Ganga Council (✅ owner 27 §6).
**Breakdown:**
- National Board for Wildlife (NBWL)
- National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA; DM Act 2005)
- National Ganga Council (NMCG oversight)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Frequently examined set (owner 27 §6).
**Links:** ENV-5055; ENV-5054; DM-033 (NDMA).
**Trap:** Naming the Union Home Minister as NDMA chair (a DM trap, DM-033).
**Mains hook:** Use the PM-chaired set to show the Prime Minister's direct stake in environment and disaster policy.
**Back:** *"Wild, Disaster, Ganga: three bodies, one chair."*

### Flashcard ENV-5057 — National Green Tribunal (NGT): specialised adjudicator
**Front:** What is the NGT's role in India's environmental institutions, and under which Act?
**Definition (learn):** The specialised statutory environmental tribunal at the adjudicatory apex of the institutional ecosystem, constituted under the NGT Act, 2010 (✅ owner 27 adv §4; NGT Act year ✅ owner 27 adv grounding).
**Breakdown:**
- Adjudicates disputes over SPCB/PCC consents, board directions, MoEFCC clearances and matters in its scheduled enactments
- Not a route for every statute: Wildlife (Protection) Act and Indian Forest Act are not independent Schedule I routes (✅ owner 27 adv §4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NGT Act 2010 (✅ owner 27 adv).
**Links:** ENV-5068 (implementation dependence); 16_Environmental-Impact-Assessment-and-NGT (Topic 16); ENV-5031.
**Trap:** Assuming NGT hears any matter under the Wildlife Act. Owner 27 says it is not a Schedule I route.
**Mains hook:** Use the NGT as a check that works only as far as state implementation does (ENV-5068).
**Back:** *"NGT judges within its schedule; executing its orders is the State's job."*

### Flashcard ENV-5058 — Statutory-basis pairing: which Act founds which body
**Front:** Which Act constitutes or empowers each of the main environmental bodies?
**Definition (learn):** Statutory basis is the discriminator, not subject matter (✅ owner 27 §6).
**Breakdown:**
- CPCB and SPCB: Water Act 1974 and Air Act 1981
- NBA, SBB, BMC: Biological Diversity Act 2002
- NTCA and WCCB: Wildlife (Protection) Act 1972
- NGT: NGT Act 2010
- NDMA, SDMA, DDMA: Disaster Management Act 2005
- BEE: Energy Conservation Act 2001
- CAQM: CAQM Act 2021
- NCZMA, SCZMA and NMCG: orders and notifications under EPA 1986
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** All dates as owner 27 §6.
**Links:** ENV-5031; ENV-5036; DM-030 (DM Act).
**Trap:** Assigning the NGT to the EPA 1986 or NBA to the Water Act. Match the Act to the body.
**Mains hook:** Start any multi-body governance answer with the statutory map.
**Back:** *"Water and Air make CPCB; Biodiversity makes NBA; Wildlife makes NTCA and WCCB; NGT is its own Act."*

### Flashcard ENV-5059 — NIRANTAR: MoEFCC environmental research platform (2026 PYQ)
**Front:** What is NIRANTAR, which four verticals does it list, and what does it not confer?
**Definition (learn):** NIRANTAR is a MoEFCC coordination and knowledge platform, not a statutory regulator (✅ owner 27 §13.4, from the official NIRANTAR portal as recorded by the owner; not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Ecosystem Survey and Analysis: BSI
- Research and Management of Ecosystem Service: ICFRE
- Capacity Development Support: IIFM
- Climate Change Support: NCSCM (also includes GBPNIHE; the NIRANTAR page uses a variant label)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q77 (owner 27 PYQ block). Answer not recorded here; the 2026 key is provisional.
**Links:** ENV-5047 (BSI); ENV-5049 (ICFRE); ENV-5050 (NCSCM, GBPNIHE); ENV-5036.
**Trap:** Calling NIRANTAR a new statutory regulator. Coordination confers no consent or enforcement power.
**Mains hook:** Use it as an example of coordination without new legal powers, which is itself the coordination-cost debate (ENV-5062).
**Back:** *"Four verticals, four leads: survey, service, capacity, climate. No new permit."*

### Flashcard ENV-5060 — Central Ground Water Authority: districts overexploited (2020 PYQ, legal basis)
**Front:** What is the legal basis of the Central Ground Water Authority, which handles overexploited groundwater districts?
**Definition (learn):** The 2020 Prelims GS-I PYQ asked about the legal basis for districts classed as overexploited by the Central Ground Water Authority (owner 27 PYQ block). The owner routes this as an executive-versus-statutory classification point.
**Breakdown:**
- Classification: executive versus statutory body
- Owner-routed demand; neutral rendering; answer not recorded here
- ⚠️ CGWA's constitution under the EPA 1986 is standard knowledge, verify before citing
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Q98 (owner 27). Official-final key held separately.
**Links:** ENV-5034; ENV-5061; ENV-5058.
**Trap:** Classifying CGWA as a statutory board under the Water Act. Check the constituting order.
**Mains hook:** Use the groundwater authority to show how executive bodies can regulate resource use.
**Back:** *"Check the constituting order before you call it statutory."*

### Flashcard ENV-5061 — Body constituted under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 (2022 PYQ)
**Front:** Which bodies are constituted under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986?
**Definition (learn):** The 2022 Prelims GS-I PYQ asked which body is constituted under the EPA 1986 (owner 27 PYQ block; neutral rendering, answer not recorded here).
**Breakdown:**
- Routed owner demand: body constituted under EPA 1986
- Candidate bodies in this file's map: NCZMA and SCZMA (EPA orders, ENV-5053); NMCG (EPA order, ENV-5054); CGWA (⚠️ verify)
- Do not confuse with the Water Act (CPCB) or the NGT Act (NGT)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Q75 (owner 27).
**Links:** ENV-5058; ENV-5053; ENV-5054; ENV-5060.
**Trap:** Picking a body by its environmental subject rather than its founding Act.
**Mains hook:** Use the Act-first method for any "which body" question.
**Back:** *"Match the Act, not the subject."*

### Flashcard ENV-5062 — Specialisation versus coordination cost (advanced)
**Front:** Is India's fragmented, multi-body environmental-institution architecture a strength or a weakness?
**Definition (learn):** Both. Deliberate functional specialisation gives technical focus, but it creates a coordination cost. Owner 27 cites the WPA–FRA relocation-sequencing gap (Topics 06, 11), the CAQM-versus-State-SPCB challenge (Topic 13) and the CAMPA-versus-Green-India-Mission tracking divergence (Topic 12) (⚠️ owner 27 adv §1, analytical).
**Breakdown:**
- Strength: dedicated expertise per function
- Cost: overlapping jurisdictions and inconsistent outcomes
- Fix: deliberate coordination mechanisms such as CAQM
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 adv §1 and §6.
**Links:** ENV-5031; ENV-5051 (CAQM); ENV-5063; ENV-5066 (auditor independence).
**Trap:** Calling fragmentation purely a design flaw with no benefits. Owner 27 lists this as a trap.
**Mains hook:** Argue for targeted coordination bodies where cross-jurisdiction problems recur, not for a single super-regulator.
**Back:** *"Specialise for expertise, coordinate for outcomes."*

### Flashcard ENV-5063 — SPCB and PCC capacity as the "last-mile" gap (advanced)
**Front:** Why are State Pollution Control Boards and PCCs considered the "last-mile" weak point in pollution governance?
**Definition (learn):** SPCBs and PCCs ordinarily grant and revoke Consent to Establish and Operate and enforce on site. Parliamentary standing committees, CAG-type audits and independent assessments have flagged variable technical capacity, staffing shortfalls and, in some instances, concerns about independence from State government or industry (⚠️ owner 27 adv §2, documented critique).
**Breakdown:**
- Standards are national; enforcement is State-level
- Capacity: technical staff, monitoring, budgets
- Independence: boards answer to their State governments
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Capacity claims must be attributed to a specific report, audit or study (⚠️ owner 27 adv §5).
**Links:** ENV-5035; ENV-5036; ENV-5027 (local constraint); ENV-5068 (NGT dependency).
**Trap:** Generalising a capacity failure to every board without a dated report.
**Mains hook:** The 10-mark answer: name the SPCB consent-monitoring gap and cite one audit or committee report.
**Back:** *"A national standard is only as strong as the State board that enforces it."*

### Flashcard ENV-5064 — Science-to-policy independence: government-linked research bodies (advanced)
**Front:** What is the independence question for government-linked scientific institutions such as WII and FSI?
**Definition (learn):** Autonomous institutes and survey organisations provide technical credibility, but their funding and administrative link to the ministry they advise raises a structural question about scientific independence. Owner 27 presents this as a governance consideration, not a finding of misconduct (⚠️ owner 27 adv §3, §5).
**Breakdown:**
- WII: population, habitat and connectivity studies inform tiger and wildlife decisions
- FSI: forest-resource evidence; canopy method cannot prove legal status or ecological quality
- BSI and ZSI: taxonomic and distribution evidence; survey coverage varies
- Safeguards: sustained funding, transparent methods, professional protection
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 adv §3 table.
**Links:** ENV-5042 (WII); ENV-5046 (FSI); ENV-5047 (BSI); ENV-5048 (ZSI).
**Trap:** Presenting an independence concern as an allegation against a specific institute.
**Mains hook:** Credibility of evidence depends on funding stability and transparent method, not on the title of the institute.
**Back:** *"Good science needs funding that does not bend the findings."*

### Flashcard ENV-5065 — PARIVESH 3.0 and the Environment Audit Rules, 2025 (📰 reform direction)
**Front:** What reform direction does the Economic Survey 2025–26 (Chapter 10) describe for environmental clearance and monitoring?
**Definition (learn):** The direction is verification-outsourcing rather than new regulators: PARIVESH 3.0 as a single-window clearance and compliance-monitoring hub; Environment Audit Rules, 2025 creating certified third-party environmental auditors; uniform consent guidelines across all SPCBs; a new Blue industry category; and October 2025 green-belt rationalisation by pollution potential (📰 owner 27 §8, owner 27 adv §9).
**Breakdown:**
- PARIVESH 3.0: single window
- Environment Audit Rules, 2025: certified third-party auditors
- Uniform consent guidelines across SPCBs
- CPCB industry categories extended to Blue
- Green-belt norms rationalised (October 2025)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Economic Survey 2025–26, Ch. 10 (owner-cited; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-5063; ENV-5066; ENV-5033 (CPCB categories).
**Trap:** Treating a portal or certified auditor as independent enforcement by itself.
**Mains hook:** Name both the logic (shift verification to accredited auditors, retain sanction with the State) and its failure mode (ENV-5066).
**Back:** *"Survey says: portal, auditors, uniform consents, Blue category, greener belts."*

### Flashcard ENV-5066 — The auditor-independence failure mode (advanced)
**Front:** What is the balanced critique of certified third-party environmental auditing?
**Definition (learn):** Auditors certified in a regime where the audited entity pays can face independence problems that have undermined comparable regimes elsewhere (⚠️ owner 27 adv §9, analytical).
**Breakdown:**
- Logic: auditors bridge the SPCB capacity gap
- Failure mode: the audited entity pays the auditor
- Safeguards: conflict-of-interest rules, measurement-reporting-verification (MRV) checks
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 adv §9.
**Links:** ENV-5065; ENV-5063; ENV-5062.
**Trap:** Treating certification as proof of independence.
**Mains hook:** Name both the design logic and the failure mode in one sentence.
**Back:** *"Who pays the auditor decides whether the audit can bite."*

### Flashcard ENV-5067 — Decriminalisation and why the Water Act needed Article 252(1) (📰 advanced)
**Front:** Why was the Water Act, 1974 criminal provisions amended through a different constitutional route from EPA, Air Act and Indian Forest Act?
**Definition (learn):** The owner records that EPA 1986, Air Act 1981 and Indian Forest Act 1927 were decriminalised through the Jan Vishwas route, while the Water Act 1974 criminal provisions were amended under Article 252(1) because water is a State List subject (📰 owner 27 §8; ⚠️ owner 27 adv §9).
**Breakdown:**
- EPA, Air Act and Forest Act: Jan Vishwas route
- Water Act: Article 252(1) route, linked to State List competence
- Public Liability Insurance Act relief and Environmental Relief Fund for contaminated-site remediation under the 2025 Rules (📰 owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Economic Survey 2025–26, Ch. 10 (owner-cited; not fetched).
**Links:** ENV-5058 (statutory pairing); ENV-5068; ENV-5065.
**Trap:** Describing decriminalisation without explaining why the constitutional route differed. Owner calls this a rare GS-II/GS-III crossover.
**Mains hook:** Explain legislative competence first: Article 252(1) is the route the owner gives for a State List subject. Verify the exact amendment route before citing it in an exam.
**Back:** *"Water is a State subject, so the route was different: decriminalise by competence."*

### Flashcard ENV-5068 — NGT orders depend on executive implementation (advanced)
**Front:** Does an NGT order ensure that pollution is cleaned up?
**Definition (learn):** No. An NGT order directing a State, SPCB, PCC or implementing authority to act is only as effective as that body's capacity and willingness to implement it (⚠️ owner 27 adv §4).
**Breakdown:**
- NGT: adjudicatory apex within its scheduled statutes
- Implementation: executive bodies
- Effect: depends on capacity and compliance
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 adv §4.
**Links:** ENV-5057; ENV-5063; ENV-5062.
**Trap:** "NGT orders are automatically and fully implemented." Owner 27 lists this as a trap.
**Mains hook:** Use the NGT as an example of a check that works only through executive capacity.
**Back:** *"NGT orders the cleanup; the State pays for it."*

### Flashcard ENV-5069 — Legal-form ladder: executive ministry, board, authority, institute, survey, tribunal (advanced)
**Front:** What are the distinct legal forms that environmental bodies take, and why does the form matter?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 27 says to cite each body's precise statutory basis and separate these forms: executive ministry under allocated business; statutory body; authority constituted by notification; subordinate or government survey organisation; autonomous institute or council/society; registered society exercising delegated powers; expert committee; and tribunal (⚠️ owner 27 §8 interpretation caution).
**Breakdown:**
- Executive ministry: MoEFCC (allocation of business)
- Statutory body: CPCB, NBA, NTCA, WCCB, BEE, CAQM
- Authority by notification: NCZMA, SCZMA (EPA)
- Survey organisation: FSI, BSI, ZSI
- Autonomous institute or council: WII, NCSCM, GBPNIHE, ICFRE
- Registered society with delegated powers: NMCG
- Tribunal: NGT
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 27 §8.
**Links:** ENV-5031; ENV-5058; ENV-5032; ENV-5054; ENV-5057.
**Trap:** Calling a research institute or survey organisation a regulator. Form decides power.
**Mains hook:** A precise classification at the start of an answer prevents most conflation errors.
**Back:** *"Ministry allocates, boards statute, authorities notify, institutes advise, surveys count, tribunals decide."*

### GROUP — 28 Species and Current Affairs Tracker (Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard ENV-5070 — Species record as a dated, source-controlled evidence record
**Front:** What is the purpose of a species current-affairs record, and what fields must it separate?
**Definition (learn):** A species record converts a species mention into a dated, source-controlled entry. Identity, place, ecology, threat, IUCN status, Indian law, CITES, CMS, action, outcome and expiry are kept in separate fields (✅ owner 28 §1, §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Identity: common name, accepted scientific name, taxon, synonym
- Place: habitat, occurrence, natural range, native or alien, endemic or not
- Status: IUCN, WPA Schedule, CITES Appendix, CMS listing (four separate systems)
- Action and outcome: institution, programme, survey and as-on date
- Expiry: recheck date and next controlling source
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 §5 required record table.
**Links:** ENV-5071 to ENV-5081 (method cards); ENV-5077 (expiry); ENV-5073 (four systems).
**Trap:** Filling a missing field from memory. Owner 28 says mark it "verification pending".
**Mains hook:** Build species answers as identity to range to threat to four status checks to institution to outcome.
**Back:** *"One species, many fields: identity, place, status, action, outcome, expiry."*

### Flashcard ENV-5071 — Accepted name, synonym and common name
**Front:** Why must a species card separate the common name, accepted scientific name and synonym?
**Definition (learn):** The accepted scientific name is the currently accepted name in a recognised taxonomic source. Common names can be non-unique, and synonyms and former combinations must be retained (✅ owner 28 §2, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Common name: may be shared by different taxa
- Accepted name: the controlling identity
- Synonym: former or alternative name, kept for older statutes and news
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 §3 change-control steps.
**Links:** ENV-5080 (split and lump); ENV-5088 (Foxtail orchid); ENV-5089 (Gooty tarantula).
**Trap:** A common name in the news proves the taxonomy. It does not.
**Mains hook:** Start every species answer with the accepted name and say which name a statute uses.
**Back:** *"Common names mislead; the accepted name is the anchor."*

### Flashcard ENV-5072 — Native, endemic, occurrence and natural range are different fields
**Front:** Which of native, endemic, occurrence, natural range and captive presence answer different questions?
**Definition (learn):** Native means naturally occurring without human introduction. Endemic means naturally restricted to a defined region. Occurrence is verified presence at a stated place and date (✅ owner 28 §2, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Native: not introduced by humans
- Endemic: restricted to a region; not a synonym for native, rare, threatened or protected
- Occurrence: presence at one place and date; does not prove breeding, abundance or complete range
- Natural range: the full natural distribution
- Captive presence: does not establish wild range
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 §6.
**Links:** ENV-5070; ENV-5088 (range beyond NE India); ENV-5089 (range extension); ENV-5036.
**Trap:** One locality proves endemism. It proves occurrence only (owner 28 §7).
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder: occurrence, then range, then endemism. Each needs its own source.
**Back:** *"Occurrence is a point, range is a map, endemism is a boundary."*

### Flashcard ENV-5073 — Four status systems: IUCN, WPA, CITES and CMS
**Front:** Which four status systems must be kept separate for a species, and what does each control?
**Definition (learn):** IUCN evaluates extinction risk. The Wild Life (Protection) Act supplies Indian legal status. CITES regulates international trade. CMS coordinates migratory-range conservation (✅ owner 28 §3).
**Breakdown:**
- IUCN: global or regional category, criteria, assessment year
- WPA: Schedule (legal status in India)
- CITES: Appendix (trade)
- CMS: Appendix or instrument (migratory cooperation)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Each has its own legal or assessment source and operative date (owner 28 §6).
**Links:** ENV-5074 (IUCN); ENV-5083 (WPA Gazette); ENV-5084 to ENV-5085 (CITES); ENV-5086 to ENV-5087 (CMS); 05, 08, 09, 10 (owners).
**Trap:** An IUCN category automatically fixes WPA, CITES or CMS status. Check each instrument.
**Mains hook:** Present four status checks as four sentences, each with its own date.
**Back:** *"IUCN fears extinction, WPA protects in India, CITES trades, CMS migrates."*

### Flashcard ENV-5074 — IUCN category: scale and assessment year
**Front:** What must accompany an IUCN Red List category in a current-affairs statement?
**Definition (learn):** Every IUCN category needs its scale (global or regional), criteria where relevant, and the assessment year. The assessment year does not change when a webpage is retrieved (✅ owner 28 §2, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Scale: global or regional
- Category: for example CR, EN, VU
- Criteria: where relevant
- Assessment year: fixed
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 §6: category must include scale and assessment year.
**Links:** ENV-5089 (CR, assessed 1 January 2008); ENV-5090 (EN, cited 2019); ENV-5091 (LC, cited 2021); ENV-5082 (training).
**Trap:** A current webpage means a current assessment. It does not.
**Mains hook:** Justify using the assessment year as a required field in any species answer.
**Back:** *"Category, scale, year: never one without the others."*

### Flashcard ENV-5075 — Assessment, publication and operative dates
**Front:** Why must a species record separate the publication date, the assessment or survey date, and the operative or effective date?
**Definition (learn):** Publication date is when a source appeared. Assessment or survey date is when the evidence was collected. Operative or effective date is when a statute, notification or treaty listing took legal effect (✅ owner 28 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Publication: when the news or document appeared
- Assessment or survey: when the evidence was gathered
- Operative: when legal effect began
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Example: CMS entry into force 27 June 2026 (ENV-5087); CITES CoP20 closed 5 December 2025 (ENV-5084).
**Links:** ENV-5076; ENV-5082 to ENV-5087.
**Trap:** Using the publication date as the operative date.
**Mains hook:** State the operative date when claiming a legal change.
**Back:** *"Published is not operative; assessed is not published."*

### Flashcard ENV-5076 — Status verbs: proposed, adopted, notified, effective, launched, recorded, recovered
**Front:** Which status verbs are not interchangeable in a species or policy statement?
**Definition (learn):** Proposed, adopted, notified, effective, launched, funded, implemented, assessed, recorded, rediscovered and recovered each describe a different stage (✅ owner 28 §2; advanced table §13.1).
**Breakdown:**
- Announced or targeted: policy intention, not achievement
- Adopted: decision taken at a meeting, not necessarily in force for every party
- Notified or listed: legal status, not implementation
- Launched or funded: programme started, not completed
- Recorded or rediscovered: detected at a place and date, not a resident population
- Reintroduced or released: human-assisted return, not self-sustaining
- Assessed or downlisted: risk reassessed, not programme-caused recovery
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 §13.1 table.
**Links:** ENV-5075; ENV-5086 (adopted); ENV-5087 (entry into force); ENV-5078 (causal attribution).
**Trap:** Substituting "adopted" for "in force", or "launched" for "achieved".
**Mains hook:** Each claim needs its verb, date and scope.
**Back:** *"Adopted is a decision, notified is a law, recovered is a population."*

### Flashcard ENV-5077 — Expiry, recheck and "verification pending"
**Front:** When must a species or current-affairs field be re-checked, and what do you write if you cannot verify it?
**Definition (learn):** No card is permanently current. Each dynamic field carries a recheck trigger and a controlling source. If the source cannot be rechecked, write "verification pending" (✅ owner 28 §5.1; advanced §5).
**Breakdown:**
- IUCN: recheck each exam cycle and on reassessment
- WPA: after Act or Gazette amendment
- CITES and CMS: after CoP amendment, reservation or annotation
- Population: when a comparable newer survey appears
