- Court and programme: after later order or implementation report
- Taxonomy: after cited revision
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 §5.1 control table.
**Links:** ENV-5070; ENV-5079 (reconciliation); ENV-5083 to ENV-5087 (dated docket).
**Trap:** Filling an expired field from memory.
**Mains hook:** A maintained tracker is a dated tracker. Show the recheck trigger.
**Back:** *"Every card expires; say so, or say verification pending."*

### Flashcard ENV-5078 — Fact, inference and causal attribution (advanced)
**Front:** What three layers must a species answer keep separate?
**Definition (learn):** (1) Fact: the primary source states the record. (2) Inference: the record supports a bounded ecological or governance interpretation. (3) Causal attribution: evidence that the intervention, rather than changed method, coverage, taxonomy or external conditions, produced the outcome (✅ owner 28 adv §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Downlisting may reflect recovery, better evidence or revised taxonomy
- A higher count may reflect wider survey coverage
- Captive-breeding output may strengthen ex-situ capacity while wild survival is unchanged
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 adv §6.
**Links:** ENV-5070; ENV-5076; ENV-5079.
**Trap:** Treating a count increase as biological growth (owner 28 adv §8).
**Mains hook:** Attribute an outcome to a programme only with a method comparison.
**Back:** *"Fact says what, inference says so what, attribution says because of what."*

### Flashcard ENV-5079 — Advanced reconciliation matrix for conflicting records
**Front:** When two species records conflict, what is the first check for each type of conflict?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 28 adv §2 lists six conflict types and the safe treatment for each.
**Breakdown:**
- Two scientific names: accepted name, synonym, split or lump; retain both and state the concept
- Global and Indian status differ: scale, criteria, year; record both
- Two population figures: survey area, method, season, reference year; do not average
- News and Gazette differ: publication date, authority, operative date; the legal instrument controls legal status
- CoP outcome and live Appendix differ: adoption, entry into force, reservation, annotation; the treaty database controls
- Programme and field report differ: announced versus measured outcome
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 adv §2.
**Links:** ENV-5080; ENV-5081; ENV-5076.
**Trap:** Silently choosing the more dramatic figure. Expose the reason for difference.
**Mains hook:** Use the matrix to explain why a newer report fails to supersede an older record.
**Back:** *"Name, scale, method, instrument: check the match before you pick a figure."*

### Flashcard ENV-5080 — Taxonomic change control: splits, lumps and legal names (advanced)
**Front:** How should a tracker handle a species split, a lump or a legal schedule that keeps an older name?
**Definition (learn):** Record the accepted name and authority. Preserve synonyms. On a split, decide which population belongs to which successor taxon before transferring range or status. On a lump, do not add estimates mechanically. A legal schedule may keep an older name; taxonomy does not amend the law (✅ owner 28 adv §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Split: assign former populations to successors
- Lump: check methods and coverage before adding counts
- Shared common name: an ambiguity flag, not proof
- Legal schedule may keep an older name
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 adv §3.
**Links:** ENV-5071; ENV-5088 (Foxtail orchid: a former name *Epidendrum retusum* as basionym); ENV-5093 (Prosopis: taxonomy change).
**Trap:** An accepted taxonomic revision changes a statutory schedule automatically. It does not.
**Mains hook:** Connect old and new names without pretending taxonomy amended the law.
**Back:** *"Taxonomy renames; the Gazette still decides the Schedule."*

### Flashcard ENV-5081 — Source authority is claim-specific (advanced)
**Front:** Is an "official" source a universal quality score?
**Definition (learn):** No. Source authority depends on the proposition: a Gazette is strongest for legal notification, not population trend; IUCN is strongest for its dated assessment, not Indian status; a treaty database for listing and annotation, not habitat condition; a repeated named survey for comparable biological trend; a press release for what was announced, not independent outcome (✅ owner 28 adv §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Gazette: legal notification
- IUCN: global assessment
- Treaty database: listing and annotation
- Repeated survey: trend
- Press release: announcement
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 adv §7.
**Links:** ENV-5079; ENV-5083; ENV-5084; ENV-5086.
**Trap:** A news article as the sole legal authority. Owner 28 lists this as a trap.
**Mains hook:** Match the source to the exact claim.
**Back:** *"Match the authority to the claim, not the claim to the authority."*

### Flashcard ENV-5082 — IUCN India assessor training, 8–11 July 2026 (📰)
**Front:** What did the IUCN report of 31 July 2026 say about India's Red List assessment capacity?
**Definition (learn):** Training for a pool of Red List assessors was held 8–11 July 2026, intended to contribute to a Vision 2030 goal of assessing 11,000 taxa (✅ owner 28 §5.2; IUCN story dated 31 July 2026, not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Event: fourth IUCN Red List assessors training, India
- Dates: training 8–11 July 2026; story 31 July 2026
- Objective: 11,000 taxa (Vision 2030); an objective, not completed assessments
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8–11 July 2026; 31 July 2026; 11,000 taxa target (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5074; ENV-5081; 05_IUCN-Red-List-and-Endemism (Topic 05).
**Trap:** Treating training as completed assessments or species recovery. Owner 28 says it is capacity, not outcome.
**Mains hook:** Capacity is a precondition for evidence, not evidence itself.
**Back:** *"Training in July, objective of 11,000, outcome not yet."*

### Flashcard ENV-5083 — Gazette S.O. 2360(E): amendment to Schedules I, II and IV (📰)
**Front:** What does Gazette S.O. 2360(E) do, and what are its dates?
**Definition (learn):** Dated 27 May 2025 and published in the issue dated 28 May 2025; it amends Schedules I, II and IV of the Wildlife Protection Act. It does not replace the post-2022 four-Schedule structure (✅ owner 28 §5.2; eGazette link cited, not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Dated: 27 May 2025
- Published: 28 May 2025
- Amends: Schedules I, II and IV
- Does not: replace the four-Schedule structure
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 May 2025; 28 May 2025 (owner 28).
**Links:** 08_Wildlife-Protection-Act-and-Schedules (Topic 08); ENV-5073; ENV-5075.
**Trap:** Treating the publication date as the operative date, or the 2025 amendment as a new structure.
**Mains hook:** Check the Gazette for the current Schedule before using it.
**Back:** *"Gazette 2360 amended I, II and IV in May 2025; the structure stayed."*

### Flashcard ENV-5084 — CITES CoP20: closed 5 December 2025, 82 new listings (📰)
**Front:** When did CITES CoP20 close, and what did the Secretariat outcome record?
**Definition (learn):** CoP20 closed on 5 December 2025; the Secretariat outcome was published on 16 December 2025 and records 82 new listings (✅ owner 28 §5.2; CITES news page cited, not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- CoP20 close: 5 December 2025
- Secretariat outcome: 16 December 2025
- New listings: 82
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 5 December 2025; 16 December 2025; 82 listings (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5085 (Appendices valid); 09_CITES-and-Wildlife-Trade (Topic 09); ENV-5075.
**Trap:** Treating the CoP close as the date of entry into force. ENV-5085 gives the Appendices' validity date.
**Mains hook:** Separate adoption, outcome and legal validity.
**Back:** *"Closed in December, listed 82, valid from March."*

### Flashcard ENV-5085 — CITES Appendices valid from 5 March 2026
**Front:** From what date is the official CITES Appendices edition valid?
**Definition (learn):** The official Appendices edition is valid from 5 March 2026 (✅ owner 28 §5.2; CITES PDF cited, not fetched). Use the current Checklist for any named species.
**Breakdown:**
- Edition: official Appendices
- Valid from: 5 March 2026
- Named-species check: current CITES Checklist
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 5 March 2026 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5084; ENV-5073; 09_CITES-and-Wildlife-Trade (Topic 09).
**Trap:** Using an older Appendix edition after a CoP amendment.
**Mains hook:** Cite the Appendix edition and its date with every trade claim.
**Back:** *"Appendices in force from 5 March 2026; check the Checklist for a species."*

### Flashcard ENV-5086 — CMS COP15: 40 taxa adopted (Notification 2026/006)
**Front:** What did CMS Notification 2026/006 record for COP15?
**Definition (learn):** The notification records 40 taxa adopted at COP15 for amendments to the Appendices (✅ owner 28 §5.2; CMS PDF cited, not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Source: CMS Notification 2026/006
- Taxa: 40 adopted at COP15
- Adoption is a decision, not yet entry into force
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 40 taxa (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5087; ENV-5076 (adopted vs effective); 10_CMS-Bonn-Convention-Migratory-Species (Topic 10).
**Trap:** Treating "adopted" as "in force for every party".
**Mains hook:** Use the adoption-versus-entry-into-force gap to show treaty timing.
**Back:** *"Forty adopted at COP15; adoption starts the clock."*

### Flashcard ENV-5087 — CMS entry into force 27 June 2026 (Notification 2026/012)
**Front:** When did the COP15 Appendix amendments enter into force under CMS Notification 2026/012, and what qualifies it?
**Definition (learn):** Entry into force on 27 June 2026, subject to reservations (✅ owner 28 §5.2; CMS PDF cited, not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Notification: 2026/012
- Entry into force: 27 June 2026
- Qualifier: reservations
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 June 2026 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5086; ENV-5075; ENV-5077 (reservation check).
**Trap:** Ignoring reservations. The entry-into-force date is qualified.
**Mains hook:** Pair the date with the reservation check before asserting a species is CMS-listed.
**Back:** *"Adopted at COP15, in force 27 June 2026, unless reserved."*

### Flashcard ENV-5088 — Foxtail orchid (Rhynchostylis retusa): identity, range and State-flower caution
**Front:** What is the accepted identity of the foxtail orchid, what is its life-form, and what must not be inferred from its State association?
**Definition (learn):** Accepted name *Rhynchostylis retusa* (L.) Blume, Orchidaceae; basionym *Epidendrum retusum* L. It is an epiphytic orchid; epiphytic support is not parasitism (✅ owner 28 §5.3; ✅ Wikipedia; GBIF backbone record cited by owner, not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Accepted name: *Rhynchostylis retusa* (L.) Blume
- Family: Orchidaceae (subfamily Epidendroideae, subtribe Aeridinae)
- Life-form: epiphyte on tree trunks in open forests or forest margins, about 300–1,500 m
- Natural range: South and Southeast Asia, including Bhutan, Cambodia, China (Guizhou, Yunnan), India, Indonesia, Laos, Malaysia, Myanmar, Nepal, Philippines, Singapore, Sri Lanka, Thailand, Vietnam (✅ Wikipedia)
- Within India: most common in the Northeast, Odisha and Andhra Pradesh (✅ Wikipedia)
- State flower: Arunachal Pradesh and Assam (Assam name *kopou phool*) (✅ Wikipedia; verify the competent State source when the designation is tested, per owner 28)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ: 2026 Prelims GS-I Q36 (Set A, page 19); provisional 2026 key held locally; answer not recorded here (✅ owner 28 PYQ block).
**Links:** ENV-5071 (accepted name); ENV-5072 (range vs endemism); ENV-5080 (basionym/taxonomy); ENV-5047 (BSI).
**Trap:** Calling the species endemic to north-east India because it is a State flower. The natural range extends well beyond NE India; State association does not establish endemism.
**Mains hook:** Use the orchid as a taxonomy-first example: identity and range before any legal or conservation claim.
**Back:** *"Accepted name first; epiphyte, not parasite; State flower is not endemism."*

### Flashcard ENV-5089 — Peacock or Gooty tarantula (Poecilotheria metallica): identity, assessment year and range
**Front:** What is the assessed status of *Poecilotheria metallica*, and what assessment year must be stated?
**Definition (learn):** An arboreal spider with a restricted natural Indian forest range; its direct IUCN record is global Critically Endangered, assessed 1 January 2008 (✅ owner 28 §5.4; IUCN 3.1 category ✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Common names: peacock tarantula; Gooty sapphire tarantula; Gooty sapphire ornamental tree spider; metallic tarantula (✅ Wikipedia)
- Category: Critically Endangered (IUCN 3.1); global scale
- Assessment year: 1 January 2008 (✅ owner 28; the Wikipedia reference is an IUCN 2008 entry)
- Habitat: deciduous forest in a small reserve forest of under 100 km²; lives in holes of tall trees, builds funnel webs (✅ Wikipedia)
- Range: native to Andhra Pradesh; a 2019 sighting near Gingee extended the range to the Eastern Ghats of Tamil Nadu (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ: 2025 Prelims GS-I Q37 (Set A); key available locally, answer not recorded here (✅ owner 28 PYQ block).
**Links:** ENV-5074 (assessment year); ENV-5072 (restricted range and occurrence); ENV-5078 (captive or pet-trade occurrence is not wild range); ENV-5034; ENV-5048 (ZSI taxonomy).
**Trap:** Describing the species as freshly assessed in 2025 or 2026 because it appeared in a PYQ or article. Owner 28 says state 2008. Also: the owner's "restricted natural Indian forest range" wording does not mention the 2019 Tamil Nadu record that Wikipedia reports. Check the current IUCN range before writing a range claim.
**Mains hook:** Use the species to show that an assessment year must travel with the category; qualify with the range extension.
**Back:** *"Critically Endangered in 2008, Andhra-born, holes in tall trees, not 2026."*

### Flashcard ENV-5090 — Western hoolock gibbon (Hoolock hoolock): Topic 05 owner, tracker cross-link
**Front:** What is the accepted name and IUCN category of the western hoolock gibbon, and which owner handles its PYQ?
**Definition (learn):** *Hoolock hoolock* (Harlan, 1834); IUCN Endangered (IUCN 3.1), citing a 2019 assessment (⚠️ Wikipedia; the assessment year must be re-checked against the IUCN record). The PYQ belongs to Environment Topic 05, not Topic 28 (✅ owner 28 §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Accepted name: *Hoolock hoolock* (Harlan, 1834); originally *Simia hoolock*
- Category: Endangered (cited 2019 assessment, ⚠️)
- India: Assam, Mizoram and Meghalaya; a subspecies *H. h. mishmiensis* in the Mishmi Hills; Namdapha National Park mentioned (✅ Wikipedia)
- Habitat: contiguous broad-leaved, wet evergreen and mixed evergreen forests, often in hilly terrain (✅ Wikipedia)
- Diet: mainly ripe fruit (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ: 2026 Q22 (owner 28 §9; owner 05 holds the substantive doctrine).
**Links:** 05_IUCN-Red-List-and-Endemism (Topic 05); ENV-5074; ENV-5072; ENV-5070.
**Trap:** Answering a Topic 05 IUCN question from the Topic 28 tracker. Use the Topic 05 owner for doctrine and this card only for the dated field.
**Mains hook:** Use the gibbon as a canopy-forest species when linking forest cover to species status; qualify that canopy loss is not the only threat without a source.
**Back:** *"Hoolock: Endangered, Northeast forests, canopy-bound, route to Topic 05."*

### Flashcard ENV-5091 — Amur falcon (Falco amurensis): Topic 10 owner, migration and IUCN Least Concern
**Front:** What is the accepted name and IUCN category of the Amur falcon, and what is its route through India?
**Definition (learn):** *Falco amurensis* (Radde, 1863); IUCN Least Concern, citing a 2021 BirdLife assessment (⚠️ Wikipedia; re-check the IUCN record). Its CMS treatment belongs to Topic 10 (✅ owner 28 §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Breeding range: East Asia (Transbaikalia, Amurland, northern Mongolia, parts of North Korea; SE Siberia and northern China) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Non-breeding range: southern and East African coasts; reaches southern Africa (✅ Wikipedia)
- India: flocks on a "broad front" through India and Sri Lanka (✅ Wikipedia)
- Indian stopover and hunting: Pangti village, Nagaland, mass trapping reported in 2012 and a later protection campaign; hunting in parts of north-eastern India (✅ Wikipedia)
- Route: across the Arabian Sea, sometimes via the Maldives (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ: 2026 Q27 (owner 28 §9). Topic 10 owns the CMS status.
**Links:** 10_CMS-Bonn-Convention-Migratory-Species (Topic 10); ENV-5073 (CMS as separate system); ENV-5086 (CMS COP15); ENV-5087 (entry into force).
**Trap:** Inferring CMS listing from the migration route or from IUCN Least Concern. Each needs its own instrument.
**Mains hook:** Use the falcon's route to show weakest-link migratory governance: a species is only as protected as its least protective range state.
**Back:** *"Least Concern on IUCN, mass trapping in Nagaland, migrating to Africa; CMS is a separate check."*

### Flashcard ENV-5092 — Behaviour of big cats: lions, cheetahs and leopards (2024 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2024 Prelims GS-I PYQ on big-cat behaviour ask, and how should a tracker card handle it?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2024 Q25 demand on the behaviour of lions, cheetahs and leopards (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). The owner records no status or answer. Keep the card to behaviour facts with a source.
**Breakdown:**
- Lions: social, pride-living (⚠️ general knowledge; verify before citing)
- Cheetahs: typically solitary or in coalitions; sprinting predator (⚠️)
- Leopards: solitary, arboreal-capable (⚠️)
- Status fields: not owned in file 28; verification pending for any IUCN, WPA, CITES or CMS claim
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Q25; key available locally, answer not recorded here (✅ owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5073 (four status systems); ENV-5077 (verification pending); 06_Protected-Area-Network-India (Topic 06).
**Trap:** Mixing behaviour facts from different species (for example, pride living applied to leopards).
**Mains hook:** Use behaviour only to explain a conservation claim, not as a status claim.
**Back:** *"Lions gather, cheetahs sprint, leopards hide: check each species separately."*

### Flashcard ENV-5093 — Prosopis juliflora (accepted name now Neltuma juliflora): 2018 PYQ invasive impact
**Front:** What did the 2018 Prelims GS-I PYQ ask about *Prosopis juliflora*, and what is its current accepted name?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2018 Q86 demand on the ecological and biodiversity impact of *Prosopis juliflora* as an invasive plant (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). Wikipedia gives the accepted name as *Neltuma juliflora* (Sw.) Raf., formerly *Prosopis juliflora* (✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Native: Mexico, South America, the Caribbean (✅ Wikipedia)
- India: brought by the British to Tamil Nadu in 1877 to plant in arid southern India (✅ Wikipedia)
- Invasive weed in India, Africa, Asia and Australia; on the EU list of Invasive Alien Species of Union concern since 2019 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Impacts: monoculture that denies native plants water and light; blocks water access and erodes grassland (Australia); shelters feral pigs and cats; invades grassland near Bundala National Park, Sri Lanka; neurotoxic pods poison livestock; regrowth from roots; sugar sources for mosquitoes (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q86 (owner 28). The owner uses the older name; Wikipedia gives the newer accepted name.
**Links:** ENV-5080 (taxonomic change control); ENV-5071 (synonym); 04_Biodiversity-Levels-and-Hotspots (Topic 04) for invasives context.
**Trap:** A species name in a PYQ may now be a synonym. Record both names and the taxonomic concept.
**Mains hook:** Use Prosopis to show that invasive species removal differs from restoration (owner 28 adv §4).
**Back:** *"Mexican import, British planting, now a weed: check the name before the impact."*

### Flashcard ENV-5094 — Himalayan nettle (Girardinia diversifolia): 2019 PYQ on sustainable uses
**Front:** What do the 2019 PYQ demand and the Wikipedia record say about the Himalayan nettle?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2019 Q31 demand on sustainable uses of the Himalayan nettle (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). Wikipedia gives the scientific name *Girardinia diversifolia* (Link) Friis, family Urticaceae (✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Habitat: hilly and mountainous regions of southern and eastern Asia; tropical Africa and Madagascar (✅ Wikipedia)
- Altitude: 100–2,800 m in one passage; "up to 3000 m" in another (⚠️ Wikipedia conflict, flag)
- Uses: bast fibre (*allo* in Nepal) for clothing, bags, mats and rope; fodder, fuel wood, live fences and traditional medicine (✅ Wikipedia)
- Caution: leaves have stinging hairs causing a painful rash lasting over a week (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Q31 (owner 28). Altitude conflict unresolved.
**Links:** ENV-5070 (use claims must not replace conservation evidence); 11_Forest-Types-and-Forest-Rights-Act (Topic 11) for forest-use context.
**Trap:** Presenting a use-value as proof that the species is conservation-secure.
**Mains hook:** Plant-fibre use can support livelihoods, but the tracker must still carry status and range fields.
**Back:** *"Nettle makes fibre and rash; check the altitude before you quote it."*

### Flashcard ENV-5095 — Indian elephant: ecology, gestation, calving and State distribution (2020 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2020 Prelims GS-I PYQ on Indian elephants ask?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2020 Q72 demand on the ecology, gestation and calving of Indian elephants and their State distribution (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). Status fields are not owned in file 28.
**Breakdown:**
- Ecology: herbivorous, herd-living (⚠️ general knowledge)
- Calving and gestation: specific reproductive facts to be sourced before use (verification pending)
- State distribution: verification pending from a named survey (for example, the periodic elephant survey)
- Status: IUCN and WPA fields to be checked separately (verification pending)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Q72 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5073; ENV-5077; 05 and 08 owners for IUCN and WPA.
**Trap:** Quoting a gestation or population number from memory without a dated survey.
**Mains hook:** Use elephant corridors as a species case, with a dated survey and a named corridor.
**Back:** *"Elephant facts need a dated survey, not a memory."*

### Flashcard ENV-5096 — Indian birds: frogmouth, barbet, minivet and redstart identification (2020 PYQ)
**Front:** What identification skill does the 2020 Prelims GS-I PYQ on Indian birds test?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2020 Q74 demand on the identification of Indian bird species: frogmouth, barbet, minivet and redstart (✅ owner 28 PYQ block).
**Breakdown:**
- Frogmouth: nocturnal insectivorous bird with a wide gape (⚠️ general)
- Barbet: fruit-eating, with a loud call (⚠️ general)
- Minivet: brightly coloured, flycatcher-like (⚠️ general)
- Redstart: small insectivore, often on streams or rocks (⚠️ general)
- Status: not owned here; check the IUCN and WPA fields separately (verification pending)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Q74 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5070; ENV-5071 (identity first); 06 and 08 owners for protected-area and status.
**Trap:** Naming a bird by its colour alone. Use the identity fields (family, call, habitat).
**Mains hook:** Use bird identity as the first step in any avian conservation case.
**Back:** *"Identify by form and call, then status; colour alone misleads."*

### Flashcard ENV-5097 — Himalayan musk deer (Moschus leucogaster): habitat in Himalayan protected areas (2020 PYQ)
**Front:** What is the accepted name of the musk deer in the Himalayan protected-area PYQ, its IUCN category and the main threat?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2020 Q77 demand on the natural habitat of musk deer in Himalayan protected areas (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). Wikipedia gives *Moschus leucogaster* (Hodgson, 1839), IUCN Endangered, citing a 2015 assessment (Timmins and Duckworth) (✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Habitat: high alpine environments above 2,500 m in the eastern Himalaya (✅ Wikipedia)
- Range: Nepal, India, possibly Bhutan and China; genetic confirmation only for Nepal (✅ Wikipedia)
- Indian records: Yordi Rabe Supse Wildlife Sanctuary, West Siang, Arunachal Pradesh, camera-trap and carcass records unconfirmed genetically (⚠️ Wikipedia)
- CITES Appendix I for populations in Afghanistan, Bhutan, India, Myanmar, Nepal and Pakistan (✅ Wikipedia)
- Main threat: hunting and trade for musk; snares kill females and young (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Q77 (owner 28). IUCN 2015 (✅ Wiki). Not checked against IUCN directly.
**Links:** ENV-5073; ENV-5074; ENV-5075; 09_CITES-and-Wildlife-Trade (Topic 09); 06_Protected-Area-Network-India (Topic 06).
**Trap:** Mixing Himalayan musk deer with the black musk deer (a different species). Genetic confirmation matters for Indian records.
**Mains hook:** Trade-driven species decline shows why CITES Appendix status and protected-area enforcement must be read together.
**Back:** *"Above 2,500 m, hunted for musk, CITES Appendix I for the listed populations."*

### Flashcard ENV-5098 — Plants as natural mosquito repellents (2021 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2021 Prelims GS-I PYQ on mosquito repellents ask?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2021 Q21 demand on plants used as natural mosquito repellents (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). Plant identity and status must be sourced; no answer is recorded here.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: plant names and their repellent uses
- Identity rule: accepted scientific name for each plant
- Status fields: not owned in file 28 (verification pending)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 Q21 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5071 (accepted names); ENV-5093 (Prosopis as a mosquito-sugar-source example); 04 owner for plant biodiversity.
**Trap:** Taking a folk repellent claim as a scientific claim without a source.
**Mains hook:** Use plant-based repellents as a bio-resource example that needs benefit-sharing checks (ENV-5038 for access rules).
**Back:** *"Name the plant first, then the repellent claim."*

### Flashcard ENV-5099 — Animals that roll up to protect vulnerable body parts (2021 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2021 Prelims GS-I PYQ on animals rolling up ask?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2021 Q23 demand on animals that roll up to protect vulnerable body parts (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). The owner does not record which animals; verify each against a source.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: defence behaviour (rolling up)
- Identity: name each animal with its accepted scientific name
- Status fields: verification pending
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 Q23 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5070; ENV-5096 (behaviour and identity); ENV-5092 (behaviour cards).
**Trap:** Generalising a defence behaviour to all animals in a group.
**Mains hook:** Use defence behaviour to illustrate adaptation, only with a named species.
**Back:** *"Behaviour belongs to a named species, not to a group."*

### Flashcard ENV-5100 — Moringa, drumstick and tamarind: properties and uses (2021 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2021 Prelims GS-I PYQ on Moringa, drumstick and tamarind ask?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2021 Q63 demand on the properties and uses of these tree species (✅ owner 28 PYQ block).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: properties and uses
- Identity: accepted names for each tree
- Status: not owned in file 28 (verification pending)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 Q63 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5071; ENV-5098 (plant use); ENV-5094 (use without conservation evidence).
**Trap:** Mixing edible and medicinal uses. Check the specific property claimed.
**Mains hook:** Use common trees as examples of bio-resource value needing accurate identity.
**Back:** *"Tree, property, use: name each before you claim."*

### Flashcard ENV-5101 — Himalayan forest fungus: characteristics and cultivation (2022 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2022 Prelims GS-I PYQ on a Himalayan forest fungus ask?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2022 Q45 demand on the characteristics and cultivation of a Himalayan forest fungus (✅ owner 28 PYQ block; stem verified, OCR artefact resolved).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: fungal characteristics and cultivation
- Identity: accepted name of the fungus (not named in owner; verify)
- Status: verification pending
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Q45 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5071; ENV-5094 (use claim); ENV-5070.
**Trap:** Assuming all forest fungi are cultivable. Check the species.
**Mains hook:** Use a fungus as an example of a resource species where cultivation may reduce wild pressure, but only with evidence.
**Back:** *"Name the fungus; cultivation claims need its species."*

### Flashcard ENV-5102 — Nocturnal behaviour among Indian fauna (2023 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2023 Prelims GS-I PYQ on nocturnal behaviour ask, and what qualifier must be kept?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2023 Q14 demand on nocturnal behaviour among Indian fauna, with a qualifier on population-specific behaviour (✅ owner 28 PYQ block).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: which fauna are nocturnal
- Qualifier: behaviour can vary by population
- Status: verification pending
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Q14 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5092 (behaviour cards); ENV-5096.
**Trap:** Treating a behaviour as species-wide when the source describes one population.
**Mains hook:** Qualify behaviour claims with population and location.
**Back:** *"Night behaviour belongs to a population, not always the species."*

### Flashcard ENV-5103 — Honeybee waggle dance (2023 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2023 Prelims GS-I PYQ on the waggle dance ask?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2023 Q15 demand on the waggle dance communication of honeybee colonies (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). General knowledge: the dance signals the direction and distance of food sources (⚠️ verify before citing).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: communication of food location
- Species: honeybees (Apis)
- Status: not a conservation claim; verify
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Q15 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5070; ENV-5092 (behaviour cards).
**Trap:** Treating the dance as a mating or defence signal.
**Mains hook:** Use it as a communication example only.
**Back:** *"The waggle dance tells the hive where the food is."*

### Flashcard ENV-5104 — Indian squirrel: nesting, food storage and diet (2023 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2023 Prelims GS-I PYQ on the Indian squirrel ask?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2023 Q17 demand on nesting, food storage and diet of Indian squirrels (✅ owner 28 PYQ block).
**Breakdown:**

- Identity: name the squirrel species (verify)
- Status: verification pending
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Q17 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5096 (identity); ENV-5070.
**Trap:** Generalising one squirrel's habits to every Indian squirrel.
**Mains hook:** Use squirrels as seed-dispersal examples only with a source.
**Back:** *"Name the squirrel, then its nest and its store."*

### Flashcard ENV-5105 — Orangutan tool-making (stick scraping for insects) (2023 PYQ)
**Front:** What does the 2023 Prelims GS-I PYQ on orangutan tool-making ask?
**Definition (learn):** The owner routes a 2023 Q19 demand on orangutan tool-making, including stick scraping to obtain insects (✅ owner 28 PYQ block). Orangutans are great apes native to Southeast Asia (⚠️ general knowledge).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: stick tool-making for insect extraction
- Genus: *Pongo*; Southeast Asia (⚠️ verify)
- Status: verification pending (IUCN and CITES checked separately)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Q19 (owner 28).
**Links:** ENV-5073; ENV-5092; ENV-5070.
**Trap:** Placing orangutans in India. Their natural range is Southeast Asia.
**Mains hook:** Use great-ape tool use as a behaviour example, not as a status claim.
**Back:** *"Orangutans use sticks in Southeast Asia, not in Indian forests."*

### Flashcard ENV-5106 — Trap: PYQ routing blocks are answer-neutral
**Front:** Why do the PYQ routing blocks in file 28 not state the answer or the truth set?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 28 keeps every routing block answer-neutral: demand, source status and owner requirement only. Correct letters, statement truth sets, option elimination and dropped-question claims stay in the solved PYQ corpus (✅ owner 28 §10, PYQ notes).
**Breakdown:**
- Routing blocks: demand and provenance
- Solved PYQ corpus: answer control
- Provisional 2026 keys: not inferred
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 28 §10.
**Links:** ENV-5088 (Foxtail, provisional 2026 key); ENV-5089 (2025 key available); ENV-5102.
**Trap:** Inferring an answer from the owner routing block or from a species name in the PYQ.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable to this Prelims-only routing; use the solved PYQ corpus for answers.
**Back:** *"The routing block names the demand; the answer key lives elsewhere."*
